A finished co-op replay now offers Download JSON, one `jackioh-duos-replay` v1 file of the seat's views with each step's index and round, the seat and the build's catalog version, with no seed, log, deck, match id, account or ping — though it carries the teammate's duel as the team shares it, so sharing the file shares that — and the recent games page opens such a file again on the co-op replay player. It takes R1242000, the first number of issue #1242's block. It edits [[§9.5]], [[§13.9]] and [[R1141000]].

**[[R1253000]] is a live action the engine panics on (issue #1253, 2026-10-19)**: a live `reduce` ran with no unwind guard, so a panic killed the actor's task, its sender was never answered, and a clock's action that panicked was never tried again, leaving the match stalled until the reaper's ceiling draw. Now the actor answers it `internal`, keeps the answer under its nonce and files the finding, and a clock's action that panics, or any server action in a co-op match, ends the match at once as the ceiling's draw. It takes R1253000, the first number of issue #1253's block. It edits [[§9.5]], [[§13.9]] and [[R112]].
**[[R1256000]] counts fatigue, traps, switches and Radiant upgrades per seat (issue #1256, patch v0.3.X)**: the pillars [[§1]] rests the design on had no measure, so each seat in a game record keeps six counts — `fatigueDraws` and the nominal pre-Armor `fatigueDamage` off the `fatigue` events ([[§2.4]], [[R3]]), `trapsSet` off the plays from hand whose `cardPlayed` carries a `formerId` ([[R227]]), `trapsFired` off the `trapFired` events' `controller` ([[R154]]), so a Trap fired by the opponent's effect counts for its controller, `positionSwitches` off each `positionSwitched` for its controller in the after-state, and `radiantUpgrades` off an instance's first `radiantSet` cue that finds it Radiant, for that card's controller. They are absent on older records, and a count that is not a non-negative whole number is refused, as [[R1147000]]'s malformed fields are. A new `GameOverReason` for fatigue was rejected: the notebooks' "fatigue kill" is the stated approximation of a `hero-death` game whose loser took a fatigue hit. It takes R1256000, the first number of issue #1256's block. It edits [[§9.11]] and [[R376]].

**[[R1266000]] is the win-rate table a replay folds against (issue #1266, patch v0.3.15)**: Meditative #50 CN Tech's table had shipped empty, and filling it would have refused every earlier game that cast CN Tech or Meditative+ #2 NA Tech, since a fold under an older shipped catalog still read the build's table ([[R767000]]). Each carried version now keeps the table it shipped with: each its own file since [[R1329000]], the empty table before, and a fold reads that table before a test's and the build's. Issue #1266 filled the table for the first time, from a development run of v0.3.15's cards, so it is `provisional`. It takes R1266000, the first number of issue #1266's block. It edits [[§8.8]], [[R1101]] and [[R767000]].

**[[R1279000]] defers every opponent-held prompt to the holder's next turn (issue #1279, patch v0.3.X)**: no card lets the opponent decide while they are not the active player. C #8 asks its mode questions, C #9 its keep and C #90's reward G its discard at the start of the holder's next turn, each on the holder's own turn clock with a timeout answered by the AI policy ([[R79]]); a discard "of your choice" still asks its discarder while it resolves ([[R682]]), on the discarder's turn now. Answering for the holder, randomly or by the controller, was rejected: the issue prescribes the start-of-turn shape. It takes R1279000, the first number of issue #1279's block. It edits [[§8.6]] (rows 8, 9 and 90) and [[§10.6]].
**[[R1284000]] is a development run with a card forced, and two runs compared (issue #1284, 2026-10-19)**: a balance patch's notes quoted a card's win rate over about 150 of 2,000 All Random games, and #1149's own notes put that noise at five points. Now `cargo jackioh stats --force <id>` plays a run in which the card is in one seat's deck in every game, and `cargo jackioh stats compare` reads two such runs of the same seeds and prints the holder's win rate on each side, the paired difference with its intervals, and a line when the card is in the shadow ban's or watch's table. It takes R1284000, the first number of issue #1284's block. It edits [[§9.11]].
**[[R1285000]] lets a finished match's socket reopen only inside its rematch window (issue #1285, patch v0.3.Y)**: a seat could reopen the socket of any match it ever finished, and the server folded the whole log back into an actor to show it. Months later that fold landed on a log the retention purge had taken, which showed the opening deal of a game with a result. On a game recorded before a rules change ([[R1156000]]) it could refuse a move and file a report for the night bot. A seat no longer in the match now reopens it only within `REMATCH_OFFER_TTL_MS` of the row's `finishedAt`, the window a rematch offer lives in ([[R672]]). Past that it is refused as a stranger is (4403) and no actor is rebuilt, and a finished match whose log is empty is not found (4404), as a co-op one is. The game stays in Replays. It takes R1285000, the first number of issue #1285's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1295000]] is the card cosmetics a player's own cards wear (issue #1295, part of #1294, 2026-10-19)**: a player could pick a hero portrait ([[R641]]) and nothing for their cards. Now a foil, a finish and a border, three items each and a default that draws the face as before, all unlocked, are listed once in a registry the server checks and the client reads, picked in the settings and kept on the account as a fifth group ([[R633]]), and worn by the viewer's own cards only, by owner, on a board the viewer plays, each on an attribute of its own apart from the Mythic and Radiant foil, with no border in the Radiant gold and every foil still under Reduce Motion. An id the client does not know is kept and drawn as its kind's default. A border on the opponent's cards was left for a `Cosmetics` frame and its own ruling. It takes R1295000, the first number of issue #1295's block. It edits [[§9.1]], [[§10.10]] and [[R633]].

**[[R1297000]] is the deck slots a player's own side wears: a playmat, a backdrop and a card back (issue #1297, part of #1294, 2026-10-19)**: the cosmetics of [[R1295000]] dressed a player's cards and nothing else. Now three more kinds, three items each and a default that draws the board as before, all unlocked, join the registry: a table, the playmat under the lanes, a field, an ambient backdrop behind the board, and a card back. Each saved deck carries its own picks beside its portrait, saved through the deck upsert and checked against the registry by the server, and the viewer's side wears the deck's, then the account's, then the default, an unknown id drawing as the default. Only the viewer's half wears them: the opponent's side, their backs and their pile draw as before, a back depends on the seat alone and carries no text, the backdrop holds still under Reduce Motion with its particles capped and takes no input. Online matches, hotseat and Duos use the account's choice until a frame carries a deck's. It takes R1297000, the first number of issue #1297's block. It edits [[§9.4]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R1298000]] keeps one pity counter set per account (issue #1298, part of #1294)**: whether an account keeps one set of counters for all its card packs or one per kind of pack was [[§14.4]]'s pity math sub-issue's to rule. One shared set: every card pack the account opens, of its set's kind or the global kind, increments the same three counters, and a pack holding a tracked rarity resets only that rarity's counter. It takes R1298000, the first number of issue #1298's block. It edits [[§14.4]].

**[[R1300000]] and [[R1300001]] are the dev accounts (issue #1300, part of #1294, 2026-10-19)**: [[§14.10]] named the dev accounts' tools and left what they write, and how a new catalog's grant skips them, to their sub-issue. [[R1300000]]: the server reads `DEV_PROFILE_IDS` at boot and decides alone, `isDev` on `/api/auth/me` and a 403 on every dev route for anyone else; a dev account gives itself XP on a set's track (its level stopping at `SET_TRACK_LEVELS`, no rewards paid) and Jlore, fills its collection with every collectable card and every cosmetic but the defaults, and clears its cards, cosmetics, Jlore, counters or tracks, or all five, every row with the reason `dev`, marked `dev`, the collection's ledger taking `dev` as a new reason. [[R1300001]]: `seed-catalog` hands its stamp's transaction the dev accounts, and the grant of a new catalog version passes over them as it passes over a ladder bot. They take R1300000 and R1300001, the first numbers of issue #1300's block. They edit [[§14.10]] and [[§14.11]] and amend [[R481]].

**[[R1301000]] gives a card pack's slots their rarities from the pity math's pack (issue #1301, part of #1294)**: the pity math says which tracked rarities a card pack holds ([[§14.4]]), and [[§14.3]] that each slot rolls one. The first slot holds the pack's best, or Rare when it holds none; each other tracked rarity it holds takes one more slot, highest first; the rest are Common, or Rare at `PACK_FILLER_RARE_RATE`. Rolling each slot on its own was rejected, since §14.4's rates are per pack. It takes R1301000, the first number of issue #1301's block. Issue #1339's tiered roll supersedes it: every slot now rolls its own rarity on one weight table, the first floored at Rare, and pity forces on top ([[R1339000]], [[R1339001]]).

**[[R1301001]] keeps a slot's rarity when the pack's pool lacks it (issue #1301, part of #1294)**: the slot keeps its rolled rarity, so its pity counter still resets ([[§14.4]]), and draws its card from the nearest lower rarity the pool holds, else the nearest higher one. Rerolling was rejected, since it would bias the rates. It takes R1301001 of issue #1301's block.

**[[R1301002]] runs the dupe chain slot by slot, a duplicate's cosmetic on its own scale (issue #1301, part of #1294)**: a card an earlier slot unlocked is a duplicate in a later one; a duplicate rolls its cosmetic's rarity on its own scale, at `COSMETIC_DUPLICATE_RATES` until issue #1339's `COSMETIC_TIER_WEIGHTS` ([[R1339000]]), not its card's, the owner's decision over [[§14.3]]'s "of the same rarity"; the cosmetic is a per-card one of that rarity, not a default nor a starter, that the account does not own on the card, a full art only on its own card, picked uniformly, and with none the slot adds points to both bars, one row per bar per opening. Every cosmetic [[R1295000]] and [[R1297000]] shipped is a starter, so until #1833 only an Epic duplicate of one of the six full arts' cards ([[R1313000]]) dropped a cosmetic and every other duplicate went to the bars; #1833's foils, finishes and borders, of every tier and on any card, make a duplicate spent only once its card holds every one of its rolled tier ([[R1833000]]). Chases drop as [[R1333000]] says. It takes R1301002 of issue #1301's block.

**[[R1301003]] seeds, logs and locks a pack opening (issue #1301, part of #1294)**: each opening draws a fresh 16-byte seed, takes its draws in one fixed order, logs its pack, catalog version, droppable cosmetics, seed and result (slots, pity counters, draws) in the `ref` of every economy row it writes, and replays as `roll_opening` over the holdings before it, the logged catalog's pool and the logged cosmetics; it takes a lock on its account for its transaction, so two openings at once run one after the other, and commits whole or not at all ([[§14.11]]). It takes R1301003 of issue #1301's block.

**[[R1302000]] to [[R1302007]] are what a finished ladder game pays at the edges (issue #1302, part of #1294)**: a drawn game pays each human side the loss rate with no win bonus ([[R1302000]]); a concede pays as the recorded result, the winner the win rate, the conceder the loss rate ([[R1302001]]); a reaper-resolved game pays as the result the reaper writes, its ceiling draw unrated yet paid as a loss over the log's actions ([[R1302002]]); older set tracks unlock nearest-older first, one per payout, once the newest track reaches `SET_TRACK_UNLOCK_LEVEL` ([[R1302003]]); an action the player took is a move of the game, never a row the server writes itself or the automatic end of turn's setting ([[R1302004]]); and a track stops at its last level, `SET_TRACK_LEVELS`, the XP past it landing but reaching no level ([[R1302005]]); the hook's callers pay only while `ECONOMY_LIVE` is on ([[R1302006]]); and a practice game's XP is the ladder's times `XP_PRACTICE_SCALE`, rounded down ([[R1302007]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1302's block. They edit [[§14.7]] and [[§14.8]].

**[[R1303000]] to [[R1303003]] are how a practice game earns (issue #1303, part of #1294, 2026-10-20)**: [[§14.8]] had the server issue a practice game's seed, fold the claimed log and check every AI move before paying, and left to this sub-issue which games pay, a seed's lifetime, a reconnect and an abandoned game. Now only an active account's free game on a server seed, started from the setup or Play again, asks for one and claims, never a lesson, a sandbox game, custom cards, Try it or a URL seed, and the server folds every claim as a plain game ([[R1303000]]); a claim burns its seed, paid or refused, a seed lapses unclaimed after `PRACTICE_SEED_TTL_SECONDS`, a game resumed after a reload claims on its own seed, and the next issue prunes an abandoned one ([[R1303001]]); every AI move is re-run at `AI_BUDGET` with no clock, so a move the browser's wall-clock cap cut short to another pays nothing, rejecting a client-logged node count, which would let a client weaken the AI ([[R1303002]]); and a paying game brings the last board the server kept from the last paid game, rejecting the device's, which C+ #29 would let a forged board buy a start with ([[R1303003]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1303's block. They edit [[§9.9]], [[§14.8]], [[R187]] and [[R508]].

**[[R1304000]] to [[R1304002]] are the daily quests' clock (issue #1304, part of #1294, 2026-10-20)**: an account's first visit starts its clock, one quest falls due every `QUEST_INTERVAL_HOURS` on that rhythm, a top-up dates each arrival when it fell due so a long absence returns exactly `QUEST_MAX_HELD`, one falling due with no room is skipped rather than banked, and each is drawn uniformly among the entries not held from a fresh, logged seed ([[R1304000]]); a game counts toward every quest held when it ends, one that fell due as it ended among them, never past a quest's target, and a completed quest pays once ([[R1304001]]); and none arrives while `ECONOMY_LIVE` is off ([[R1304002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1304's block. They edit [[§14.9]].

**[[R1310000]] to [[R1310004]] are the economy's going live (issue #1310, part of #1294, 2026-10-21)**: [[§14.12]] left to its going-live sub-issue the starter cards, as data, the ruling that replaces the launch grant and each catalog version's grant, and the switch. Now `ECONOMY_LIVE` is on in the server and the client, a new account becoming active owns one copy of each of `STARTER_CARDS`, the 39 Core cards of the three practice decks, `seed-catalog` writing the list for the activation grant to read, and is paid its newest set's track's first level; queueing checks ownership under L5 as it did, and a deck save still checks structure alone, rejecting a save that refuses an unowned card, which would lose drafts ([[R1310000]]); a new catalog version grants nobody its cards, migration 0050 dropping the stamp's grant and `seed-catalog` its read of the dev accounts ([[R1310001]]); every account active before the launch keeps every card, and its Jlore, packs, tokens, counters, bars, tracks, quests and full arts are zeroed once by offsetting `launch` rows, dev accounts' included, the starter cosmetics needing no row ([[R1310002]]); a dev account can fill either token bar so it pays at once, rejecting an end-to-end spec that opens about 30 packs per bar ([[R1310003]]); and practice, hotseat and every end-to-end spec keep every card and cosmetic but spec 45, which walks the economy on a fixture account of its own, `e2e-econ`, a dev account in end-to-end mode alone, and sees the full-art gate a development build turns on for it alone ([[R1310004]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1310's block. They edit [[§9.1]], [[§9.4]], [[§14]], [[§14.2]], [[§14.7]], [[§14.10]], [[§14.11]] and [[§14.12]], amend [[R111]], [[R141]], [[R1300000]], [[R1302006]], [[R1304002]], [[R1313000]] and [[R1454]], and supersede [[R481]] and [[R1300001]].

**[[R1313000]] makes the six full-art faces owned cosmetics (issue #1313, part of #1294, 2026-10-19)**: the hero portraits' cards printed full art for every viewer on every copy whenever the Full-art cards setting was on ([[R1017001]]), and [[§14.2]] made each a per-card cosmetic to own without saying how the registry holds one, who reads whose ownership, or what a copy shows before the opponent's ownership can travel. Now each is an Epic entry of a new `fullArt` kind, naming its card, never a default or a pick, on the cosmetics' own rarity scale as the owner clarified, not its card's; an active account reads its own cosmetics from a new `GET`; and once the economy is live a copy prints its full art only on the viewer's own cards and when they own it, every other copy its normal face, while the landing page's fan, the Almanac, practice, hotseat and the development and end-to-end builds print every one. Until `ECONOMY_LIVE` turns on, nothing changes. [[R1333000]] brought [[§14.2]] in line. It takes R1313000, the first number of issue #1313's block. It edits [[§10.10]] and [[R1017001]].

**[[R1329000]] is each shipped version's own win-rate table, claimed by its patch (issue #1329, 2026-10-19)**: [[R1266000]] gave each carried version its table by reading the table's `patch` label, which the next refresh would have broken, and no fragment claimed the table, so nothing held it to a card patch ([[R1101]]). Now a pending fragment claims it (`winRates`), `patches check` fails a change no fragment claims, a claim of a table whose rows did not change and two claims of one, `patches ship` writes each version's table to `crates/cards/patches/win_rates/<version>.json` with its blob in `shipped.json`, and a version with no file, as every version before v0.3.15, carries the empty table; v0.3.15, v0.3.16 and v0.3.17, which shipped first, take the filled table [[R1266000]] gave them from the three files issue #1329 wrote. Meditative+ #25 KY's Intuition's code blocks stay the build's, since no final hash holds them, and a later compiled table a fold reads answers from the fold's catalog first or takes a ruling. Shipping the table as the fragment's adding commit left it was rejected for [[R1280000]]'s claims as the tree holds them, and so was recording each lookup's answer per game. It takes R1329000, the first number of issue #1329's block. It edits [[§8.8]], [[§8.9]], [[R1101]], [[R1266000]] and [[R767000]].

**[[R1333000]] and [[R1333001]] bring the built economy in line with the owner's numbers (issue #1333, part of #1294, 2026-10-20)**: parts of the economy were built before the owner's numbers and the clarification on cosmetic rarity reached their builders, so every constant of [[§14]] now takes #1333's value and `CHASE_DROP_RATE` is gone. A cosmetic's rarity is its own scale and its own type, `CosmeticRarity`, which nothing converts to or from a card's `PrintedRarity`; Legendary and Mythic are the chase tier, the registry's chase mark must agree with the rarity (its two Legendary starters are marked), and a chase drops when a duplicate's roll or a cosmetic pack slot lands on its tier, as well as from the Chase Shard bar ([[R1333000]]). A game's XP numbers, 20 an action, 600 for a win and 2,000 at most, are measured over 40 recorded games of the AI playing itself, where a ladder game averages 999.75 XP and a practice game half of it, and a test holds both means within 5% ([[R1333001]]). Dropping the chase mark was rejected, since every logged opening's cosmetics carry it, and so were the golden traces, a random policy's games. They take R1333000 and R1333001, the first numbers of issue #1333's block. They edit [[§14]], [[R1301002]], [[R1305002]], [[R1305003]], [[R1306000]] and [[R1313000]].

**[[R1339000]] to [[R1339002]] make every drop a tiered roll (issue #1339, part of #1294, 2026-10-21)**: the economy rolled a card pack's Epic, Legendary and Mythic as per-pack chances whose base rates were tuned until the rates with pity landed on targets, dealt the rest of the slots as fillers, and kept three float tables of cosmetic odds. The owner replaced it all with a tiered roll, so a published rate is a plain number in one table and changing a drop is one edit. Every drop, a card pack slot's card rarity, a duplicate's cosmetic rarity and a cosmetic pack slot's, is one integer draw below `TIER_WEIGHT_TOTAL` against `CARD_TIER_WEIGHTS` or `COSMETIC_TIER_WEIGHTS`, which set the tiers above Common, Common the fallback taking what is left; one slot of each pack, the first, is floored, a card pack's at Rare and a cosmetic pack's at Epic, a result below the floor becoming the floor; a tier with nothing to give falls down to the roll's fallback, then up; and both pack screens show the server's tables, the fallback included, the card packs' with a pity line ([[R1339000]]). Pity keeps its counters and thresholds and sits on top: a pack in which a counter would reach its threshold forces that rarity onto one slot, Mythic first, the floored slot first, never taking a rolled Epic, Legendary or Mythic away, and a seeded simulation reports the rates with pity (about 31%, 11% and 6%) above the table's 30.4%, 9.6% and 4.9% and tunes nothing ([[R1339001]]). An opening's draws are its slots' tiers first, its log as it was, and an opening logged before the change replays only under the old roll ([[R1339002]]). `PACK_BASE_RATES`, `PACK_TARGET_RATES`, `PACK_RATE_TOLERANCE`, `PACK_FILLER_RARE_RATE`, `COSMETIC_DUPLICATE_RATES`, `COSMETIC_PACK_RATES` and `COSMETIC_PACK_GUARANTEED_RATES` are gone. Forcing the floored slot whatever it rolled was rejected, since pity would then take a rolled rarity away, and so was keeping the tuning loop. They take the first numbers of issue #1339's block. They edit [[§14]], [[§14.3]], [[§14.4]], [[§14.5]] and [[§14.6]], supersede [[R1301000]], and amend [[R1301001]], [[R1301002]], [[R1301003]], [[R1306000]], [[R1306001]], [[R1306003]] and [[R1333000]].

**[[R1351000]] mutes a blocked pair's in-match emotes (issue #1351, 2026-10-20)**: a block stopped requests, challenges, chat and replay shares, but a match the two met in still relayed their emotes, voice lines among them. Now the match's actor reads the block as it arms and hears of one made mid-match, and relays neither seat's emote to the other while the game plays on as before: the hand check and the rate gate run first, a card that hears emotes still hears it, and nothing tells the sender. The mute lasts the match. It takes R1351000, the first number of issue #1351's block. It edits [[§9.4]] and [[§9.5]].

**[[R1365000]] makes a change's kind and tier what it does to the game (issue #1365, part of #1360, 2026-10-20)**: a patch's tier was the guess its issue's title made (`v0.3.X` or `v0.3.Y`, [[R650]], [[R743]]), and most `Patch` titles changed no card. `cargo jackioh classify` reads the change between two commits instead: a set added to `SHIPPED_SETS` is a major ([[R1420]]), `PATCH_MIN_CARDS` (10) or more card families that play differently a patch, anything smaller that changes a card a micro, wording alone however many cards; otherwise the change is AI, App or Internal by what it touches. A card plays differently when its catalog entry changes a field other than its wording, or its script's code changes (its tests, comments and layout are not code), and with `--parent-bin` the base build confirms it: a script change counts only when a divergence in the card's forced games touches it, a change to the engine or the cards crate's shared code counts the cards its unforced divergences touch, and a change to the AI counts only when its self-play logs differ. No flag, title or label overrides it. Keeping the title's guess was rejected, since it was wrong about as often as right, and so was naming the tier by the paths a change touches, which cannot tell a refactor from a rule change. It takes R1365000, the first number of issue #1365's block; issue #1366 names versions from it, amending [[R646]], [[R650]] and [[R743]].

**[[R1369000]] logs the changes that ship no card data and opens each release line at its major (issue #1369, part of #1360, 2026-10-20)**: a change to the client, the server, the AI or the tooling either bumped no number and went unrecorded, or took a `Patch` title and a catalog version it did not need. `cargo jackioh patches log <base>..<head>` classifies each commit of the range ([[R1365000]], stage 1) and appends every App, AI and Internal commit, keyed by its commit, to the newest shipped X's technical log, `crates/cards/patches/technical.json`, with its pull request, its title without the prefix, its kind and the player line its body's "Player note" gives; a card commit adds nothing. The Patch notes page shows the App and AI lines under their X, and the release lines take their first version from each Z's major instead of a hand-written `first`: a recorded tier, or before issue #1366 the patch that adds a card of a set no earlier patch held. Keeping `first` by hand was rejected, since #1360's retcon moves every major. It takes R1369000, the first number of issue #1369's block; it edits [[R724000]].

**[[R1366000]] names a patch's version from its tier when it ships (issue #1366, part of #1360, 2026-10-20)**: a fragment named its version, the designer's guess from the issue's title ([[R650]], [[R743]]), and `patches ship` kept it. Now a fragment is `pending/<slug>.json` after its title and names no version; besides the catalog cards it claims, it may claim cards whose play changed with no catalog change (`--plays`) and a rule every game meets (`--rule`), as `cargo jackioh classify`'s second stage finds them ([[R1365000]]). `patches ship` reads each patch's tier from its snapshot, the one before it and those claims through the classifier, and names a major `v0.(Z+1).0`, a patch `v0.Z.(X+1)` and a micro the newest version with the next letter, recording the tier and the families that play differently on the patch. Claiming changed scripts from the diff was rejected, since the Luau port touched 343 scripts and changed no card. The old command form still writes a fragment, its version ignored. It takes R1366000, the first number of issue #1366's block, and amends [[R646]], [[R650]] and [[R743]].

**[[R1370000]] holds a pull request's fragments to what it does (issue #1370, part of #1360, 2026-10-20)**: `patches check` proves every catalog change claimed once, but a card whose script or engine rule changed how it plays has no catalog change to claim. CI's `rust (classify)` runs `cargo jackioh classify --claims` on every pull request, with stage 2 against main's build of the base whenever stage 1 leaves something unchecked. It fails when a card plays differently with no fragment claiming it, or a fragment claims a play or a rule the base build does not show, and it labels the pull request with its kind and tier. Leaving the claims to the builder was rejected, since the shipped history undercounted them (v0.3.12 recorded 11 cards where 17 played differently). It takes R1370000, the first number of issue #1370's block.

**[[R1305000]] to [[R1305004]] are the token bars (issue #1305, part of #1294)**: a bar that reaches its size pays in its opening's transaction and keeps the overflow, the paying write replacing the accrual row so still one row per bar per opening ([[R1305000]]); the cosmetic cycle's order Rare, Epic, Rare, Epic, Rare, Cosmetic Pack with its stored position in the `CosmeticTokenCycle` counter, a Cosmetic Pack step adding an unopened pack #1306 opens ([[R1305001]]); a card token unlocking one chosen new card of a set that ships, written to the collection as a `craft` since the closed reason set takes no new reason without a migration ([[R1305002]]); a Rare or Epic cosmetic token buying one cosmetic at its tier or below on the cosmetics' own scale, never a chase, a default, a starter, an owned one, a per-card one for an unowned card, nor Legendary or Mythic, which no token reaches ([[R1305003]]); and one `GET` reading back both bars' points and sizes, the cycle's next step and the tokens held ([[R1305004]]). Every failed redemption spends nothing. They take the first numbers of issue #1305's block.

**[[R1306000]] to [[R1306003]] are the cosmetic packs and the Chase Shard bar (issue #1306, part of #1294)**: an earned cosmetic pack opens `COSMETIC_PACK_SLOTS` slots, each rolling a cosmetic rarity on the cosmetics' own scale at the odds of a duplicate's cosmetic, the first slot at Epic 75%, Legendary 22.5%, Mythic 2.5% so every pack holds an Epic or better, with no pity, the first slot since issue #1339 the same table floored at Epic ([[R1339000]]); a slot grants one unowned cosmetic, per-card or account, never a default or a starter, chases included since [[R1333000]], its card weighted toward the cards the account owns, and the card too when the account lacks it ([[R1306000]]); a tier with nothing unowned falls to the nearest tier below, then above, the guaranteed slot staying Epic or better while any is open, and a slot with nothing at all pays both token bars' points ([[R1306001]]); each opening adds one to the Chase Shard bar, which stops at full and holds, and a full bar buys one chosen Legendary or Mythic cosmetic the account does not own and empties, each refusal a 409 that writes nothing, with one `GET` reading the bar and the packs held ([[R1306002]]); and each opening's seed, draw order, log and lock follow [[R1301003]] ([[R1306003]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1306's block.

**[[R1384000]] pages a conversation by a cursor of time and id (issue #1384, 2026-10-20)**: an earlier page of a chat was asked for by the oldest message's time alone, so the messages that shared that millisecond with it were skipped, and `send`'s stamp cannot prevent a tie between two concurrent sends. A page now answers `nextCursor`, the opaque time-and-id cursor of its oldest message while it holds `CHAT_PAGE` messages, the client sends it back as `before`, and both stores read the messages before it in `(sent at, id)` order; a `before` the server did not write is refused 400 before anything is read. Relying on unique timestamps was rejected, since concurrent sends tie. It takes R1384000, the first number of issue #1384's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1483000]] caps what an account's games pay in a day, and the practice seeds it holds (issue #1483)**: with the economy live every practice game pays a little Jlore, a loss and a game conceded on its first move included, and nothing limited how many an account could start, so a script that started a game and conceded it at once, over and over, would have earned a pack every few seconds; [[§9.8]] counted farming as handled by a game's XP cap, which is no cap on Jlore. Now the Jlore an account's games pay it, ladder and practice together, stops at `JLORE_GAMES_DAILY_CAP` over the UTC day, Hearthstone's daily limit on the gold its games pay: the game that crosses it is paid the part that fits, the ones after it no Jlore, and XP, quests and the quest and track Jlore are as before; a practice claim and a ladder result say when the cap held a game's Jlore back, and the result lines read "Daily Jlore reached". An account also holds at most `PRACTICE_SEEDS_OPEN_MAX` open practice seeds, a request past it refused. A cap in games played and a cap for each mode were rejected. It takes R1483000, the first number of issue #1483's block. It edits [[§9.8]], [[§14.8]] and [[§14.11]].

**[[R1398000]] lets a finished co-op match's socket reopen only inside the window a 1v1 match's does (issue #1398, patch v0.3.Y)**: [[R1285000]] stopped the 1v1 socket from rebuilding a long-finished match. A seat of a finished co-op match could still open `/ws/duos` onto it for as long as its log is kept (`MATCH_ACTION_RETENTION_DAYS`), and the server folded the whole log back into an actor each time. On a game recorded before a rules change, that fold could refuse a move and file a report for the night bot. With no actor in memory, a seat now reopens the match only within `REMATCH_OFFER_TTL_MS` of the row's `ended_at`. Past that it is not found (4404) and no actor is rebuilt. The game stays in its replay. It takes R1398000, the first number of issue #1398's block. It edits [[§13.9]].

**[[R1379000]] moves a socket's sign-in out of its address (issue #1379)**: the 1v1, co-op and watch sockets carried the bearer token as `?token=`, which a proxy log or a diagnostic could keep. Each now offers it as a `jackioh.bearer.` subprotocol, the token's UTF-8 in base64url, beside `jackioh.v1`, and the server answers with `jackioh.v1` alone; no usable credential is still [[R148]]'s 4401, and a renewed token reconnects as [[R194]] and [[R1115000]] say. The server still reads `?token=` from an upgrade that offers no credential, for production's client of the day before, until that client is promoted past this change. It takes R1379000, the first number of issue #1379's block. It edits [[§9.1]] and [[§9.2]].

**[[R1435000]] reads back who a replay is shared with (issue #1435, patch v0.3.Y, 2026-10-20)**: "Share with a friend" knew only the shares made while it was open, so after a reload it offered Share to friends who already had the replay and never offered Stop sharing again, which left [[§9.5]]'s "until the sharer withdraws it" true only during the visit that made a share. `GET /api/replays/:matchId/shares` answers the friends the caller shared that replay with who may still read it, by the friend's own read's rule; anyone who held no seat in the finished match gets the share route's 404. The control reads it with the friend list as it opens. Keeping the shares in the browser was rejected, since another device would not see them. It takes R1435000, the first number of issue #1435's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1437000]] gives every card one flavor, for its animations only (issue #1437, part 1 of #1436, 2026-10-20)**: #1436 animates each card by what kind of thing it is (its projectile, cast, melee impact, summon entrance, death exit and trap reveal) and needs a catalog field that names that kind. `flavor` is one of fifteen names, display only: no rule, pool, query, validator or AI reads it. A transient or custom definition carries none, so no state or replay moves, and the client draws Physical for it, or a fused card's first ingredient's flavor. A patch and `classify` leave it out, so a flavor-only change claims no card and ships no catalog version. Recording it as a card change was rejected: it would claim every card and move the version over nothing a game reads. It takes R1437000, the first number of issue #1437's block. It edits [[§5]], and narrows what [[R1420]]'s shipped view, [[R388]]'s patch history and [[R1365000]]'s classifier read.

**R1484000 asks before the card creator drops changes not yet saved (issue #1484, 2026-10-21)**: the creator kept a draft only in the page and asked only when the player opened another card, so Back, a reload or a closed tab threw a half-written card away, a long hand-written script included. Now, while the draft holds unsaved work by that same test, the page's Back shows the same note and a `beforeunload` listener makes the browser ask before a reload or a closed tab, as a lesson in progress does ([[R668]]). Keeping the draft on the device, as the deck builder keeps unsaved edits ([[R256]]), was rejected as more than the loss needs: a reload would have to say which of two copies it opens. The browser's own Back between two of the client's pages unloads nothing and is not held. It takes R1484000, the first number of issue #1484's block. It edits §12.11.

**[[R1487000]] and [[R1487001]] put a face-down card's lasting text to sleep (issue #1487, 2026-10-21)**: a lasting text C+ #31 fused into a Trap worked from the moment it was set and so named the hidden card; as [[R1149000]] did for C #88, it now waits until the card is revealed or face-up ([[R403]]). [[R1487001]] lets `classify` name a fused card's ingredients.

**[[R1489000]] holds the training lanes' games clean, as the quality gates hold theirs (issue #1489, 2026-10-21)**: #1467 made the everyday gates fail on any game whose search caught a panic, but the arena counted only "no move" and `promote` counted only wins, so a candidate that leaned on fallbacks could still promote. Now the arena counts each seat's fallbacks and caught panics, `promote` fails on any game that is not clean on either seat, naming its seed and the candidate's seat, and self-play marks a faulted game with `faults`, which `positions` leaves out of what the learned evaluation learns from.

**[[R1497000]] keeps a teammate's finished co-op games when the other account is deleted (issue #1497, 2026-10-21)**: migration 0039 took a co-op match, its result and its log with either of its people, so deleting one account erased every finished co-op game its teammate had played with it, and a match that started between `DELETE /api/account`'s refusal and its delete was deleted mid-game. Migration 0051 gives a co-op seat the rule 0012 gives a 1v1 seat: a deleted account's seat on a finished co-op match empties and the match, its result and its log stay, so the teammate still opens its replay, and a delete that would empty a seat of a live one is refused by a check, behind `DELETE /api/account`'s own refusal. Cascading finished co-op matches away and refusing only live ones was rejected, since it still erases the teammate's games. It takes R1497000, the first number of issue #1497's block. It edits [[§13.9]].

**[[R1502000]] pays unranked games, games of a Conquest series and co-op games (issue #1502, 2026-10-21)**: [[§14.8]] paid only ladder and practice games and left the other modes to a sub-issue. Now an unranked game, each game of a Conquest series and each person of a co-op game earn Jlore, XP and quest progress through the reward hook, at rates of their own, in the transaction that writes their result and under the daily cap.

**[[R1121000]] settles which card's tags the face-up skip reads (issue #1121, 2026-10-21)**: when a play records another card's play (`records_play_as`), the skip reads the recorded card's printed tags as well as the played card's tags as played, so C #57 Echo copying a fused Book of Stats and T-AI-5 Autocomplete leaves the previous last face-up record standing and the next Autocomplete copies the card before the fusion; `lastSpell` still takes what Echo copied. It edits [[§8.7]].

**[[R1560000]] lets a ladder bot's rating re-settle after an AI promotion (issue #1560, 2026-10-21)**: a bot's rating measures the AI that plays it, and four promotions since the seeds were calibrated left settled bots rated on an older, weaker AI, their small deviations moving them little per game. Now each bot's rating row names the AI generation it was last rated on, and a rated game against a bot from an older generation widens its deviation first, by `BOT_PROMOTION_DEVIATION_BOOST` in quadrature per promotion and capped at a new player's, as [[R609]]'s season reset widens a player's; the rating is kept, and its games against people carry it to the new AI's strength. It takes R1560000, the first number of issue #1560's block. It edits [[§9.12]] and [[R610]].

**[[R1555000]] has the top ladder bots grind when people outrank every bot (issue #1555, 2026-10-21)**: [[R1453]] gave a player rated above every bot none, and a bot's rating moves only with its games ([[R610]]), so once the best people passed the best bot the top of the ladder waited on an empty queue and the leaderboard showed a ceiling the bots could never climb past. Now, while the season's third-highest placed person is more than `BOT_GRIND_GAP` above every active bot, each sizing of the pool sets the top `BOT_GRINDERS` bots grinding, until a bot is within the gap less `BOT_GRIND_HYSTERESIS`. A grinder is on `BOT_GRIND_ACTIVE_HOURS` a day, steps up a strength every `BOT_GRIND_STEP_GAMES` rated wins (its rating still its games' alone), may be paired with a player above every bot within `MAX_FAIR_GAP`, and, while the queue holds no ticket, plays a ranked Best-of-1 match against the closest bot, one at a time and `BOT_VS_BOT_MIN_GAP_SECONDS` apart, rated for both and offered no rematch. None of it changes what a client reads, and Jlorious and the percentiles stay people's. The whole pool grinding was rejected, as was a coin for the seats of a match between two bots. It takes R1555000, the first number of issue #1555's block. It edits [[§9.5]] and [[§9.12]], and amends [[R610]], [[R639000]], [[R640000]], [[R640001]], [[R747000]], [[R748000]], [[R1452]], [[R1453]] and [[R1454]].

**[[R1563000]] to [[R1563010]] are the Meditative+ balance patch and the Float keyword (issue #1563, 2026-10-21)**: Float is a turn's unspent mana, and "Float reduces cost" takes a card's cost down by its owner's last Float, as M #49.1 YileGPT Prime and Core #29 GIGA Glowy Jelly Bean now print and Core #18 Bread and Butter's Trap reveals on ([[R1563000]]); M #18 Expedition12's Radiant Cleave reaches the hand picks beside its own ([[R1563001]]); Spell Damage printed on a face-up Field Spell counts, for C #23 Devil's Pact ([[R1563002]]); M+ #1 Bob's Plan Discovers a helper for each line M #20 Aestheticize the Game wins on, two new AI generated cards among them ([[R1563003]]); M+ #22 Mountaintop Garden's Radiant price reaches every card that is not Radiant ([[R1563004]]); M+ #20 KY's Competition's AI answers right 85% of the time, 93% Radiant ([[R1563005]]), and a sequence has 60 seconds, kept by its host ([[R1563006]]); a craft prompt offers four keywords, two hats and three effects, Kazakus's way ([[R1563007]]); a crafted Potion is a Spell under When cast on 4 points, a HUGE one 20 ([[R1563008]]); "Gain Armor" is no craft block ([[R1563009]]); and Scratch Pad is coded as text ([[R1563010]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1563's block. They edit [[§6]], [[§7]], [[§8.6]], [[§8.8]], [[§8.9]], [[R881]], [[R883]] and [[R1182000]] to [[R1182002]].

**[[R1582000]] counts four more of the player's statistics (issue #1582, part of #1576, 2026-10-21)**: the device's tally ([[R639]]) also counts, per card, what the opponent's Allure took, the lethal blow of a game the player won, the player's Traps fired and the player's Units standing when a game they won ended, read off the views and their new events for the landing's showcase; none syncs ([[R1142000]]). It takes R1582000, the first number of issue #1582's block. It edits [[§9.11]] and amends [[R639]].

**[[R1571000]] says why a graveyard card cannot be played (issue #1571, 2026-10-21)**: a card in your own graveyard that a permission admits but no play of which is listed said nothing when clicked, since the Play button simply was not there. It now carries `playReason` in the view while you may act, read in [[R979000]]'s order with the price step asked of the permissions, and the graveyard's sheet shows it where "Play" would be, in the grid and opened large. Presentation only. It takes R1571000, the first number of issue #1571's block, and edits [[§10.8]] and [[R979000]].

**[[R1577000]] gives the tavern landing's three doors their destinations (issue #1577, part of #1576)**: Play leads to `/practice`, the one play screen needing no account; Collection to `/collection`; Shop to a new `/shop`, the Shop screen of [[R1580000]] since then. No old path moves, so no redirect is added. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1578000]] makes `/play` the Play hub (issue #1578, part of #1576)**: one screen with online play first and largest (rank, queue setup, Find a match) and Practice, Play together, Replays and Play a friend smaller beside it (Card creator too until [[R1646000]]); the landing's Play door opens it, a visitor with no account sees it with a Sign in where the online controls would be and nothing read, and practice's "More ways to play" links give way to its Back. No old path moves, so no redirect is added. It edits [[§9.5]] and amends [[R1577000]].

**[[R1596000]] files again a game record that failed to write (issue #1596, 2026-10-22)**: [[R376]] only logged a failed filing, so the game was lost once the purge took its log; the retention loop now refiles it while the log is held. It takes R1596000, the first number of issue #1596's block, amends [[R376]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1579000]] makes the Collection screen the hub of decks, cards, cosmetics and the card builder, and the one place a cosmetic is equipped (issue #1579, part of #1576, 2026-10-22)**: `/collection` offers four doors, Decks (`/decks`) and the Card builder (`/creator`) where they are, Cards and Cosmetics opened in place by `?section=`; the page never scrolls, a section's body does. The six selects of [[R1295000]] and [[R1297000]] move from the settings to Cosmetics with the same store and `cosmetics` group, the settings and the account screen offer none, and a cosmetic pack's reveal links there. Back on Decks and on the creator leads to the hub. It takes R1579000, the first number of issue #1579's block. It edits [[§9.1]], [[§10.10]], [[R1295000]], [[R1297000]], [[R1310002]] and [[R1577000]].

**[[R1583000]] is the Pocket Pick and the player base's play rates (issue #1583, part of #1576, 2026-10-21)**: the server serves each card's share of the human live seats that played it, read off the totals ([[R1044000]]), and the client picks the card whose device rate ([[R639]]) is the most times it, once the device has logged enough games and plays. It takes R1583000, the first number of issue #1583's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1523000]] holds every profile's game in one seat ledger in the server (issue #1523, 2026-10-21; supersedes #1498)**: #1498 found three interleavings that still seated one person in two games, because each door asked its own advisory question in a throwaway transaction and [[R890000]]'s compare-and-set guarded only the in-match flag, which a series between games and a co-op match never set: two guests joining one host's two rooms, the queue sweep pairing a ticket while its owner joined a co-op room, and one person joining two rooms at once. Now every door reserves all its seats in one in-memory step before it writes, the flag stays the record and a second guard, and a seat epoch in the database fences a claim made by the server a deploy is replacing. Row locks on the profiles were rejected by the owner ("Better to have a centralized server locking service vs. putting the weight on the database"), and a session advisory lock would stay held through the transaction-mode pooler. It takes R1523000, the first number of issue #1523's block. It edits [[§9.5]] and amends [[R890000]].

**[[R1580000]] makes the Shop screen the one place to buy and open packs, see pity, redeem tokens and open cosmetic packs (issue #1580, part of #1576, 2026-10-22)**: `/shop` holds the Jlore shop and its wallet, the card packs with their pity, odds and opening, the cosmetic packs and the token picker, each re-reading when another changes the account. Its body scrolls and the page does not ([[R1579000]]), and the opening still plays full screen. Pity shows once, next to the packs held, since one counter set counts every card pack ([[R1298000]]). The account screen offers none of it, and linked to the Shop until [[R1646000]]; no address moved, so no redirect is added. It takes R1580000, the first number of issue #1580's block. It amends [[R1310000]] and [[R1577000]].

**[[R1584000]] shows the player's statistics on the floor below the tavern (issue #1584, part of #1576, 2026-10-22)**: "Your table" cycles through the stats the device kept ([[R639]], [[R1582000]]) and the Pocket Pick ([[R1583000]]) one at a time, each as a real card face with the player's number, the card playing the board's own animation for what the stat counts (the death, Allure's mark and steal, a Trap's reveal, an entrance, a lunge, the Radiant flash, the Divine Shield breaking) on a one-card stage, with no new effect; it holds under a pointer, focus, a hidden page and reduced motion like the homescreen's hand, and the floor with it is one screen. It takes R1584000, the first number of issue #1584's block, edits [[§10.10]] and amends [[R1582000]].

**[[R1581000]] makes every menu one screen (issue #1581, part of #1576, 2026-10-22)**: at a desktop's 1280x720 and a phone's 390x844 the page of every menu (the sign-in, the almanac, the patch notes, the statistics, the practice setup, the account, the leaderboard, the creator, the replays, Play together, a series and the rest, not the boards or the static legal pages) is no taller or wider than the window, with its top staying put and its one region scrolling what is taller; no type shrinks, and under 501 px tall the page scrolls as before. The tavern home alone may be two screens tall. Spec 46 holds it on every pull request. It takes R1581000, the first number of issue #1581's block.

**[[R1566000]] offers the way back to a live co-op game on the menus (issue #1566, part of #1576, 2026-10-22)**: `GET /api/auth/me` names the caller's live co-op match (`currentDuosMatchId`); the main and practice menus' banner offers it with Rejoin to `/duos/<id>`, as it offers a live match, and the Play hub's `already_in_match` refusal links the co-op match it names. A co-op game is no pairing, so the queue follow and the hub's take-back keep to the match and the series. It takes R1566000, the first number of issue #1566's block, amends [[R765]] and edits [[§13.8]].

**[[R1615000]] takes Charge and Indestructible out of the combat keyword pool (issue #1615, 2026-10-22)**: the designer found both too swingy as a random grant, as Immutable would be, and wants keywords handed out more freely. Plastic Surgery, Zao Gao, the Conjure Rush Tokens, a Buff's keyword row and Keymaster Keenus's Aura no longer draw either; the other fourteen keep their order. [[R1025000]] stays as the record of the sixteen, as R781005 stays as the v0.3.7 record. It takes R1615000, the first number of issue #1615's block. It edits [[§6.1]] and [[R21]].

**[[R1638000]] refuses a rematch across a card patch (issue #1638, 2026-10-22)**: a rematch copied its finished match's catalog version, so one made after a card patch went live played the old cards while the client showed today's, and an All Random rematch dealt today's cards to play under the old rules. Every other door into a match already refused a stale catalog. Now an offer on a match played on another version answers `409 update_required` before it is recorded, a person's or a ladder bot's. Starting the rematch on today's catalog was rejected: a frozen deck may not be legal under it, and it would add a validation path no other door has. It takes R1638000, the first number of issue #1638's block. It edits [[§9.4]] and [[§9.5]].

**[[R1639000]] shows a Heroic Power used this turn and an empty deck's fatigue count (issue #1639, 2026-10-22)**: a used power looked like an unaffordable one, or like nothing on the opponent's side, and an empty deck read 0 between draws. Now a used power wears "Used" and a title that says so, and an empty deck that has dealt fatigue shows the count so far, giving way to [[R318]]'s notice; both seats, presentation only. It takes R1639000, the first number of issue #1639's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R1646000]] takes the cross-menu links off the account screen and the Play hub, and the top and lowest card tiles off the statistics (issue #1646, 2026-10-22)**: the account screen links neither the Collection nor the Shop, whose tavern doors are the ways in ([[R1577000]]); the Play hub's ways are Practice, Play together and Replays, the Card creator staying the Collection's door ([[R1579000]]); and the statistics' fixed top keeps only the games tile of its summary, so a phone has room for the table ([[R1581000]]). No address moved, so no redirect is added. It takes R1646000, the first number of issue #1646's block. It amends [[R1578000]] and [[R1580000]].

**[[R1655000]] makes the foil, finish, border and full art of a card a pick per card, worn by the viewer's own copy wherever it is drawn as theirs (issue #1655, part of #1651, 2026-10-22)**: the picks sit in a device store beside the account's (`jackioh.cosmetics.cards.v1`), the `cosmetics` settings group keeping its six keys, so no client overwrites another's; a card with no pick of a kind wears the account's pick (the Full-art cards setting, for full art); a pick is worn only where the viewer owns it on that card, or where practice, hotseat and [[R1310004]]'s builds count every cosmetic as owned; the viewer's own copy wears them on the board and in its hover preview, touch sheet and detail view, in their mulligan and in the showcase of their own play, and every other face draws the defaults. It takes R1655000, the first number of issue #1655's block, amends [[R1295000]] and [[R1313000]] and edits [[§14.2]]; the Collection that sets the picks is a sibling part of #1651.

**[[R1661000]] and [[R1661001]] make the account cosmetics Epic or better and give a duplicate a global roll for one (issue #1661, part of #1651, 2026-10-22)**: the six playmats, backdrops and card backs below Epic become Epic, each kind's default staying Common and every one a starter, and the registry's check refuses an account cosmetic below `GLOBAL_COSMETIC_FLOOR_RARITY` that is not its kind's default; so a Rare cosmetic token buys none. A duplicate with an account cosmetic left to own first draws one integer below `TIER_WEIGHT_TOTAL`, and below `GLOBAL_COSMETIC_ROLL_WEIGHT` (10%) drops one, Epic or better on the floored cosmetic table, owned for the account in place of its per-card cosmetic; pity, a new card's unlock and the bars are untouched. With nothing left to own the draw is not taken, and on the shipped registry of their day, whose account cosmetics were all starters, nothing was, so every opening drew as before until #1827's portraits and #1833's playmats, backdrops and card backs added the first that are not ([[R1827000]], [[R1833000]]). `GET /api/packs` publishes the line as `odds.globalCosmetic`. They take R1661000 and R1661001, the first numbers of issue #1661's block, amend [[R1301002]] and [[R1339002]] (an older opening replays under the roll of its day) and edit [[§14.2]], [[§14.3]] and [[§14.5]].

**[[R1665000]] moves the pack odds and the pity counters to a Purchases page, off the pack screen, the Shop and the cosmetic packs panel (issue #1665, part of #1651, 2026-10-22)**: `/purchases` is public like `/terms` and opens with a disclaimer that Jlore is earned by playing and never sold; signed in, it shows the server's odds as percentages (a card pack slot's and the guaranteed slot's, a duplicate's cosmetic, the global roll's line, a cosmetic pack slot's and its guaranteed slot's) and the account's pity counters once for every card pack, and signed out it shows a link to sign in and makes no request. The site footer and one small `Odds & pity` link at the foot of `/shop` lead to it. Every figure is still the server's, and no price, table, threshold or pity rule changed. It takes R1665000, the first number of issue #1665's block, supersedes the placement in [[R1339000]] (What players see) and [[R1580000]] (Pity) and edits [[§14.4]]; the opening and store parts of #1651 take the old panels away.

**[[R1651000]] puts the Jlore pill in the top bar of every signed-in screen, the account screen's included, linking the Shop (issue #1651, 2026-10-22)**: the owner's brief for the economy's glow-up asks for the pill on every screen of a signed-in person that shows a top bar, and for a click on it to open `/shop`; the account screen's body still links no Shop, and the pill is the one link there. The board, the sign-in and password-reset screens and a screen whose Back asks before it leaves (the card creator's, R1484000) show none. It takes R1651000, the first number of issue #1651's block, and amends [[R1646000]].

**[[R1675000]] lets a sweep of record take off the cards it cleared (issue #1675, 2026-10-22)**: the 17 shadow bans were all `timeout` rows measured on the hand evaluation, and generation 13 (the learned evaluation of #1477) had never been swept; but `sweep --write` kept the row of any card the newest report neither banned nor watched, so a sweep could not take a cleared card off. Now a report also lists, under `Cleared ids:`, every card it swept, neither banned nor watched and affordable at some tier, and `--write` gives each of them no row, whatever an earlier report said. Issue #1675's sweep of record swept those 17 Meditative cards alone and banned one: in its 1,088 games no game had an error or reached 600 actions, and of the 11 decisions its clock found over 2 s, one, The Maestro's (`meditative-069`) at Hard, was still slow timed again, so `SHADOW_BAN` holds that row, 5 are watched and 11 cleared (`training/history/sweep-2026-10-22.md`, which also sorts the slow state by cause). It takes R1675000, the first number of issue #1675's block. It edits [[R1186000]].

**[[R1676000]] files the learn lane's self-play exports as development records (issue #1676, part of #682's audit, 2026-10-22)**: the gather lane's training box is being given back, and the learn lane already plays the same kind of game at far greater volume; `cargo jackioh stats from-selfplay` replays each game of its logs under the catalog it was played on and files, as a development record, each that still reaches its hash, finished and clean, counting the rest. Its ids begin `dev:<patch>:selfplay:`, and `stats --series` refuses a series that begins `selfplay:`, so no `stats` run files one. It takes R1676000, the first number of issue #1676's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1688000]] tries a practice claim's pay again when the store fails it (issue #1688, 2026-10-23)**: a claim burns its seed before its check ([[R1303001]]), so a pay the store failed once left the seed spent and the game unpaid for good, every later claim answering `already_claimed`, a day's capped Jlore lost with it. Now the pay, one transaction, is tried `PRACTICE_PAY_ATTEMPTS` times, the waits doubling from `PRACTICE_PAY_BACKOFF_MS`, as [[R1437]] tries a match's result; the check runs once and a refusal is never tried again. Giving the seed back and a sweeper over seeds claimed but unpaid (it needs a migration) were rejected. It takes R1688000, the first number of issue #1688's block, and edits [[§14.8]].

**[[R1689000]] ends a friendship's open challenges with it, and lets only a friend join a challenge's room (issue #1689, 2026-10-23)**: a block canceled every open challenge between the two and freed its room, but removing a friend did not, so the friend could still join the room by its code and play someone who was no longer a friend until the challenge lapsed. Removing a friend now cancels them as a block does. A challenge's room refuses anyone but its friend, and refuses its friend once the two are no longer friends or either blocks the other, with the answer an unknown code gets. A challenge whose friendship ends while it is being made is not opened. It takes R1689000, the first number of issue #1689's block, and edits [[§9.4]] and [[§9.5]].

**[[R1704000]] to [[R1704003]] are the designer's second Meditative+ balance round (issue #1704, 2026-10-23)**: sixteen cards and tokens of all five sets change, most by a number. C+ #62 KY's Papaya always Discovers 4 cells, each among 2 on the base face and 3 on the Radiant, and destroys what its curve meets ([[R1704000]], amending [[R422]] and [[R831003]]); C #52 Final Gambit cancels the lethal hit, a copy that stays on the field one hit a turn ([[R1704001]]); C+ #7 The House places 3 Plague Counters with its Cry and spends one with a once-a-turn Activate to summon the twin its controller names ([[R1704002]], amending [[R406]]); and M #37 CN in a Bottle's card costs (1) more, never past (4) ([[R1704003]]). C #32 Felinor Feelings steals only Units, Core #54 Straaza's pool is (3) Cost Units only, M #30 Fickle E-Kitten asks at the end of its controller's turn, C+ #40 Appropriations' options and C #90 In Too Deep's quests 3, 5 and 6 are tuned (amending [[R404]] and [[R542]]), and the rest are costs, stats and keywords. Each changed script keeps the old one for the replays of v0.4.0f and before ([[R767000]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1704's block. They edit [[§4.4]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§7]], [[§8.3]], [[§8.6]], [[§8.7]], [[§8.8]] and [[§8.9]].

**[[R1705000]] to [[R1705005]] raise the standard deck from 20 cards to 25 (issue #1705, 2026-10-23)**: `DECK_SIZE` is 25, so a deck, a seat with no handicap and Easy's AI hold 25 cards and a Conquest trio needs 75 distinct ones; Medium and Hard keep their edge of `MEDIUM_EXTRA_DECK_CARDS` (5) and `HARD_EXTRA_DECK_CARDS` (10) cards past it, 30 and 35, rejecting 25 and 30, which would shrink it to none and five; two decks that do nothing now fatigue out at player-turn 57, not 47, and the cap of 60 stays a backstop ([[R1705000]]). A deck saved at 20 is not rewritten: it stays a draft and fails L2 when queued, naming the deck, and a ladder bot holding one is dealt again, rejecting a migration that filled or deleted 20-card decks behind their players' backs ([[R1705001]]). The practice decks grow to 25, the starter cards from 39 to 50, and migration 0055 grants the eleven new ones to every active account, rejecting a grant to new accounts alone, which would leave an existing account unable to queue a preset ([[R1705002]]). A game made at 20 still folds as it was made: `create_recorded_game` holds a recorded game's seats with no handicap to `EARLIER_DECK_SIZE` when one of them holds that many cards, rejecting the issue's own proposal of keying the size on the catalog version a game folds under ([[R767000]]), since the golden traces carry no version and a rule-only change goes live at merge, before `patches ship` moves the version ([[R1705003]]). `cargo jackioh classify` reads a base build that refuses the head's 25-card decks as a rule every game meets, not a failure ([[R1705004]]). A lesson keeps its fixed 20-card human deck under `TUTORIAL_HUMAN`, rejecting re-scripting four lessons and re-tuning 25 seeded end-to-end specs ([[R1705005]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1705's block. They edit [[§1]], [[§2]], [[§2.4]], [[§2.5]], [[§2.6]], [[§9.4]], [[§9.9]], [[§9.10]], [[§13.1]] and [[§14.12]], and amend [[R180]], [[R184]], [[R291]], [[R380]], [[R389]], [[R1284000]], [[R1310000]] and [[R1370]].

**[[R1714000]] keeps every record its viewer may not read off a dry run's concealed copy (issue #1714, 2026-10-23)**: the copy [[R222]] made for [[§10.7]]'s dry run and the autopilot's tries ([[R1183003]]) blanked the hidden cards and kept the rest, the decks the match began with, the Glitch boards, the other seat's last board and unrevealed secret choices and an open problem's answer key among it, so the autopilot answered a problem by the real key and a guess tried on the copy was judged against the other seat's real choice. The copy now drops each of those records, keeps every continuation's shape so a try still resumes it, strips a hidden card's stand-in of all else it carried and hides the other seat's face-down traps in Rollback's snapshots; the AI's redaction ([[R185]]) already did the same and is unchanged. Pruning the copy's definitions to what visible cards name was rejected, since `def_of` must resolve every card a snapshot, a delayed effect or a memory still names. It takes R1714000, the first number of issue #1714's block, and edits [[§10.7]].

**[[R1715000]] sets aside a game record the fold cannot read (issue #1715, 2026-10-23)**: the fold of the records written before the totals parsed a whole batch before marking any folded, so one record whose detail did not read failed its batch on every pass, held the head of the queue for good and counted nothing behind it, and failed `stats-export` too. Now each record is read on its own: one that does not read is marked folded, counted nowhere and has its detail emptied, with a `game_records.fold_poison` alert naming its id, and the rest of the batch folds. It takes R1715000, the first number of issue #1715's block, amends [[R1044000]] and [[R1044001]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1717000]] raises an alert for an account or address the API's rate limit keeps refusing (issue #1717, 2026-10-23)**: [[R835000]] alerted on a seat's refusals in a match, but a request [[R109]]'s API limit refused was only logged, so an account could hammer the API with no alert. Now the router counts each refusal against [[R157]]'s key, and the 21st inside `API_REJECTION_ALERT_WINDOW_MS` raises `api.rejections.unusual`, once a window, on a record of at most 21 refusals a key. It takes R1717000, the first number of issue #1717's block, and edits [[§9.8]].

**[[R1723000]] has the Duos AI pass a card only when the trade is worth it (issue #1723, 2026-10-23)**: [[§13.7]]'s AI seat passed every card its teammate could play, whatever the teammate gained or the card was worth in the seat's own hand. Now it passes one only when the teammate's best bounded follow-up with it beats the teammate's best without it by more than the card's value in the seat's hand, `DUOS_PASS_MARGIN_PER_COST` per crystal of the pass cost and `DUOS_PASS_MIN_GAIN`, and ends the turn otherwise. Free next-turn passes and [[R1127000]]'s order are unchanged. It takes R1723000, the first number of issue #1723's block, amends [[R1127000]] and edits [[§13.7]].

**[[R1728000]] leaves a Unit standing in the backrow where it stands when something animates it (issue #1728, 2026-10-23)**: the daily plain fuzz wave (seed 553) found M #47 Taotie, a Flying Unit standing in its backrow zone ([[R1081000]]) with C+ #74 Twice Forward One Step Backwards fused into it, moved into its unit zone on the opponent's turn when that Animated Field Trap's animate step ran. A Unit standing there is a Unit already, as [[§6.1]] Animated leaves a card that is a Unit when it fires again, so animating it now moves and changes nothing. It takes R1728000, the first number of issue #1728's block, and edits [[§6.1]].

**[[R1743000]] has "Your table" skip tokens, show 35 stats in a shuffled order and drop its arrows and dots (issue #1743, 2026-10-23)**: the floor's showcase ([[R1584000]]) took the top card the catalog holds, so a token (a summoned 1/1 made again and again) could win Old Faithful, Fallen Hero and the rest, and its ten stats came in a fixed order with arrows and a dot for each, which made them feel short and countable. Now every stat, the Pocket Pick and The Record's card pass over a token, 25 stats join the ten (from the counters already kept, from new per-card counters read off the views' `damage`, `attackDeclared`, `healed`, `drawn`, `radiantSet` and `turnStarted` events from the viewer's seat, and from a win streak and the longest game), the page's random source draws them in a shuffled bag, and the showcase is itself the one control, a click, a tap, Enter or Space moving it on. The tally still counts tokens and the new counters stay on the device ([[R1142000]]). It takes R1743000, the first number of issue #1743's block, edits [[§9.11]] and [[§10.10]] and amends [[R1582000]] and [[R1584000]].

**[[R1748000]] to [[R1748003]] are the Buff priorities of issue #1748 (2026-10-24)**: on the field a Nerf or a Buff no longer moves a card's cost, its price paid, nor a declared number only its Cry reads, its Cry resolved; the catalog marks such a number `cry` ([[R1748000]]). A Buff now gives a Field Spell Indestructible, and a Spell or a Field Spell that deals damage, marked `dealsDamage`, Pierce or Lifesteal ([[R1748001]]); on the field it places a Plague Counter on a Field Spell that uses its own, marked `usesPlague`, and a Nerf removes one ([[R1748002]]). Every Spell and Field Spell was audited: eleven print numbers they now declare (Hinder's discard, Reoccurring Dream's and Selective Breeding's chances, Call to Arms' recruits, Zao Gao's tokens, Outbreak's draws per counter, CN in a Bottle's raise and five cards' set costs), and every number one still prints is fixed for a reason a test names ([[R1748003]]). Hearthstone's own Buffs never reprice a minion in play nor re-run its Battlecry, which the first follows. They take R1748000 to R1748003, the first numbers of issue #1748's block, edit [[§5]], [[§6.3]] and [[§8]], and amend [[R386]].

**[[R1750000]] makes Trample a unit's own attack's alone (issue #1750, 2026-10-23)**: the designer found it too easy to kill yourself with a plain value trade, a defender with Trample sending its excess to the hero of the unit that attacked it. Trample now applies only to the hits of the attacking unit's own attack, Cleave's included, a redirected attack on an ally's too; a defender's strike back and the damage a unit's text deals never trample, and a Spell that prints Trample tramples as before. It revises [[R63]] and [[R176]], takes R1750000, the first number of issue #1750's block, and edits [[§4.4]] steps 5 and 9 and [[§6.1]].

**[[R1751000]] tracks uses and cooldowns separately for each Activate ability on a card (issue #1751, 2026-10-23)**: when a card has several Activate abilities (such as through fusion), each ability's per-turn uses and cooldowns are tracked separately, so using one ability does not lock out other abilities on the same card during the same turn, amending [[R1087]]. On the client, a card with multiple abilities wears a single Activate control whose press opens an ability picker modal (Discover chrome) to choose which ability to activate, amending [[R510]]. It takes R1751000, the first number of issue #1751's block, amends [[R510]] and [[R1087]], and edits [[§6.2]] and [[§8.8]].

**[[R1754000]] has every way off the card creator ask before it drops changes not yet saved (issue #1754, 2026-10-24)**: R1484000 held only the page's own Back, a reload and a closed tab, so a pairing the queue followed to its game, a challenge's Accept and the browser's own Back threw a half-written card away without a word. Now, while the draft holds unsaved work, `navigate` and the browser's Back and Forward wait for the creator's note; a game kept from is offered by the menus' banner ([[R765]]) and a held challenge stays on its toast. Holding only the queue's and the toast's own moves was rejected: every in-app link and the browser's Back would still drop the draft. It takes R1754000, the first number of issue #1754's block, edits §12.11 and amends R1484000.

**[[R1758000]] has the settings dialog say when a save to the account failed, with Try again (issue #1758, 2026-10-24)**: [[R634]] had a failed request change nothing on screen and raise nothing, so a change that never reached the account went unsaid until an automatic retry sent it. Now the dialog shows a quiet line with Try again while the sync's last request has failed, gone while a request is out and once one succeeds; the device's settings and the automatic retries are unchanged. It takes R1758000, the first number of issue #1758's block, edits [[§10.11]] and amends [[R634]].

**[[R1759000]] keeps the beam's first stand-downs past its cut (issue #1759, 2026-10-24)**: a position switch is the last tier of the move order, so below the root, where the beam expands only `branching` moves, a board with that many earlier moves cut every one and the AI's own lines never stood a unit down, though the opponent it imagines tries up to `AI_REPLY.defense_switches` of them each reply. The beam now adds the first `AI_SEARCH.position_switches` stand-downs the cut left out (Attack to Defense of a unit an enemy unit would kill and survive) after its prefix and before End Turn, none twice, and no switch back to Attack. No weight moved: `AI_EVAL.position_grants` stays 0. It takes R1759000, the first number of issue #1759's block, and amends [[R1171001]]'s statement of what the beam expands.

**[[R1771000]] has the server keep the account copy of player statistics to the summary's inputs, for catalog cards only (issue #1771, 2026-10-24)**: [[R1142000]] pruned the copy on the client alone, and `PUT /api/stats/player` stored whatever a request held, so a hand-made one could make the public players tab's favorite card any words at all, past the word filter that chat messages and usernames go through. Now the server keeps only the four game totals that are whole numbers of 0 or more and, for each card the catalog holds, its four summary counters that are whole numbers above 0, and drops the rest; what `syncPayload` sends is stored unchanged. Refusing such a PUT outright was rejected: a client that sent one field more would stop syncing. It takes R1771000, the first number of issue #1771's block, edits [[§9.11]] and amends [[R1142000]].

**[[R1824000]] has every walk of a side's acting cards take a carried Unit once (issue #1824, 2026-10-25)**: walks written before carriers ("your Units on the field" scopes, the Plague count, a random field exile, price rules and more) passed by the Unit a carrier holds, a Unit for every rule ([[R446]]), while the auras found it, and the draw limits and trigger multipliers read a standing Unit ([[R1081000]]) twice. Each now reads each card once. It takes R1824000, the first number of issue #1824's block, and edits [[§3.2]].

**[[R1827000]] makes the hero portraits account cosmetics that accounts earn, so the global cosmetic roll drops them (issue #1827, 2026-10-25)**: [[R1661001]]'s roll never fired, every playmat, backdrop and card back being a starter, and [[R641]] gave every account all six portraits. Now the registry has the kind `portrait`, one entry per portrait id: `portrait-vanilla` the Common default and a starter, the other five Epic and owned only by the ledger, so they are what a duplicate's global roll drops, and a cosmetic pack or an Epic token may give one. Migration 0057 gives every person's account then signed up, pending ones included and ladder bots not, the five, with ledger rows of reason `launch` and ref `launch:0057`; a new account starts with vanilla alone. A deck's save refuses a portrait its account does not own, as a D5 issue, and the deck builder offers the owned ones while ownership decides. A ledger reason of its own, a settings group for the kind and a draft rule in the shared validator were rejected. It takes R1827000, the first number of issue #1827's block, edits [[§9.4]], [[§10.10]], [[§14.2]], [[§14.3]] and [[§14.12]], and amends [[R641]], [[R1310002]], [[R1661000]] and [[R1661001]].

**[[R1828000]] to [[R1828003]] are the designer's third Meditative+ balance round (issue #1828, 2026-10-25)**: ten cards and tokens of all five sets change. M #21 API Key Fishing's Radiant face rolls 4%, Embiggened 10%, and adds a Radiant copy, its text now saying so ([[R1828000]], amending [[R805]]); C+ #43 AI Slop fuses only (1) Cost AI generated cards, six today ([[R1828001]], amending [[R582]]); the token Core #51.1 KY's Empty Notebook shuffles 3 random non-token KY cards into its caster's deck instead of drawing ([[R1828002]]); and M #94 Shrinking Felinor is 9/9, Radiant 18/18, both faces shrinking −3/−3 and summoning a base body, so the Radiant chain runs six bodies against the base face's three ([[R1828003]], amending [[R1181]]). C+ #19.2 Jungle Loser gains Can't attack and attacks at every end of its controller's turn, its roll 100% on both faces; that attack is forced, and a forced attack skips [[§4.2]]'s step 1, where Can't attack lives ([[R53]]), so it needs no new ruling. C+ #61 Bauble Bubble gains Brittle 1, so it crumbles and pays out its Death on its own; C #80 BOOM! Big Max gains Crater ([[R783010]]); C+ #39 Book Worm takes the Plague tag; C+ #30 Felinor Fuser is 1/1, Radiant 2/2; and M+ #4 Supply Drop is renamed Jlockeed's Supply Drop. Each changed script keeps the old one for the replays of v0.4.9 and before ([[R767000]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1828's block. They edit [[§5]], [[§6.1]], [[§7]], [[§8.3]], [[§8.6]], [[§8.7]], [[§8.8]], [[§8.9]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1833000]] doubles the cosmetics registry with 36 cosmetics accounts earn, the first earnable chases and Mythics among them, and gates the account picks by ownership (issue #1833, 2026-10-25)**: past the full arts and the portraits every cosmetic was a starter, so the global roll ([[R1661001]]), cosmetic packs ([[R1306000]]), Epic tokens ([[R1305003]]) and the Chase Shard bar ([[R1306002]]) had little or nothing to give, and no chase could be earned ([[R1333000]]). The registry appends six foils, six finishes, six borders, six playmats, six backdrops and six card backs, none a default or a starter, their Legendary and Mythic ones chases; the global roll's pool is the five portraits and the 18 account ones. No account is granted any of them, existing ones included ([[§14.12]]). While ownership decides, the playmat, backdrop and card-back pickers and the account-wide foil, finish and border offer only what the account owns, a stored pick it does not own draws as its kind's default, and a deck's save refuses an unowned playmat, backdrop or card back; practice, hotseat and [[R1310004]]'s builds still own every one. Granting the 36 to existing accounts, locked entries in the selects and a client-only gate were rejected. It takes R1833000, the first number of issue #1833's block, edits [[§9.4]], [[§10.10]], [[§14.2]], [[§14.3]], [[§14.6]] and [[§14.12]], and amends [[R1297000]], [[R1301002]], [[R1305003]], [[R1306001]], [[R1306002]], [[R1310002]], [[R1333000]], [[R1339000]], [[R1661000]], [[R1661001]] and [[R1827000]].

**[[R1841000]] to [[R1841003]] are the weapon kit, the mechanics Stinkyscape's weapons are written with, built on `main` before the set (issue #1841, 2026-10-25)**: four engine primitives, proved on fixture scripts, which no card reaches until the set's cards do. An Activate that "has an extra turn cooldown" can't be used on its user's next turn after a use, that player's own turns being the clock, so an extra turn is the one it sits out ([[R1841000]]). Damage that "deals half damage to heroes" is halved on a hero, rounded down, after Spell Damage and before Armor, and a Unit takes it whole ([[R1841001]]). Equip from a pick fuses a fresh copy of each pick, Discovered, random or one of N, into the unit running the effect as an Equip play fuses its card, never through a hand and as no play, each pick from a pool that leaves out what the unit already holds ([[R1841002]]). "Float reduces Tribute requirement by 1" takes 1 off the card's Tribute X per Float of its owner, in hand and deck, never below 0 ([[R1841003]]). A field of its own on the instance for the cooldown and the lowered Tribute, which a bounce would keep, was rejected: both live in `memory`, which leaving the field resets ([[R78]]). The new state is optional and absent from every shipped game. It takes R1841000 to R1841003, the first numbers of issue #1841's block, and edits [[§4.4]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.2]], [[§6.3]] and [[§10.1]].

**[[R1849000]] to [[R1849009]] are Stinkyscape's combat and replacement verbs, built on `main` before the set (issue #1849, part of #1836, 2026-10-25)**: nine engine primitives, proved on fixture scripts, which no card reaches until the set's cards do.
