Immune to Spell Damage makes a Spell's hit on the Unit no damage instance, before its Divine Shield, while Spells still target it ([[R1849000]]). Evolve runs the Unit's own Evolve text when an effect evolves it, and "summon a copy of this, then evolve" summons the copy first ([[R1849001]]). Destroy becomes exile: while the aura acts, what the state check collects is exiled instead, judged before anything moves, the Radiant face's for the enemy's cards only, and a Sacrifice still reaches the graveyard ([[R1849002]]). A reflecting Unit deals what it takes again from itself to the enemy hero, and on the Radiant face to the attacker whose own strike it was ([[R1849003]]). The own-turn redirect moves a hit on its controller's hero, on their turn and before their Armor, to the opponent's hero, up to a cap a turn on what is taken, the Radiant face doubling what is dealt ([[R1849004]]). The Gauntlet's middle three lanes attack only each other, attacks and forced attacks alike, and a Unit's text still targets across it ([[R1849005]]). "Units cannot be in Defense Position" as an aura reaches both sides and flips a Unit already in Defense at the state check ([[R1849006]]). A forced attack by "a random Unit of yours" is drawn among the Units that may attack the target, with no draw when none can ([[R1849007]]). "When one of your Units is attacked" runs after a declared attack's trap window and before its combat, never for a hero or a forced attack ([[R1849008]]). A trap's chance to reveal is one roll each time its triggers admit an event, Luck and Lucky rolling again, and a miss leaves it set ([[R1849009]]). Hearthstone settles each where the designer's text does not; the readings a person may overrule before the cards are built are the Gauntlet covering attacks alone, the redirect acting before the Armor and the copy coming before the evolve. A `KeywordKind` for the two printed keywords was rejected, as it would widen the learned network's input ([[R1010000]]): both are static flags. The new state is optional and absent from every shipped game. It takes R1849000 to R1849009, the first numbers of issue #1849's block, and edits [[§4.1]], [[§4.2]], [[§4.4]], [[§4.5]], [[§5.1]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.2]], [[§6.3]] and [[§10.9]].

**[[R1678000]] sweeps the shadow ban again after each AI promotion and when a set ships (issue #1678, part of #682's audit, 2026-10-25)**: a shadow ban is a measurement of one AI, and the learn lane now promotes a new one every few weeks with no one in the loop, but a sweep ran only when someone opened an issue for one (#732, #1186, #1675, #1889). Now every push to `main` that moves `crates/ai/generation.json`'s `generation` starts a sweep of record on GitHub's runners over the bans and the watched Meditative and Meditative+ cards, and every one that adds a set to `SHIPPED_SETS` sweeps that set's cards (`cargo jackioh sweep --due` names them). The report goes under `training/history/`, `sweep --write` reads it last, and a changed table arrives as a pull request with auto-merge on. The sweep may ban a card again, within each set's half, so [[R732000]]'s "no lane adds one" holds for the training lanes alone; a sweep `--write` refuses opens an issue instead. It takes R1678000, the first number of issue #1678's block. It edits [[R732000]], [[R1186000]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R1679000]] makes every training lane's gate a sequential probability ratio test on paired hold-out deals (issue #1679, part of #682's audit, 2026-10-25)**: the fixed bars, 220 of 400 paired games for the improve and learn lanes and 60 of 100 for unban, were weak at the gains on offer (220 of 400 passed a candidate that truly wins 53% about 15% of the time) and played every game for a clear loser. Now `promote` plays deals 100 at a time and stops at the first deal from the 100th on whose log-likelihood ratio, H0 an edge of 0 against H1 +0.06 (53%, about +21 Elo) with a 5% chance of either error, leaves its bounds; a test undecided at 1,000 deals fails. It reads the deals in deal order, so CI's `promote --verify` stops where the lane did. On #1058's deals it promotes an equal successor about 4.5% of the time and a 53% one about 91%, in about 800 games. It takes R1679000, the first number of issue #1679's block. It edits [[R850000]], [[R1012000]], [[R732001]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R1935000]] shows the daily super run's results live on `/stats` (issue #1935, 2026-10-25)**: the daily super run said how it went only in the issue it opens on a red run. A third tab of the statistics page, Daily runs, now lists its last `CI_RUNS_LISTED` runs on `main`, newest first, each with when it started, its commit, whether it passed, failed or is still running, and a failed run's jobs that did not pass, each linking to GitHub, summed up above them: how many passed, the current streak and the job that failed most often. The server reads them from GitHub's Actions API (`GET /api/stats/ci`) and keeps the answer `CI_RUNS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, and the open tab asks again as often. Putting them in the night bot's status issue was rejected. It takes R1935000, the first number of issue #1935's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1973000]] to [[R1973002]] are the statistics page's patches, and the Daily runs tab leaves it (pull request #1973, 2026-10-26)**: [[R1973000]] takes [[R1935000]]'s Daily runs tab off `/stats` and keeps its route. [[R1973001]] reads a patch that a later version replaced before any game was played on it as a micro over the cards it changed, so it opens no statistics window for the rest; a major and the newest version keep their tier, and the version keeps its name everywhere else (a second retcon was rejected). A micro still restarts only the cards it changed: the totals cannot tell which games held a changed card. [[R1973002]] lists every patch in `GET /api/stats/cards` and gives the page a picker of the five newest patches with 1,000 games or more, and the patch before and after the one shown. They take the first numbers of pull request #1973's block, edit [[R1367000]] and [[R1935000]], and edit [[§9.11]].

**[[R1966000]] ranks each set's cards on `/stats` (pull request #1966, 2026-10-26)**: the statistics page ranked cards one by one. A Sets tab now sums, for the patch and match type the page shows, each set's cards from the rows `GET /api/stats/cards` already sends, whatever the Cards tab filters: its cards, card-games, pooled win rate with one standard error, the mean and median of its cards with the sample floor's games, its change since the previous patch, its best and worst card, and how many of its cards are `SET_STATS_OUTLIER_Z` or more standard errors over or under 50%. Tiles name the highest set by each measure, and the strongest and weakest cards are listed below, at most `SET_STATS_CARDS_LISTED` a side. The server adds nothing. It takes R1966000, the first number of pull request #1966's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1829000]] makes a card a Stack pile uncovers summoning sick (issue #1829, 2026-10-25)**: a Classic+ #4 Juhan Biggest Bat's copy, left dormant beneath it, attacked the moment the top died on a later turn of its controller's. A card that resumes as its pile's top is now summoning sick that turn, as a Reborn body is ([[R83]]), its exertion kept; an Equip host a fusion keeps keeps its turn ([[R1086]]), and a move that lifts a whole pile or Rollback's restored side uncovers nothing ([[R566]]). It takes R1829000, the first number of issue #1829's block, and edits [[§3.2]] and [[§4.1]].

**[[R1842000]] summons several Units stacked in one zone, bottom first (issue #1842, 2026-10-25)**: Stinkyscape's Drone Battleship is three layers in one zone, Meltdown at the bottom and the Shield on top, so the Shield Layer is fought first; every summon so far placed one card. Following Hearthstone, a summon onto a full board does nothing, so a pile with no free zone, or a named lane that is taken or reserved, summons nothing. Two alternatives were rejected: burying an occupied zone's Unit under the pile, and one new event for the whole pile, which would need a new client animation. It takes R1842000, the first number of issue #1842's block, and edits [[§3.2]] and [[§6.3]].

**[[R1842001]] has a lethal guard in a Stack pile guard only while it is the top (issue #1842, 2026-10-25)**: the Shield Layer's guard is [[R1204]]'s, standing on a pile, where only the top acts ([[R13]]). It takes R1842001 and edits [[§4.4]] and [[§6.2]].

**[[R1846000]] to [[R1846003]] are the card metrics, BUSTED and complicated cards (issue #1846, phase 0 of the Stinkyscape set, part of #1836, 2026-10-25)**: the designer asked for a criterion, not a table, so `crates/cards/build.rs` counts each script's code lines above its tests on every build, and compiles in the BUSTED cards, over 100 lines, and the complicated ones, over 60, which the catalog query's `busted` and `complicated` reach ([[R1846000]]); `everySet` is "every set, single-player ones too", the sets that ship and the reserved Boss and Boss-X, for Stinkyscape #109 xxFreshJack ([[R1846001]]); a custom card counts as neither (R1846002); and the lists stay the build's under a fold, since no shipped card reads them, until the patch that ships their first reader keeps each version's ([[R1846003]]). No card reads them until the set ships, and no shipped game changes. They take the first numbers of issue #1846's block. They edit [[§5]], [[§5.1]], [[§6.1]] and [[§10.7]], and amend [[R1420]].

**[[R1839000]] to [[R1839003]] add Dormant (issue #1839, the Stinkyscape set's mechanic)**: the designer asked for Hearthstone's Dormant ahead of the set that prints it (two Imprisoned Units, "Dormant for 2 turns" with "When this awakens" effects, and a Dormant Starship awakened by an Activate), and kept its name, so the spec's word for a card under a Stack pile's top became "beneath a Stack" ([[§3.2]], [[§6]]). The designer's words: on the field, it can't attack, be attacked, be targeted or be reached by any effect until it awakens, its auras and triggers off; "Dormant for N turns" counts its controller's turns and awakens at the start of the Nth; a card's own Activate may awaken it. Where they stop it follows Hearthstone: it fills its zone, it is not summoning sick when it awakens, it counts for board space but not as a Unit, a Cry still runs as it is played (Magtheridon's Battlecry), and its countdown runs before the start-of-turn triggers ([[R1839000]], [[R1839001]], [[R1839002]]). Hearthstone has no Stack: a Stack card may top a Dormant one, which lies beneath it still Dormant, its countdown waiting, reached by nothing the arrival does ([[R1839003]]); the Dormant Starship will exile such a card before it resolves, which is its text and not this rule. They take R1839000 to R1839003, the first numbers of issue #1839's block, and edit [[§2.2]], [[§3.2]], [[§6]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.2]], [[§6.3]], [[§10.3]] and [[§10.8]]; the rewording of "dormant" to "beneath a Stack" (and of a face-down card's dormant text to inert) touches the notes and rulings that used the old word, and changes no rule.

**[[R1838000]] to [[R1838003]] bring Tradable and the Trade (issue #1838, 2026-10-25)**: the designer's take on Hearthstone's Tradeable, at no mana, shipped on Meditative #53 Prestige and #98 Showdown ahead of the Stinkyscape set, whose cards use it too. [[R1838000]] decides what the keyword and the action are: one Trade per player per turn over all their Tradable cards, read from a figure of the player's own so a later card can raise it, the keyword read in the hand as Temporary's is, and one refusal for the list, the reducer and the view. [[R1838001]] keeps a Trade hidden like a draw, a public `traded` and the `shuffledIn` and `drawn` it is made of; [[R1838002]] makes its draw a draw for every rule and the Trade no play; [[R1838003]] keeps a turn with a Trade on offer from ending by itself and has the AI, the random policy and the clock answer it. They take R1838000 to R1838003, the first numbers of issue #1838's block, and edit [[§2.2]], [[§2.5]], [[§6.1]], [[§8.8]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.2]], [[§10.3]], [[§10.8]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R1848000]] to [[R1848006]] are the spell verbs of the Stinkyscape set (issue #1848, part of #1836, 2026-10-25)**, each an engine primitive merged on `main` before the set that needs it, proved on fixture cards: no card reaches one until the set ships. A Freeze may be forever, stamped with a thaw no cleanup reaches, which only leaving the field and a Vanilla end ([[R1848000]], Absolute Zero). A damage effect may trample onto the Units beside its target, its excess going to one neighbour drawn at random, as Hearthstone's Rolling Fireball rolls left or right, then to the other, each counting it only up to its health, then on the Radiant face to the hero ([[R1848001]], Meteor). A Spell's Poisonous destroys what its hits damage, and "your next Spell this turn has Poisonous" is a player modifier the next face-up Spell spends at §10.5 step 4 ([[R1848002]], Asphyxiants Cloud). An X-cost card may reach a hand with its X set, so it is played for that X and costs it ([[R1848003]], Book of Nono-Words). "Lose N mana next turn" and "gain N mana next turn" are the next refresh's rider, Hinder's, so they needed no new verb ([[R1848004]], Core, Secure and Wild System). The melee is a run of forced attacks between random pairs of Units, allies included, until one is left, at most 20 ([[R1848005]], Wild System). Inflation rolls each card of a hand and a deck, a hit costing (1) more, the chance carried on the copy a Stimmycheck makes, silently as R440 has a hidden scope ([[R1848006]], Stimmycheck). Where the designer's words stop they follow Hearthstone. They take R1848000 to R1848006, the first numbers of issue #1848's block, and edit [[§4.4]] steps 7 and 9, [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]] and [[§10.1]].

**[[R1837000]] puts five family tags on shipped cards and takes Felinor and Fruit off four (issue #1837, 2026-10-25)**: the Stinkyscape set's cards discover Terminus cards, complete Quests, add Other-Origins cards and discover a Jelly Bean, and its Fruit Launcher casts a random Fruit, so ahead of the set the designer tagged #100 Ceaseless Void Terminus, C #90 In Too Deep Quest, C+ #32 Otherworldly Removal Otherworldly, its three tokens Other-Origins and the four Glowy Jelly Beans Jelly Bean, and took Felinor off C #32 Felinor Feelings and #62 Friend of Felinors and Fruit off C+ #63 Fruit Tree and C+ #68 Organic Produce, the two Field Spells that make Fruit. The spec did not say what kind of tag the five are; following Hearthstone, which keeps its Quests apart from its minion types, they are family tags, not tribes ([[R1424]]), and no pool by one of them takes tokens ([[R382]]) until a card that names one decides otherwise. It takes R1837000, the first number of issue #1837's block, and edits [[§5]], [[§7]] and [[§8]].

**[[R1851000]] to [[R1851003]] keep Prestige in hand with nothing to De-Radiant (issue #1851, 2026-10-25)**: the designer asked that Meditative #53 Prestige be offered only while a Radiant card it could De-Radiant exists, where [[R1103]] let it be played and fizzle. Its base face's target becomes needed, as [[R703]] already allowed. Its Radiant face declares no target, so the engine gains a play gate, after Hearthstone's play requirements: one check for `legalActions`, the refusal and `playReason`, read off the face the play resolves ([[R1851000]]). A cast, an Echo repeat and a random cast never ask it, and a set Spell's Reveal waits for it, as each treats a needed pick ([[R1851001]]); a fusion needs every ingredient's gate and a copier its copied face's ([[R1851002]]). Prestige's Radiant gate asks for a Radiant enemy card its player can see, so a face-down one, whose face is hidden, does not open it though the Cry still clears it, and a Trade, being no play, is untouched ([[R1851003]]). They take R1851000 to R1851003, the first numbers of issue #1851's block, edit [[§8.8]], [[§10.8]] and [[§10.9]], and supersede [[R1103]].

**[[R1840000]] to [[R1840006]] make Resources, the Resource tray and Credits an engine primitive (issue #1840, phase 0 of the Stinkyscape set, part of #1836, 2026-10-25)**: the designer's rule is a (0) Spell token that takes no hand slot, a tray of 8 beside the hand whose overflow is sold at once, public Credits, "gain a random Stinkyscape Resource" by a rarity table, and asteroids whose Activate pays their Resources on their Death; the opponent sees the tray's count only, the designer's answer. Where it stops, Hearthstone has no tray, so the hand is the model: the tray reads as a hand does and is revealed with the hands at the game's end ([[R434]]), and its Resources are played as hand cards are ([[R1840000]], [[R1840003]]). This note decides what the rule left open, by default: a sale's price, until the Marketplace (#1853) sets one, is a draw in the face's printed sell range ([[R1840001]]); the table is taken over the rarities a pool holds, and the pool ignores [[R1420]]'s set gate ([[R1840002]]); "discard a Narcor" takes the oldest match with no prompt, and a sold or discarded Resource ceases to exist rather than reaching a graveyard ([[R1840004]]); Credits are absent until first gained ([[R1840005]]); and the asteroid's Tribute is a Sacrifice of itself ([[R1840006]]). New state is optional and skipped when absent, so no shipped game changes. They take R1840000 to R1840006, the first numbers of issue #1840's block, and edit [[§3]], [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.2]], [[§10.3]], [[§10.5]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1853000]] to [[R1853005]] make the Marketplace an engine primitive (issue #1853, phase 0 of the Stinkyscape set, part of #1836, 2026-10-26)**, built on the night market's shop ([[R1000]]) and on #1840's Resources and Credits, merged on `main` before the set and proved on fixture scripts and a fixture table: no card reaches it until the set ships, and its table is the set's data (S21). The designer's rule is a shop both players see, opened by the first Resource or Credits, one offering with a stock each, re-rolled every 6 turns from a table of lots, the price rule (ST9 Q6), a 20% Radiant lot at twice the price, prices moving each turn by a tenth, and lots an effect adds for the rest of the game. This note decides what it left open, with Hearthstone's shops (Tavern, Mercenaries' shop) as the model where it had one: it opens and re-rolls in silence ([[R1853000]]); the 6 turns are the game's `turn`, counted from the last roll, and the shipped table stays empty until S21 ([[R1853001]]); a price moves by a whole number within a tenth of its base rounded down, a Radiant Resource lot costs its Radiant face's printed buy, and a Resource's sale is quoted each turn, which [[R1840001]]'s full-tray sale fetches while the Marketplace is open ([[R1853002]]); buying and selling are direct actions with no prompt, a buy is refused into a full hand or tray rather than burned, and a purchase is hidden as a gain is while a sale is public ([[R1853003]]); a deal keeps a turn from ending by itself and is answered as a Trade is ([[R1853004]]); and an added lot is its caster's alone, its card drawn once, never Radiant, its price fixed and one copy a cycle ([[R1853005]]). New state is optional and skipped when absent, so no shipped game changes. They take R1853000 to R1853005, the first numbers of issue #1853's block, and edit [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.2]], [[§10.3]], [[§10.7]], [[§10.8]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R1852000]] to [[R1852007]] add the Starship (issue #1852, the Stinkyscape set's mechanic, after Dormant)**: the designer's list builds a ship from the Starship Pieces that die — a copy of each fuses into its owner's Dormant Starship, summoned first if they have none, into the leftmost open unit zone or else stacked on lane 3, Locked or not — launched for (3), which fires every fused Cry; and answered that launching is awakening, that only a piece that dies fuses, and that the Starship starts 0/0. Where the words stop it follows Hearthstone and the spec's own rules: the fusion is [[R77]]'s, run at the death pass's end in [[R68]]'s order with a copy of the card as its graveyard holds it ([[R1852000]]); a Fuse keeps Starship Piece only from the card it keeps, which is the designer's Equip rule and keeps the Starship from being a piece ([[R1852001]]); a stolen piece builds its owner's ship, since it enters its owner's graveyard ([[R1852002]], [[R747]]); the launch is the engine's Activate, its Cries asked as a triggered Cry's are ([[R467]]), then the new "When launched" trigger ([[R1852003]]); a launched Starship's name is drawn with tagged names preferred, as the designer asked ([[R1852004]]); the Starship remembers its pieces and "building a ship" is having a Dormant one ([[R1852005]]); "your Starship launch costs (N)" is a player price spent by the launch it prices ([[R1852006]]); and a Stack card played onto a Dormant Starship is exiled before it resolves, the Dormant Starship's text and the exception to [[R1839003]] ([[R1852007]]). They take R1852000 to R1852007, the first numbers of issue #1852's block, and edit [[§4.5]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.2]] and [[§6.3]]; no shipped card prints either keyword, so no game changes.

**[[R1854000]] to [[R1854004]] are the staged Quests of the Stinkyscape set (issue #1854, phase 0, part of #1836, 2026-10-25)**, an engine primitive merged on `main` before the set that needs it, proved on fixture cards: no card reaches it until the set ships, and its two staged Quests, #7 The Greatest Heist and #112 Job Application, are the set's own cards (S09, S10). It generalises In Too Deep's quest line rather than building a second one: a card may declare its quest as stages, run on the same line one stage at a time, the first as it enters, a stage of several goals drawing one at random as it opens, the card's own reward effects moving it on, and every stage public on the card ([[R1854000]]), as Hearthstone's Questlines run their parts. "Complete the current stage on all your Quests" (#6 Tylannt) completes every quest open on a Quest-tagged card of yours, In Too Deep's open quests included, each rewarded at the next state check ([[R1854001]]). Six goals join [[R404]]'s: an Acclaimed Unit of yours made Radiant, two of your Acclaimed Units dying, an enemy card stolen, a card played at a cost the quest card names or remembers, three cards of different costs in one turn, and one damage instance of 12 or more dealt or taken ([[R1854002]]). Stealing the enemy's mana crystals takes their crystals, which come back one a turn, and gives them to the thief, full, up to the cap, Hearthstone's crystal count read off [[§2.3]]'s turns started ([[R1854003]]). "Hear back in N turns" opens a staged quest's next stage at the start of its controller's Nth turn, before that turn's triggers and draw ([[R1854004]]). Where the designer's words stop they follow Hearthstone. They take R1854000 to R1854004, the first numbers of issue #1854's block, and edit [[§2.3]], [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1843000]] to [[R1843005]] are two new endings, voted out and fled (issue #1843, phase 0 of the Stinkyscape set, 2026-10-25)**: a mechanic merged on `main` before the set that needs it, proved on fixture scripts, which no shipped card reaches. A card votes to remove a player through a verb, and the engine keeps each player's tally for the turn, each card definition once, `VOTE_TALLY_SIZE` (3) of them removing the player at the state check's game-end point ([[R1843000]]): a loss for them and a win for the other player, the designer's answer (`docs/stinkyscape-set.md` ST9 Q9), after the hero check and before any held win, and two full tallies at once a draw, as two heroes at 0 are ([[R1843001]]). Fleeing wins the game and leaves the other player a draw, or on Edge of the Universe's Radiant face a loss as the fleer chooses, taking hold at the closing check as a win effect does ([[R850]]): a hero at 0 loses first, a win the fleer holds another way wins by it, two fleers draw ([[R1843002]]); the Radiant choice is an ordinary `mode` prompt, so the AI, the random policy and the timeout answer it ([[R1843003]]). A `fled` game is the one result that differs by side, a win for the fleer and a draw for the other player (ST9 Q10): the engine names it in `LOSER_DRAWS_REASONS`, and the ladder, the record, the rewards, the card statistics, the AI and the client all read a seat's result through it ([[R1843004]]); migration 0059 admits the four reasons ([[R1843005]]). Where the designer said nothing, Hearthstone's semantics decide: losses before wins, two losers at once a draw. They take R1843000 to R1843005, the first numbers of issue #1843's block, and edit [[§2.5]], [[§6.3]], [[§9.12]], [[§10.1]] and [[§10.3]].

**[[R1844000]] to [[R1844002]] are Stinkyscape's M10, the AI at the wheel (issue #1844, part of #1836, 2026-10-25)**: phase 0 of the Stinkyscape set builds the mechanics of #46 Circadian Halfcycler, #47 Circadian Fullcycler, #147 IN33DH3 and #147.2 Wardrum Blob as engine primitives before any card reaches them. The autopilot ([[R1183002]]) becomes an aura that plays a count of [[R456]]'s actions or the whole of each of its controller's turns ([[R1844000]]), ME-PARETO's judge ([[R1125]]) becomes a hint on the player's own view, the hand cards it would call optimal lit up ([[R1844001]]), and C+ #27's ranking of the perfect pool ([[R416]]) a free cast of its top card ([[R1844002]]). Hearthstone has none of the three, so each follows the autopilot and the judge it extends. They take the first numbers of issue #1844's block. They edit [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.7]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1845000]] to [[R1845002]] are hidden Nerfs and trade offers, the mechanics Stinkyscape #123 Gokugains and #77 Yuma are written with, built on `main` before the set (issue #1845, 2026-10-25)**: two engine primitives, proved on fixture scripts, which no card reaches until the set's cards do. A hidden Nerf is a count on a card in a hand or a deck that no seat reads, its owner included: nothing is reported, no view shows it, and the AI's redaction and the dry run's concealed copy drop it ([[R1845000]]). It lands at [[§10.5]] step 3 of the card's next play or cast as that many Nerfs, each drawn from every row but the cost's and reported as every Nerf is ([[R1845001]]). A trade offer waits for the start of the opponent's next turn; then its controller gives a card from their hand and the opponent Discovers one of their own to give back, through two ordinary prompts ([[R1845002]]). Hearthstone has neither, so each follows its nearest kin here: a Nerf's own rows, and C #9's change of hands asked as [[R1279000]] asks. Nerfs applied at once and hidden, which need a second tuning on every card, and a simultaneous swap kept hidden until both have chosen were rejected. The new state is optional and absent from every shipped game. It takes R1845000 to R1845002, the first numbers of issue #1845's block, and edits [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.5]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1831000]] has a friend's challenge accepted from its toast, with the deck picked there (issue #1831, 2026-10-25)**: [[R767]] had accepting a challenge open the lobby with the code filled in, so the friend left their screen, picked a deck and pressed Join. Now the toast shows the challenge's mode and a deck (or trio) picker that starts on the pick last used in this browser, and Accept joins the room by its code with that pick and goes to the game; an All Random challenge needs none, and a screen with unsaved work asks first ([[R1754000]]). A refused join shows the server's words and offers "Open in lobby", the [[R767]] path, which still governs room links. The pick is kept per browser, outside the settings the account syncs ([[R634]]). Keeping the lobby as the only way in was rejected. It takes R1831000, the first number of issue #1831's block, and edits [[§9.4]] and [[§9.5]].

**[[R1847000]] to [[R1847002]] are the starscape map, Stinkyscape #148 Alteran Reach's, built on `main` before the set (issue #1847, 2026-10-25)**: an engine primitive proved on fixture scripts, which no card reaches until the set's cards do ([[R1420]]). The map is the player's, as a Hearthstone Excavate track is, and not the card's, as Reach the Summit's level is: every card's travel moves the same map and a bounced Reach does not start over. It is one path of `STARSCAPE_EDGE_TRAVELS` (20) nodes, Reach the Summit's shape; a branching map was rejected, since choosing a branch is a new prompt the AI, the random policy and the timeout must all answer. It is charted from the match rng at the player's first travel and stored ([[R1847000]]); charting it at setup was rejected, since it would draw in every game and move every hash, and charting it as a card enters the field can come with the card. Its rewards are Heal 3, Draw 1, Mana 1 and Damage 2, drawn uniformly; Armor was rejected (here a per-hit cut for the rest of the game, too strong for (1)), and so were random catalog cards, whose pools move as sets ship. A travel is one node and its reward, and the edge adds the card the ability names on its base face, whichever face traveled, a full hand burning it ([[R1847001]]). A travel costs (1), and the Radiant face's first each turn, counted per player as Hearthstone counts "your first … each turn", is free, as two abilities `has` switches between ([[R752]], [[R1847002]]). A Luau binding was left out, so a custom card cannot travel a map yet. The new state is optional and absent from every shipped game. They take the first numbers of issue #1847's block and edit [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.3]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1916000]] has the language button leave the top bar and the board once a device has settled on its language (issue #1916, 2026-10-25)**: a `localStorage` counter beside the language counts the boots, back to 0 at every language change and at settings' reset, and from the third unchanged boot the button is gone while settings' Language row stays the only control, its hint naming the top button only while it shows; storage that throws never settles, so the button is never lost. Rejected in one clause: an IP or server-side fingerprint, and counting without comparing first, re-reading storage at every render, keeping the count at settings' reset, or gating at the button's call sites. It takes R1916000, the first number of issue #1916's block, edits no section and amends [[R711000]].

**[[R1850000]] to [[R1850006]] are the card-on-card verbs, the mechanics Stinkyscape's cards are written with, built on `main` before the set (issue #1850, 2026-10-25)**: seven engine primitives, proved on fixture scripts, which no card reaches until the set's cards do. A copier has a Unit's text, its Radiant text when it says so, on its own body until it leaves the field ([[R1850000]]). Duo and Squad mark a permanent, or every permanent of yours, gold and make it Radiant at the start of the marker's next turn ([[R1850001]]). An exile aura exiles every card on the field it reaches, never itself nor a face-down card, at each state check ([[R1850002]]). A permanent held beneath a card comes back, summoned for whom it returns to, once that card leaves the field any way, or is exiled with no room ([[R1850003]]). A Spell whose chosen targets are all its caster's own is countered and cast by the Trap's owner, on their own side alone ([[R1850004]]). A permanent becomes Radiant once its keyword kinds reach N ([[R1850005]]). Crater on a Field Spell Locks its backrow zone as it enters, where it did nothing ([[R1850006]], amending [[R783011]]). Built beside Dormant and the Starship ([[R1839000]], [[R1852000]]), they leave a Dormant card be as every effect does, its text off, while a Dormant or covered holder still holds; Scornboi's Starship Piece and Starship are keyword kinds an exile aura spares. Each decision the spec did not make follows Hearthstone, the rejected readings named in each note. The new state (`GameState.held`, the cast mode's `friendsOnly`, a copier's memory) is optional and absent from every shipped game, and the one new event, `held`, is hidden per zone as `exiled` is. It takes R1850000 to R1850006, the first numbers of issue #1850's block, and edits [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]] and [[§10.3]].

**[[R1834000]] keeps a card's own cosmetic picks on the account, the device's store their cache (issue #1834, 2026-10-25)**: [[R1655000]] kept them on the device that made them, but an opponent now sees them, so the server must hold them. Migration 0061's `card_cosmetic_picks` holds one row per account and card, which its own account reads and only the server writes, through `GET` and `PUT /api/card-picks` and `POST /api/card-picks/upload`, a device's one-time upload that never overwrites a card the account holds; a device's own picks go up once, with the first profile that signs in on it while it holds some, and from then on it reads the account's copy, which wins, and sends each change. Keeping the picks on the device and sending them with each queue request was rejected, since the server could check nothing. It takes R1834000, the first number of issue #1834's block, amends [[R1655000]] and edits [[§9.1]] and [[§14.2]].

**[[R1834001]] checks every cosmetic pick as it is written (issue #1834)**: a pick is now shown to another player, so the deck upsert's three slots (by the check [[R1833000]] gave them), the `cosmetics` settings group's six keys and `PUT /api/card-picks` each refuse with 400 `bad_request` an id that is not the registry's under its slot's kind, or that the account does not own where it is worn (a default, a starter or a ledger row, on that card for a per-card kind). Trusting the client's check was rejected. It takes R1834001 of issue #1834's block, amends [[R1295000]] and [[R1297000]] and edits [[§9.1]] and [[§9.4]].

**[[R1834002]] freezes a loadout per seat with the match (issue #1834)**: a ticket, a room and a trio freeze a deck's three slots with it, as [[R642]] freezes its portrait, and each seat's loadout is frozen on the match row as it starts: each deck slot the deck's pick (owned, else the default, the deck's word being final), else the account's, else the default; the foil, finish and border as their kinds' defaults; and each dealt card's own picks, owned on that card, else the default, its full art printed where owned and switched on. An All Random seat wears its account's slots, a Conquest game the picked deck's, a rematch its seats' slots again. Freezing the account's foil, finish and border, which no card wears since [[R1953001]], was rejected. Duos, a replay file and the shared GIF are left for a follow-up. It takes R1834002 of issue #1834's block, amends [[R1297000]] and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§14.2]].

**[[R1834003]] sends the loadouts in two frames and on each replay page (issue #1834)**: `loadouts`, both seats' seat-level halves, on a join, a reconnect and a watcher's attach, before the view; `cardLoadouts`, a seat's cards' own picks, after the first view to a socket that shows a card that seat's deck dealt still as it was dealt, once per socket; a replay page carries both, its cards' for the cards its steps show. A card counts by the deal and never by the view's owner field, which moves when a card changes hands, so no frame tells more of a deck than the views did ([[§10.8]]). It takes R1834003 of issue #1834's block, amends [[R1295000]] and [[R1313000]] and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§14.2]].

**[[R1834004]] has the client dress every seat in its own loadout (issue #1834)**: on an online board, a watched game and an online replay, another seat's half wears its playmat, backdrop and card back and a card its owner's picks, a stolen unit keeping its owner's look, with the rarity rules and B16 holding on every face; an opponent's full art prints where their loadout says so and the viewer's Full-art cards setting is on, printing it regardless being rejected. "Show opponents' cosmetics", on by default, draws every seat but the one the board is seen from in the defaults, drawing a replay or a watched game in only the defaults being rejected. Practice and hotseat are unchanged. It takes R1834004 of issue #1834's block, amends [[R1295000]], [[R1297000]] and [[R1655000]] and edits [[§9.1]], [[§10.10]] and [[§14.2]].

**[[R1834005]] dresses the ladder bots as people are dressed (issue #1834)**: a bot's seat in the defaults alone would tell it from a person's ([[R1452]]), and a bot owns no collection ([[R1454]]). It draws from its seed a wardrobe, kept on its `bots` row and never in the ledger, holding each cosmetic at least `BOT_COSMETIC_MIN_OWNERS` people own and first granted to a person `BOT_COSMETIC_MIN_AGE_DAYS` ago at the rate people of its account age own it, then its account picks, deck slots and card picks from it at the rates people wear them, again when its decks are dealt again and as it ages every `BOT_COSMETIC_REDRAW_DAYS`; its seat freezes and travels as a person's. Dating a cosmetic by a ship date in `cosmetics.json`, which holds none, was rejected for its first grant to a person. It takes R1834005 of issue #1834's block, amends [[R1452]] and [[R1454]] and edits [[§9.12]] and [[§14]].

**[[R1834006]] adds `jackioh-server cosmetics-fit` (issue #1834)**: beside `timing-fit`, it reads the loadouts people's seats froze over the last `BOT_COSMETIC_FIT_WINDOW_DAYS` and what those people own, buckets them by account age and prints a `CosmeticFit` row to paste into `BOT_COSMETIC_FITS` for each bucket of at least `BOT_COSMETIC_FIT_MIN_SAMPLES` seats, and a comment for the rest, which keep the prior; aggregates only, no account id or username. It takes R1834006 of issue #1834's block and edits [[§9.12]].

**[[R1955000]] lets a pack opening's reveal equip the cosmetic it just granted (issue #1955, 2026-10-26)**: a revealed card-pack slot and a cosmetic-pack slot that granted a new cosmetic offer Equip, which writes what the Collection writes with no new store and no request: a foil, finish or border and a full art as the pick of the card they were granted on, a playmat, backdrop or card back as the account's pick; a portrait is a deck's pick ([[R641]]) and offers none, and a deck's own pick still beats the account's ([[R1297000]]). The client decides no ownership: an opening has the open screen and the next read `GET /api/cosmetics` again, so the new cosmetic is worn once the ledger lists it, and marking it owned from the opening's answer was rejected. It takes R1955000, the first number of issue #1955's block, and amends [[R1579000]] and [[R1655000]].

**[[R1976000]] files a development run's file as totals only (issue #1976, 2026-10-26)**: the owner's PC can play far more development games than production's database has room for one row each, and every figure reads the totals already. `jackioh-server stats-import --totals-only <file>` checks the file as `stats-import` does, then folds its records into the totals in one transaction and writes no row of `game_records` for them; the SHA-256 of the file's contents is claimed in `game_record_batches` with the count it folded, so the same file imported twice is counted once, and a record whose id `game_records` holds, or that the file repeats, is left out. Each game is read off the run's own file. It takes R1976000, the first number of issue #1976's block, amends [[R378]] and [[R1044001]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1953000]] and [[R1953001]] weight the token bars by rarity, cut the global cosmetic roll to 2%, animate every chase cosmetic and spend a cosmetic token where a locked cosmetic is shown (issue #1953, 2026-10-26)**: the owner's next pass over the economy after #1651's. A spent slot now adds the points of the rarity it rolled to both token bars, Common 1, Rare 2, Epic 3, Legendary 5 and Mythic 10, a card pack's by its card rarity and a cosmetic pack's by its cosmetic rarity, each a table of its own since the two rarities never mix ([[R1333000]]); both bars hold 200, which a pack's mean 8.65 points fill in about 23 packs, the new target; an opening answers each slot's points, which the client flies into bars pinned at the top of the screen and counts none; and the global roll is 20 of 1,000 ([[R1953000]], amending [[R1301002]], [[R1306001]] and [[R1661001]]). Every Legendary and Mythic foil, finish, border, playmat, backdrop and card back moves wherever it is drawn, none dropped to Epic; the Collection's "Defaults for every card" is gone, so a card with no pick of its own wears its kind's default while the `cosmetics` group keeps its keys; and the shop's cosmetic token picker gives way to an offer on each locked cosmetic a held token covers, in the Collection ([[R1953001]], amending [[R1655000]] and [[R1833000]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1953's block and edit [[§14.3]] and [[§14.5]].

**[[R1855000]] to [[R1855003]] are Anomalies, the board swap, and the Scanning Probes (issue #1855, phase 0 of the Stinkyscape set, 2026-10-26)**: a mechanic merged on `main` before the set that needs it, proved on fixture scripts, which no shipped card reaches. A board replacement replaces its caster's side only, the designer's answer (`docs/stinkyscape-set.md` ST9 Q7): it exiles what the replacing card can reach there, a Stack pile whole, while a Dormant card and one it cannot reach stay and keep their zones, and lays the preset as summons into its named zones ([[R1855000]]). Once the board is laid, every laid Unit's Cry fires in the preset's order, its caster's choices drawn at random under [[R452]]'s mode, the other half of the designer's answer ([[R1855001]]). A preset card's riders are memory its own text reads, a restricted Resource pool among them ([[R1855002]]). A Scanning Probe's scan is one roll, 5% Radiant and 20% plain, each miss of that player's adding 1 and 4 ([[R1855003]]). Where the designer said nothing, Hearthstone's semantics decide: an exile fires no Death, a summon no Cry of its own, a find keeps the miss count as the cards' text reads, and Lucky rolls again. The verb is `replace_board` and not `swap_board`, which is already #87's exchange of the two boards ([[R88]], [[R171]]). The new state is optional and absent from every shipped game. They take R1855000 to R1855003, the first numbers of issue #1855's block, and edit [[§3.2]], [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]] and [[§10.3]].

**[[R1879000]] to [[R1879002]] are the Anomalies and the Scanning Probes, Stinkyscape's S20 (issue #1879, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: Anomaly Found!, its ten boards and S #166 and S #167, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate from #1855's two verbs. Anomaly Found!'s base face lays one of the seven ordinary boards and its Radiant face one of the three rich ones, each equally likely, the boards the designer's with "Small Asteroid" read as Tiny Asteroid and the asteroid fields' pools as riders ([[R1879000]]). Two of the boards need what [[R1855000]] did not lay: SP's Drone Interceptors stand in its backrow by their Flying ([[R1879001]]), and BF's Drone Battleship is its three layers in one pile, a stacked entry laid on top of the one the same replacement laid in its zone ([[R1879002]]). The Scanning Probes are [[R1855003]]'s scan, the brief's ST-S20-1, with no ruling of their own. They take R1879000 to R1879002, the first numbers of issue #1879's block, and edit [[§3.2]], [[§6.3]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1956000]] puts a card's picks on more of the viewer's own copies, and on every open tab at once (issue #1956, 2026-10-26)**: [[R1655000]] wore a card's picks only on a board the viewer plays, its inspect overlays and the Collection's card view. Now the Collection's grid wears them on a card the collection holds, a locked silhouette staying plain; the deck builder's pool and deck list tiles wear them below the screen's own copy, save a card the collection says is not held, a deck list tile showing only the foil, as a still sheen over its strip of art; and a signed-in viewer's own cards in the Almanac wear them behind the cosmetics ledger, the Almanac reading the account, its collection and the session's one ledger, endpoints the client already reads, and nothing at all signed out. Both device stores listen from their first read for another tab's write to their key, so a pick shows in every tab with no reload, and a grid's faces hold a cosmetic still so the card opened over them keeps the few moving slots. Hearthstone shows the version of a card a player owns and equipped wherever it is theirs. It takes R1956000, the first number of issue #1956's block, amends [[R1655000]] and [[R630]] and edits [[§10.10]] and [[§14.2]].

**[[R1956001]] dresses a Created card in its generator's foil, finish and border (issue #1956, 2026-10-26)**: a Created card ([[R943]]) has its own definition and found no picks, so it wore its kinds' defaults ([[R1953001]]). The engine now keeps, beside the cards, each Created card's root generator, the catalog card whose script minted it or the first card of a chain of Created cards, recorded by the effect verbs that mint a card for its generator's own owner and by Infinite Reserves' token, and left out of the state hash and the golden replay's view texts, so nothing recorded or played changes; the card's owner alone is told it, as `createdBy` on the views that carry `created`, and the AI's redacted state keeps only the seat's own. The viewer's own Created card wears the foil, finish and border its generator's copy would, owned on the generator, never its full art, by its own rarity rules. Hearthstone's golden minions summon golden tokens. It takes R1956001, amends [[R1655000]] and edits [[§10.1]], [[§10.8]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R1857000]] to [[R1857003]] are the foundation of the Stinkyscape set (issue #1857, part of #1856, 2026-10-26)**: the set is the third one [[R1420]]'s gate holds, in the catalog after Meditative+ and built card by card on `main` before its release (issue #1884) lists it in `SHIPPED_SETS`, with its four new tags, Drone Core, Weapon, War Doctrine and Swarm, waiting for the release's migration to join the server's tag check, as Potion waited for Meditative+'s ([[R1178000]]); while it is held, the census tests read the sets that ship or the entries that have landed ([[R1857000]]). The set's data the designer's list leaves open is decided: #174 and #175 for its second #21 and #122, the `T-` indices, the War Doctrines tokens, the Swarm Units, a few Radiant stats, and the Dormant Starship a card, not a token, which no pool and no deck holds, as [[R674]] keeps Glitch out ([[R1857001]]). Its Terminus tokens and War Doctrines are named by the pools that name them by tag, as [[R480]]'s are, and its Resources by [[R1840002]]'s roll, as The Coin by the setup ([[R1857002]]); and it prints five new labels, `Activate 3:` to `Activate 6:` and `Embiggened (5):` ([[R1857003]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1857's block; the S parts take theirs from their own issues' blocks (`docs/stinkyscape-set.md`). They edit [[§5]], [[§7]], [[§8]] and [[R1420]].

**[[R1883000]] gives the Stinkyscape set its look and its sound behind [[R1420]]'s gate (issue #1883, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: its cards wear a set mark of their own, a ringed green planet in deep space ([[R503]]); its four tags join the chips, Drone Core, Weapon and Swarm the deck builder's and War Doctrine the Almanac's alone, each shown only once a shipped card carries it ([[R1381]]); and its 65 Legendaries, Mythics and tokens printed so have their intros ([[R1352]]), with a crystal for the set's glint and families of their own for its Drone Cores, Weapons, War Doctrines and Terminus cards. Nothing of it reaches a player before the release (issue #1884). It edits no section.

**[[R1863000]] watches the first resolved play of each card it names for the rest of the game (issue #1863, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: Stinkyscape #44 Great Paperclip Theory's "for the rest of the game, the first time you play each tier of Drone Core" is a `never` player modifier on its caster, `ModifierKind::PlayWatch`, which answers the first resolved play of its owner's of each card it names, a cast included ([[R70]]), once each, re-entering the card's step with no `self` ([[R127]]) and badged in the card's words ([[R169]]). It answers once the play has resolved, so the next tier arrives after the played card's own effect, and a countered play, never counted nor resolved ([[R448]]), answers nothing and its card stays watched; a play before the watch counts for nothing, and two casts keep two records. It takes R1863000, the first number of issue #1863's block, and edits [[§8.10]] and [[§10.1]].

**[[R1876000]] to [[R1876004]] are the Acclaimed, Stinkyscape's S17 (issue #1876, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: eight cards built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate, two of them on new engine verbs. ZolarKeth's University can't use its own Activate, a refusal it carries while it stays on the field, no zone Locked ([[R1876000]]), and "Activate your Universities" runs each one's Activate as ZolarKeth's effect with the University its `self`, spending none of its uses ([[R1876001]]). Kwzka's Death withdraws every Allure on the field, either side's and whoever made it ([[R1876002]]), and Kwzka Allures each enemy Unit played for less than its cost now, as it arrives ([[R1876004]]). Tnack Trav copies a Legendary or Mythic Unit by its card's rarity, a token's Token ([[R1876003]]). Siva 2, iKwzki, A Very Dandy Lad, Arctic Citadel and Scornboi are [[R1850001]], [[R1850002]], [[R77]] and [[R1185004]]'s verbs with no ruling of their own. They take R1876000 to R1876004, the first numbers of issue #1876's block, and edit [[§6.3]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1859000]] to [[R1859013]] are the Starship cards of the Stinkyscape set (issue #1859, its part S02, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the Dormant Starship and the eleven cards built on [[R1852000]]'s Starship, held by [[R1420]]'s gate with the rest of the set. A fused piece's numbers are read on the face it fused on, so a Radiant piece's When launched reads its Radiant numbers in a Starship that is not ([[R1859001]]); After Burner's keywords, Warpfield Amplifier's switch to Attack and its lasting Attack, and Ultra Capacitor's count of the pieces the Starship remembers are read off the Starship it fused into ([[R1859000]], [[R1859002]], [[R1859004]]); Spacerace Stimulus reveals once, on any launch or, Radiant, on a Starship Piece played face-up, a face-down play being no piece ([[R1859003]]); Magic Module's Spells are the ones the catalog marks `dealsDamage`, which a query now names ([[R1859005]]); Firststrike Capability's Doctrine resolves inside its Cry, before the Starship's When launched ([[R1859006]]); "for the rest of the game, your Units have X" is a player modifier the layers read beside the auras, not on a Vanilla Unit ([[R1859007]]), and each War Doctrine's Starship line runs once, as it is cast ([[R1859008]] to [[R1859011]]); Prepare For Liftoff answers its owner's piece fusing into their Starship, decided against the brief's controller reading ([[R1859012]]); and the Dormant Starship's script is the hull alone ([[R1859013]]). The part also fixes [[R1852003]]'s When launched, which a piece on the field, or a Starship launched before, answered on every later launch. They take the first numbers of issue #1859's block: [[R1859000]] is the brief's ST-S02-1, [[R1859002]] to [[R1859013]] its ST-S02-2 to ST-S02-13, and [[R1859001]] the build's own. They edit [[§6.3]], [[§8.10]], [[§10.1]] and [[§10.4]].

**[[R1877000]] and [[R1877001]] are outside data and hidden hands, Stinkyscape's S18 (issue #1877, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: KY's Tricknology, Yuma, xxFreshJack, Shockwave447 and Gokugains, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate from M11's hidden Nerfs and trade offers and M12's card metrics. KY's Tricknology's cover is the build's coordinates and the Jacobian parallelogram cornered at the chosen zone, and its pieces land in open, unlocked unit zones only, a covered backrow zone taking none ([[R1877000]]). A trade offer carries the Lucky of the card that made it, and Lucky draws the opponent's Discover again, keeping the set whose cheapest card costs most ([[R1877001]]). They take R1877000 and R1877001, the first numbers of issue #1877's block, and edit [[§8.10]].

**[[R1965000]] plays a proved lethal trimmed to the actions it needs (issue #1965, 2026-10-26)**: with In Too Deep's reward L ([[R454]]), a (0) Spell in the graveyard is free to play again and again: Last Hurrah and Small Time Recruits, which draw nothing with the deck empty, and The Coin, whose mana this turn has no cap. No such replay moves `ready_gap`, and the best-first lethal walk breaks a tie toward the longer line, so it proved lethals behind the replays, and the AI, playing only a line's first action before it plans again, replayed them without end (game 4 of [[R1171000]]'s series never finished). The AI now plays a proved lethal from its shortest suffix that still wins on every determinization, less each other action it still wins without, and plays none when its budget or clock stops the trim before it has that suffix; the walks are unchanged, so every lethal they found they still find. It is the AI's decision alone, no rule of the game. It takes R1965000, the first number of issue #1965's block, and edits [[§9.9]]'s account of the search.

**[[R1896000]] has a result clear only the in-match flag that names its match (issue #1896, 2026-10-26)**: the result write cleared both seats' flags whatever match they named, so a late result for an older match erased the flag of a newer match a player had been seated in, and that player's socket could no longer get back to the live table; the clear also erred on a seat whose profile row was gone. It now uses [[R890000]]'s conditional clear, as every undo of a failed start does, which refuses nothing and never errs on a missing profile; a results row that names a missing profile is still refused by its foreign keys, and an account cannot be deleted mid-match. It takes R1896000, the first number of issue #1896's block. It edits [[§9.5]] and amends [[R890000]].

**[[R1989000]] seats every 1v1 door by the match seed's coin, and a rematch swaps the seats (issue #1989, 2026-10-26)**: a queue pair, a room join, a ladder-bot pairing, a bot-vs-bot kickoff and a Conquest series start all draw Player 1 from `{seed}:seats` at cursor 0, so a fixed seed set deals both seat orders and the same seed always seats the same way; decks, portraits, slots and leans travel with the profile while All Random deals stay seat-keyed. A Conquest series draws its p1 by the same coin and keeps [[R335]]'s game-number alternation from there. A rematch flips no coin: it seats the finished match's previous Player 2 as Player 1, double-or-nothing included ([[R672]]). Seeded end-to-end specs pin their seats beside their seed ([[R143]]). It amends [[R1453]] and [[R335]] and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§9.12]]. Where the spec gives the first turn to Player 1 ([[§2.1]]) but does not say who sits there, Hearthstone's coin decides. It takes R1989000, the first number of issue #1989's block.

**[[R1999000]] gives every opened slot its bar points (issue #1999, 2026-10-26)**: the owner's decision. Until #1999 only a spent slot filled the token bars, a card pack's duplicate with nothing of its rarity left to own or a cosmetic pack's slot with nothing unowned, so a young collection, whose slots are mostly new, barely moved them. Now every opened slot adds the points of the rarity it rolled to both bars, whatever it grants, on top of what it grants: a card pack's slot by its card rarity, pity's force included, and a cosmetic pack's by the cosmetic rarity it rolled, not the one it fell to. An opening's `slotPoints` holds every slot's points and the client flies each into the bars; the rolls and their logs are unchanged, so every logged opening replays, and the sizes and targets stay, since [[R1953000]]'s mean already counted every slot and now holds on any collection. It takes R1999000, the first number of issue #1999's block, amends [[R1301002]], [[R1306001]], [[R1661001]] and [[R1953000]] and edits [[§14.3]], [[§14.5]] and [[§14.6]].

**[[R1996000]] keeps the queue readout for a few seconds (issue #1996, 2026-10-26)**: every waiting client asks for the queue readout every `SERIES_POLL_SECONDS`, and the server counted the hour's claimed tickets and ended games again for each request. It now keeps what it counted for `QUEUE_READOUT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (5 s) and answers every readout inside that time with it, so its numbers may be that much older. Readouts that miss together each count rather than wait for one, since the numbers need not be exact. Migration 0062's indexes, which let each count read only its hour, change no rule and are no ruling. It takes R1996000, the first number of issue #1996's block, amends [[R640002]] and edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R2000000]] drops the public player statistics opt-out and the landing's Clear (issue #2000, 2026-10-26)**: Settings loses its Public player statistics switch and the client sends no `isPrivate`, so the unchanged server keeps a row's privacy and makes a new row public, and the landing's stats lose their note and Clear. It takes R2000000, the first number of issue #2000's block, amends [[R654]], [[R1452]] and [[R1584000]] and edits [[§9.11]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R1994000]] keeps each patch's game figures past the week's purge and shows them on /stats (issue #1994, 2026-10-26)**: the owner weighs seat changes such as [[R1156000]]'s, and no figure from people's games older than a week existed, since who went first and won, how a game ended, how long it ran and [[R1256000]]'s counts lived only in a record's detail, which [[R1044001]] empties. Each record folded now folds its whole game into the totals under its records key, kept by migration 0063's server-only `game_totals`; the engine reads the game figures off them for both `stats report --games` and the new public `GET /api/stats/games`, provisional below [[R654]]'s gate and split by both seats' pilots; and the Cards tab of `/stats` opens with a Game figures panel that names no source ([[R661]]). Records folded before it have no game figures, and the panel says how many of the patch's games it counts. It takes R1994000, the first number of issue #1994's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1860000]] to [[R1860009]] are the ten readings Stinkyscape's S03 part takes (issue #1860, part of #1856, 2026-10-26)**: the twenty scripts of `S03, Starship Pieces on existing keywords`, each proved in its own card file and, for the two new engine capabilities, in the engine's rules tests too. "Survives an attack" is any combat the card fights, as attacker or defender, declared or forced, after which it still stands — read by `afterAttack`'s `survived` and by `afterAttacked`'s lack of a death snapshot — while a Cleave splash is no attack and a spent Divine Shield counts ([[R1860000]]). "Place N Plague Counters" with no card named is [[R689]]'s single prompt over every permanent, the card itself among the choices ([[R1860001]]), and "per Plague Counter on this" is one hit of counters times damage to each enemy, nothing at none ([[R1860002]]). "All allies" is the hero and every Unit of the side, the card included ([[R1860003]]). "Attack" is the Attack died with, buffs included, dealt by the new `damageRandomEnemies` verb as one hit each to that many different random enemies, fewer when fewer stand, none at 0 ([[R1860004]]). A turn-start "+N attack" lasts and stacks ([[R1860005]]). "Armor" for the hero is [[§6.1]]'s Armor, kept for the game ([[R1860006]]). "Stats equal to this unit's" are the died-with Attack and maximum Health, whose token the Radiant face doubles with Divine Shield through `summon`'s new `keywords` rider ([[R1860007]]). "Stratos" is S #68, and a full board summons none ([[R1860008]]). "Freeze an enemy" is an enemy Unit, never a hero, once a turn and twice Radiant ([[R1860009]]). Where the designer said nothing, Hearthstone's semantics decide. They take R1860000 to R1860009, the first numbers of issue #1860's block, and edit [[§6.2]] and [[§6.3]].

**[[R2031000]] puts "More cards from" beside every random deck a player can start a game on (issue #2031, 2026-10-26)**: [[R924000]]'s picker was on All Random's lobby and rematch and on practice's Random deck, but not on an All Random challenge to a friend, sent or accepted, and a Duos room's Random deck still had [[R1372]]'s switch for the newest set alone. All three now show the picker and send its pick as `leanSet`, which the server already read, sharing the device's online pick with the lobby. It takes R2031000, the first number of issue #2031's block, and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§13]].

**[[R1864000]] to [[R1864004]] are Stinkyscape's trade cards (issue #1864, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: Siva 1, The Syndicate and The Trade Union, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. [[R1864000]] makes Siva 1's Cry one random pick, drawn as it resolves, among the enemy Units Allure reaches whose tags hold Acclaimed, Allured as [[R1045000]] has it, and nothing with none; [[R1864001]] has its Radiant face, with none, summon a random shipped Acclaimed Unit for the opponent, Frozen and Allured, and nothing on a full row. [[R1864002]] makes "Cards in your hand have Tradable" an aura over its controller's hand and reads Tradable through the layers, extending [[R1838000]]. [[R1864003]] hands a trigger on `traded` the card the Trade put into the deck and gives The Syndicate one roll on it, exiled, made Radiant or left, and none on a card drawn straight back. [[R1864004]] lets a card acting on its controller's field give them Trades a turn, the most holding. They take R1864000 to R1864004, the first numbers of issue #1864's block, and edit [[§6.1]], [[§8.10]] and [[§10.9]].

**[[R1862000]] to [[R1862006]] are the weapon rack, Stinkyscape's S06 (issue #1862, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the set's twenty-one weapons and Weapon carriers, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. A weapon's "Deal N damage to an enemy" takes a declared enemy Unit or the enemy hero, its Cleave the same hit on each Unit beside a Unit target and its Pierce `ignoreArmor`, neither keyword given to the weapon ([[R1862000]]). "All enemies, twice" is whole rounds, each after the last one's state check, so a Unit a round destroys is not hit again ([[R1862001]]). "Discover N Rare Weapons and Equip them into this" Discovers from the Weapon-tagged cards of the rarity, the pool naming the set, and a Radiant carrier wears its picks on their Radiant faces ([[R1862002]]). Rush Token Launcher's token attacks a declared enemy Unit, Taunt Units first, and the Activate waits for an open unit zone and an enemy Unit ([[R1862003]]). "Cast a random X on it" is a random cast aimed at the declared pick where it may take it, `cast_random_at`, the engine's one new verb ([[R1862004]]). Jade Launcher deals the Auspicious Rock's 2 to an enemy and rolls the Rock's reward ([[R1862005]]), and Book Launcher's harmful Books are Blood, Flame, Wildfire and Nerf, its helpful ones Heal, Stats and Buff, Vital Kill in neither ([[R1862006]]). They take R1862000 to R1862006, the first numbers of issue #1862's block, and edit [[§6.3]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1865000]] to [[R1865002]] are Stinkyscape S10, Job Application and its Paycheck (issue #1865, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the set's second staged Quest, built on [[R1854000]]'s lines behind [[R1420]]'s gate. A hand card is now set aside beneath a card as [[R1850003]] holds a permanent, and given back to its owner's hand at a price and Buffed, or exiled if its holder leaves the field first ([[R1865000]]). The resume's wait to hear back is the card's own evaluation, the designer's "algorithm based on the card uploaded" with a little RNG: a merit of rarity, cost and Radiance picks one of three weighted tables, an iKwzki always waiting 6 ([[R1865001]]). The Offer is one Activate of two modes, a re-application triples every reward to at most ×9, and Death resummons the card into its own zone at Stage 1 ([[R1865002]]). It takes R1865000 to R1865002, the first numbers of issue #1865's block, and edits [[§6.3]] (Hold beneath) and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1866000]] to [[R1866004]] are the three Brains (issue #1866, S11 of the Stinkyscape set, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the card part that builds the Brains on [[R1843000]]'s vote, held by [[R1420]]'s gate until the set's release. Imitated Instrumentality summons MELCHIOR, BALTHASAR and CASPER in that order into its caster's leftmost open backrow zones, one with no zone not summoned, each Radiant on its Radiant face, and three votes in one turn are its caster's loss ([[R1866000]]). MELCHIOR counts the Units acting on the field, a Dormant card none, and its Activate is a Discover of three different combat keywords whose pick goes to each of its controller's Units, its Radiant face giving each Unit its own three random ones ([[R1866001]]). BALTHASAR reads its seat's own starting health through the new `starting_health_of`, a handicap's included, and copies [[R451]]'s last face-up play at +1 ([[R1866002]]). CASPER remembers the Float of each of its controller's turn ends it stands on the field for and votes on the last one, keeping no new player state (`docs/stinkyscape-set.md` D3), so one that saw none does not vote ([[R1866003]]). Its Activate Allures one random enemy Unit, and on its Radiant face one random Unit card of the opponent's hand by a hand watch, the caster seeing only the count ([[R1866004]]). Where the designer said nothing, Hearthstone's semantics decide, except CASPER's Float, which is the card's own memory and not the player's. They take R1866000 to R1866004, the first numbers of issue #1866's block, and edit [[§8.10]].

**[[R1870000]] to [[R1870007]] are the Terminus cards of the Stinkyscape set (issue #1870, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: Olivian Expanse, its six Terminus tokens and Dice of Doom, S22, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. Olivian Expanse Discovers from the seven Terminus cards named by id, its pick at (0) on an empty library, and on the Radiant face with an empty hand too the pick carries a win in its own memory, which its play claims ([[R1870000]]). Farewell exiles each Stack pile whole, the field before the graveyards, a Dormant card staying ([[R1870001]]). KY's Last Exam asks one of a bank of impossible problems, every answerer guessing one in four; a right answer deals 30 and gains the card one Echo repeat, at most `SELF_ECHO_GAIN_CAP` a play, and the Radiant cheat sheet is a ✓ on the right option seen in its chooser's prompt alone ([[R1870002]]). Event Horizon owes an extra turn every time it resolves ([[R1870003]]). Last Word prevents the lethal hit and ends the turn; its controller's next turn, an extra one on their own turn, gives their Units Charge and ends in their loss, the Radiant face's 100 mana landing after the refresh ([[R1870004]]). 1v1 Final Destination fires at the end of the opponent's turn, leaves one lane drawn at random open and Locks the other four, sets both heroes to 1 and discards every Spell in hand, its controller's first, the Radiant fill counted after the discards ([[R1870005]]). The Big Crunch casts one of each Call to Chaos edition in catalog order ([[R1870006]]), and Dice of Doom's Embiggened Lucky is kept for good in its tuning, counted in the roll it is gained for ([[R1870007]]). They take R1870000 to R1870007, the first numbers of issue #1870's block, and edit [[§6.3]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R2035000]] puts the held quests on the tavern landing, behind a bookmark on its doors' box (issue #2035, 2026-10-27)**: an active account while `ECONOMY_LIVE` is on gets a quiet ribbon under the three doors with the held count, opening `/collection`'s rows in a parchment panel over the floor, read once on mount and once per due time while open. It takes R2035000, the first number of issue #2035's block, and edits [[§14.9]].

**[[R1869000]] to [[R1869003]] are the rulings of Stinkyscape's S16 cards, its auras and rule benders (issue #1869, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: thirteen cards built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate, two of them on new engine reads. [[R1869000]] makes Rod of Ages a host that lends Equip, so any Unit card its controller plays may be played onto it as an Equip play while it is no fusion, extending [[R1086]]. [[R1869001]] has Z'Zoth and A'Zoth summon their graveyard's Units by the running face's own Death or Cry, oldest first and with no Cry, and A'Zoth trigger each Cry it summoned, in that order, with every choice of its controller's random as [[R1855001]]'s are, its Radiant face firing each Cry its repeats before the next. [[R1869002]] frees Tmack Trav to attack again after each Unit its attack destroys, forced attacks counting, unlike [[R424]]'s Golem. [[R1869003]] makes Sanctum's text an aura lending Flashback to every card in its controller's deck and graveyard, each graveyard card played from there once ([[R783012]], [[R783013]]). They take R1869000 to R1869003, the first numbers of issue #1869's block, and edit [[§6.1]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1878000]] to [[R1878003]] are Jlockeed's space program, Stinkyscape's S19 (issue #1878, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the seven Jlockeed cards, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. "A random Starship Piece" is a card that prints the keyword on its base face, from the set that prints it: the catalog query's new `keywords` filter ([[R1878000]]). "Embiggened (N): Starship Piece" is a keyword the Embiggened version's text lends it, as Core #56 Jilliax's Equip comes with its price, and a Fuse keeps a lent piece only on the card it keeps, as a grant ([[R1878001]]). Missile Pod divides its Attack as it stands at the end of turn, and equipped it divides its host's ([[R1878002]]). "It gains Starship Piece" is a keyword given to a card as it reaches the hand, which it keeps onto the field: `add_random_from_catalog`'s `keywords` rider ([[R1878003]]). The brief's ST-S19-2 (Permanent Procurement Lobbying's rest-of-game start of turn and its discount) needs no ruling of its own: it is C+ #52's, [[R458]], [[R62]] and [[R65]]. They take R1878000 to R1878003, the first numbers of issue #1878's block, and edit [[§6.1]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1872000]] to [[R1872003]] are the Miners, Stinkyscape's S05 (issue #1872, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the five Miners, Ozark, Orca and Galaxy, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. A Miner's Activate gains one Resource by [[R1840002]]'s roll into the tray of whoever controls the Unit it is on, an equipped host's controller included, and "Activate N" is N uses a turn ([[R1872000]]). Ozark equips a random plain Miner, and its Radiant face two different Radiant ones ([[R1872001]]). Orca offers 2 plain Miners, Radiant ones when played Embiggened, and twice on its Radiant face ([[R1872002]]). Galaxy discovers 2 Radiant Miners, and its Radiant face fuses a Radiant copy of each of the five, v1 to v5 ([[R1872003]]). All four are built from verbs already on `main` (`gain_resources`, `equip_picks` and the extra turn cooldown), so no engine change. They take R1872000 to R1872003, the first numbers of issue #1872's block, and edit [[§8.10]].

**[[R1873000]] to [[R1873003]] are The Greatest Heist and Tylannt, Stinkyscape's S09 (issue #1873, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the set's first staged Quest and the Unit that completes Quests, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate from [[R1854000]]'s lines. Tylannt completes the current stage of its controller's Quests. Its Radiant face summons The Greatest Heist, whose Cry, its Discover, runs before the completion because the summon names it ([[R411]]), and that Heist's first stage is completed with the rest ([[R1873000]]). For that, a triggered Cry reaches a face-up Field Spell on the field as it reaches a Unit there ([[R1873003]]). The Heist's Discover is one pool, the cards tagged Acclaimed or Jelly Bean ([[R1873001]]), and Stage 2's finds are rolls, each an Anomaly Found! or a Resource at even odds ([[R1873002]]). They take R1873000 to R1873003, the first numbers of issue #1873's block, and edit [[§6.3]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1880000]] to [[R1880002]] are the bundles, Stinkyscape's S24 (issue #1880, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: Vanguard Coalition, ETHICS, Golden Accord, Pegasus, Star-jumper Core and Swear Loyalities, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. A bundle adds each card it names in the order printed, on its own face at its printed price, and its Radiant face's "a Radiant X, Y and Z" makes every card listed Radiant ([[R1880000]]). Swear Loyalities' "wherever it is" is its caster's library, hand and graveyard, walked in Genn's Greed's order once the chosen bundle is added, the opponent's copies untouched ([[R1880001]]), and its choice offers all five bundles as declared modes chosen with the play ([[R1880002]]). They take R1880000 to R1880002, the first numbers of issue #1880's block, and edit [[§8.10]].

**[[R1875000]] to [[R1875007]] are the rulings of Stinkyscape's S15 cards, its traps and reactions (issue #1875, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: ten cards built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate, one of them on a new engine point. [[R1875000]] has Masochism Unmasked reveal once, on a hit its controller's hero takes on their own turn, an extra turn included. [[R1875001]] lets Ticketed hold another face-up permanent of either side, released however Ticketed leaves the field. [[R1875002]] makes Gatinsir's and Aerelret's ambush answer only an attack an enemy Unit declares, the attack going on as declared after the summons. [[R1875003]] gives the Warp Disruptors' Attack to every Unit and their First Strike to their controller's, and answers an enemy Unit's arrival as C #5 Tesla does. [[R1875004]] and [[R1875005]] stuff a played enemy Unit into a Sack once it resolves, its controller's Sack or on the Radiant face the trap's, released however the Sack leaves. [[R1875006]] makes Pocket Peptides a would-die replacement for a Unit killed in combat whose cast resolves before any card moves, the check starting over, its casts aimed only. [[R1875007]] has Wormhole steal only a Spell aimed at its opponent's own side. They take R1875000 to R1875007, the first numbers of issue #1875's block, and edit [[§4.5]], [[§6.3]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R1861000]] to [[R1861002]] are what Stinkyscape's Resources and asteroids part needed of the engine (issue #1861, S04 of the Stinkyscape set, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: its 22 cards are scripted on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate, and three of their lines asked for rules the spec did not have. A card whose face sets `handSale`, S #89 Spice, sells from its owner's hand at the open Marketplace as a tray Resource does, at a quote drawn each turn after the Resources', a Rare Resource's range (3 to 10 Credits, Radiant 6 to 20) since the list prints none ([[R1861000]]). "Lower this card's Brittle by 1", S #39 The Narcor Project's Activate, takes 1 off the count in force and crumbles the card at once when it reaches 0, and "if Brittle destroyed this" reads that count spent on the Death's snapshot ([[R1861001]]). "For the rest of the game, … at the end of each of your turns" is [[R458]]'s rest-of-game effect re-entered at the end of its player's turns, the turn it is made on included ([[R1861002]]). Each follows Hearthstone where the spec is silent, the rejected readings named in each note. New state is optional and skipped when absent, the hand-sale quotes are drawn only while a Marketplace is open, which no shipped card opens, so no shipped game changes. They take R1861000 to R1861002, the first numbers of issue #1861's block, and edit [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§8.10]] and [[§10]].

**[[R1867000]] to [[R1867010]] are the spells on existing primitives, Stinkyscape's S13 (issue #1867, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate, each card's reading of the designer's text on verbs the engine already had. Freeze Ray Freezes the target and its neighbours in its row on its side, whichever side it stands on ([[R1867000]]). Otherworldly Endurance's Radiant face adds all three of its spells Radiant, as #11's and #12's do ([[R1867001]]). Inner Fire sets Attack as a buff of the difference, never below 0 ([[R1867002]]), and Divine Spirit doubles the Health a Unit has left ([[R1867003]]). Frostbolt hits a Unit or a hero, and its Radiant hit on the neighbours is the Spell's own damage, not Cleave ([[R1867004]]). Shadow Word Death and Pain read Attack through the layers as the target is chosen, and their Radiant faces take the neighbours whatever their Attack ([[R1867005]]). Psychic Scream shuffles every permanent on the field into the opponent's deck as their card ([[R1867006]]). Moonberry's random Other-Origins card is any of the tag's tokens in the sets that ship ([[R1867007]]). Fortify Flesh rolls its hero's Armor uniformly, Lucky keeping the higher roll ([[R1867008]]). Flash Freeze Freezes every Unit, and the side its Radiant face spares keeps its attacks ([[R1867009]]). Catalyst Comet adds a random Catalyst card, then shuffles itself into its owner's deck ([[R1867010]]). They take R1867000 to R1867010, the first numbers of issue #1867's block, and edit [[§8.10]]; their paragraph was written by issue #2176 (the M12 audit's B-8), since #2044 merged without one.

**[[R1868000]] and [[R1868001]] are two of the spells that bend a rule, Stinkyscape's S14 (issue #1868, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. An X card's declared target is checked at the X of its play, which [[§10.5]] step 1 now stamps before it reads the choices, and not again as the Spell resolves, as Hearthstone checks a target's requirement only when it is chosen: No-No Words reaches a Unit below 4X Attack, its pick needed ([[R1868000]]). Secure System's "set all Units' Attack to 0" and Unsecure System's "double it" are Buffs of each Unit's Attack as it stands when the Spell resolves, since the spec has no set of Attack alone ([[R1868001]]). They take R1868000 and R1868001, the first numbers of issue #1868's block, and edit [[§8.10]]; their paragraph was written by issue #2176 (the M12 audit's B-8), since #2064 merged without one.

**[[R1881000]] to [[R1881007]] are the Marketplace's table and its ten AI cards, Stinkyscape's S21 (issue #1881, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: T-AI-1 to T-AI-10 as the brief's ST15 designs them and the set's table of lots, built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate. Mining Subroutine gains only a Common or Rare Resource, so a bought copy never sells for more than it cost ([[R1881000]]). "A Starship Piece" on Route Optimizer and Salvage Protocol is [[R1878000]]'s pool, never a token, and Route Optimizer's pick costs (1) less ([[R1881001]]). Predictive Shield cancels an enemy Unit's declared attack on its controller's Unit (Radiant: or hero) and Freezes the attacker; a forced attack springs nothing ([[R1881002]]). Launch Sequencer's price waits for its caster's next launch, Starship or none ([[R1881003]]). Combat Subroutine gives each other Unit of its controller's its own random combat keyword, a Dormant card passed by ([[R1881004]]). Drone Uplink's and Honeypot's "It gains" is the summon's `keywords` rider, and the copy a granted piece fuses is a plain Drone Interceptor ([[R1881005]]). `MARKETPLACE_TABLE` is ST3's ten lots, the Starship Pieces' a new pool by keyword, `MarketPool::SetKeyword` ([[R1881006]]). A Pocket Peptides fused into a permanent, not spent as it fires, answers no death while a CN Peptides its controller cast is resolving, so it cannot answer its own cast's check forever ([[R1881007]]). They take R1881000 to R1881007, the first numbers of issue #1881's block, and edit [[§6.3]], [[§8.10]] and [[R1853001]].
