**[[R2422000]] settles a practice claim once, after a restart or a failed pay (issue #2422, 2026-10-31)**: a practice claim burned its seed and then ran its check and pay in a task of its own, so its pay was lost three ways, each leaving the seed burned and a later claim answering `already_claimed`: a shutdown's drain closed the store under a settle whose caller had gone, a crash took the settle, or all three tries at the pay failed ([[R1688000]]). The claim is now kept with its seed until it settles, the pay marks the seed paid in its own transaction and only where it has no mark, a refusal marks it refused, the drain waits for every settle running, and the retention loop checks again and settles any claim still unsettled `PRACTICE_SETTLE_GRACE_SECONDS` after it was made, paying it once with no board (migration 0071). It takes R2422000, the first number of issue #2422's block, amends [[R1688000]] and [[R1303001]], and edits [[§14.8]].

**[[R2477000]] has the AI price an own backrow card's start-of-turn and end-of-turn effects by one firing (issue #2477, 2026-10-30)**: the AI's evaluation priced a Field Spell by its cost alone, so Mana Well's mana and Unbiased Immigration's card were worth nothing and it rarely played them. At each decision's root it now fires each start-of-turn and end-of-turn hook of its own backrow and of its hand's Field Spells (set first) once, and a card counts `AI_EVAL.turn_hook` of its controller's evaluation after the firing less before it, each crystal gained at a next-turn crystal's worth. It takes R2477000, the first number of issue #2477's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2478000]] refuses a spent account before the database and counts socket upgrades (issue #2478, 2026-10-30)**: the API's limit of 300 requests a minute per account ([[R109]]) was checked after the router had read the caller's profile, so a request past it was refused with 429 only after it had checked one of the pool's ten connections out, and one signed-in client in a loop kept them all busy while live matches waited to save their moves; the match, co-op and watch sockets read the profile too and were counted by nothing, `WS_MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_ADDRESS` limiting the sockets open at once and not how often they reopen. The server now remembers, in memory and at most `KNOWN_PROFILES_MAX` users, the profile id each verified auth user last resolved to, and refuses a request from a known account whose budget is spent before it opens a transaction, with [[R192]]'s 429, logged and counted as [[R1717000]] says; the map holds an id only, so every request it lets by still reads the profile and meets [[§9.4]]'s gate, a user it does not know still reads the store and a new account is still created lazily, and two tokens of one account still share one bucket ([[R157]]). Each match, co-op and watch upgrade whose token verifies counts as one request against that bucket, and one past it gets the `forbidden` frame and close 4429, which the match and co-op clients retry on their backoff ([[R1441]]) and the watch page takes as a refusal, as it does any `forbidden` frame, a refused upgrade not being counted. An HTTP 429 before the handshake was rejected, since every refusal of [[R148]] arrives after it, and so was caching whole profiles, which would let a stale ban pass the gate. It takes R2478000, the first number of issue #2478's block, edits [[§9.8]] and amends [[R148]].

**[[R2479000]] Back in a live online match asks first (issue #2479, 2026-10-30)**: the board's Back and the browser's Back left a live online match at once, and nothing said that the match goes on and counts as a loss unless the player is back within the disconnect grace. The match screen now holds every way off with the creator's leave guard ([[R1754000]]) and asks, naming `DISCONNECT_GRACE_SECONDS`. A finished, voided or refused match is left at once, and the co-op table is unchanged. It takes R2479000, the first number of issue #2479's block, and amends [[R765]].

**[[R2480000]] asks before a cosmetic token is spent (issue #2480, 2026-10-30)**: the Collection's offer of a held Rare or Epic cosmetic token ([[R1953001]]) spent it on the first press, on a tile over the tile's own button and, with no Rare token held, an Epic token on a Rare cosmetic. Its press now asks in its place, as the shop's card token does: "Unlock {cosmetic}? This spends 1 {Rare|Epic} token.", with Confirm, which sends the redemption, and Cancel, which spends nothing, takes the focus and answers Escape. It takes R2480000, the first number of issue #2480's block, and amends [[R1953001]].

**[[R2490000]] prices a Unit standing in the backrow once, as a Unit (issue #2490, 2026-10-30)**: a Flying Unit standing in the backrow is a Unit for every rule ([[R1081000]]), but the AI's evaluation priced it twice, as the Unit the unit walk priced and again as a backrow card. The backrow walk now skips every card `is_standing_unit` already priced, behind a switch on in `AI_EVAL` and off in the frozen `GREEDY_EVAL`; a Dormant Starship standing there counts its slumber price alone, and an Animated card standing as a Unit its unit price alone, with no `animated_share` top-up. It takes R2490000, the first number of issue #2490's block, amends [[R2380000]] and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2491000]] A card's drill-down reads its patch history in one load (issue #2491, 2026-10-30)**: [[R978000]] kept each patch's report under a bound sized for 30 patches and three pilots, and once `?mode=` split the views four ways over 80 patches, opening a card's page in every view within the TTL pushed out what the next page needed, so each later page loaded every patch again, one after another. The drill-down's history is now one kept entry per pilot and match type, read off every patch's totals in one load as the list route's windowed report is and shared by every card's page, while its turn and co-played figures still read the current patch's kept report: a page that misses makes at most two loads, and every figure is byte for byte what it was. It takes R2491000, the first number of issue #2491's block, amends [[R978000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2500000]] ends the loop of Siphon Squad's self-Tribute with a self-resummoning Death (issue #2500, 2026-10-30)**: Classic #88 Siphon Squad's "When your opponent has no Units, Tribute this", fused or granted onto a Unit with S #112 Job Application's "Death: Resummon this at Stage 1", sacrificed the Unit at every state check of a board whose opponent held no Units, and the Death brought it back for the next, so the board never settled; the daily fuzz of 2026-10-30 panicked on it in the 1v1 wave (seed 7243) and in Duos (seed 1231), both built by M #47 饕餮's end-of-turn fusing. The condition now holds nothing on a body that is not Siphon Squad itself and arrived this turn, [[R2197005]]'s terminator for Floodgates' sweep on such a body, so the Unit returns once a turn and the board settles; a printed Siphon Squad is Tributed the moment it is set, as before. It takes R2500000, the first of issue #2500's block.

**[[R2511000]] and [[R2511001]] are the Training Grounds Legacy cards, named and tagged apart and decked apart from their Core cards (issue #2511, 2026-10-30)**: the owner asked that Training Grounds #41–#60, the frozen copies of Core cards, be called `Legacy: <name>`, carry a new Legacy tag and be separate from their Core versions, reversing the 2026-10-28 choice of one name and one card per deck. Each is renamed `Legacy: ` and its Core card's name (in Chinese, 传统： before it), so every name is unique again, and tagged Legacy, a family tag no rule reads that the server admits from migration 0069; its cost, stats, text, rarity, set, script and picture are unchanged ([[R2511000]]). A deck may hold a Legacy card beside its Core card, every random deck may deal both, and a Discover may offer both, Zephrys' included; the build's catalog drops `snapshotOf`, while the two Discovers still read it from the carried catalogs of v0.5.5 to v0.5.7, so a replay of an older game offers what it did ([[R2511001]]). They supersede [[R2195000]] and [[R2195001]], amend [[R2204001]], [[R2194000]], [[R2194002]] and [[R2195002]], and edit [[§2.6]], [[§5]], [[§8.11]] and [[§9.4]].

**[[R2521000]] A dead Starship Piece fuses its base version (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: the owner's first Stinkyscape balance pass asked that "only the base version" of a dead piece fuse into the Starship, its fused cards and its Buffs left behind. The copy [[R1852000]] fuses is now a fresh card of the piece's base definition: for a fused card, the card the others were fused into, its last ingredient (a fusion keeps its kept card last, [[R1852001]]); its tuning is not carried, so no Buff or Nerf goes with it; a Radiant piece still fuses Radiant, as the owner chose, and a token keeps its X/X. Hearthstone brings a minion back without its enchantments or Magnetic upgrades. Carrying the whole fusion, as before, was rejected. It takes R2521000, the first number of issue #2521's block, and edits [[§6.1]] Starship Piece.

**[[R2521001]] Flak's random damage: one roll for each enemy (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: Flak v3 to v5 now deal "a random 1 to 3 damage to each enemy" (Radiant 2 to 4, Lucky 1), weighted 60, 30 and 10. The spec did not say whether the roll is one for the sweep or one for each target; this follows Hearthstone's Lightning Storm, whose minions each take their own roll: each target rolls just before its hit, in the sweep's order, a round of v4 or v5 rolling anew, and Lucky repeats each roll and keeps the highest. `damage_all` takes the odds and the Lucky count; without them it draws nothing, so every other sweep plays as before.

**[[R2521002]] Tmack Trav's extra attack keeps its summoning sickness (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: a Tmack Trav played with Rush that destroyed a Unit was offered the enemy hero the same turn, because `may_attack_again` lifted its sickness for the turn. The owner reported it; now its extra attack keeps the sickness (`keepSick`), so on its summon turn Rush lets it attack again a Unit only, and on a later turn the extra attack may go face. C+ #73.1 Classic Golem still lifts it ([[R424]]).

**[[R2521003]] A University Activates at most once a turn (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: ZolarKeth's "Activate your Universities" ran each one's Activate spending none of its uses ([[R1876001]]), so two ZolarKeths, or a University's own use beside one, ran a University twice. Now the run spends one of the University's uses and passes by one with none left this turn (`spendUse`), whoever spent it, and ZolarKeth's Cry summons a University on its base face. The refusal of the summoned University's own Activate stands ([[R1876000]]).

**[[R2521004]] Swear Loyalities exiles only another faction's cards, from its caster's hand and deck (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: its new text reads "exile every card in your hand and deck that doesn't belong to that faction", and its example exiles a card of another faction. The owner chose the narrower reading: a faction is its Faction card and the cards that card adds, a card of no faction is never exiled, and Tylannt, of ETHICS and of Vanguard Coalition, stays when either is chosen. The literal reading, which would exile nearly the whole deck, was rejected. It narrows [[R1880001]]: the graveyard is no longer read.

**[[R2521005]] Pestilent Slime spreads its Plague Counters (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: "Death: Spread its Plague Counters among your permanents." places each counter it died with, one placement each, on a random permanent its controller has on the field, face-down cards included, so a Pestilent Slime there multiplies each; with none there, they are lost. Hearthstone has no counterpart; one placement per counter keeps [[R689]]'s count of placements.

**[[R2521006]] Fruit Launcher's pool states its tokens (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: asked to confirm that Fruit Launcher launches Fruit tokens, its Fruit pool already held the Grapes ([[R382]]); its query now states them, as Dropshipping's does, and no draw changes.

**[[R2521007]] CN Tech and NA Tech fall back to hard-coded ends (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: with no row past the games floor, CN Tech added a random card and NA Tech fused random ones. The owner asked for the last patch's ends hard-coded: Lunar Eclipse (33 of 48) for the leader and Infinite Reserves (7 of 43) for the trailer, the ends of the newest shipped table. Both cards still read the compiled table ([[R1100]], [[R1183000]]); that table is v0.4.0's provisional run until `winrates-refresh.yml` has its `STATS_DATABASE_URL`.

**[[R2521008]] Star Chart, Bob's Plan's plan for Alteran Reach (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: the owner asked for a partner card for Alteran Reach. Meditative+ #1.5 Star Chart, a (0) token, travels its caster's starscape map 2 nodes (Radiant 4), each a travel as Alteran Reach's ([[R1847000]], [[R1847001]]), stopping at the edge; a travel it makes counts as that turn's travel for Alteran Reach's free one ([[R1847002]]). Bob's Plan adds one with Alteran Reach.

**[[R2521009]] A Resource's face prints its Credits (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: a card whose definition carries `resource` prints `Credits: <min>–<max>` under its text, the sell range of the face shown, on every face the client draws and in the reader's language; a card without `resource` prints none. The client reads the definition only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Writing the line into the catalog text was rejected. It edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2521010]] The unban lane lifts Wildar and Trigor (issue #2521, 2026-10-30)**: issue #2452 asked that no card be shadow banned. `SHADOW_UNBANNED` lifted only Meditative cards ([[R732000]]); it may now name a card of any set the ban takes, so S #149 Wildar and TG #25 Trigor come off and `SHADOW_BAN` is empty.

**[[R2532000]] renames the v0.5 line by today's tiers (issue #2532, 2026-10-30)**: the owner asked for the shipped v0.5 line to be named by [[R2455000]] and [[R2499000]]'s tiers. `cargo jackioh patches retcon --from v0.5.0` computed the map: ten patches became six, v0.5.11 is now v0.5.7, and the three that were patches by a rule alone were measured against their base builds (1, 1 and 2 of 200 games diverged without a card) and are micros. `VERSIONS_SCHEME` is 3, `patches/aliases.json` maps a file marked 2 through its new `marked`, and migration 0068 renames the database's versions. It takes R2532000, the first number of issue #2532's block, and amends [[R1371000]], [[R1973001]] and [[R2455000]].

**[[R2533000]] and [[R2533001]] are the tutorial opponent holding and playing only Training Grounds cards that are not Legendary or Mythic (issue #2533, 2026-10-30)**: the owner ruled "AI should also have only Training-Grounds {non mythic or legendary} cards", in place of the Core-only opponent of issue #2433. A handicap's `tutorialCardsOnly`, which `AI_TUTORIAL` sets in place of `coreOnly`, bars the seat from playing, setting or casting any card but one of `TUTORIAL_CARD_SET` (Training Grounds) whose rarity is none of `TUTORIAL_BARRED_RARITIES` (Legendary, Mythic), a token judged by its printed rarity, however the card reached its hand; everything else [[R2433000]] said holds ([[R2533000]]). Every lesson's opponent deck is now twelve such cards, themed to its lesson, and `AI_TUTORIAL` sets `deckCopies` so lesson 1's is mostly copies of the set's vanilla units, as its human's is ([[R2533001]]). They supersede [[R2433000]], amend [[R2434001]] and [[R2433001]], and edit [[§9.10]], [[R290]] and [[R1012003]].

**[[R2560000]] folds a practice claim to its result and no longer re-runs its AI moves (issue #2560, 2026-10-31)**: the owner asked to remove the server's re-run of a practice claim's AI moves ([[R1303002]]) for three reasons: it refused games the player really played, when the browser's clock cut a move short or its AI drifted from the server's, which [[R2208000]]'s long cap only narrowed; it ran the AI at the full `AI_BUDGET` on the blocking pool for every claim; and practice pay is already held to the day's Jlore cap ([[R1483000]]), so a forged AI side earns no more than an honest game. A claim still pays only when its log folds from its seed's setup, under the worker's nonces and seats, to a finished, unvoided game with the claimed result: an action the engine refuses, one under the wrong nonce or seat, an unfinished log, a voided game and a wrong result are refused as before, and no refusal is `ai_mismatch`. A legal move of the AI's seat the AI would not make, a concession among them, now pays. The browser drops what existed for the re-run: `PRACTICE_EARNING_AI_CLOCK_MS`, so a game on a server seed decides under `PRACTICE_AI_CLOCK_MS`, the `clockCut` mark and its "ran out of time" wording. Keeping the long clock was rejected, since slow devices still failed; refusing what [[R188]] keeps from the AI as a cheap guard was rejected, since the cap bounds what it could earn. `PRACTICE_VERIFY_CONCURRENCY` stays, since the fold still runs on the blocking pool. It takes R2560000, the first number of issue #2560's block, supersedes [[R1303002]] and [[R2208000]], and edits [[§9.9]] and [[§14.8]].

**[[R2564000]] has the AI price a unit's Activate by one use, its own units' and the enemy's (issue #2564, 2026-10-31)**: the AI's evaluation priced a unit by its body alone, so Stinkyscape #52 Cannon v3, which destroys a Unit every turn, read as a 0/4 wall: the AI left an enemy Cannon standing and traded its own away cheaply. At each decision's root it now ends its own turn and then the enemy's on a copy, tries each visible unit's Activate at the start of its controller's next turn as the opponent's reply tries one, and a unit counts `AI_EVAL.activation` of its controller's evaluation after the best use less before it, on either side of the board. It takes R2564000, the first number of issue #2564's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2565000]] has the AI price a Unit's start-of-turn and end-of-turn effects, on either field, and a readable enemy Field Spell's, by one firing (issue #2565, 2026-10-31)**: [[R2477000]] priced only an own backrow card's turn hooks, so the AI counted Gatling Pea, which hits the enemy hero and grows every turn, and Gravedigger, which returns a card every turn, as their bodies, whether its own or the enemy's, and left an enemy engine alone to hit a plain body of its size and traded its own away as if it were blank (`training/improve.md` traces the loss). At each decision's root it now fires each start-of-turn and end-of-turn hook of every unit on either field, of the cards it can read in either backrow and of the Units and Field Spells in its own hand (a Unit placed first, as [[R2424000]]'s Death probes place one) once, each priced as its controller's evaluation after the firing less before it, so an enemy's engine is worth the enemy what it gives it. `unit_worth` adds `AI_EVAL.turn_hook` of the price, so a kill of an engine, which `kill_worth` counts whole, is worth its price, and `material` adds it for a readable backrow card of either side; no face-down enemy card is ever probed ([[R185]]), and a hook that harms its holder prices below zero. `AI_TURN_HOOK_PROBE_NODES` goes from 12 to 24. It takes R2565000, the first number of issue #2565's block, and edits [[R2477000]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R2566000]] has the AI price a Starship Piece's death by the copy it fuses into its owner's Starship (issue #2566, 2026-10-31)**: [[R2424000]]'s Death probe took only units with a live Death, and a piece's fusion ([[R1852000]]) is the death pass's step, not a Death hook, so the AI read the kill of an enemy piece as clean removal and its own pieces as their stats. The probe now takes each Unit that is a Starship Piece too, after every Death so no piece takes a Death's nodes, and a piece counts `AI_EVAL.death` of its price and a kill of it the whole price less. It takes R2566000, the first number of issue #2566's block, amends [[R2424000]] and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2567000]] the first player's win share counts only decided games (issue #2567, 2026-10-31)**: the Game figures panel and `stats report --games` took the first seat's share and its Wilson interval over every game, so a draw sat in the denominator as a first-seat loss and the first player could look weaker than they are: with even seats and 4% draws it read 48%. The share and its interval are now over `games - draws`, and the share is null (unknown) when every game was drawn, with the interval [0, 1]; `games`, `draws`, `firstWins` and the stored totals do not change, so there is no migration. The owner approved it without the draw figures, so the panel reads "First player wins X% of decided games (±m)" and shows no draw share. It takes R2567000, the first number of issue #2567's block, amends [[R1994000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2574000]] The view marks every hand card of the viewer's that the Trade rule passes as Tradable, the aura's included (issue #2574, 2026-10-31)**: an aura over a hand ("Cards in your hand have Tradable", S #164 The Syndicate, S #165 The Trade Union, [[R1864002]]) made cards Tradable for the reducer and the Trade bar, but the Tradable pip in hand read only `face.keywords`, so aura-lent Tradable cards lacked the pip and failed to dim when the turn's Trades were spent. The engine now marks every hand card of the viewer's that `trade::is_tradable` passes with `tradable: true` in `CardView` ([[§10.8]]). The client's Tradable pip reads `card.tradable`, sparing the client from working out field auras (CLAUDE.md rule 7); `keywords` still shows what the card is made of ([[R1864002]]). It edits [[§10.8]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R2577000]] A unit on the field draws only the keywords that can still act there or as it leaves (issue #2577, 2026-10-31)**: the issue asked that the board minion stop drawing keywords that do nothing on the field, since their chips and treatments took room from live ones. Equip, Tradable, Cloaked, Crater, Flashback, Stack and Temporary X are marked `onField: false` in `KEYWORD_VISUALS`: no treatment and no chip on the field, their badges hidden in the DOM; every other keyword, Starship Piece included, stays, and the hover preview and the inspect sheet still list them all. Hearthstone draws no board mark for Magnetic or Tradeable either. It amends [[R438]].

**[[R2575000]] doubles the practice AI's budget (issue #2575, 2026-10-31)**: the owner asked for the practice AI's thinking budget to be doubled, so `AI_BUDGET` is 1,200 nodes, 300 of them the lethal solver's, in the search shape of before, at every tier, and the gates, the shadow-ban sweep and the lethal hint follow it; the browser's cap, `PRACTICE_AI_CLOCK_MS`, is 3 s, since the old 1.5 s would have cut the doubled search short. The ladder bots' two middle strengths (`BOT_MID_STRENGTH_BUDGET`, the budget their seed ratings were calibrated on) and the Duos AI seats (`DUOS_AI_BUDGET`) keep 600 and 150. At 1,200 nodes the timing gate failed on its pinned improve-dev:200 position (about 1.42 s against 1,000 ms), so a fat state scales its allowance from at most `AI_SEARCH.fat_budget_nodes`, 600, and spends what it did. Its screen against the AI of before, 800 paired games of improve-dev, read 406-394, edge +0.015 (-0.007 to +0.037), inside noise, which the owner accepted in advance. Doubling the bots' and Duos' budgets too, keeping the 1.5 s cap and raising the gate's limits were rejected. It takes R2575000, the first number of issue #2575's block, and edits [[§9.9]] and [[§13.7]].

**[[R2603000]] has the AI price a face-up backrow card's Activate, a Field Spell's, by one use, its own and the enemy's (issue #2603, 2026-10-31)**: [[R2564000]] priced only a unit's Activate, so the 22 shipped Field Spells that print one, Classic #20 The Power to Punish and Classic+ #78 Claude's Datacenter among them, counted by their cost alone: the AI played them rarely and left the enemy's alone. Its activation probes now also try the Activates of the cards acting face-up in either backrow, within the same budget, and `material` adds `AI_EVAL.activation` of the price, inside the card's Brittle share; no face-down card is ever probed ([[R185]]). It takes R2603000, the first number of issue #2603's block, and edits [[R2564000]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R2609000]] A watcher's socket keeps a seat's limits on its later hellos and its junk frames (issue #2609, 2026-10-31)**: a watcher's socket had every later hello answered with its last view under the actor's lock, and its other frames went uncounted, while a player's socket coalesces its hellos ([[R835001]]) and is closed with 1008 past `MALFORMED_FRAMES_PER_WINDOW` malformed frames ([[R835002]]). A watcher now keeps a `SeatGuard` of its own: at most one later hello per `WATCH_HELLO_INTERVAL_MS` (1 s) is answered and sooner ones are dropped, and its frames that are not a hello, still unanswered, count against `MALFORMED_FRAMES_PER_WINDOW`; past it the socket is closed with 1008 and the players hear the new count. It takes R2609000, the first number of issue #2609's block, amends [[R1103000]] and [[R835002]] and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§9.8]].

**[[R2614000]] The Terms say when ranked seats a ladder bot, what it looks like and does, and that nothing marks it (issue #2614, 2026-10-31)**: an audit of the site against a launch checklist found that the ladder bots pass as people at every rank while the Terms said only "When no suitable opponent is available, ranked may pair you with an AI opponent." ([[R640001]]). The owner chose to keep the bots as they are and disclose them as Hearthstone (patch 24.4), Fortnite and Apex Legends disclose theirs, in public text with no mark on the seat. The Terms' "Ranked play" section becomes "AI opponents": when ranked may pair a player with one and why, at any rank and in every queue mode; that it has a username, portrait, cosmetics, rank and leaderboard place as a player does, never in Jlorious; that it plays at a person's pace and emotes, concedes, answers draws, times out and rematches; that nothing in the game marks it; that it sees only what its seat could see and its games are rated like any; and that a friend's challenge is always that friend. `TERMS_VERSION` moves, so every account agrees again. It amends [[R640001]] and [[R1452]], and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§9.12]].

**[[R2615000]] and [[R2615001]] make watching and online play's data part of playing, with no opt-out (issue #2615, 2026-10-31)**: the owner decided that friends may always watch a player's live games, the way out being to remove or block the friend, and that what online play records is always collected, since it is usage data about the game and how the cards are balanced. [[R2615000]] removes the "Let friends watch my games" switch (`allowSpectators`): the server stops reading `profiles.allow_spectators` (the column stays until a later migration, so a server on the old code reads it safely while a deploy overlaps), `GET` and `PUT /api/settings` answer the groups alone, and Settings and Play together lose the switch, Play together's Watching panel saying instead how to stop someone watching. [[R2615001]] changes nothing that is collected and holds it there: game records, play telemetry, the card statistics and the player statistics have no switch, and none is to be added. The Privacy Policy now says both, and lists the public player statistics it had left out, practice games' counts among them ([[R639]], [[R2000000]]). Both ship with [[R2614000]] in one change, so `TERMS_VERSION` moves once. They edit [[§9.5]] and [[§9.11]].

**[[R2617000]] makes a touch held on a card its hover, on either turn and on every screen (issue #2617, follows #165, 2026-10-31)**: on a touch screen, a finger held on a card, a face-down card or a pile opens its hover preview while it stays down, on either turn and on every screen. The lift closes the preview and sends no click, and a drift past the slop closes it and still drags. #165 had kept the modal inspect sheet on the viewer's own turn and off the board. The user asked for the hold to be the hover everywhere. With Hover previews off, a hold opens the sheet as before, and a hold with its own use (the hero portrait's view, the card detail of the deck builder's pool and the Almanac) keeps it. Presentation only. It takes R2617000, the first number of issue #2617's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2618000]] sends a card that dies to the graveyard of the player who controlled it, and a played card to its player's (issue #2618, 2026-10-31)**: issue #2606 asked for a badge to warn that a stolen card on your side went back to the other player's graveyard if it died ([[R12]]), and the designer rejected the badge and changed the rule. A card that leaves the field for a graveyard goes to its controller's, and a card that leaves the resolving zone for one goes to the graveyard of the player who played or cast it; it lands as that player's own card, as a Bounce makes a card its controller's for the hand ([[R747]]), so a replacement of the landing ([[R460]]) sends it to that player's exile pile or library. An Exile or a shuffle from the field still goes to the owner's pile. It takes R2618000, the first number of issue #2618's block, amends [[R12]], [[R172]], [[R360]], [[R453]], [[R747]], [[R803]], [[R1282]], [[R1852002]] (a stolen Starship Piece now builds the thief's ship) and [[R1859012]], and edits [[§3.2]], [[§4.5]] and [[§6.3]].

**[[R2630000]] and [[R2630001]] open a gated screen without waiting on what the page already knows (issue #2630, 2026-10-31)**: a launch-speed pass after a developer's write-up of cutting an app's cold start with three fixes (trust what the device has, show a saved snapshot at once, make requests in parallel) found two waits on every gated screen. Each screen's gate read `/api/auth/me` before showing anything, even a moment after the last screen read it, and a screen that calls the engine asked for the engine's module and its own code only once that read answered. [[R2630000]] opens a screen at once on the page's last answer, kept with the menus' ([[R2125000]]), for an active account that owes nothing, reads again behind it, and drops it on `announceAccountChange()`, so no redirect or prompt rests on it. [[R2630001]] starts the screen's code, and the engine's module for a screen that calls it, as the gate mounts, beside the account read. They edit [[§10.10]].

**[[R2631000]] `/ai` says how JackiOh uses AI (issue #2631, 2026-10-31)**: a second launch checklist counted "no AI policy" and "no proof of AI" against an app; JackiOh had neither page nor record in one place, and its voice lines had no record of how they were made. `/ai`, "How JackiOh uses AI", is a public statement beside `/accessibility`, linked from the footer and in the sitemap: the computer opponents (Practice and the tutorial, ranked's AI opponents under the Terms' disclosure, [[R2614000]], and the co-op team), what was made with AI (music composed as code by an AI model, text-to-speech voice lines, procedural card art, AI-assisted code) and what AI does with a player's data (nothing sent to an AI service; game records used as the Privacy Policy says). `assets/voice/SOURCES.md` records how the voice lines are made and leaves their licence to the owner. It is no part of the Terms, so no account agrees again.

**[[R2646000]] a prompt names its asking card to its chooser, and every short choice is drawn as choice cards (issue #2646, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked that every choice of four options or fewer be a Discover of choice cards, as Hearthstone draws a Choose One, since an option that named no card was a bare box of text and an engine Choose one said neither which card asked nor what its options do. `DISCOVER_OPTION_LIMIT` is 4, and each such option is a choice card, procedural art, its label and what it does, which is no card and opens no inspect overlay; route 2's mode, ability, Equip or Stack, face-down and Plague Counter choices and route 1's modes, answers and quest rewards are drawn so, five or more keeping their lists. The chooser's `PendingPromptView` names the card asking as `sourceDefId` and `sourceRadiant`, only while the state holds that card unchanged and the chooser may read it where it is, by `sourceId`'s test ([[R1088]]); the other seat's view never does, and as view fields they move no hash. Following a replaced card, a generic detail for a hidden one and `MODE_TEXT`'s labels over the engine's were rejected. It takes R2646000, the first number of issue #2646's block, and edits [[§10.6]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R2647000]] takes the Token and Created marks off the card faces and keeps them in the hover preview (issue #2647, 2026-11-01)**: a card's Token and Created status almost never matters in combat, yet a small face, a full-art face and the board minion wore a token mark ([[R1177001]]) and every face in play a "Created" pill ([[R943]]), cluttering the board, the hand and the backrow. The faces now wear neither; a token's full face still prints "Token Unit", which the hover preview shows, and the preview's state notes still say a card was Created. Presentation only. It takes R2647000, the first number of issue #2647's block, amends [[R943]] and [[R1177001]], and edits [[§7]], [[§8]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R2648000]] Your table's stats name the right kind of card (issue #2648, 2026-11-01)**: a Spell was named Unbreakable, since a Spell never sends `destroyed` and so is never destroyed, and the player's own Tributes, Sacrifices and Brittle crumbles counted as losses, since a Unit given up that way does send `destroyed`. Now `destroyed` and `defeated` count a death only when the other seat caused it (a `killerId` on that side, or that side's last play, ability, Trap or attack before it in the batch), never after a `crumbled` of the same card, and not when the card's own side acted last with no killer of the other's; totals already stored keep their counts. Unbreakable, Glass Cannon and Workhorse name only a Unit, Unbreakable one that prints no Indestructible and has at least 5 plays; Medic credits a Lifesteal hit's heal to the attacker; Hot Streak names the card with the most wins played in, The Record the card played in the most games, and Ride or Die reads `survived` per win played in, which is the only game `survived` counts in, with Survivor's line saying a win; a lunge plays a new stage animation `cast` (the board's `cardPlayed`) on a Spell or Field Spell and `trap` on a Trap or Field Trap; and Comeback Kid is Lucky Charm. A death with no killer and no actor counting for nobody, the cause being the seat across from the card's owner, any other `damage` clearing a heal's source, and The Record being left out while no card has a game played in were chosen, against counting it for the seat whose turn it is, the controller, keeping the source, and falling back to the top `seen` card. It takes R2648000, the first number of issue #2648's block, amends [[R1582000]], [[R1584000]] and [[R1743000]], and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2649000]] Your table adds 30 stats on the game's own mechanics (issue #2649, 2026-11-01)**: the 35 stats of "Your table" said nothing about what makes the game its own, so 30 more are added, 65 in all: Use It or Lose It and Just in Time (Temporary), Sand Castle and Outlasted (Brittle), Good and Bad Feng Shui, Hot Potato and Trade-In Value (Tradable), Mass Produced (Created cards), Executioner, Giant Slayer, Second Wind, Bubble Wrap, Iron Wall, Punching Bag, Double Tap, Vampire and Martyr (combat and its keywords), Cold Snap, Brain Freeze, Spring Thaw and Shatter (freezes), Encore (Flashback), Bargain Hunter, Denied, Nope, Burnt Toast, Frequent Flyer, Liftoff (Starships) and Brick. Each reads a new per-card counter off the views from the viewer's seat, and the view before the batch where it needs what a card was. The counters are stored under the same key and never synced, `biggestKill` a maximum and the rest sums. The stage gains 15 animations, each a board event on the stage's card. Three of them, `crumble`, `expire` and `inTime`, are led by the aura the board's card wears before what it warns of: Brittle's last count in gray, Temporary's in fuchsia. The first beat waits a gap longer so the warning reads first, the aura is gone as it plays, and the still card wears it under reduced motion. Several readings were chosen against the issue's or another: Hot Potato reads the Tradable card the hand lost, since the card shuffled in reads as the sentinel to its trader too (rejected: the `shuffledIn`, which never counts). Trade-In Value counts the first draw after each trade (rejected: every draw of the batch). Use It or Lose It counts a discard after the viewer's own turn end (rejected: any in the batch). Martyr counts a death with no actor before it for the batch's first actor, so a Tribute counts (rejected: the actor before it only). Mass Produced counts each instance once for the seat that shows it (rejected: [[R2089000]]'s `creator`, which no view forwards), and the Coin, a token in the catalog, never wins it (rejected: exempting it). Nope plays `trap` on anything but a Spell (rejected: a lunge). The first beat waits one gap after the way in (rejected: the aura over the way in only). It takes R2649000, the first number of issue #2649's block, amends [[R1584000]] and [[R1743000]], and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2652000]] and [[R2652001]] are coin flips and die rolls (issue #2652, 2026-11-01)**: players saw what a coin or a die did, never how it landed. Every flip and roll of #4 Gary the Gambler, C #65 Ace in the Hole, C+ #19.3 Mid Loser and S #154 Dice of Doom is now a `rolled` batch, one per verb call or hook, holding each coin or die in draw order with every face Lucky and Luck drew and the one kept; it is a report nothing answers, so no draw, no number in [[R68]]'s order and no state hash moves ([[R2652001]]). A batch a face-down trap made reaches its controller alone unless the trap fires in the same action, as a Secret's is seen ([[R2652000]]). They edit [[§10.3]]'s list and [[§10.8]], and BUILD M5-T4 gains the `rolled` row.

**[[R2655000]] Every full-art card has an Epic, a Legendary (animated) and a Mythic (signed) full art, and a card's pick chooses which owned one it wears (issue #2655, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked for two chase tiers over every full art. Each full-art card's one Epic full art ([[R1313000]]) gains a Legendary, `full-art-<slug>-animated`, and a Mythic, `full-art-<slug>-signed`, both chases, none granted to any account; they drop by the chase routes and no token buys them. The registry's check refuses two full arts of one rarity on a card. A card's pick is `false`, `true` (its Epic) or a full art's id, held to ownership by the server, kept in migration 0072's `full_art_id` and frozen into a match's loadout as the tier worn. Amends [[R1313000]], [[R1333000]], [[R1655000]] and [[R1834001]].

**[[R2655001]] The cosmetics registry, not the portrait roster, is the list of full-art cards, and fifteen more cards join it (issue #2655, 2026-11-01)**: [[R1017001]] made the hero portraits' cards the full-art cards. A card now prints full art when the registry has a `fullArt` entry for it, and fifteen iconic cards of every type join the six, each with three full arts. Amends [[R1017001]] and [[R1313000]].

**[[R2655002]] A listed token prints its parent card's full art, at the tier its parent's copy would wear (issue #2655, 2026-11-01)**: the tokens of five of the new full-art cards print their parent's full art, behind the parent's pick, ownership, setting and lesson gates, through a data map of parent to tokens rather than the generator, which an opponent's copy never carries. Amends [[R1956001]].

**[[R2655003]] The animated and signed full arts move by transform and opacity alone, hold still under reduced motion, and sign the card's English name in a self-hosted Dancing Script (issue #2655, 2026-11-01)**: the issue left the motion's gate, the signature's face and its language to the builder. The motion is gated under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` and the app's own motion settings; the signature is Dancing Script (OFL), self-hosted, and signs the English name on a Chinese face too.

**[[R2657000]] Can't Defend is a keyword (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the owner's second Stinkyscape balance pass asked for "Can't Defend", the short successor to "Can't be in Defense Position". It became a keyword the cards print, which the combat validator and the state check read, and which a Buff may take off. The static flag it replaced stays for the scripts kept for older versions.

**[[R2657001]] A Field Trap reveals once (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the request had a Field Trap's later firings count as normal triggers, without the long reveal animation. `trapFired` marks them `again`, so the client and "when one of your Traps reveals" tell a reveal from a later firing.

**[[R2657002]] Float works from the hand alone (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the request had "Float reduces cost" trigger only while the card is in its owner's hand. The six cards with a Float trigger lose their deck trigger.

**[[R2657003]] A Trade never draws its own card back while the deck holds another (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the request had a traded card redraw itself only when it is the only option. It goes in under the top card.

**[[R2657004]] Summoning sickness lasts its whole turn (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the request named a Chaos Golem whose refreshed attack could hit the hero on the turn it was summoned. Every "may attack again" now keeps the sickness, as R2521002 did for Tmack Trav alone.

**[[R2657005]] The Buff's and the Nerf's new keywords (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the request named "Reminisce" (a card; the owner chose Flashback) for Buffs, Crater in a hand for Nerfs, and Can't attack and Can't Defend both ways. The owner settled the last: a Buff removes either, and a Nerf adds neither.

**[[R2657006]] KY's Constant's further numbers (issue #2657, 2026-11-01)**: the request asked KY's Constant for more valid targets. The owner chose more numbers on every card: a Temporary card's count and its Plague Counters, and the amounts the Units and Traps printed, now declared.

**[[R2660000]] ladder bots concede only facing an unstoppable lethal (issue #2660, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked that ladder bots concede only when a lethal shows on the board and they have no way to stop it, and never where there is none. A bot whose temper concedes at all (`BOT_CONCEDE_FIT.share`, still fitted) now concedes at its turn start only when `jackioh_ai::unstoppable_lethal` proves it: on the bot's redaction, its opponent's hand and face-down cards taken out, every line of its turn is played through the engine and each loses, by its own turn or to the lethal solver's line on the opponent's board alone, played again. A cut walk, a failed step, a roll of the game's random numbers, a card moved in a library the bot cannot see into, a prompt the bot would answer in the opponent's turn and a panic all play on; a turn that ends with the Marketplace open ends without it. The deficit threshold and `min_turn` are no longer read. Summing the board's attack, counting the opponent's hand, keeping the guessed face-down cards, trusting the worlds' rolls and draws as samples, and having every bot concede whatever its temper were rejected. It takes R2660000, the first number of issue #2660's block, and edits [[§9.12]], [[R639000]], [[R1452]] and [[R188]].

**[[R2660001]] ladder bots may offer a draw (issue #2660, 2026-11-01)**: the owner allowed that a bot may offer a draw when it thinks it is losing, which [[R639000]] had forbidden. At the start of its own turn, when it does not concede and [[R36]] lets it offer, a bot that trails its opponent by at least `BOT_DRAW_OFFER.min_behind` (10, on the standing's even-centered scale) offers with the chance `BOT_DRAW_OFFER.chance_per_turn` (0.05) on its turn's own stream, both hand-set since `timing-fit` fits no offer rate; the person answers it as a person's ([[R269]]), and [[R188]] still holds for `decide` and the practice AI. Fitting the rate now and offering later in the turn were rejected. It takes R2660001, the next number of issue #2660's block, and edits [[§9.12]], [[R639000]], [[R1452]] and [[R188]].

**[[R1933000]] Production's database holds live games alone (issue #1933, 2026-11-01)**: production and staging each got a database of their own, and the development lanes (the gather lane, the learn lane's import) file through `STATS_IMPORT_DATABASE_URL`, staging's, so production's public figures count live games only and [[§9.11]]'s gate finds no development games to pad them with: below `PUBLIC_STATS_MIN_LIVE_GAMES` they are labelled provisional and a card shows "not enough games" until its window holds `CARD_STATS_MIN_SAMPLE` ([[R654]]), and [[R2386000]]'s provisional refresh waits for live games too. The owner chose figures that rest on players' games; copying the development totals into production once was rejected, since it would pad only the patches already gathered. It takes R1933000, the first number of issue #1933's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2683000]] Buying a chase asks before it spends the full Chase Shard bar (issue #2683, 2026-11-01)**: after `CHASE_SHARD_PACKS` cosmetic packs the bar is full and each chase's Buy spent all of it on its first press, so a slip on the wrong row cost that many packs' worth of progress, and nothing could take it back, though a card or cosmetic token already asked first ([[R2480000]]). The press now asks in the row's place, "Buy Galaxy? This spends your full Chase Shard bar.", with Confirm and Cancel, Cancel focused and Escape answering it; Confirm sends the one purchase. The cosmetic pack panel's Open and chase Buy also wait while any spend of the tab is out, as the Shop's Buy does ([[R2378000]]). Asking only for a Mythic chase and an undo were rejected. It takes R2683000, the first number of issue #2683's block, and edits [[§14.6]] and [[§14.11]] ([[R1306002]]).

**[[R2685000]] a 1v1 rematch offer is taken only within `REMATCH_OFFER_TTL_MS` of the finished match's end (issue #2685, 2026-11-01)**: the server kept one clock per finished match, not one per offer, and every offer from either seat restarted it, so a player who offered and closed the tab could be held to that offer for hours: the other player sent a mismatched offer every few minutes to keep the entry alive and then the matching one, and a game began with the first player absent, who could lose it by disconnect, in ranked with their rating. An offer is now refused once the window from the match's `finishedAt` has closed, as a co-op Play again's already is ([[R1398000]]): it lapses silently, records nothing and makes no game, while an entry that made one inside the window still answers its id. It amends [[R672]]'s offer window and edits [[§9.5]], and is the first number of issue #2685's block. A clock per seat's offer was rejected: more state, and the 1v1 and co-op windows would be counted differently.

**[[R2686000]] A player's later hellos on one socket are answered at most once per interval, and a new socket's first at once (issue #2686, 2026-11-01)**: a seat's hellos coalesced only while one was queued ([[R835001]]), and the flag clears as the task starts, so a client that sent again as each answer arrived kept one queued for ever and had the actor rebuild and send the whole view, the clock, the portraits and the cards' picks each time under the lock both players' actions wait on; a watcher's socket had been given one answer per `WATCH_HELLO_INTERVAL_MS` for this ([[R2609000]]). A seat's socket, on `/ws/match` and on `/ws/duos`, now holds the same interval through its `SeatGuard`, which keeps the socket generation with the time of its last answered hello: a hello of the same generation sooner than the interval is dropped unanswered, the socket stays open, and the first hello of a new generation, a reattach, is always answered and starts its own interval. An action is never held back by it. It rejects closing the socket on a hello flood, which would end an honest page's game on a reconnect storm. It takes R2686000, the first number of issue #2686's block, amends [[R835001]] and edits [[§9.5]], [[§9.8]] and [[§13.9]].

**[[R2687000]] has the AI price a backrow card's Death, a Field Spell's, on either side and in its hand (issue #2687, 2026-11-01)**: [[R2424000]] priced only a unit's Death, so the five shipped Field Spells that print one, Classic+ #61 Bauble Bubble and Stinkyscape #155 Comet Crash among them, counted by their cost alone, and the AI rarely played them. Its Death probes now also price, after the units and within the same budget, each backrow card the AI can read on either side and each Field Spell in its own hand, set first. They read a destroy by the card having left the field, so Stinkyscape #112 Job Application, whose Death returns it, is priced too. `material` adds `AI_EVAL.death` of the price outside the card's Brittle share, since a crumble is a destroy. No face-down enemy card is ever probed ([[R185]]). It takes R2687000, the first number of issue #2687's block, and edits [[R2424000]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R2688000]] has the AI price a unit's "when this attacks" and "after this attacks" text by its next attack, its own units' and the enemy's (issue #2688, 2026-11-01)**: the AI's evaluation priced a unit by its body alone, so Stinkyscape #91 The Propagation's Wasp, which summons a copy of itself after each attack, read as a plain 3/3, and an own Stinkyscape #75 Badger, which gains a Resource each attack, as a plain 2/3. At each decision's root it now ends its own turn and then the enemy's on a copy, plays each visible unit's attack at the start of its controller's next turn, on the enemy hero where it may and else on the first target the rules allow, beside the same attack by a twin whose text is switched off and whose printed keywords are kept, and a unit that may attack counts `AI_EVAL.attack_hook` of the difference in its controller's evaluation, on either side of the board. It takes R2688000, the first number of issue #2688's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2689000]] has the AI count a turn hook as many times as the engine fires it, and price Living Bomb's start of the opponent's turn (issue #2689, 2026-11-02)**: the AI priced each start-of-turn and end-of-turn hook by one firing whatever multiplied it, so a board with Meditative #9 Joint Filing on it was underrated, and it never fired a start-of-the-opponent's-turn hook, so Classic #62 Living Bomb's Radiant face read as a blank Field Spell. It now counts those two hooks 1 + the turn-hook multiplier's extra runs times, read for the card's controller on the state evaluated, so a line that removes Joint Filing loses the extra firings, and it probes the start of the opponent's turn by one firing, counted once, since no multiplier repeats it ([[R821]]). Multiplying the whole price, which would count Living Bomb's base face's start of the opponent's turn twice, was rejected. It takes R2689000, the first number of issue #2689's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2691000]] the practice AI reads its own library as its owner was shown it (issue #2691, 2026-11-01)**: Selective Breeding (#42) and Knockoff Temu Glowy Jelly Bean (#28) turn cards Radiant inside a deck, where their owner does not see it ([[R311]]), but the AI's redaction kept the true cards of its own library, so it planned with how many of its coming draws were Radiant. It now rebuilds each card of its own library it was shown from that card's `knownAs` record, keeping the record and the Created mark, unless its own open prompt offers the card, which the prompt shows in full. Rejected: hiding the whole own library as the dry run does ([[R222]]), which throws away what the owner was legitimately shown, and keeping a change the owner saw before the card went back in, which the list does not show. It takes R2691000, the first number of issue #2691's block, and edits [[§9.9]] and [[R312]].

**[[R2701000]] a dungeon run's deck holds at most the library cap (issue #2701, 2026-11-02)**: a run deck grows after every fight and from shops and events, and each fight's setup gives the human's handicap the deck's length as its deck size, which the engine refuses past `LIBRARY_CAP` ([[R184]]), so a long run could reach a fight it cannot start. Following Hearthstone, whose full deck gains no more cards ([[R80]]), a run deck holds at most `RUN_DECK_MAX`, the library cap: a pack's keep count is also at most the room left, so a full deck keeps none; a shop refuses to sell a card to a full deck; and an event adds no card to one, its other changes paid. Dealing a fight only the deck's first `LIBRARY_CAP` cards, and refusing the pack outright, were rejected: the first drops cards the player chose without saying so, and the second leaves a full deck no way past a fight's rewards. It takes R2701000, the first number of issue #2701's block, and edits [[§15.7]] and [[§15.8]].

**[[R2702000]] a deck's marks at setup (issue #2702, 2026-11-01)**: the dungeon run keeps the cards it upgraded between fights, each a fresh game, so `createGame` takes `deckMarks`, per seat a list of `{ index, radiant, upgrades }` naming cards of the deck list as given, and deals each marked card on its Radiant face or Buffed that many times, each change drawn from the game's rng, after every deck is dealt and before [[§2.1]]'s shuffle. It is setup, not an action: no event reports it, the opening record keeps it so a Glitch reset deals it again, a fold, a replay and a summary pass the same marks, a game without them hashes as before, and a mark past its deck, two on one index or negative `upgrades` refuse the game. Applying the marks in `begin_game` from a new state field, dropping them on a Glitch reset, and capping `upgrades` were rejected. It takes R2702000, the first number of issue #2702's block, and edits [[§2.1]].

**[[R2706000]] a dungeon run's fight in the practice worker (issue #2706, 2026-11-01)**: the dungeon run's plan (`docs/dungeon-run.md` DR14) has each fight of a run played as a practice game from the run's setup, and leaves open how such a game stands beside the free games practice keeps. Following Hearthstone's Dungeon Run, whose fights count in the run alone, a fight plays its setup's seed, decks, handicaps and deck marks whatever the start config says; it brings and leaves no last board, so C+ #29 fizzles in it; no seat is counted dealt; it is never saved and leaves the free game's save and the page's memory of it as they were, so a reload deals it again from its seed; it is not kept for replay and earns nothing; and once it is over the worker answers `runReport` with the run's `FightOver` for the seat the human ended in, never the log. Resuming a fight after a reload from a save slot of its own, keeping fights among the practice replays, and bringing in the practice last board were rejected. It takes R2706000, the first number of issue #2706's block.

**[[R2715000]] Every full-art card has a fourth cosmetic, the Animated art: an Epic that prints the card's normal face with its art moving, stored, picked, frozen, dropped and bought as the Epic full art is (issue #2715, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked for a golden card's tier beside [[R2655000]]'s three. Each full-art card gains `animated-<slug>`, a `fullArt` Epic, no chase, granted to no account: its normal face with its art moving. The registry's check takes it beside the still Epic, `true` still names the still Epic, migration 0075 widens the pick's id check to take it, and a match freezes it as its id; it drops where an Epic per-card cosmetic drops and an Epic token buys it. Amends [[R2655000]].

**[[R2715001]] A full-art card's moving art is its own picture's layers moving on beats written for that card, by transform and opacity alone, on the Animated, Legendary and Mythic tiers, each taking a moving foil's slot (issue #2715, 2026-11-01)**: the issue left the motion's rationing to the builder. Each full-art card's procedural picture moves its own layers and particles on a recipe of its own, each face takes one of `COSMETIC_FOIL_ANIMATED_MAX`'s slots (`near.ts` fires only once, so it cannot hand motion back), a lazy grid face never moves, and the face draws the still picture under reduced motion and with Animated foil off. Amends [[R2655003]].

**[[R2715002]] A Mythic signs its name in under a second behind a pen tip, lands with a burst and a flourish, shines now and then, and signs in its set's hand or its card's own (issue #2715, 2026-11-01)**: the owner added in a comment that the signature should sign faster and harder, in a face that suits its card. It signs in 840 ms behind a pen tip through a slanted, feathered mask, then bursts, slashes a flourish and shines every 4.2 s; each of the six sets signs in an open-licensed, self-hosted face of its own, and two cards in theirs. Amends [[R2655003]].

**[[R2718000]] prints each set's watermark behind a card's rules text and gives Meditative+ a set mark (issue #2718, 2026-11-01)**: the owner's reference was Hearthstone, whose cards carry their expansion's symbol, large and faint, behind their text. Every face that prints a rules box, a Radiant, a Chinese and a blank box included, now carries its set's mark in one ink, centred in the box under the words, in a shade of the box's own colour whose contrast with the box's inks the tests hold; Meditative+, which had no mark and wore the fallback diamond, has one of its own, the ensō with a plus. A full-art card, which prints no box, wears its watermark behind its Full text and detail rules; Glitch, a face too small for its box, a card back and a set with no symbol (Boss, Boss-X, Custom) wear none. A **Set watermarks** switch in the Visuals settings, on by default and kept on the device, takes them all off. Presentation only. It takes R2718000, the first number of issue #2718's block, and edits [[§9.1]] and [[§10.10]].

**[[R2720000]] Every finish shows on a full-art face (issue #2720, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked that a finish read while a full art is worn, but the art covers the panel a finish paints. On a full-art face each finish now also paints a band of itself round the art's edge, at most 0.6 opaque (FULL_ART_FINISH_OPACITY) at the rim and fading to nothing toward the middle, under the gems, the banner, the signature and the stats; Gilded edge, Obsidian and Starlit move on their finish layer as on any face. It takes R2720000, the first number of #2720's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2721000]] takes the rarity foil off Legendary and Mythic faces by default and adds a Rarity foil setting that brings the Mythic's back (issue #2721, 2026-11-01)**: with the default settings a Mythic face carries `data-foil="none"`, no rainbow sheen, no rainbow edge and no Mythic portrait ring; its edge and ring take its rarity colour as every rarity's do, and a Legendary face is unchanged, so the two are told apart by the crest at the top (the Mythic's tinted), the rarity gem and the intro music ([[R1350]]). Rarity foil is the viewer's own switch in the card settings store beside Hover previews, off by default and kept on the device: the account's `cards` settings group ([[R633]]) leaves it out both ways, as it does Set watermarks ([[R2718000]]); on, the Mythic wears today's look exactly, Animated foil deciding whether the sheen moves. A Radiant face keeps its foil ([[R277]]) whatever the setting says, and a Mythic wearing no rarity foil wears its card's cosmetic foil, none with it on. It takes R2721000, the first number of issue #2721's block, amends [[R1295000]] and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2722000]] makes every Legendary's and Mythic's intro an orchestral fanfare, a Mythic's longer than every Legendary's, and drops the bed away under it (issue #2722, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked for intros far bigger than R1352's few bars. Each is rendered again around its melody: a riser of timpani, cymbal and tremolo strings into the first downbeat, a Mythic's own motif on brass and choir, the full ensemble under the melody and an ending held to the bar line, on FluidR3's instruments alone. A Legendary's file runs at most 9 s and every Mythic's longer, to 12 s (`MUSIC_INTRO_MAX_S`, from 6 s); the music set grows from about 40.5 MiB to about 45.4 MiB on disk, so `MUSIC_BUDGET_BYTES` goes from 42 MiB (where the stations' auditioned tracks had raised it from 28 MiB) to 49 MiB. The bed under an intro ducks to 0.12 (from 0.35), the intro plays at 1.4 on the music bus, and the `entrance` sting is rebuilt to stand beside it and no longer ducks the music. It takes R2722000, the first number of issue #2722's block, and amends [[R1350]] and [[R1352]].

**[[R2722001]] has a readable Mythic's entrance take over the board for one capped beat the animation queue holds (issue #2722, 2026-11-01)**: after Hearthstone's Deathwing, the board darkens, a card-shaped silhouette sweeps across it, light rays and shockwaves cross both sides and the board shakes hard as the card lands. [[R200]] let the effects layer pace nothing, so the queue gives one readable Mythic entrance per action a pinned, speed-scaled hold of `TAKEOVER_HOLD_MS` (1 s), none under Reduce Motion or with the effects off, which shows the viewer's own Mythic as a still card instead; `cy.settled()` waits on it through `data-animating` as on any entry. Four marquee Mythics take a look of their own ([[R670]]), the rest a shared one, and a Legendary's entrance grows (a ring, a harder shake) with no takeover. It takes R2722001 and amends [[R200]], [[R670]] and [[R701]].

**[[R2722002]] has a hidden card make its entrance as it reveals, never as it is set (issue #2722, 2026-11-01)**: a Trap's and a Cloaked card's at its first firing (a Cloaked card's Activate: Reveal), on each seat whose view names it, both since [[R2734000]]; a Cloaked card's face-down set, which opened its intro on its controller's seat until now, and one destroyed unrevealed play nothing of it. It takes R2722002 and amends [[R203]], [[R669]] and [[R1350]].

**[[R2723000]] A full-art card in your own hand draws its art alone (issue #2723, 2026-11-02)**: a full-art card ([[R1017001]], at any tier of [[R2655000]]) in its owner's hand printed its name banner across the foot of the art, with its rarity gem, its set mark and its crest, and a Mythic signed its name across the art too, though the player who holds it knows what it is. On a board the viewer plays, the full-art faces in their own hand now draw only their art, their cost gem with its live cost and tone and a Unit's attack and health, with the hand's states, the scrim, the tier's motion ([[R2655003]]) and any finish, border or foil kept; they draw no name banner, signature, rarity gem, set mark or crest, and the name stays in the face's words, visually hidden, so a screen reader, the tooltip with hover previews off and the by-name finders still find it. Every other view is unchanged: the hover preview, the sheet and the detail view show the whole card with its name, and so do the board, a mulligan's and a Discover's options, the deck builder, the Collection, the Almanac, an opponent's revealed hand and a watched or replayed game, whose hand nobody wears ([[R1295000]]). It rejects hiding `.card-name` by CSS alone, which jsdom cannot see and so no unit test could prove, and a new `aria-label` on the card's generic root, which a `div` without a role does not announce. It takes R2723000, the first number of issue #2723's block, amends [[R1017001]] and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2724000]] redraws each Training Grounds Legacy card in the set's yard (issue #2724, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked that the twenty Legacy cards, #41–#60, which drew exactly their Core cards' pictures, each get one of its own that still reads as the card it freezes. Each draws the yard under its Core card's subject, beside a touch no other Legacy card shares; no other picture moves. Presentation only; it takes the first number of issue #2724's block, amends [[R2202000]] and [[R2511000]] and ships as a micro ([[R2529000]]).

**[[R2727000]] A Buff in a hand may give Cloaked, and a Nerf there takes it off (issue #2727, 2026-11-01)**: the owner asked that in a hand a Buff and a Nerf move a card's Cloaked and Crater in opposite directions. A Buff's keyword row now offers Cloaked, last, to a Unit, a Spell or a Field Spell in a hand that lacks it of its own (`TUNE_BUFF_HAND_KEYWORDS`), never to a Trap, a Field Trap or a Vanilla card; a Nerf's row already took off a Cloaked the card had of its own, never a lent one, and is now proven to.

**[[R2727001]] A Buff in a hand may take Crater off (issue #2727, 2026-11-01)**: the other half of the request. In a hand a Buff's row also removes a Crater the card has of its own, printed, given or added by a Nerf (`TUNE_BUFF_HAND_REMOVES`), from a card of any type; in a deck or on the field a Buff leaves it, and a Nerf never removes it.

**[[R2728000]] The cosmetic token cycle gains a Legendary step and a bar size per step (issue #2728, 2026-11-01)**: the card and cosmetic token bars were both 200 points and took the same points from every slot, so they filled on the same openings ([[R1953000]]), and no token reached above Epic ([[R1305003]]). The owner's numbers in #2728 make `COSMETIC_TOKEN_CYCLE` Rare, Epic, Legendary, Rare, Epic, Cosmetic Pack, each step at a size of its own (`COSMETIC_TOKEN_BAR_SIZE_RARE` 50, `COSMETIC_TOKEN_BAR_SIZE_EPIC` 75, `COSMETIC_TOKEN_BAR_SIZE_LEGENDARY` 100 and `COSMETIC_TOKEN_BAR_SIZE_PACK` 100, a cycle of 450 points), with one target per step in place of `COSMETIC_TOKEN_BAR_TARGET_PACKS` (6, 9, 12 and 12 card packs). The cosmetic bar settles step by step, its overflow compared with each next step's size, so one gain can pay several steps in order, and a bar the old size left part filled settles whole at its next opening; nothing is migrated. An opening answers `bars.cosmeticSizes`, the size of each step its sweep crosses, and the cosmetic bar's `size` is the step it stands at, there and in `GET /api/tokens`, which answers `legendary` as a next step and `cosmeticLegendary` among the tokens held. A Legendary token buys any unowned cosmetic up to Legendary, a Legendary chase included, and no token a Mythic; the dev fill fills the cosmetic bar to its current step's size. The card token bar is unchanged. It rejects one size for the whole cycle, the client working out the step sizes itself (CLAUDE.md rule 7), resetting existing bars and a Mythic token. It takes R2728000, the first number of issue #2728's block, amends [[R1305001]], [[R1305003]], [[R1305004]], [[R1310003]], [[R1333000]], [[R1953000]], [[R1953001]] and [[R1999000]], and edits [[§14.2]], [[§14.5]] and [[§14.10]].

**[[R2730000]] A held token is spent from the Collection's fullscreen preview of the locked item it covers (issue #2730, 2026-11-01)**: the inline `TokenOffer` on every covered locked tile and strip item is gone, and so is the shop's card-token browser with its Unlock button. Holding any account cosmetic tile, card-cosmetic strip item or owned-cards grid card, or its inspect key, opens the item fullscreen, modal and focus trapped; a plain tap or click still equips or opens, as today, and a hold fires no click. Only that preview offers a held token covering the locked item, a cosmetic token per `offerTier` or a card token on an unowned card, asking first with Cancel focused and spending through one redeem request, owned afterwards in the preview and the grid. Rejected: a button on every tile; keeping the shop browser, which gives one token two ways to be spent; a new hold timer. It takes R2730000, the first number of issue #2730's block, and edits [[§14.5]].

**[[R2731000]] drops the Collection's list of owned cosmetics (issue #2731, 2026-11-02)**: the Cosmetics section listed every cosmetic the account owns, one row per cosmetic and one per card for per-card ones, below the Equipped tiles, which grew too long to use after a few packs and repeated what the tiles and the cards' own strips show. The section is now the Equipped heading, its text and the tiles; `GET /api/cosmetics` is still read by the tiles' and cards' ownership, the deck builder and the Shop. A capped or folded list was rejected. It takes R2731000, the first number of issue #2731's block, and amends [[R1579000]] and [[R1827000]].

**[[R2734000]] A Trap's reveal names it to both seats (issue #2734, 2026-11-01)**: when the opponent's Trap fired, its reveal showcase and its flip played on the other seat as a card back, and the card was named only a moment later by the graveyard it lay in, because [[R154]] keyed `trapFired`'s `instanceId` and `defId` to the trap's controller, though [[R97]] conceded the identity was not concealed. `trapFired` now follows [[R97]]'s zone rule on both seats: named in a graveyard, an exile pile or face-up on the field to both, in a library to neither, in a hand to that hand's player; `row`, `lane`, `controller` and `again` travel as before, and since sound follows the view the other seat now hears a fired Trap's line and a Legendary or Mythic Trap's intro. It takes R2734000, the first number of issue #2734's block, and amends [[R97]], [[R154]], [[R763]], [[R203]], [[R1350]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R2755000]] adds the Jlorious card back, owned only by reaching Jlorious (pull request #2755, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked for the logo's J★ card as a cosmetic that only reaching Jlorious ([[R608]]) obtains. `card-back-jlorious` is a Mythic card back marked `jlorious` in the registry and never a chase; a rated game grants it, with the ledger reason `jlorious`, to each person it leaves Jlorious, migration 0074 to each person who held Jlorious before, and no roll, pack, token, Chase Shard bar, dev fill or ladder bot's wardrobe offers it. It takes R2755000, the first number of pull request #2755's block (it has no issue), amends [[R1300000]], [[R1333000]], [[R1833000]] and [[R1834005]], and edits [[§14.2]], [[§14.10]] and [[§14.11]].

**[[R2756000]] /stats's card breakdown picks the art a full-art card's faces print (issue #2756, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked to see a card such as Gary the Gambler in its full art or its normal face on the Statistics page. Under a full-art card's faces the breakdown offers No full art, Epic, Legendary and Mythic; the one pressed is what both faces print, starting on what the Full-art cards setting printed, held from card to card while the page is open and never in the address. The public page prints every tier whatever the viewer owns, as the Almanac does ([[R1313000]]), and writes no card pick ([[R1834000]]). It rejects a two-way switch, which hides the chase tiers, and writing the pick. It takes R2756000, the first number of issue #2756's block, and edits [[§10.10]] and [[R1313000]].

**[[R2777000]] A card's adjusted win rate (issue #2777, 2026-11-02)**: the owner found `/stats`' win rates misleading: a card with a few dozen games topped the table on noise, and a card in a strong deck showed the deck's strength. Each card now has an adjusted win rate, 50% plus its drawn delta ([[R2427000]]) kept in the share τ²/(τ² + SE²), where SE² is the delta's sampling variance at its widest and τ, `ADJUSTED_WIN_RATE_PRIOR_SPREAD`, five points: the posterior mean of its lift, so a small sample's swing is mostly discounted and a large one's mostly kept. `GET /api/stats/cards` answers it and lists the cards by it, and the Cards tab shows an Adjusted column and sorts by it by default. A Beta-shrunk win rate, which keeps the deck's strength, and the played delta were rejected. It takes R2777000, the first number of issue #2777's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2777001]] Constructed apart from All Random (issue #2777, 2026-11-02)**: `?mode=constructed` reads Best of 1 and Conquest together and no All Random record, on the cards, the drill-down and the game figures, the gate still counting every mode's live games; `/stats` opens on Constructed, offers All Random, Best of 1 and Conquest beside it, and no longer offers "All modes". It takes R2777001 and edits [[§9.11]] ([[R377]]).

**[[R2777002]] All Random counts a card once seen (issue #2777, 2026-11-02)**: under `?mode=random` a card's games, win rate, play rate, sample floor and per-patch history count only the decks that drew it; a deck dealt it and never drawing it is the never-drawn baseline, not a game of the card's. Constructed keeps every deck that held the card. It takes R2777002 and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2777003]] Only Easy and ladder games (issue #2777, 2026-11-02)**: the player's own statistics ([[R639]]) count a practice game only against Easy, `PRACTICE_TALLY_DIFFICULTY`, since Medium and Hard hand the AI resources ([[R180]]); the public card figures already counted no handicapped game (live games, development runs on a person's resources, and no practice game, [[R187]]), which a test now holds the development run to. Filing Easy practice games as records was rejected. It takes R2777003 and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2778000]] Constructed and All Random are two ranked ladders (issue #2778, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked for the ranks to be split into Constructed and All Random. A season now holds two ladders, Constructed (the Best of 1 and Conquest queues) and All Random. Each has its own hidden rating, placements, rank, floor, peaks, streak, record, Jlorious and leaderboard, for people and ladder bots alike. A ranked game moves only its mode's ladder, and a ticket's rating, the bot fallback and the grind compare on that ladder. Each read names its ladder: `?ladder=` on `GET /api/ranked` and `GET /api/leaderboard`, the match's own on the match screen. The account screen shows both ranks, the lobby the picked mode's, and the leaderboard switches between them. Migration 0076 copies each rating and this season's rows onto All Random, so nobody loses a rank. One rating behind two ranks and an All Random ladder started from nothing were rejected. It takes R2778000, the first number of issue #2778's block, and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§9.12]].

**[[R2778001]] More ladder bots, on more of the day (issue #2778, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked for more bots, so the ladder can be ground. The pool holds one active bot per weekly active person, between 40 and 400 (a quarter, between 8 and 200, before). Each new bot is on 12 to 18 hours a day (8 to 16 before), still under a grinder's 20. At the floor about 20 bots are on at any hour, more than twice the recent opponents a player may not meet again. It takes R2778001 and amends [[R1454]] and [[R1555000]].

**[[R2783000]] A card's hook is marked as it resolves, and only a seat that could read the card sees the marker (issue #2783, 2026-11-02)**: issue #2782's keyword and card animations had nothing to anchor a Death or a turn hook on. The engine now emits `hookResolving` (`sourceId`, `defId`, `controller`, `hook`) once, just before the effects of each first run of a Cry, a Death, a start-of-turn or end-of-turn hook, a Whenever trigger whose condition holds and an Activate; a hook that fizzles is marked too, a continuation after a prompt is not, nor a card set by its Cloaked whose text is inert ([[R1040]]), a Trap keeps its `trapFired` alone, and a Cry run with no card names its definition with `sourceId` `""`. Nothing answers it: it is withheld from the trigger loop as [[R1361]]'s report is, takes no number in [[R68]]'s order and moves no hash. A seat that could not read the card where it stood as the hook began never sees it, unless that trap's `trapFired` follows in the same action ([[R2652000]]'s rule); otherwise it names its card as the card itself is named ([[R97]]). It takes no room in [[R168]]'s floor, and the golden texts leave it out. It rejects the issue's suggestion of redacting the card as [[R154]] does a firing Trap, since even a card back would tell the other seat that a hidden card's hook answered. It takes R2783000, the first number of issue #2783's block, and edits [[§10.3]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R2783001]] `via`: the keyword behind a hit, a heal, a death or a summon, and First Strike's unanswered strike (issue #2783, 2026-11-02)**: the client guessed Poisonous from the view and could not tell a Cleave hit, a Trample excess, a Lifesteal heal or a Reborn return from any other. `damage`, `damageAbsorbed`, `healed`, `destroyed` and `summoned` now carry an optional `via`, set by the engine where the keyword applies, never by a card, and on the wire only when set: Cleave on a neighbour hit, Trample on an excess, First Strike on the strike [[§4.3]] moves ahead when exactly one side has it, else Pierce on a hit that skipped Armor its target had; Lifesteal on step 8's heal; Poisonous on the death of a unit a Poisonous hit marked, else First Strike on the death of a unit that fell to that strike and so struck nothing back; Reborn on §4.5 step 4's return. One keyword per event, the hit's route first; the trigger loop holds every event without it and a view hides it with its card. It rejects the numbered `Keyword`, a list per event and a `combatStep` field, from which the client would still have to infer the missing strike back. It takes R2783001, the next number of issue #2783's block, and edits [[§10.3]].

**[[R2784000]] gives every card its signature animation as an entry in its set's effects table: none, a bespoke recipe, or a shared motif per hook (issue #2784, 2026-11-02)**: the foundation of issue #2782's card animations. One JSON5 table per set (Stinkyscape's split in two at 091) holds every catalog card in catalog order as `"none: <reason>"`, `{ bespoke }` or a motif on its `cast` or `cry`, keyed by the definition of the readable play resolving now, so a hidden play keys none ([[R202]]). The first ten motifs, the damage and removal family, are pure planners over the existing cues in four beats inside the entry ([[R200]]), scaled by the effects speed ([[R201]]) and drawn not at all with the effects off or under reduced motion; twelve cards across the sets wear them as examples, and a dev-only `/dev/fx` gallery plays every motif and entry. It takes R2784000 and amends [[R502]] and [[R670]], whose one `CARD_FX` table becomes the per-set tables.

**[[R2789000]] How a combat keyword shows: a beat inside its entry keyed on `via`, a shared callout, and a still callout under reduced motion (issue #2789, 2026-11-02)**: with issue #2783's `via` on the event stream, each of the nine combat keywords (Cleave, First Strike, Trample, Pierce, Lifesteal, Windfury, Poisonous, Ranged and Flying) gets a beat, issue #2782's smallest tier, drawn inside the entry of the event it keys on from [[R2784000]]'s motif cues, holding nothing ([[R200]]), scaled by the effects speed ([[R201]]) and reading no definition ([[R202]]). The keyword's icon pops over its unit as one shared callout for `FX_CALLOUT_MS`, a hit's beats staggered `FX_BEAT_STAGGER_MS` apart with at most `FX_CALLOUTS_PER_HIT` callouts; a First Strike that kills shows the dying defender's counter-blow breaking off, one that does not lands it after a beat; Poisonous's burst moves from a guess at the source's keywords to the death's `via`. It amends [[R200]]: under reduced motion the layer still draws a keyword's callout as a static icon, and nothing else. It rejects guessing from the view, callouts without a cap and holding the queue for a beat. It takes R2789000, the first number of issue #2789's block.

**[[R2826000]] the practice AI samples each side's hidden cards against that side's shown cards (issue #2826, 2026-11-02)**: the AI's determinization sampled every hidden card against one set, the cards the opponent had shown, so a dealt deck's library ([[R433]]) could hold a second copy of a card the seat already showed, against [[§2.6]], and could never take a card only the opponent had shown.
