Each hidden card is now sampled against what its own side has shown and what the determinization has given that side, with a card of the seat's library minted for the opponent's deck ([[R73]]) on the opponent's side, and every draw in the same order. Rejected: one shared set, the old sampler; every hidden card of the seat's library against the seat's set, which lets a card swapped in from the opponent's deck repeat one the opponent shows; and every placeholder by the deck it was minted for, which moves the worlds of every game with a steal or a board swap. It takes R2826000, the first number of issue #2826's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R1933001]] Production's card statistics count the development lanes' games as staging's do (issue #1933, 2026-11-03)**: [[R1933000]] kept production's database to live games alone, the development lanes filing into staging's. The owner reversed it once production was live: with fewer than 100 players online at once a patch seldom reaches `PUBLIC_STATS_MIN_LIVE_GAMES` (1000) live games before the next ships, so `/stats` on jackioh.com would say "not enough games" nearly always, and CN Tech's and NA Tech's table could never cover a new set. The gather lane and the learn lane's import now file each file into staging's database and then production's (`PRODUCTION_STATS_IMPORT_DATABASE_URL`), each claiming it by its SHA-256 on its own, and the development rows filed before were copied into production once (`cutover.yml`, step `dev-stats`). Production's figures are padded below the gate as staging's are, labelled provisional ([[R661]]), and `STATS_DATABASE_URL` reads production through a read-only login no import uses. Pointing `STATS_DATABASE_URL` at staging, and a one-time copy without filing on, were rejected. It takes R1933001, the next free number of issue #1933's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2725000]] A card wears one version and the augments layered on it, each a per-card cosmetic offered card by card: Full art (Legendary, moved up from Epic), Animated (Epic) and Signature (Mythic); the 84 full arts are retired, owned still and dropped by nothing, and the scale stays Common to Mythic (issue #2725, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked for a deeper ladder of card looks after Pokémon TCG Pocket's art rarities, full art rarer. Part 1 of the issue's tree adds the `version` and `augment` kinds, owned per card like a foil, and three entries, `version-full-art`, `augment-animated` and `augment-signature`; a card's pick names a version in `fullArt` and lists its `augments` (migration 0079). The 84 full arts keep their owners and drop or sell no more; no sixth tier is added. Amends [[R1313000]], [[R1333000]], [[R2655000]] and [[R2715000]].

**[[R2725001]] A version's or an augment's offer is data in the registry, clauses of card types, rarities, sets and ids; each reaches a floor of cards in every set that ships, and one a card is not offered is never dropped, sold, picked or shown for it: the server refuses it with a 400 (issue #2725, 2026-11-02)**: the issue left which cards take which versions to the plan, as data. The three are offered on every Rare and Epic Unit, every Legendary and Mythic card and the 21 full-art cards, 243 cards in all seven sets; `VERSION_OFFER_MIN_CARDS` and `VERSION_OFFER_MIN_PER_SET` are the floors, and the client reads the offered cards from `COSMETIC_OFFERS`.

**[[R2725002]] A retired full art counts as owning, on its card, the version and augments it combines: the still Epic Full art, the Legendary Full art and Animated, the Mythic those and Signature, the Animated art Animated; read where it is worn, never written (issue #2725, 2026-11-02)**: the issue asked that nobody lose anything. The server reads the implication in `row_owns` and the client in `ownedCardCosmetics`; no row is written, and a pick made before #2725 looks as it did.

**[[R2725003]] Animated and Signature layer on every version they are offered with, a foil, finish and border on every version; part 1 rules out one pairing, Animated on a Legacy card, whose picture never moves; the Signature on the normal face signs over its art window (issue #2725, 2026-11-02)**: the issue asked that the other pulls layer on any version and that a pairing that cannot work be ruled out. Both augments work on the normal face and on Full art; a card with no motion of its own borrows one of the 21's, and the four Legacy cards the Animated augment's offer would take are named in its `except`.

**[[R2725004]] No version or augment wears Radiant's gold: the Signature's ink is a cool silver and its pen glow and burst white, and a borrowed motion draws no gold particle; a Radiant face keeps every Radiant mark under every look (issue #2725, 2026-11-02)**: the issue asked that Radiant stay easy to tell apart. The Signature's parchment ink and gold pen glow sat inside the Radiant gold window, so they are recolored; every look keeps a Radiant face's frame, marks, stats and art paint.

**[[R2739000]] The other seat sees how many cards a card picker offers, never what, and only when the count hides nothing (issue #2739, 2026-11-02)**: the owner asked to watch the opponent decide, as Hearthstone shows an opponent's Discover as its backs, where the other seat saw only that a prompt was open. Its `PendingElsewhereView` now carries `optionCount` for a Discover, or a Choose one, a quest's rewards or a problem's answers of `PROMPT_CARD_OPTION_LIMIT` (4) cards or fewer, asked by a card that seat may read, whose options are modes or cards it may read and whose script does not read them off a hidden zone (`secret_option_counts`: KY's Private Tutor, KY's Constant's Radiant face, Portal to the Past's base face); never on the chooser's view, a custom card's prompt or the mulligan ([[R265]]), and no hash moves. A pick from a library or a hand shows no count, since how many cards matched is that zone's secret; capping the count at the printed number was rejected, as a short deck would still give it away. It takes R2739000, the first number of #2739's block, and edits [[§10.6]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R2739001]] The opponent's hovered option, outlined on its back (issue #2739, 2026-11-02)**: with the backs shown, the owner asked to see which one the opponent's pointer is over, as R738's arrow shows their aim. The chooser's card picker sends a top-level `choiceHover` with the option's place, or `null`, when it changes; the actor relays it to the opponent alone, coalesced by `AIM_RELAY_INTERVAL_MS`, dropped while the opponent is shown no count or past it, and cleared with a `null` when the prompt closes, the sender's socket closes or the game ends. It is never an action, a log row or part of a view (CLAUDE.md rule 7). The outline is still, as the aim's mark is under Reduce Motion; a breathing one was rejected. Duos relays no aim, so it relays no hover either. It takes R2739001, #2739's second number, and edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R2785000]] has a set piece hold the game for one capped, skippable beat per action, set by Big moments (issue #2785, 2026-11-02)**: the owner wants the biggest moments, a wipe, a Starship's launch, a system's climax (issue #2782), to own the board for a beat, where [[R200]] let only [[R2722001]]'s takeover hold one. That hold becomes the general one: whatever a card or system claims as a set piece, the takeover first, holds ahead of its motion, speed-scaled, pinned against the burst budget, once per action and never past `SET_PIECE_HOLD_CAP_MS` (3 s of real time). A tap, a click or Space skips it at once behind a "Tap to skip" hint, the first skip control there is; Big moments (`full`, `short`, `off`) beside the effects speed sets it; Reduce Motion, the effects off and the Duos teammate's silent board take none, while replays, watching and both hotseat seats play it. The clocks are unchanged, so a hold eats the watching seat's real time, which the cap bounds. The `/dev/fx` gallery ([[R2784000]]) plays a card as the one fixture set piece until issue #2782's land. It takes R2785000, the first number of issue #2785's block, and amends [[R200]], [[R2722001]] and [[R2126000]].

**[[R2787000]] gives a Field Spell, a Field Trap or a lasting modifier its card's aura, drawn from the shown view, at most `AURA_MAX_PER_ZONE` over a zone, nearest scope first (issue #2787, 2026-11-02)**: issue #2782's one persistent motif. A set's effects table ([[R2784000]]) may give a card an `aura` of eight looks over its zone, its lane, its side or the board, in its palette; every face-up Field Spell or Field Trap wears its own, and a modifier the view lists wears that of the readable play that added it, since its view names no source ([[R169]]), while a face-down card wears none ([[R202]]). An aura fades in with a burst as the board starts to show it and out as it stops, on one budgeted frame loop that pauses while the page is hidden, paces nothing ([[R200]]) and is a still tint under reduced motion ([[R438]]) and nothing with the effects off; ten Field Spells across the sets wear one as examples. It takes R2787000 and extends [[R2784000]].

**[[R2791000]] gives each of the ten tempo and card keywords a beat inside its entry: Charge's speed lines, hero-lean and first-attack dash, Rush's unit-lean and dash, Equip's flight-and-snap onto its host, Tradable's swirl out and back on the `flight` motif, Stack's offset landing with its count pulse, Spell Damage's "+N" sigil on the resolving source, Lucky's clover glints over a batch's extra rolls, Deft's feint after a same-turn switch-then-attack, Flashback's rise from the graveyard pile and exile dissolve, and Temporary's dissolve (issue #2791, 2026-11-03)**: presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7), keyed on [[R2783001]]'s `via` where one exists and otherwise on the existing events plus the keywords and ids the view shows, hand keywords read as a delta over the printed face, never a hidden card ([[R202]]), through the shared callout and the stagger, cap, reduced-motion and intensity rules of [[R2789000]]. It takes R2791000 and extends [[R2784000]].

**[[R2795000]] fills Classic+'s card animation table and adds three Flourishes (issue #2795, 2026-11-03)**: issue #2782's pass over Classic+. Every Spell, Field Spell, Trap and Field Trap of its 78 cards names its cast's motif, a set piece or a bespoke recipe, every Unit whose hook changes the board names a motif on that hook, every Field Spell given an entry wears an aura ([[R2787000]]), and at least 55% of its cards have an entry other than `none`; a token takes one only where it earns one. Motifs follow `cargo jackioh fx census` with each override given a reason. The cards it makes take issue #2793's `send`, its fuses `transmute` and its player modifiers issue #2796's `seal`, so no motif joins the vocabulary, and its per-card table is `fx/cards/classicplus.md`; #35 Rollback, #41 KY's Constant and #56 Book of Pain join Crushing Walls as Flourishes, each keyed on its readable play and inside its entry ([[R200]]); the `/dev/fx` gallery plays every entry. [[R2722001]] is unchanged. It takes R2795000 and extends [[R2784000]].

**[[R2800000]] has a board clear's removals play as one wipe its set piece holds: Twisting Nether's vortex, Ceaseless Void's fold and a shared vortex (issue #2800, 2026-11-02)**: the owner wants the cards that clear the board, the game's most dramatic plays, to stop looking like a stack of ordinary deaths (issue #2782's loudest tier). A card's effects-table entry may now name a set piece ([[R2784000]]); its hook's first run of removals, from its marker ([[R2783000]]) or a Trap's firing, is one pinned entry the queue holds as the action's one set piece ([[R2785000]]): #88 Twisting Nether's vortex, which swallows the board, or only the enemy side on its Radiant choice; #100 Ceaseless Void's fold, which replaces its takeover look inside one hold; and a shared vortex for every other card that clears both sides, each with a sound bed of existing recipes. It keys only on a definition the seat reads ([[R202]]), a Trap's centres on its zone ([[R154]]), and the clears of one side take the `wipe` motif. It takes R2800000, the first number of issue #2800's block, and amends [[R670]], [[R2722001]], [[R2784000]] and [[R2785000]].

**[[R2827000]] the Duos AI passes a card it can play itself when its teammate does clearly more with it (issue #2827, 2026-11-02)**: an own-playable card is a this-turn pass candidate when its teammate can play it this turn, and passes only past both hurdles: [[R1723000]]'s worth-it check and the new own-use hurdle, that check's gain less what the seat's own best bounded play of the card gains it in its own duel, gain against gain since two duels' scores do not compare. It takes R2827000, the first number of issue #2827's block, and edits [[§13.7]].

**[[R2855000]] a set Unit token leaves the field as a token (issue #2855, 2026-11-02)**: the daily fuzz found Unit tokens (Knowledge Breaker Prime, Solarius Prime, Salvage Protocol) set face-down by a lent Cloaked reaching a graveyard or an exile pile once destroyed, exiled or bounced, since the engine read a set card as the Field Trap it lies as. [[R11]] rules it over [[R783008]]: a set Unit token that leaves the field unrevealed ceases to exist, as a face-up one does.

**[[R2875000]] a news entry's reward is claimed once per account and entry (issue #2875, 2026-11-03)**: `POST /api/news/:newsId/claim` is a spend, in the account's turn ([[R2378000]]) and under an Idempotency-Key whose hash covers the entry ([[R2124000]]); it pays ledger rows with the new reason `news` and keeps the claim in migration 0077's `news_claims`, whose key refuses a second one. It takes R2875000, the first number of issue #2875's block, adds [[§14.13]] and edits [[§14.11]].

**[[R2875001]] a news entry lives NEWS_TTL_DAYS from its postedAt (issue #2875, 2026-11-03)**: on the server's clock, an entry not yet posted is left out and refused as unknown, and one past its days is left out and refused, claimed or not. It takes R2875001 and is written in [[§14.13]].

**[[R2875002]] unread is unclaimed (issue #2875, 2026-11-03)**: the landing's News bookmark beside the quests' ([[R2035000]]) counts the live entries the server marks unclaimed, shows no bubble at zero and only to an active account while `ECONOMY_LIVE` is on and some entry is live. It takes R2875002 and is written in [[§14.13]].

**[[R2875003]] a news reward's kinds and its default (issue #2875, 2026-11-03)**: Jlore, card packs, card tokens, cosmetic tokens of a tier or cosmetic packs, each paying its named amount; an entry naming none takes a global card pack for a patch that adds or reprints cards, and Jlore otherwise. It takes R2875003 and edits [[§14.1]], [[§14.3]], [[§14.5]] and [[§14.6]].

**[[R2881000]] lets a friend watch a live co-op game (issue #2881, 2026-11-03)**: a friend could watch a friend's live 1v1 match but not their co-op game, which [[§13]] left out of scope. The issue asked for the same watch of one co-op seat: friends only, under `SPECTATORS_PER_MATCH`, `SPECTATE_DELAY_MS` behind, with no action offered and nothing a watcher sends reaching the game, the count told to both people and the socket closed after the result's view. The watcher sees the friend's seat's [[§13.6]] view and never a ping, and the friend's own teammate is refused. As that view holds the teammate's duel, a block between the watcher and the friend's teammate refuses a watch and ends one running, as a block with the friend does ([[§9.4]]). It takes R2881000, the first number of issue #2881's block. It edits [[§9.5]], [[§9.8]], [[§13.6]], [[§13.9]], [[§13.10]] and [[R2615000]].

**[[R2895000]] A card's 95% ranges, behind a button (issue #2895, 2026-11-03)**: the owner asked for `/stats` to show how sure each card's figures are, since a card at 53% and one at 56% read as different when their ranges overlap. `GET /api/stats/cards` now answers each card's 95% ranges: its win rate's, the Wilson interval over the games the rate counts ([[R850000]], [[R1994000]]; the seen games under All Random, [[R2777002]]), and its adjusted rate's, [[R2777000]]'s posterior, the rate ± `STATS_INTERVAL_Z` · √(weight · SE²), never wider than about ±9.8 points and narrowing with the games. The Cards tab's Show 95% ranges button, off by default, puts them under Win rate and Adjusted in the table and the grid, with a line saying what a range means; a row below the floor shows none ([[R654]]). Ranges computed in the client and a frequentist interval of the shrunk rate were rejected. It takes R2895000, the first number of issue #2895's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2777004]] `/stats` opens on every mode together, with Constructed, All Random, Best of 1 and Conquest beside it (amends [[R2777001]]; issue #2777, 2026-11-04)**: the owner asked for the page to open on all game modes, always, after production's page opened on an empty Constructed table with 12,000 All Random games one choice away.

**[[R2794000]] gives every Classic card its signature animation: a marquee entrance beside its hooks, the recall motif and three Flourishes, played in the gallery (issue #2794, 2026-11-03)**: issue #2782's pass over Classic. Each of its cards has its final entry in its set's effects table ([[R2784000]]), picked from the effects census and overridden only with a reason, 79 of its 90 cards more than `none`, every non-Unit a `cast` entry and every Field Spell an aura ([[R2787000]]). A Unit's entry may now name its marquee entrance beside its hooks ([[R670]]), so #45 Nature Titan keeps its entrance and gains a Cry; three cards that return a card to the hand take a new `recall` motif; #3 Book of Heal, #30 Recycle and #85 King Wagtoggle each have a Flourish built from the motif primitives; and the `/dev/fx` gallery plays every Classic entry, a hook's motif through its own director. It keys only on a play the seat reads ([[R202]]) and restyles no entrance or takeover ([[R2722001]]). It takes R2794000, the first number of issue #2794's block, and amends [[R2784000]].

**[[R2797000]] plays a card's Death, turn hook, Whenever trigger or Activate from its marker and a Trap's cast from its firing, and fills Stinkyscape 001 to 090's animation table (issue #2797, 2026-11-03)**: the first half of issue #2782's Stinkyscape pass. A hook's `hookResolving` marker ([[R2783000]]) opens its card's entry in the effects layer's memory until the next marker, the play's end, an attack, a turn's end or start or the game's end, so its entry's `death`, `trigger` or `activate` motif plays as a play's `cast` or `cry` does, and a Trap's `trapFired` opens its `cast`; a hidden marker keys nothing ([[R202]]) and a hook's modifier wears no aura ([[R2787000]]). `hex` draws a mark put on or lifted, `transmute` an Equip's fusion, and Olivian Expanse, Imitated Instrumentality and Drone Core X take recipes of their own inside their entries ([[R200]]). The range's 110 entries leave 16 `none`, with every non-token Spell, Field Spell, Trap and Field Trap on its `cast` and every Field Spell wearing an aura. It takes R2797000 and amends [[R2784000]], whose hooks waited for the marker.

**[[R2801000]] gives the Starship its moment: pieces fusing into one Starship are one fuse run that bolts each on, and its launch is a set piece, a countdown, an ignition and a lift-off scaled by its pieces (issue #2801, 2026-11-03)**: the owner wants Stinkyscape's signature system, whose fuse and launch each played one plain row ([[R1852000]], [[R1852003]]), to be a dramatic moment (issue #2782's tiers). Pieces fusing one after another into one Starship play as one fuse run, each dead piece's card back flying in a stagger apart and bolting on, and a Dormant Starship's face bolts on a hull plate a piece. Its launch is the `starship` set piece, held as [[R2785000]] holds every set piece: a 3-2-1 countdown over the ship, the board shaking harder at each count, rocket exhaust at the ignition, then the lift-off with a contrail and the landing, its exhaust, contrail and shake grown with its pieces, a sound bed across it, and one still frame that holds nothing under Reduce Motion. Launch Sequencer's `tithe` glints on the launch's price ([[R1852006]]), the Retrofitting Program's `send` and the Launchers' `conjure` wear the rocket's look, and nothing keys on a hidden card ([[R202]]). It takes R2801000, the first number of issue #2801's block, and amends [[R2784000]], [[R2785000]] and [[R2791000]].

**[[R2803000]] audits the finished card-animation work and reworks the shared entrances to land in the motif language's two-wave landing (issue #2803, 2026-11-04)**: 551 of 634 non-token cards animate, past the audit's floor of half; every Spell, Field Spell, Trap and Field Trap wears a `cast`, a recipe, a set piece or the one `none` a Starship part may wear, and every Field Spell an aura; every table entry's cues end within `FX_MAX_TAIL_MS`, every set piece's hold within its cap, one hold per action; the census's 364 disagreements each carry their set pass's reason, and the four motifs below three cards are folded (`chain` into `nova`, amending R2784000's Examples-table citation; `mirror` into `transmute`; `ward` into the rows with `FX_ARMOR_TRAUMA`; `glimpse` into bespoke `mindJudge` with `FX_MIND_JUDGE_TRAUMA` and `bless`), 31 drawn motifs remaining with no sub-look unused; a summoned or cast Legendary's ring carries its echo, the prismatic sweep has its own keyframes, the recoil lands in two steps and the reduced-motion still echoes the takeover at rest, the music, the entrances' timing and the one capped hold unchanged; all five phone scenes (cap check, Twisting Nether, Starship launch, Mythic takeover, board of 4 auras) hold the phone frame budget after the audit's scale-back, and the gallery captures every set piece and every motif, `shift` proving its DOM ghosts (see the report §7). It takes R2803000 and amends [[R2722001]] and [[R2784000]].

**[[R2882000]] today's deal (issue #2882, 2026-11-03)**: issue #2882 asked for one shared practice deal a day, so friends compare how they did on the same cards. Play today's deal starts a free game on the UTC day's seed and seat, with a random deck at the setup's difficulty and pick and no last board; it asks for no server seed, so it earns nothing ([[R1303000]]).

**[[R2922000]] A handicap's `heroArmor` (issue #2922, 2026-11-03)**: a dungeon run's Combat artifacts give the human's hero Armor for each fight, and a handicap had no field for it. A handicap takes one more optional field, `heroArmor`, the Armor that seat's hero starts the game with: the hero's own Armor, as C+ #46 leaves on a hero, which [[§4.4]] step 2 takes off every hit, which adds to every other source ([[R124]]) and which nothing spends, rather than Hearthstone's pool that a hit uses up. A negative value is refused; 0 means none, and it is serialized only when set and stored only above 0, as `heroHealth` is ([[R290]]), so no other game's hash, replay or golden trace moves. Only a run's fight sets it. It takes R2922000, the first number of #2922's block, and edits [[§4.4]].

**[[R2922001]] A dungeon run's curses and Combat artifacts change its fights (issue #2922, 2026-11-03)**: curses raised a run's score and changed no fight, so taking every curse was free score, and Combat artifacts did nothing in one. Each curse and Combat artifact may now carry a fight effect, which `run_fight_setup` folds into the fight's handicaps after Presence: each curse taken once into the enemy's (more hero health, more opening cards, or more crystals every turn, its bonus and cap together under [[R181]]), each Combat artifact held once per level into the human's (more opening cards, [[R182]], or starting Armor, [[R2922000]]). A negative amount counts as none, an enemy's own artifacts other than Presence change nothing, and a run with neither gets the setup it got before. The placeholders' numbers are strawmen ([[§15.13]]); Thick Skin's 3 Armor became 1 a level, since Armor cuts every hit. A Sandbox run saved on a device before this change resumes with its curses and Combat artifacts acting. It takes R2922001, #2922's second number, and edits [[§15.4]], [[§15.6]], [[§15.9]] and [[§15.12]].

**[[R2940000]] Part 2 appends three versions, each offered on a seeded share of cards of its own: Alt art (Rare) on half of every card but the Legacy ones, Sketch (Rare) on Units and Legendary and Mythic cards, Alt full art (Legendary) on Epic Units and Legendary and Mythic cards; granted to no account, and the scale stays Common to Mythic (issue #2940, 2026-11-03)**: part 2 of #2725's ten new card versions (tracker #2729), the three that draw the card's subject anew. The owner asked that each version go to a varied set of cards of its own, so each clause samples by the version's own id ([[R2949001]]) and no two versions take the same cards; a version a card is not offered is refused with a 400 by a pick, a token or the Chase Shard bar and never listed for it. It takes R2940000, the first number of issue #2940's block, and amends [[R2725000]] and [[R2725001]].
**[[R2940001]] How part 2's versions draw: Alt art re-seeds the card's painter in the normal frame, keeping its subject; the Sketch draws the normal picture in graphite on slate paper, its base frame a pencilled outline; Alt full art is Alt art's picture full bleed; Radiant keeps every mark and no sketch colour is gold (issue #2940, 2026-11-03)**: the issue described each look; this fixes how each is drawn, a pure function of the card as every look is. Alt art keeps what the card's name, tags and type pick and grows a new scene from its own seed, as the Signature look does ([[R2949000]]); the Sketch is one SVG filter over the normal picture; a delivered file stays the normal picture's. It amends [[R2725004]].
**[[R2940002]] Animated, a foil, a finish and a border layer on Alt art, the Sketch and Alt full art; Signature on none of them, by R2949000's rule, refused by the picker and the server; no other pairing is ruled out (issue #2940, 2026-11-03)**: the issue asked that every augment but Signature layer on each new version; Signature's own rule ([[R2949000]]) already refuses it beside any version but Full art, so this part adds no mechanism of its own. A worn border keeps its edge and bezel on a Sketch. It amends [[R2725003]].

**[[R2942000]] Part 3 appends three Epic versions, each offered on a seeded share of cards of its own: Extended art on Spells and Legendary and Mythic cards, Pixel on Rare and Epic Units and Traps and Legendary and Mythic cards, Comic on Rare and Epic Units, Spells and Traps and Legendary and Mythic cards; granted to no account, and the scale stays Common to Mythic (issue #2942, 2026-11-04)**: part 3 of #2725's ten new card versions (tracker #2729), the three Epic restylings of the card's own art. The owner asked that each version go to a varied set of cards of its own, so each clause samples by the version's own id ([[R2949001]]) and no two of the seven versions take the same cards; a version a card is not offered is refused with a 400 by a pick, a token or the Chase Shard bar and never listed for it. The issue left the subsets to the builder, who chose the types each style suits. It takes R2942000, the first number of issue #2942's block, and amends [[R2725000]] and [[R2725001]].
**[[R2942001]] How part 3's versions draw: Extended art runs the card's own picture past its window behind a translucent frame and rules box; Pixel samples the normal picture into 8-bit cells in a retro frame, its name in a pixel face; Comic flattens it to pop art with halftone dots and ink outlines, its name in a burst; Radiant keeps every mark and no new colour is gold (issue #2942, 2026-11-04)**: the issue described each look; this fixes how each is drawn, a pure function of the card as every look is. Pixel and Comic are SVG filters over the normal picture, as the Sketch is ([[R2940001]]); Pixel's name face is a 5×7 bitmap built in rather than a downloaded font, a name past `PIXEL_NAME_MAX_GLYPHS` glyphs or on a small face printing in the normal face since its cells would fall under a pixel there (the review of #2970), and Comic's burst is cyan since yellow lies in Radiant's gold. It amends [[R2725004]].
**[[R2942002]] Animated, a foil, a finish and a border layer on Extended art, Pixel and Comic; Signature on none of them, by R2949000's rule, refused by the picker and the server; no other pairing is ruled out (issue #2942, 2026-11-04)**: the issue asked that every augment but Signature layer on each new version; Signature's own rule ([[R2949000]]) already refuses it beside any version but Full art, so this part adds no mechanism of its own. A finish shows through Extended art's translucent frame, and a worn border keeps its edge and bezel on Pixel and Comic. It amends [[R2725003]].

**[[R2943000]] Part 4 appends two Legendary versions, each a chase offered on a seeded share of cards of its own: Set showcase on Legendary and Mythic cards, Out of frame on Rare to Mythic Units but the Legacy cards and the Starship; granted to no account, and the scale stays Common to Mythic (issue #2943, 2026-11-04)**: part 4 of #2725's ten new card versions (tracker #2729), the two that restyle the whole card. The owner asked that each version go to a varied set of cards of its own, so each clause samples by the version's own id ([[R2949001]]) and no two of the nine versions take the same cards; a version a card is not offered is refused with a 400 by a pick, a token or the Chase Shard bar and never listed for it. #2725 §1 named the cards each suits (each set's signature cards, Units with a strong subject); the builder read those as Legendary and Mythic cards and Rare to Mythic Units, and left out the cards whose pictures have no subject of their own. It takes R2943000, the first number of issue #2943's block, and amends [[R2725000]] and [[R2725001]].
**[[R2943001]] How part 4's versions draw: Set showcase draws the card in its set's own look, its picture in its set's treatment and on a base face its set's edge, bezel and name banner, Classic+ and Meditative+ in their parents'; Out of frame cuts the picture's subject out of a smaller window over the frame, inside the card's rim and under its gem, crest, name, stats and rules box; Radiant keeps every mark and no new colour is gold (issue #2943, 2026-11-04)**: the issue described each look and asked that Out of frame keep the cost gem, crest, stats and rules text readable; this fixes how each is drawn, a pure function of the card as every look is. Set showcase's treatments are SVG filters over the normal picture, as the Sketch's, Pixel's and Comic's are ([[R2940001]], [[R2942001]]), and its lettering the display and text faces, since the sets' signature hands have no metric-matched fallback. Out of frame's subject stays inside the card's rim rather than past its outer edge, so it never covers a neighbouring card, a border or Radiant's gold edge. It amends [[R2725004]].
**[[R2943002]] Animated, a foil, a finish and a border layer on Set showcase and Out of frame; Signature on neither, by R2949000's rule, refused by the picker and the server; no other pairing is ruled out (issue #2943, 2026-11-04)**: the issue asked that every augment but Signature layer on each new version; Signature's own rule ([[R2949000]]) already refuses it beside any version but Full art, so this part adds no mechanism of its own. A worn border keeps its edge and bezel on Set showcase, and Out of frame's cutout takes no foil over its empty corners. It amends [[R2725003]].

**[[R2949000]] Signature is worn only beside the Full art version and the Animated augment, each owned and offered on the card, and shows the Signature look, a full art and a motion of its own with the signature; never on the normal face or another version (issue #2949, 2026-11-03)**: the owner's answer to #2725's second question, before the ten new versions are built. Signature stays its own Mythic augment, owned card by card, and includes neither of the other two; a pick naming it without both, each owned and offered there, is a 400 and writes nothing, a change that leaves a held one without them drops it, and a match freezes it only beside both. With it on the face draws a picture of its own and moves by a motion of its own, base and Radiant, no gold ([[R2725004]]); a retired Mythic, which owns all three, now shows it. It takes R2949000, the first number of issue #2949's block, and amends [[R2725000]], [[R2725002]] and [[R2725003]].
**[[R2949001]] An offer clause may take a seeded share of its cards; Full art's offer is 60 in 100 of the cards its clauses took, the 21 full-art cards always among them, so cards differ; Signature's lies inside Full art's and Animated's (issue #2949, 2026-11-03)**: the owner's answer to #2725's third question, that the versions be spread so that cards differ. A clause's `sample` takes the cards whose seeded FNV-1a hash falls below its percent, data the server reads and the client reads by id; Full art keeps its floor in every set and the 21, Signature is offered where both are, and Animated does not change. It amends [[R2725001]].

**[[R2972000]] Giving up a dungeon run or leaving its fight asks first; the run map links back to Play (issue #2972, 2026-11-04)**: abandoning a run asks in place (Cancel focused, Escape cancels, only Confirm abandons), leaving a fight with no result is held by the leave guard with a question saying the fight starts over, and the run map links back to the Play menu with the run kept saved.

**[[R2973000]] Leaving a live co-op game asks first, and says the teammate plays on and the AI takes the seat (issue #2973, 2026-11-04)**: amends [[R2479000]] for co-op games. While a co-op board shows a view with no result on a socket neither closed nor refused, every way off it is held by the page's leave guard ([[R1754000]]) and asks "Leave the match?" plus the co-op line, naming the teammate playing on and the AI taking the seat after `DISCONNECT_GRACE_SECONDS` ([[§9.5]], [[§13.8]]). Stay (focus, Escape, Back per [[R2127000]], click outside) sends nothing; Leave makes the held move; a result, a closed-for-good socket or a refused one releases the guard and closes an open question; a forced `navigate` is never held.

**[[R2975000]] A card whose every listed choice step 1 refuses says why, and the fuzz holds every card to a reason (issue #2975, 2026-11-04)**: a hand card `legalActions` listed no play of could still say nothing, which [[R979000]]'s reason was added to end. Two gaps remained: a card whose every choice combination [[§10.5]] step 1 refuses for its targets' discards or its Tribute pick, as a Spell needing two targets with two Classic #89 Paul Allen's Ghosts too dear together as the only ones; and Classic #57 Echo copying an X-cost Spell with no mana left for X once its own price is paid. Each now says step 1's own refusal, while no reason a card already gave changes; the fuzz monitor's new I13 holds every sampled state to the rule, hand and admitted graveyard cards alike ([[R1571000]]). Presentation only. It takes R2975000, the first number of issue #2975's block, and edits [[§10.8]] and [[R979000]].

**[[R2976000]] has the beam keep, while the opponent's board threatens lethal, up to two switches to Defense Position that lower that threat past its cut (issue #2976, 2026-11-04)**: [[R1759000]] kept only stand-downs past the cut, and a unit that survives its fight is no stand-down, so with a full hand the switch that would wall every attacker was never tried: at 7 life with a 7/7 in Attack Position against five enemy 7/7s, the AI played out its hand, ended its turn and died. While `face_threat` reaches `effective_life`, the expansion now also keeps up to `AI_SEARCH.position_switches` switches from Attack to Defense Position the cut and the stand-downs left out that lower that threat, each tried on a copy of the state, the least threat left first. No constant or weight moved. It takes R2976000, the first number of issue #2976's block, and amends [[R1759000]].

**[[R2977000]] The AI's mulligan keeps up to its seat's mana (issue #2977, 2026-11-04)**: Medium and Hard get a crystal more every turn than a human ([[R181]]), so a four-drop is their turn-3 play, yet the AI's mulligan sent back every card above `AI_MULLIGAN.keepMaxCost`, 3, at every tier; the maintainer asked that it work for any starting mana, as a dungeon run's enemy will often hold more ([[R2922001]]). The bound is now `keepMaxCost` plus the seat's `manaBonus`, within its `manaCap` and never below 0, read from its handicap ([[R180]]), and the shaped exception moves up with it; `firstTurnMaxCost` and the greedy baseline's mulligan are unchanged, and a seat with no bonus keeps what it kept. The daily hard-vs-easy fixture smoke, whose first 4 games fell to 2 wins of the 3 needed, now plays the CLI smoke's 20. Rejected: the bound in `decide`, and a `firstTurnMaxCost` raised with the bonus. It takes R2977000, the first number of issue #2977's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2982000]] Claim all claims every unclaimed news entry through the entry's own claim, newest first, one spend each: the list and the bookmark's count move per answer ([[R2875002]]), the first refusal stops the run with its reason, and the wallet is announced once per run that paid Jlore (issue #2982, 2026-11-04)**: the issue asked for a Claim all button above the news list and for news and quest entries to render as framed cards. Each Claim-all press is N individual spends ([[R2875000]], each with its own `Idempotency-Key` per [[R2124000]]); the server decides each claim, while every Claim and Claim all wait ([[R2378000]]). Entries render as cards sharing one `.entry-card` frame on the landing's parchment and `/collection`'s shell, in light and dark and at phone width. It takes R2982000, the first number of issue #2982's block.

**[[R2983000]] Google signs in by ID token through Google's own button on this site's origin, with a hashed nonce, falling back to R666's PKCE redirect (issue #2983, 2026-11-04)**: Google's sign-in screen read "to continue to `<ref>.supabase.co`", the host R666's redirect sends the player back to, and the owner wanted it to say jackioh.com without paying for the auth provider's custom-domain add-on. Signing in on the page itself, with Google Identity Services' button, makes this site's origin the one Google names; its ID token is exchanged with a hashed nonce, and R666's redirect stays for every case Google's button cannot serve. It takes R2983000, the first number of issue #2983's block, and extends [[R666]].

**[[R2990000]] A watcher throws emotes from the sidelines, relayed live to both seats and the other watchers and never to its sender (issue #2990, 2026-11-04)**: friends watching a live game over `/ws/watch` ([[§9.5]], [[R2881000]]) could only look; they now react with the very `emote` frame a seat sends. It stays out of `ActionBody`, so no reducer, log, hash, record, telemetry, card that hears emotes ([[R1127]]) or bot persona ([[R645]]) sees it; an id outside the pool is `malformed`. The actor relays it at once as `spectatorEmote` with the watcher's `name#tag` to each person's socket and every other watcher, never the sender. A watcher sees only the friend's seat, `SPECTATE_DELAY_MS` late, so what an emote can carry to the opponent beyond that is accepted as the feature's cost. It takes R2990000, the first number of issue #2990's block, and amends [[R1103000]], [[R2609000]] and [[R2881000]].
**[[R2990001]] A watcher's emote hand is emoji only, dealt from the match seed and its own account, and sent to it alone (issue #2990, 2026-11-04)**: a watcher holds `SPECTATOR_EMOTE_HAND_SIZE` (5) distinct emoji and no voice line, which would sound like a player speaking, dealt on the stream `{seed}:spectator-emotes:{profile id}` as [[R1341]] deals a seat's, and sent to it alone as `spectatorHand` after the loadouts. An emote it does not hold is dropped silently, before the limit, as [[R1342]] drops a seat's.
**[[R2990002]] A watcher's emotes have a limit of their own, stricter than a seat's, which the server enforces and the menu greys out from (issue #2990, 2026-11-04)**: [[R643]]'s one gate now takes the limit it applies; a watcher's is `SPECTATOR_EMOTE_COOLDOWN_MS` (3 s), `SPECTATOR_EMOTE_WINDOW_MAX` (3) and `SPECTATOR_EMOTE_WINDOW_MS` (30 s), in the server's config and generated for the web, so the menu and the actor agree. A drop is silent and no refused action, and the actor keeps each watching account's sends across its sockets.
**[[R2990003]] A block between a watcher and a seat or another watcher keeps the watcher's emotes from that one alone (issue #2990, 2026-11-04)**: changed from the issue, which took every such block to close the watch: [[§9.4]] closes it only between the watcher and the friend (and, in co-op, the friend's teammate, [[R2881000]]), so a block with the 1v1 rival or another watcher leaves the watch open and the relay reads the blocks of each recipient before it sends, as [[R1351000]] mutes a blocked pair in a match; a failed read sends the emote to no one.
**[[R2990004]] Spectator emotes show in a sideline strip beside the board, one per watcher, which a player mutes for the match or on the device (issue #2990, 2026-11-04)**: a strip at the board's edge, a row along the top on a phone, shows each watcher's newest emote by name on a 1v1 board, a co-op game's own boards and the watch screen, with the watcher's menu there; a player mutes it for the match from the strip, or in every match with the device setting "Mute spectator emotes" beside [[R643]]'s "Mute opponent emotes". A watcher's own emotes still show on its own strip.

**[[R3011000]] /stats's card breakdown drops the art picker (issue #3011, 2026-11-04)**: amends [[R2756000]] to retire the choice. The breakdown renders no art buttons and its faces print the setting's still Epic while the Full-art cards setting is on and the normal face while it is off, the Radiant face the same under Show Radiant, a listed token its parent's; the page still reads no ownership and writes no pick.

**[[R3012000]] The Patch notes page nests micro patches under their patch, and a legend on the side jumps to any entry (issue #3012, 2026-11-04)**: amends [[R724000]], [[R507]] and [[R1369000]]. The owner asked for the patch notes to be streamlined by tier: a major and a patch are entries, and a micro patch (`v0.Z.Xb`) is appended under the nearest major or patch before it in `patches.json`'s order, oldest first, with its version, date and notes, and no art. A sticky legend (a `<nav>` that becomes a collapsible bar on a phone) lists the lines, majors and patches with micros nested; following a link, or an address hash such as `/patch-notes#v0.5.3`, opens the line, loads the entry's cards as [[R507]] does for an entry opened by hand, scrolls to it and marks it `aria-current`. The tier is the record's own (never a parsed version, [[R105]]); a micro with nothing before it in its line stays an entry of its own, and the static `/patch-notes` HTML keeps listing every version flat.

**[[R3012001]] Release art follows the tier, a still poster for a major, art for a patch and none for a micro patch (issue #3012, 2026-11-04)**: amends [[R724000]] and [[R1369000]] where #2681's art gave every patch a picture and animated a major's. Nothing moves any more; a major's poster is richer (the line's name large, headline cards by rarity, a frame), a patch's art is the same style at its scale, and a micro has none. Old `/release-art/<version>.svg` links are kept (CLAUDE.md rule 13): a major's `-still.svg` serves the poster too, and a micro up to `RELEASE_ART_LEGACY_MICRO_LAST` (the last version of `patches.json` when this shipped) keeps its file. A micro's GitHub release carries no image. Decided here: the old micros keep their files, as the issue's default asks, rather than being dropped.

**[[R2944000]] Part 5 appends two Mythic versions, the top chases, each offered on a seeded share of the marquee cards of its own: Immersive on Legendary and Mythic cards but the Starship, Prismatic on every Mythic card and a share of the Legendary ones; granted to no account, no token buys one, and the scale stays Common to Mythic (issue #2944, 2026-11-04)**: part 5 of #2725's ten new card versions (tracker #2729), the last, the two Mythic chases. #2725 §1 gives both to a few marquee cards per set, which the builder read as Legendary and Mythic cards; the owner asked that each version go to a varied set of cards of its own, so each sampled clause samples by the version's own id ([[R2949001]]) and no two of the eleven versions take the same cards, and a version a card is not offered is refused with a 400 by a pick, a token or the Chase Shard bar and never listed for it. Prismatic takes every Mythic card whole, since one clause sampling Legendary and Mythic cards alike left Meditative+ under its floor, and Immersive leaves out the Starship, whose picture has no subject of its own. No cosmetic token is Mythic ([[R2728000]]), so none buys either. It takes R2944000, the first number of issue #2944's block, and amends [[R2725000]] and [[R2725001]].
**[[R2944001]] How part 5's versions draw: Immersive lays the card's subject on a near layer over its backdrop hazed as far off, inside its window; Prismatic draws its picture in mirror chrome under an iridescent sheen and on a base face a rainbow edge, a chrome bezel and a chrome banner, chrome and never gold; Radiant keeps every mark and no new colour is gold (issue #2944, 2026-11-04)**: the issue described each look, asked that Prismatic be chrome and never gold, and named a palette test it called `RADIANT_GOLD_CLEARANCE`, which the constants name as the `RADIANT_GOLD_*` window; this fixes how each is drawn, a pure function of the card as every look is. Immersive's two layers are Out of frame's two parts ([[R2943001]]), the backdrop under an SVG haze and the subject drawn as it is over it, inside the window's own shape; Prismatic's chrome is an SVG filter over the normal picture, as the other restyles are ([[R2942001]]), and its sheen's spectrum leaves out yellow and orange and runs there and back, so no two neighbours mix to gold. It amends [[R2725004]].
**[[R2944002]] Animated, a foil, a finish and a border layer on Immersive and Prismatic; Signature on neither, by R2949000's rule; Immersive's near layer follows the pointer and the tilt and breathes in a motion slot, the one Animated shares on its face, and holds still under reduced motion, with Animated foil off and on a lazy face (issue #2944, 2026-11-04)**: the issue asked that Immersive's motion take motion slots and hold still under reduced motion and with Animated foil off, and that every augment but Signature layer on each new version; Signature's own rule ([[R2949000]]) already refuses it beside any version but Full art, so this part adds no mechanism of its own. The builder chose one slot a face for the depth and Animated's motion together, the pointer's place in the viewport (which reads no layout) over its place in the card, and the near layer alone moving, since a moving backdrop would show the window's edge; a device that asks leave to report its tilt is not asked. It amends [[R2725003]].

**[[R3013000]] A card's proficiency counts the seat's own dealt plays from hand in the games the server runs and pays for (issue #3013, 2026-11-04)**: the owner asked for a card "proficiency" that levels up as the same card is played; the issue left open which games and plays count and the curve, so a play is an entry of the fold's `played` from the seat's own deck, Created cards and tokens apart, ladder, unranked and Conquest count in the result's transaction, practice, co-op and bots count nothing, nothing is backfilled, and the five levels take 3, 15, 40, 100 and 200 plays and never fall.

**[[R3013001]] Level n unlocks stamp n, and a card wears at most one, kept on its proficiency row (issue #3013, 2026-11-04)**: the issue asked that each level unlock a stamp and the server check every pick; a pick above the card's level is a 400 writing nothing, the pick takes the account's turn, the dev fill tops every card's level with no ledger row, a bot wears none, and the pick stays out of the card picks the cosmetics rework is rebuilding.

**[[R3013002]] A stamp shows on its owner's copies and to other viewers through the frozen loadout once its card is shown, clear of every part of the face and in no Radiant gold (issue #3013, 2026-11-04; amends [[R1834003]] and [[R2725004]])**: the issue asked the stamp to show like the other per-card picks; the loadout freezes each dealt card's stamp and the `cardLoadouts` frame and replay pages carry it by R1834003's send rule, the opponent-cosmetics switch hides it, and each of the five SVG designs sits in a fixed slot the component test measures.

**[[R3016000]] A mouse resting on a card of the Collection's owned-cards grid, or the keyboard's focus, opens its hover preview: its look as the card, in hand and on the board, drawn by the game's own faces, with a Radiant toggle; the fullscreen preview shows the same views (issue #3016, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked to see, on hovering a card in the Collection, the card, what it looks like in the hand and what it looks like on the board. The preview opens after the board's own hover delay, `HOVER_DELAY_MS`, behind both "Hover previews" switches, and stays while the pointer is on the card or on it, so its Radiant toggle can be reached; it closes `LOOK_HOVER_CLOSE_MS` after the pointer leaves both, and at once on the focus leaving, Escape, a scroll or a press. Each view is the face a game draws, `CardFace` or `MinionFace`: the whole face, the hand's face (a full-art card's art alone, [[R2723000]]), and a Unit as a lane's minion, a Spell as it resolves, a Trap or Field card as the backrow shows it. Touch has no hover, so its long press opens the fullscreen preview ([[R2730000]]) as before, which now shows the same views; no token is spent from the hover panel ([[R1953001]]). The card's look strip and the equip and buy screen wait for #3015's ladders, #3016's second part. It takes R3016000, the first number of issue #3016's block, and amends [[R2730000]] and [[R2723000]].

**[[R3022000]] A card's balance tier, and its adjusted win rate read against the field (issue #3022, 2026-11-04)**: the owner asked for one figure on `/stats` to balance every card into a band of 45% to 55%. [[R2777000]]'s adjusted rate read 50% as a drawn delta of 0 rather than as the average card, assumed real cards differ by five points, and shrank a small sample into the band. The cards a page reads now give a field, every deckable card of the report with the sample floor's games on both sides of its drawn delta, from which DerSimonian and Laird's random-effects estimate reads the mean delta and the spread τ of real card lifts (`stats_field`; below 30 such cards, R2777000's centre of 0 and τ of 5 points). The adjusted rate is 50% plus τ²/(τ² + SE²) times the delta less the field's mean, its 95% range [[R2895000]]'s at that τ. Each card with enough games gets a tier: S when its rate is above 55% and its range wholly above 50% (a nerf candidate), F when below 45% and its range wholly below 50% (a buff candidate), and otherwise A, B, C or D by the band's quarters from the top. `GET /api/stats/cards` answers `tier` and the field, and the Cards tab shows a sortable Tier column and a line giving the band and the measured spread. Quantile tiers, a fixed τ, the band's edges as S's and F's test, and a field read from the filtered cards alone were rejected. It takes R3022000, the first number of issue #3022's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3024000]] An emailed link waits for the player's press (issue #3024, 2026-11-04)**: the takeover check on staging found a mail scanner opening a confirmation link 8 seconds after it was mailed, from a datacenter, which confirmed an address nobody had confirmed, and with Google's sign-in a password then outlived the link. An emailed link now opens `/login` and does nothing until its one button is pressed, and only that goes on to the auth provider's verify address, built by the client from the token and type alone.

**[[R3025000]] Linking and unlinking a sign-in provider on the account screen (issue #3025, 2026-11-04)**: a Google account with an address other than the JackiOh account's had no way to join it, since automatic linking matches verified addresses only. `/account` now links each provider the build names, through the auth provider's own linking with the session's token and a PKCE challenge, and unlinks it, never the account's last way in. The owner turned the projects' manual linking on for this.

**[[R3059000]] A second deletion within `UNDO_REPEAT_MS` of the first is ignored (issue #3059, 2026-11-04; amends [[R2119000]])**: in the deck workshop's split view the neighbour deck opens under the pointer after a delete, and a double click on Delete deck then sent the first deletion at once and held the neighbour for deletion too, as [[R2119000]]'s second deletion does; Remove on a friend's row did the same, the next friend moving up under the pointer. `useUndoHold().hold` now answers `false`, sending and holding nothing, for a deletion within `UNDO_REPEAT_MS` (500 ms, on the hook's own timer) of the last one taken, and its callers (deck, trio, friend) then neither hide the item nor move to the next. The guard ends with the hold (Undo, a send at once, `pagehide`, the window's end, leaving the screen), and a refused press does not extend it. Disabling each button, not opening the neighbour, reading `Date.now()`, queueing the second deletion and a guard that outlives Undo were rejected. It takes R3059000, the first number of issue #3059's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R3060000]] One press is sent once per view (issue #3060, 2026-11-04)**: the board's moves stay live until the next view arrives and `net.ts` gives every send a fresh nonce, so a double click sent End turn twice, and a double tap sent a one-tap choice or the mulligan's Ready twice. Usually the second was refused and left "it is not your turn" on the board, but after a card that gave an extra turn ([[R846]]) the same player was still active, so the second End turn was accepted and ended the extra turn unseen. `Game.tsx`'s `dispatch` now drops an `endTurn`, `answer` or `mulligan` equal to the last one sent against the same view; a new view, or an error that gains text, releases it. Nothing the engine decides changed.

**[[R3061000]] An Activate legalActions does not list says why, and the fuzz holds every ability to the listing (issue #3061, 2026-11-05)**: an Activate ability's `usable` came from its own refusal alone, but `legalActions` also drops one with no choice combination, a required pick the board cannot answer ([[R703]]), and one whose every combination costs discards the hand cannot pay ([[R450]], [[R682]]), so such an ability read usable with no reason and its greyed button said only that it could not be activated now; S #112 Job Application's Upload Resume with an empty hand was one. `usable` now holds exactly when an `activate` of it is listed, and otherwise `reason` is its own refusal or `reduce`'s refusal of its first combination, or of the ability sent with no picks ("Job Application cannot be played without 1 legal target"). The fuzz monitor's I15 holds every Activate ability a view shows to the listing, `usable` exactly when an `activate` of it is listed and `reason` exactly when not, read off I6's view and listing as I13 is ([[R2975000]]). Presentation only. It takes R3061000, the first number of issue #3061's block, and edits [[§10.8]].

**[[R3064000]] The AI prices what a Dormant card does when it wakes (issue #3064, 2026-11-04)**: [[R2380000]] priced a Dormant card as the unit it wakes as, a share of it for each turn it waits, and nothing for its "When this awakens" text, so the AI seldom played Stinkyscape's Imprisoned Cosmality or Imprisoned Ship Smith and read an enemy one as its body alone. Once a decision, on a sandbox copy of its probe world, the AI now wakes each Dormant card with a wake trigger on either field, and each such Unit in its own hand, placed first, beside the same wake of its hookless twin, and the card counts `AI_EVAL.awaken` of the difference in its controller's evaluation inside the slumber share, the seat's and the enemy's alike. `AI_EVAL.awaken` is 1.0 and the frozen `GREEDY_EVAL` keeps 0. It reads both fields and the seat's own hand only, so hidden cards change no decision. A weight above 1, a flat premium per trigger and any engine change were rejected. It takes R3064000, the first number of issue #3064's block, and edits [[§6.1]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R3067000]] A patch the server does not ship reads only its own totals, and every kept window and history reads one snapshot of every patch (issue #3067, 2026-11-04)**: `GET /api/stats/cards` and `GET /api/stats/games` take any `?patch=`, and a name the server does not ship (neither in `patches.json` nor the current patch) was computed on every request and never kept ([[R978000]]), off a load of every game total of every patch, though its window is that patch alone ([[R1367000]]): a script looping over made-up names, signed in or not, could make hundreds of those loads a minute on the server that also runs every live match. Every real key missed the same way, one load each, and the kept cache's 256 slots hold fewer than its keys. The windows and the drill-down's history ([[R2491000]]) are now cut from one snapshot of every patch's totals, kept for `CARD_STATS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` and loaded once however many keys miss it, each ending with its snapshot; an unshipped patch reads its own totals alone, still never kept, and takes its list of patches from the kept window of the current patch. No figure changes, only the reads; a report of another pilot, mode or source that misses within the TTL counts the records the snapshot held. Keeping an unshipped patch, raising `CARD_STATS_KEPT_REPORTS` and giving each window its own clock were rejected. It takes R3067000, the first number of issue #3067's block, amends [[R978000]], [[R1367000]] and [[R2491000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3070000]] Every card's adjusted win rate and tier, against the average deck where it has no never-drawn games (issue #3070, 2026-11-04)**: the owner balances every card by its tier ([[R3022000]]), yet the 15 Quickdraw cards, which start in the opening hand ([[R640]]) and so are never left undrawn, and the Dormant Starship, which no deck holds ([[R1857001]]), had no adjusted rate and no tier. The field now holds a baseline, the average card's in-deck win rate over the report. A card with fewer never-drawn games than `CARD_STATS_MIN_SAMPLE` is read against it: 50% plus τ²/(τ² + SE²) times its seen win rate less the baseline, SE² the seen side's alone, with [[R2895000]]'s range and [[R3022000]]'s tier. The Dormant Starship's row reads its Created games, the seats that made one ([[R2089000]]), against the same baseline. `GET /api/stats/cards` answers `tiers.baseline`, and the tiers note says what these cards are read against. An even game as the baseline, a test by the Quickdraw tag, and a new catalog class for the starship were rejected. It takes R3070000, the first number of issue #3070's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3074000]] The dev dashboard: a separate app only a dev account reaches, behind three locks, strict on production, and an audit row for every dev and admin action (issue #3074, 2026-11-04)**: the owner asked for a dev dashboard, a separate admin panel on staging and production that only a dev account can open, "assuming all of this is safe". It is `apps/admin`, at `admin.jackioh.com` and `admin-staging.jackioh.com`, behind Cloudflare Access, the account's own sign-in and the server's `DEV_PROFILE_IDS` check on every route; the server's new `DEV_STRICT` (on for production, off for staging) makes every `/api/admin/*` route need the two-step code. Every dev tool and dashboard write leaves an append-only audit row, and each dev grant is capped by a named constant. The issue's open questions took its own proposals: "dev mode" is the Dev tools as they are, and `/account`'s Dev tools stay.

**[[R3074001]] A dev account may hand-set its own rating; a strict server keeps it off the public ladder until it is reset (issue #3074, 2026-11-04)**: a rating pairs, ranks and moves the percentiles and Jlorious, so on production a hand-set rating takes the account off the public leaderboard, the percentiles and Jlorious until a reset puts back the rating its games had earned; staging sets it freely. Every set and reset writes a rating ledger row marked `dev`. Restoring the season row with the rating was rejected, since a season may open between the two.

**[[R3074002]] The dev dashboard reads the whole ladder, each row marked person or bot (issue #3074, 2026-11-04)**: `GET /api/admin/leaderboard` answers a dev account every row of a ladder with its kind, its rating and a bot's strength and persona, the one exception to [[R1452]] and [[R612]]; every player route, a dev account's included, still carries no kind and no rating.

**[[R3074003]] A dev may post news with no reward from the dashboard (issue #3074, 2026-11-04)**: a reward mints currency for everyone, so a post from the dashboard has none: plain text checked as `news.json`'s entries are, listed beside them for the news days, its claim marking it read and paying nothing, and taken down at will. A post with a reward still goes through a pull request to `news.json`.

**[[R3100000]] The coach names its attacks by the engine's forecast of them, which reads only the board (issue #3100, 2026-11-05)**: `apps/web/src/tutorial/advice.ts` did combat maths of its own (an attack less Armor, nothing through a Divine Shield), which was a rule in the client and which a Roblox port (#2898) would have had to translate. The owner's aim for the tracker (#2956) is that a second client rewrites as little as possible, so the engine answers it: `combat_forecast` (`crates/engine/src/query.rs`) projects, for each attack a seat may declare, the damage it deals and takes, whether it kills, whether the attacker survives and whether it breaks a Divine Shield, and whether the attackers that reach the enemy hero finish it, all from the board's stats through the damage pipeline's own readers. The practice worker asks it of a lesson's game and the coach only picks among the numbers.

**[[R3103000]] Whether a ladder bot passes for a person is a setting of its seat, and a bot's decisions are the match host's machine's (issue #3103, 2026-11-05; amends [[R1452]], [[R1453]] and [[R639000]])**: the owner asked that a Roblox server get ladder bots without porting any bot code, and that what exists only to hide a bot be a switch the server turns on; a bot is a seat of the machine whose moves, pace, aim, emotes, habits and rematches come out as its timers and actions, `hidden` gates what only disguises it (the pace, the aim, the emotes, the concessions and draw answers, the idle turns, the rematch and the fallback time), and a bot that is not hidden plays as the practice AI does.

**[[R3110000]] The Terms and the Privacy Policy are the owner's short, plain-language text (issue #3110, 2026-11-05)**: the owner found the pages, about 520 and 1,800 words, long and machine-written, and replaced them with short text that names data by category and leaves out every field, retention period and implementation detail, which amends what [[R2614000]], [[R640001]], [[R2615000]], [[R2615001]] and [[R757000]] required them to say. The Terms say only when ranked may match a player with an AI opponent and that nothing marks which are AI; the policy says that friends and the opponent's friends can watch online games, that removing or blocking stops it and that player stats are public, and keeps only confirmation of the minimum age. What is collected does not change, so the rulings still hold; `TERMS_VERSION` moves, and every account is asked to agree again.

**[[R3112000]] The learned evaluation reads what each card the seat can name is, beside its id, so a card the network never saw still counts (FEATURES_VERSION 3, issue #3112, 2026-11-05)**: the owner expects hundreds of new cards a month, and the network's sparse features named a card by its id alone, so a card it was not fitted on added nothing until the next refit. Each card the features name by id now also has keys for its type, printed cost, tags, keywords, printed attack and health (clamped at 10) and Radiant face, only where it already had an id key ([[R185]]). Printed numbers were taken over the current ones, which the dense features already hold; rarity and set were left out, since the issue did not list them; one key per stat was taken over one key for attack and health together, which would share less between cards.

**[[R3112001]] A network fitted on an older feature layout, to which this one only added sparse keys, reads it as it was fitted (FEATURES_READ_SINCE, issue #3112, 2026-11-05)**: a refit needs the learn lane's 100,000-game export, about 40 hours on the owner's PC, so the pull request that bumped `FEATURES_VERSION` to 3 shipped no new network. Generation 13, fitted on version 2, has no row for any key version 3 added, so it reads version 3 exactly as it was fitted and the AI plays as before; [[R1010000]]'s test now takes any version from `FEATURES_READ_SINCE` (2) on. Relabeling the shipped weights as version 3 was rejected, since it would misstate what they were fitted on; keeping version 2 was rejected, since the lane would then fold the new keys under the old version and its loader's guard against mixing versions would be lost.

**[[R3112002]] `positions` reports the share of the cards its rows name that the build's network has no row for (issue #3112, 2026-11-05)**: so a refit is asked for by a number rather than by a guess, the lane's fold step prints `unknownShare` in the line its run keeps as `positions.json`. It is measured on the games the lane folds rather than on the catalog, which counts cards nobody plays, and in `positions` rather than in `cargo jackioh gate`, whose games are never folded.

**[[R3114000]] A set is one row of the set table (issue #3114, 2026-11-05)**: at hundreds of cards a month a set lands every month or two, and each one was written into many places: `SetName`, the fixed-size `SHIPPED_SETS` and `CATALOG_SETS`, the cards build's folder list, the deck codes' offsets, `spec check`'s set names, the client's own `SetName` and `SHIPPED_SETS`, and the shadow ban's sets and ceilings. One table in `catalog_types.rs` now holds a row per set, its stage, id segment, abbreviation and shadow-ban share, and every one of those reads it, `SetName` generated from it so the wire and the client's type are unchanged. Rows are only appended, so every set keeps its place in the catalog order and the deck codes, and the table sits in the range the sweep workflow compares. Adding a set touches its row, its census entry and its scripts folder.

**[[R3114001]] A set's shadow-ban ceiling is its share of its cards (issue #3114, 2026-11-05)**: Stinkyscape's ceiling was `STINKYSCAPE_BAN_CEILING = 35`, 20% of the set's 175 non-token cards worked out by hand, which went stale as the set grew to 178. A ceiling is now the set's share in the set table times its non-token cards, rounded down: half for the Meditative sets and Training Grounds, a fifth for Stinkyscape, still 35. No ceiling changes.

**[[R3119000]] The 404 pages are a small game: a lost card that speaks, a path that glitches and settles, and three face-down ways out (issue #3119, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked for fun, dynamic 404 pages that use the assets the client already ships. The client's 404 panel shows a lost card picked by a hash of the path, which says its voice line when tapped, names the path in Glitch's glyphs that settle into it, and deals three face-down cards that turn over into Play, Practice and the Almanac; the static page fans three of the brand's card backs into 4, 0 and 4 in CSS alone. Reduce motion leaves everything dealt, face up and settled.

**[[R3127000]] Cloudflare's 404 is the client, with a script-free fallback whose hand is three placeholder card faces: Error 4, Not 0, Found 4 (issue #3127, 2026-11-05; supersedes the static page of [[R3119000]])**: the owner asked that the page Cloudflare serves for a path nothing routes boot the client's playable 404 and that its script-free fallback draw three real card faces, since the hand-drawn rectangles looked like nothing in the game; the build writes `404.html` from `index.html` (entry script, `noindex`, the 404 title, no canonical), its fallback is never hidden and holds the tavern board, the stacked logo, the game's fonts and the button home, the hand's markup is written from the art builders `CardArt` calls and held to `CardFace`'s render by a test (react-dom/server cannot render `CardFace`), and the client's own `NotFound` keeps no hand.

**[[R3147000]] Roblox signs in as Supabase's custom OIDC provider `custom:roblox`, which names no address (issue #3147, 2026-11-05)**: the owner set Roblox up in both projects as a custom provider, since Supabase has none built in, and asked for a sign-in with Roblox button; the identifier is the name the build and the client use, and Roblox gives third-party apps no email address, so a Roblox sign-in never matches an older account by one. It extends [[R666]] and [[R3025000]].

**[[R3147001]] An account with no address adds one on the code screen, as R663's email change (issue #3147, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked that an account be able to add an email afterwards; §9.4 step 1 still asks for a verified address before a code, so the code screen asks a Roblox sign-up for one where it would ask for a confirmation, and R663's change confirms it.

**[[R3147002]] A pending account a provider made can be removed so the provider links to the account the player had (issue #3147, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked for linking with existing accounts; a provider's identity can be linked to only one account, so the new pending account (which holds nothing) is deleted as "Delete my account" deletes, and the device remembers the provider to point the next sign-in at `/account`'s Link. Supabase cannot move an identity from one user to another, which rules out merging the two.

**[[R3147003]] The username prompt starts from the provider's username (issue #3147, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked for usernames picked automatically; the prompt after activation stays ([[R1435]]), its field holding the provider's `preferred_username` and asking the preview at once, so the server still judges every name.

**[[R3156000]] The Dev tools open from a Dev button on the front screen, for a dev account on a computer alone, in a dialog; the account page has none (issue #3156, 2026-11-05; amends [[R1300000]])**: the owner asked for the dev panel to be its own button on the front screen, shown to a dev account and scoped for a computer; the button sits in the landing's top bar when the server says `isDev` and the window is no phone's or tablet's (at least `DEV_PANEL_MIN_WIDTH_PX` wide, its main pointer not coarse), opens the same Dev tools in a modal dialog, and `/account` drops its toggle.

**[[R3156001]] Unlock all cosmetics: POST /api/dev/cosmetics grants every cosmetic the fill grants and nothing else (issue #3156, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked for a button to get all cosmetics; the fill also gives every card and tops every proficiency, so a new dev route grants its cosmetics alone through the fill's own grant, marked `dev`, audited, refused to a non-dev and writing nothing the second time.

**[[R3184000]] /stats' Cards tab shows no When drawn, Drawn vs not drawn, Radiant or Normal (issue #3184, 2026-11-05)**: the adjusted win rate ([[R2777000]]) is the page's headline read of a card's strength, so the drawn win rate, the drawn delta and the face rates beside it had become noise. The Cards tab's table drops the four columns with their show/hide toggles and sorts, the grid its Radiant and Normal figures, and the row's "not enough data" goes with the drawn delta; an address that sorts by one of them opens on the default sort, as [[R2446000]]'s old sorts do. `GET /api/stats/cards`, the client's wire types, the card's stats block, the reports and the drill-down's Show Radiant face are unchanged. Presentation only. It takes R3184000, the first number of issue #3184's block, amends [[R1147000]] and [[R2427000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3184001]] A card's tier fit on /stats: how much of its Adjusted range lies inside its tier (issue #3184, 2026-11-05)**: a tier ([[R3022000]]) said where a card's adjusted rate sits, not how firmly. With Show 95% ranges on, each card's tier now shows beneath it the share of its Adjusted 95% range ([[R2895000]]) inside its tier's band, to a whole percent: S from the band's top up, F from its bottom down, A to D its quarters from the top, a range of no width wholly in or out, and none below the floor or without a tier, a range or a band. Sorting by tier orders a tier's cards by it, the higher first descending and a card with none after, then by the adjusted rate, whether or not the ranges show. The client computes it from the server's figures, interval arithmetic and no statistic; the probability inside the band read off a normal was rejected as a statistic the client should not compute. It takes R3184001, the second number of issue #3184's block, amends [[R2895000]] and [[R3022000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3184002]] /stats' Created cards table sorts by any of its columns (issue #3184, 2026-11-05)**: the Created cards table ([[R2089000]]) kept the server's order alone. Each of its headers now sorts it, the first press descending (the card's name ascending) and the next flipping it, with an arrow and `aria-sort` on the sorted header; a figure the row does not show, below [[R654]]'s floor or withheld, sorts after the rows with one either way, ties keep the server's order, and the table keeps the server's order until a header is pressed. The sort is not kept in the address. Presentation only. It takes R3184002, the third number of issue #3184's block, amends [[R2089000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3191000]] Claim all is one spend: one request claims every news entry the account has not claimed, in one transaction whose statements do not grow with the entries, each entry decided and paid as its own claim would be, and its one answer moves the list and the count (pull request #3191, 2026-11-05)**: the owner found Claim all laggy. [[R2982000]] had sent it as one claim per entry, one after another, each its own request and transaction, so about fifty live entries cost fifty requests and about a thousand database statements. `POST /api/news/claim-all` is one spend in the account's turn, under its lock and an optional `Idempotency-Key` of its own route; it takes no list, and claims every entry live now and every dev post standing now that the account has not claimed, the test one claim puts to one entry. The claims go in one statement, whose key decides what is paid, and the rewards in one statement per ledger, each entry still its own ledger row with the reason `news`. The client sends one request and its answer replaces the list. Claims sent side by side, an id list from the client and all or nothing were rejected. It takes R3191000, the first number of pull request #3191's block, amends [[R2982000]] and edits [[§14.13]].

**[[R3015000]] A card's look is a variant, one picture, and each variant a ladder of levels, its art levels first, then animated, then signed above a full art alone; the seven one-art versions get no full-art level, a Legacy card's ladders stop before animated, Alt art's offer takes Alt full art's cards too, and the Animated and Signature augments retire (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R2725000]], [[R2725001]], [[R2725003]], [[R2940000]], [[R2940002]], [[R2942000]], [[R2942002]], [[R2943000]], [[R2943002]], [[R2944000]], [[R2944002]] and [[R2949001]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-04 that a card's looks become variants, each a ladder of levels, each level the one below it plus one thing: the variant's art in its normal frame, then its full art where it has one, then animated, then signed above an animated full art, owning a level owning every level below it, foils, finishes and borders apart, and nobody losing anything. Every version entry gains `level: { variant, rank, adds }`, the Default variant's first level the normal face, and #3015 appends fifteen levels; the registry's check holds every ladder to that shape and every level to its variant's first level's offer, the Default's every non-token card. The issue left open which variants get a full art: Full art, Alt art (Alt full art already was one) and the Sketch do, and the seven one-art versions do not, since Extended art and Out of frame already break the frame, Immersive and Prismatic already fill and move it, and Pixel, Comic and Set showcase restyle the picture in their own frame. A moving level is never offered on a Legacy card, whose picture never moves; Alt art's offer became the union of its own and Alt full art's, so no card loses either; and the two augments retire with the full arts, each row still owning the levels it maps to.

**[[R3015001]] A level's rarity: a variant's first level, and every level that was a version before, keeps its rarity; each new level is a tier rarer than the level below it, up to Mythic, and a signed level is always Mythic (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R2725000]], [[R2942000]], [[R2943000]] and [[R2944000]])**: the issue left each level's rarity to the plan. Every new level steps one tier above the level below it and stays at Mythic, a signed level is Mythic, and the check refuses a level not rarer than the one below unless Mythic; the Default's Animated takes the Animated augment's Epic, so the normal face's motion is bought and pulled as before, and the Sketch's full art is Epic, one above the Sketch's Rare. Thirteen of the fifteen new levels are chases and nine are Mythic, which no token buys. Rejected: every animated level Mythic, which makes the commonest motion the top chase, and every one Epic, under which a level would be commoner than the art it needs.

**[[R3015002]] Every level of a variant draws its first level's picture, a level adding only the full-art frame, its picture's own motion or a signature; R2949000's Signature look is gone and nothing is redrawn; the Sketch's full art draws the sketch across the face (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R2725003]], [[R2725004]], [[R2940001]], [[R2940002]], [[R2942001]], [[R2942002]], [[R2943001]], [[R2943002]], [[R2944001]], [[R2944002]] and [[R2949000]])**: the owner asked that the art be the same within a variant, every level drawing exactly the same picture. So a signed level is its variant's full art, moving by the card's own motion, with the name signed in the signature's silver, and the Signature look's own picture and motion (`"signature"`, `SIGNATURE_MOTION`) are removed; a retired Mythic shows Full art's signed level, the card's own picture full bleed. The builder drew the Sketch's full art as the normal picture under the Sketch's filter at the full-art shape. Foils, finishes and borders layer on every level, each variant's frame rules hold at every level, and no level wears Radiant's gold.
