**[[R3015003]] A pull grants a variant's next level; where a level past the next is open at the rolled tier, one draw below TIER_WEIGHT_TOTAL comes first, and below LEVEL_SKIP_WEIGHT the pull grants such a level with every level below it (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R1301002]] and [[R1306000]])**: the owner asked that pulls grant the next level in order and that a separate, far rarer draw may grant a level past the next together with every level below it. A duplicate's cosmetic and each tier a cosmetic pack slot tries take the extra draw only where an out-of-order level is open, so nothing else draws differently; `LEVEL_SKIP_WEIGHT` is 40 in `TIER_WEIGHT_TOTAL`'s 1000, and a seeded simulation holds out-of-order pulls to `LEVEL_SKIP_MAX_PERCENT_OF_IN_ORDER`, 10 percent of in-order ones. Both odds `GET`s list the chance as `levelSkip`, and the Purchases page shows it.

**[[R3015004]] A cosmetic token or the Chase Shard bar buys only a variant's next level: aimed past it, it is a 400 that writes and spends nothing (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R1305003]] and [[R1306002]])**: the owner asked that tokens and the Chase Shard buy only the next level. A request for a level some level below which the account does not own on that card answers `"that level needs every level below it owned first"`, after the retired and offer checks' 400s and before every 409, and a retired full art or augment answers `"that cosmetic is retired"`; the Collection offers a held token only on a next level.

**[[R3015005]] A card's pick names one level in `fullArt`, and every older stored pick maps to the level it drew (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R2655000]], [[R2725000]] and [[R2949000]])**: the owner asked that a card's pick name one owned level and that old ids still resolve, so frozen loadouts and replays still draw. `fullArt` is `false`, `true` (Full art's first level on a full-art card) or a level's id, offered and owned on the card; a retired full art's id and a non-empty `augments` are 400s, and any write of `fullArt` clears the augments held. An older pick maps to the level it drew: a retired full art to the highest level it maps to, then Animated and Signature each one rank up where the next level adds it, so R2949000's Signature look is Full art's third level. The issue left Alt art and the Sketch with Animated open: each stays at its first level, since the level above it is a full art, and the normal-frame Alt art and Sketch no longer move. A match freezes one level by its id and no augments.

**[[R3015006]] Retired entries count as owning the levels they map to, and migration 0084 writes every combination an account owns as those levels, inserting only; Signature without both Full art and Animated owns no level (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R2655000]] and [[R2725002]])**: the owner asked that nobody lose anything. Every retired full art and the Animated augment own their levels by reading, as R2725002 read them, and migration 0084 writes the fifteen new `public.cosmetics` rows and, per account and card, the levels the full art, animated and signed flags read off the held rows cover, never a moving level on a Legacy card, each with a `cosmetic_grants` row (reason `launch`, ref `levels:0084`). Signature was only ever worn beside Full art and Animated, so without both it owns no level; its row stays, and whether to compensate its owner is the owner's call. The migration rewrites ownership, so its pull request asks a person to merge it.

**[[R3015007]] The Collection shows a card's variants as ladders, each level owned, next or locked, a locked level past the next naming what it needs and only the next offered a held token (issue #3015, 2026-11-05; amends [[R2725000]] and [[R2949000]])**: the issue left the Collection's layout to the plan. Each variant is a section of rungs, lowest first, the Default's first the normal face; a click wears an owned rung on that card, a locked one opens its preview, and a locked rung past the next shows what it needs. The version and augment strip is gone, and the strip keeps the foil, the finish and the border.

**[[R3016001]] A card's equip and buy screen, opened by Equip or buy on its hover or fullscreen preview, lists its variant ladders: an owned level is equipped, the next is bought with a held token that covers it or a full Chase Shard bar after a question naming what it spends, Buy is disabled saying what is missing, and a later level names what it needs; hovering a level shows it in the three views (issue #3016, 2026-11-05)**: the second part of #3016, built on #3015's ladders ([[R3015007]]). The owner asked for an equip and buy screen beside the preview, where buying prompts for a token or such. Equip wears an owned level ([[R3015005]]); Buy, on the next level alone ([[R3015004]]), asks as the Chase Shard's purchase does ([[R2683000]]): the level, what it spends, a choice of each held token that covers it and, for a chase, the full bar, then Confirm or Cancel. Nothing that pays disables Buy with what is missing, a card short of `MAX_COPIES` among it, and a refusal shows the server's words and changes nothing. The screen is fullscreen and modal, no list or grid, so no list carries a spend button ([[R1953001]]). Hovering a level here or on the card view's ladders opens [[R3016000]]'s hover preview wearing it. It takes R3016001, the next number of issue #3016's block, and amends [[R2730000]] and [[R3015007]].

**[[R3038000]] to [[R3038002]] make Starship a tag with Flying and Ranged (issue #3038, part 1 of #3000, 2026-11-04)**: the owner's pass over the Stinkyscape set turns Starship from a keyword a launched Starship took into a tag the Dormant Starship carries, and gives every card that holds it Flying and Ranged in the keyword layer, Dormant or launched, the entry printing the two as well; the hull, its memory and every "your Starship" read stay as they were, and #115 Scornboi spares the tag ([[R3038000]]). The launch, which costs (4) from this patch, gives no keyword, and the Starship keyword stays only for the shipped snapshots that print it and the replays folded under them ([[R3038001]]). The Drone Battleship's layers print Flying and Ranged where they printed the Starship keyword: no Starships, never launched nor fused into, and Scornboi exiles them ([[R3038002]]).

**[[R3038003]] A decision that holds a Starship scores its finalists by the hand evaluation alone (issue #3038, part 1 of #3000, 2026-11-05)**: the tag's Flying and Ranged failed the AI's removal test of [[R2566000]], which prefers the vanilla over the Starship Piece when it has one removal, and the first build blamed those two keywords. They are part of it: the hand evaluation still prefers the vanilla by 12 to 15 points in every determinization, but the network that corrects it, fitted before any Starship, moved the vanilla's line by −23.7 and the piece's by +3.3, −8.6 and +8.3, mostly for the crystals the enemy holds after the reply, which the launch spends and a hand the AI cannot see does not. A decision whose root holds a Starship or a Starship Piece now keeps the hand evaluation, which prices what the piece fuses into ([[R3038003]]).

**[[R3039000]] and [[R3039001]] make an awakening for good and keep a Flashback spent (issue #3039, part 2 of #3000, 2026-11-04)**: the owner's pass over the Stinkyscape set ends the "once" of a Dormant card. An awakened card is never Dormant again: every awakening marks it (`CardInstance.awakened`, kept in every zone and through [[R78]]'s and [[R766]]'s resets), so played again from its graveyard, Reborn, returned to a hand and replayed or summoned again it enters awake, while a card that left the field still Dormant is Dormant again; an awakened Dormant Starship that returns stands awake with a fresh, empty memory, unlaunched, and the next piece to die fuses into a Dormant Starship, a new one if there is none, rather than launching it again with nothing fused ([[R3039000]], amending [[R1839000]]). The Flashback half was undone on the owner's word (2026-11-05), once a (0) Flashback card made a free board wipe repeatable in one turn: a play by the card's own Flashback still spends it, so a card is flashed back once, and [[R783013]] stands ([[R3039001]]). It takes R3039000 and R3039001, the first numbers of issue #3039's block, and edits [[§6.1]] Dormant and Flashback and [[§6.3]] Play.

**[[R1862003]] is amended: the Rush Token Launcher's Activate is offered while its only free unit zone is Locked; and every Weapon's Health is halved (issue #3040, part 3 of #3000, 2026-11-04)**: the owner's pass over the Stinkyscape set halves the Health of the sixteen Weapons (Rare 0/4 → 0/2, Radiant 0/8 → 0/4; Epic 0/6 → 0/3, 0/12 → 0/6; Legendary 0/8 → 0/4, 0/16 → 0/8), card data only, Attack staying 0 and the Radiant faces still about twice their base ([[R275]]); an Equip sums stats into its host ([[R1086]]), so a host's Health after one drops with it. The same part lets the Rush Token Launcher into a Locked zone: its Activate's gate read `open_zones`, which leaves a Locked zone out, while the summon it makes with no lane named already enters the leftmost open zone and else the leftmost empty Locked one ([[R688]], `first_entry_zone`), so the gate refused an Activate whose summon would have landed. The gate now reads `first_entry_zone` too, and the Activate is offered, and its Rush Token summoned into the Locked zone and forced to attack, while the only free unit zone is Locked; a row of Units in every zone still leaves it not offered ([[R430]]). The card's words say nothing of zones and do not change. The old gate is kept as `script_v0_5_11o` for the replays folded under the versions through v0.5.11r ([[R767000]]). No new ruling: it takes R688's Hearthstone-semantics reading of the Lock and edits [[R1862003]], [[§8.10]] rows 50 to 61 and 140 to 143.

**[[R3041000]] makes Asphyxiants Cloud deal its damage before it gives the next Spell Poisonous, and amends [[R1878002]], [[R1850005]] and [[R1869000]] (issue #3041, part 4 of #3000, 2026-11-04)**: the owner's pass over the Stinkyscape set changes eight cards' numbers and words, with no engine change. Jlockeed's Missile Pod rounds its Attack ÷ divisor up ([[R1878002]]: a 2/1 Pod now hits for 1); Circadian Fullcycler costs (4); Rod of Ages loses its Quickdraw tag and its Flying and becomes Radiant at 12 keywords, Animated counting among them ([[R1850005]], [[R1869000]]); Drone Hanger costs (3) and its start-of-turn summon is an Activate, once a turn ([[R384]]); ZolarKeth is 2/7 → 4/14 and its Cry's second option gives an End of turn that summons the random (Radiant) Starship Piece at its controller's end of turn ([[R62]]), where it gave an Activate; Ultra Capacitor costs (1); The Propagation's Hornet is 3/3 → 6/6; and Asphyxiants Cloud prints Flashback in place of Poisonous, deals 1 (Radiant 2) damage to all enemies, gives the next Spell Poisonous and draws no card nor, Radiant, repeats with Echo. The issue left one thing to decide, the Cloud's order, and its words put the damage first; it takes R3041000, the first number of issue #3041's block. Each card whose script changed keeps the script it replaces as `script_v0_5_11o` (Missile Pod, Rod of Ages, Drone Hanger, ZolarKeth's third and Asphyxiants Cloud), which `KEPT_SCRIPTS` names in the reconcile, so a replay on v0.5.11r or before folds as it was played ([[R767000]]). It edits [[§6.1]]'s Radiant-at-N-keywords row and §8.10's rows 13, 16, 26, 47, 92, 129, 133 and 168.

**[[R3042000]] makes In Too Deep's quests about a fifth harder, quest 10 half as much again, and amends [[R404]], [[R540]], [[R541]] and [[R542]] (issue #3042, part 5 of #3000, 2026-11-04)**: the owner's pass over five older cards changes their numbers and words, with no engine change. In a hurry costs (3) and summons 4 Rush Tokens (Radiant ones too); MC Tech steals when the opponent controls 5 or more permanents; Jlockeed's J15 Fighter has Can't Defend on both faces again, a catalog keyword with no script of its own ([[R2657000]]); Doom Shroom costs (4). In Too Deep's goals were the one decision the issue left open: each is its old one times 1.2 rounded up, at least one more (2 → 3 draws, 2 → 3 kills, 4 → 5 permanents, 3 → 4 and 5 → 6 mana floated, 15 → 18 damage, totals of 13 → 16, 3 → 4 exiles), and quest 10, the last before reward M, asks 9 Units in the graveyard (from 6; the rule alone would give 8). The table is in [[R3042000]]; the script that ran through v0.5.11r is kept as `script_v0_5_11o` for the replays that played under it ([[R767000]]).

**[[R3043000]] gives True Craft a Card about 20% fewer points and amends [[R880]] and [[R883]] (issue #3043, part 6 of #3000, 2026-11-04)**: the owner's pass asked that Meditative #17 True Craft a Card craft with about 20% fewer points, and it alone. Meditative+ #26 Scratch Pad uses the same unframed budget, so the budget is keyed on the `craft` prompt's mode, not changed at its constants. True Craft's prompt has no mode and gets `TRUE_CRAFT_POINTS_PERCENT` (80) of the crafter's `2 + 5 ×` the cost, to the nearest whole point: 2, 6, 10, 14 and 18 at costs 0 to 4, where it had 2, 7, 12, 17 and 22. Scratch Pad's `scratch` prompt keeps the whole. The issue left the rounding open. Rounding to the nearest point lands closest to "about 20%" (17% across the five budgets, where rounding down takes 25% and halves a cost-0 craft). The answer check, the editor's preview, the offer and the presets all read the mode's budget, and a Potion's 4 points still sit below the crafter's at its cost ([[R1563008]]). It edits [[§6.3]]'s Craft row and [[§8.8]]'s row 17.

**[[R3044000]] makes Twinspell an Activate whose grant is its player's for the turn, and amends [[R30]] and [[R209]] (issue #3044, part 7 of #3000, 2026-11-05)**: Twinspell was a lasting grant, an `echoNextSpell` rider its permanent installed standing on the field, which waited past cleanup and buried its Twinspell when a Spell took it. It is now "Activate: The next Spell you play this turn gains Echo +1. Then this goes to the graveyard.", and its Radiant face the same without the graveyard, so it serves once each turn; `echoGain` is 1 on both faces (Radiant 2 before). The Activate is once a turn ([[R384]]) and puts a rider with no source on its controller, ending at this turn's cleanup. The issue left the verb of "goes to the graveyard" to be ruled: no verb moved a card from the field to the graveyard without a death, so the engine gains one, `send_to_graveyard`, a plain move that is no death and that Indestructible does not hold. It takes R3044000, the first number of issue #3044's block. The engine keeps the lasting grant for the replays of v0.5.11r and before, which fold under the kept `script_v0_5_11o` that `KEPT_SCRIPTS` names in the reconcile ([[R767000]]); [[R30]] is retitled as that grant and [[R209]] names Twinspell as its example through v0.5.11r. It edits §8.3's row 79.

**[[R3045000]] makes a fused Miner or Weapon keep its host's art and a fused Dormant Starship keep its name (issue #3045, part 8 of #3000, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's pass over the Stinkyscape set asked that a Unit with a Weapon or a Miner equipped keep its own picture, and that a Starship that takes in pieces stay "Dormant Starship". The engine's Fuse unions the ingredients' tags and joins their names, and the client drew from what it was handed, so a fused card wore a weapon's or a drill's art and a fused hull read "Dormant Starship + Miner v1 + …". `<CardArt>` now resolves the card whose art it draws: when the fused definition's ingredients hold a Miner (Stinkyscape #62 to #66) or a Weapon-tagged card, it draws the last ingredient that is neither, the host an Equip is fused onto, from that card's id, name and tags, nested fusions walked to their leaves and a digest id ([[R468]]) that hides them leaving the art as it was. A face that the view says is a Starship, and a fused definition that holds the hull where no view does (the log's fused line, the showcase), shows the name it launched under or the hull's catalog name, in Chinese for a reader of Chinese. Both are the client's: the engine's definition keeps its joined name and union of tags, since a rule can read them, and a Starship in play keeps its own art ([[R2249000]]). The change is a Cards micro of no family ([[R2529000]]). It edits no section and amends no ruling: [[R2249000]]'s Starship art is as it was.

**[[R3046000]] prints full art on the board and amends [[R1017001]] (issue #3046, part 9 of #3000, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's pass asked that a card which prints full art in a hand look full-art on the board too, its name below the art. [[R1017001]] had kept "a compact face and a board minion" to their windows, an oval portrait with the name plate over its rim and a framed window. A Unit's `MinionFace` and a face-up backrow card's compact `CardFace` now ask the full face's own decision (`boardLookOf`: the Full-art cards setting, ownership [[R1313000]], the tiers [[R2655000]], a listed token's parent [[R2655002]], the Full art and Alt full art versions [[R2725000]] [[R2940001]], a lesson's board [[R1108000]]) and, where it says full art, lay the art across the face, still, with the name in a band below it: the Unit's plate at the foot of the panel with the attack and health gems on the art's lower corners, a backrow card's band 24% of the face. The tiers' motion and the augments stay on the full faces ([[R2715000]]). The art covers what stood behind the oval, so Taunt wears a steel ring over the art's edge and the Stack keeps its chip, while the veils fill the art and Divine Shield's bubble glows along its edge. The compact face has a type line no longer when it is full art, as no full-art face does. The issue left open whether a prop should limit it to the board; the compact face is drawn the same wherever it is, so none does. It edits [[R1017001]]'s sentence on the board and the comment on `showsFullArt`.

**[[R3047000]] to [[R3047004]] give the Marketplace four offers a turn at a new price curve, sell every card at (0), roll some offers Buffed or Nerfed and show a Resource's quote (issue #3047, part 10 of #3000, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's pass over the Stinkyscape set (issue #3000, section 3) reprices and widens the shop. A roll draws `MARKETPLACE_OFFERS_PER_TURN` (4, was 3) offers, and a card of printed cost c is priced 5 + 10 × min(c, 4) + 15 × max(c − 4, 0) Credits, a (0) card 8: 8, 15, 25, 35, 45, 60, 75, 90, then 15 more a mana, twice that Radiant, where it was 10 a mana to (4), 5 past it and 5 for (0). `MARKETPLACE_CREDITS_PER_MANA` stays 10, which the AI reads by name ([[R3047000]], amending [[R2250002]] and [[R1853002]]). The card a buy adds costs `MARKETPLACE_BOUGHT_COST`, (0): a cost override set before it reaches the hand, which a graveyard or exile resets to the printed cost as it resets any ([[R3047001]], amending [[R1853003]] and [[R766]]'s reach). After its Radiant roll an offer is Buffed at `MARKETPLACE_BUFF_CHANCE` (0.2), rolled again while it lands, and only with no Buff Nerfed at `MARKETPLACE_NERF_CHANCE` (0.1), the same, never both; the issue left the bound and the rounding to the builder, who decided: the repeat stops at `MARKETPLACE_TUNE_MAX` (8) with no draw past it, so the cheapest lot, 8, Nerfed 8 times still costs 2, and the price is the designer's price, Radiant doubled, times 120% per Buff or 85% per Nerf, compounding, rounded down once at the end (three Buffs of 8 are 13, where rounding each step gives 12), then the spread. Additive steps, per-step rounding and rounding to the nearest are rejected. The offer stores `buffs` and `nerfs`, absent while 0, and the view shows them; a lot an effect added is never rolled, and its draws are unchanged ([[R3047002]]). A buy lands each Buff as one Upgrade, as Book of Buff's, and each Nerf as one Degrade sparing the cost row (`degrade_sparing_cost`), so the card stays at (0), after `marketBought`, `marketCreditsChanged` and `addedToHand`, each reported as any Buff or Nerf is and hidden from the other seat ([[R3047003]]). A Resource in its owner's hand shows what it sells for this turn while the Marketplace is open, the Marketplace's quote for its face, as an R280 preview labeled "Credits" which the client prints as `{N}`; a `handSale` card, sold from the shop's Sell button, shows none ([[R3047004]], extending [[R280]]). It takes R3047000 to R3047004, the first numbers of issue #3047's block, edits [[§6.3]] Credits and Marketplace, and the Marketplace lines of [[§10.1]], [[§10.7]], [[§10.8]] and [[§10.10]]; the draw order is in §10.7's Marketplace line. R2250002's and R1853002's titles and R1853001, R1853003, R1853005, R1881006, R2250001 and R2521009 are amended where they name the old figures.

**[[R3048000]] to [[R3048002]] give Echo The Coin's text before any Spell, and Death by 1000 cuts X from the mana left and one target (issue #3048, part 11 of #3000, 2026-11-05)**: the Echo and Death by 1000 cuts rows of #3000's balance pass, which needed the engine. [[R3048000]]: Echo reads "This has the text of the last Spell either player played, or of The Coin if none has been", where it had no text, did nothing and recorded nothing when no Spell had been played. The issue left to be ruled what Echo then records as played, The Coin or nothing; it records The Coin, base face, as the Spell it copied, so `lastSpell` is never left null by a Spell play and a second Echo copies The Coin too, never itself, and the fallback is Echo's own (`staticFlags.copyFallback`), so the fixture copiers that have none still have no text. It amends [[R399]], [[R546]] and [[R547]]. [[R3048001]]: Death by 1000 cuts reads "X is {echo} times your mana left after paying for this", `unspent_mana_of` read at [[§10.5]] step 4 after its own (1) is paid, where [[R802]] had it from the max mana, so cast with exactly 1 mana it cuts once; a cast pays nothing and reads the mana its caster holds. [[R3048002]]: every Echo repeat of Death by 1000 cuts hits the first resolution's target with no new prompt, through the new script field `echoKeepsTargets`, and a pick that has died, left the field or come back by Reborn fizzles the repeats left, which ask nothing and never fall back to another Unit; it amends [[R81]] and [[R802]], and edits §6.2's Echo X row, §10.5 step 6 and the catalog rows of C #57 and M #5. The engine keeps both cards' scripts as they were for the replays of v0.5.11r and before (`script_v0_5_11o`, [[R767000]]), which `KEPT_SCRIPTS` names in the reconcile.

**[[R3134000]] to [[R3134002]] give Last Hurrah a counted discard, Joint Filing a pool of turn hooks and Heroic Power a Cry that Discovers its power (issue #3134, part 1 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's "Stinkyscape Balance Changes IV" (issue #3128) changes three cards that each needed a small piece of the engine. C #37 Last Hurrah no longer discards the whole hand but 6 cards, at the end of this turn (Radiant: of its controller's next): the delayed discard of the hand takes an optional count, kept in the entry, that discards that many random cards through the path of every "discard N" ([[R682]]), each card a discard that C #64 Malzahar's Recycler sees, all of a smaller hand, and an entry with no count the whole hand as before; the 6 is a declared number, `discard`, fewer being better ([[R3134000]], amending [[R458]]). M #9 Joint Filing gains a Cry that adds a random card with a Start of turn or an End of turn effect to the hand, and its Radiant face one card with each, a Start of turn one and then an End of turn one, not one with both: the catalog query gains `turnHook`, read off the registered base-face script as `tribute` is, over the non-token cards of every set that ships ([[R3134001]]). #98 Heroic Power's "Start of game: Gain one of 13 random powers" becomes "Cry: Discover one of 13 powers": a Choose one of 3 different powers, captioned by their titles, whose answer is the card's power, the other seat reading only that a prompt is open; a copy in a hand or a library has no power until it is played, and one that reaches the field with no Cry (Summoned, Recruited, fused, Reborn, Flickered) keeps R151's arrival roll so it is never dead ([[R3134002]], amending [[R43]], [[R151]] and [[R752]]). No card prints "Start of game" after this; its §6.2 row stays as engine vocabulary. Each card keeps its old script as `script_v0_5_11o`, which the reconcile adds to `KEPT_SCRIPTS`, so a replay played through v0.5.11r still folds ([[R767000]]). They take R3134000 to R3134002, the first numbers of issue #3134's block, and edit [[§6.2]] and [[§6.3]], and the rows of #98 in [[§8.5]], of C #37 in [[§8.6]] and of M #9 in [[§8.8]].

**[[R3135000]] nerfs Call to Chaos (Training-Grounds Edition) entry by entry, and makes its ninth entry add a Call at its printed price at the start of each of its caster's turns, amending [[R2200000]] to [[R2200003]] (issue #3135, part 2 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's list for TG #39 changes eight of its ten entries. Refresh 4 → 3 (entry 2); entries 3 and 6 add their cards at (1), not (0), and gain 2 and 1 mana (after the cards are added, with `gain_mana`, whatever a full hand burned); mana 8 → 6 (entry 4); permanents 6 → 5 (entry 5); Rush Tokens 5 → 3 (entry 7); the Fortify, Golem and recast entries are unchanged. Entry 9 reads "At the start of every turn, add a Call to Chaos to your hand", which the owner confirmed in its literal form: nothing is added when the card resolves, and its caster gets a rest-of-game start-of-turn effect that adds a random Call to Chaos at its printed (4) at the start of each of their own turns, an extra turn one of them ([[R846]]), up to the cap of 3 effects and a roll at the cap doing nothing as before. `CHAOS_TG_COST` goes, the cards' price being `CHAOS_TG_CARD_COST` and the Calls' their own; `CHAOS_TG_EVERY` is 1, and the badge and the entry's `label` are one clause (`CHAOS_TG_ALTERNATE_LABEL`). The other reading, "every turn, free, and one now", was rejected as a buff in a pass that lowers every other entry. The card's script does not change and its numbers are the engine's, so no script is kept: a replay of a game that rolled a changed entry is refused by its final hash ([[R767000]]). It edits [[§8.11]] row 39 and the four notes it amends.

**[[R3135001]] tunes three modes of Call to Chaos (Meditative Edition) and three of (Scape Edition) down one step, amending [[R1242]], [[R1243]] and [[R1882008]] (issue #3135, part 2 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the owner asked for "a few percent" off each by tuning a few of its modes down and gave no figures, and the repo holds no per-mode win-rate data, so each edition has three modes cut by one step, the ones that hand over the most material for a roll, under the rule that every nerf is a number in `config.rs`, at most four modes, none to zero. M #95: the hand fusion adds 1 copy, both cards at (0) (`CHAOS_MED_FUSE_COPIES` 2 → 1); the hero gains 5 Armor and heals 5 (6 → 5); 2 Acclaimed permanents are summoned (3 → 2). S #151: 2 Drone Corvettes (3 → 2); 3 Starship Pieces added to the hand (4 → 3); 3 Pieces fused into the Dormant Starship before it launches (4 → 3). Were the ten modes worth the same, each card loses about 8 percent of a roll's material. The labels the roll names, the printed texts, [[§8.8]] row 95 and [[§8.10]] row 151 say the new numbers; no script is kept, as for TG #39: a replay of a game that rolled a changed mode is refused by its final hash ([[R767000]]).

**[[R3135002]] lowers each of C+ #40 Appropriations' four options by one more point and floors every number at 1 (issue #3135, part 2 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: Military +(2X−1) → +(2X−2) Attack (Radiant 4X−2 → 4X−3), Education 2X−1 → 2X−2 Books (4X → 4X−1), Culture 7X% → 6X% (17X% → 16X%), Healthcare +(2X−1) → +(2X−2) Health (5X → 5X−1), the Armor staying X and 2X, as issues #1149 and #1704 each took one point. At X = 1 the base 2X−2 would be 0, so no number reads below 1 and the options do at X = 1 what they did. The script through v0.5.11r is kept as `script_v0_5_11o`, beside `script_v0_4_0f` and `script_v0_3_7b` ([[R767000]]). It amends the paragraph of issue #1704 that tuned the four options and edits [[§8.7]] row 40.

**[[R3138000]] has a Flashback play of an embiggen card offered at both of its prices (issue #3138, part 4 of 7 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's Stinkyscape Balance Changes IV (issue #3128) gives M+ #5 Spirit Lash, "(0) embiggen (1)", Flashback on both faces. [[R783012]] says a card with Flashback is played from the graveyard "at its current cost (X chosen at the play)" and [[R783013]] spends the keyword as the card is played by it ([[R3039001]]), but neither says which price an embiggen card pays from there. A decision the spec does not make follows Hearthstone semantics, where a card with more than one way to be played offers each of them wherever it is played from, so the card is offered at (0) and Embiggened at (1), and a play at either price spends its Flashback. The engine's price list already does this, so no code changes; the ruling records it and Spirit Lash's tests prove it. It edits §6.1's rows for Flashback, Tradable and Divine Shield (the cards that print them) and §6.3's Play row, and §8's rows and the Radiant audit follow the other eleven cards' costs, stats and keywords, none of which needs a ruling.

**[[R3139000]] makes Palantir steal and cast the opponent's (2) Cost Spells, and amends [[R831001]] (issue #3139, part 5 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: Classic #4 Palantir answered the (1) Cost Spells since patch v0.3.10. It answers the (2) Cost ones, on both faces, the text and the constant `STOLEN_COST` saying 2; the draw limit, the base face's Tribute and the Radiant threshold of 3 are as they were. The cost is the Spell's own out of play ([[R65]]), so a (1) Cost Spell a `costMod` made (2) is answered and a (1), a (0) and an X Spell pass. [[R831001]]'s note reads (2) since. The scripts of v0.5.11r and before keep the (1) Cost steal under `script_v0_5_11o` in the reconcile ([[R767000]]).

**[[R3139001]] makes Masochism Mask count its downsides (issue #3139, part 5 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: both faces gain "After you take 6 downsides from this, make your hand Radiant." A downside is a pick of the Mask's own Start-of-turn Choose that is not "nothing". The decision the issue left: the count restarts at 0 after the sixth, so it repeats every six with no limit (the request has no "once"), the Radiant face's two picks count one by one and its "nothing" counts none, the cards in the hand at the sixth are made Radiant, the turn's own draw coming after the trigger (R62) and so staying plain, and the count goes with the Mask (R78). `downsides` is a declared number, 6 on both faces.

**[[R3139002]] makes Snom Bunny Mind Control steal any enemy permanent and gives it Float, and rewrites its Legacy card (issue #3139, part 5 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: Core #49 costs (5) and reads "Float reduces cost. Steal an enemy card." (Radiant: "… Make it Radiant."). The owner confirmed that "card" is any enemy permanent (a Unit, Field Spell, Trap or Field Trap, the face-down one by its id alone), the target v0.3.7b declared and issue #1149 narrowed to Units; "Float reduces Mana" is read as the one Float trigger the engine has, "Float reduces cost", in the hand and not the deck. TG #52 Legacy: Snom Bunny Mind Control reads "Steal an enemy Unit. Set its stats to 4/4." and, literally, "Steal an enemy card." (no "Make it Radiant"), at (4) still, its own copy of the Core script ([[R2195003]]).

**[[R3139003]] makes Big Felinor destroy three random non-Felinor Units (issue #3139, part 5 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: its Cry destroyed every non-Felinor Unit of its scope. It destroys `destroys` (3) of them, picked at random without replacement from the Units on both sides (Radiant: the enemy's), all of them when fewer, in one state check. The decision the issue left: an Indestructible Unit is in the pool and a pick of it is spent while it survives ([[R46]]), so the three picks may destroy fewer than three Units.

**[[R3139004]] makes Corpse Eater attack again after each attack on a Unit it survives, with Brittle (issue #3139, part 5 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: both faces gain "After this attacks a Unit, it may attack again." (Tmack Trav's `may_attack_again` with `keepSick`, no cap, none after an attack on a hero) and Brittle, 1 on the base face and 3 on the Radiant face, which the printed Brittle ticking from the second turn after its arrival ([[R638]]) makes a stay of one and three turns of its controller's. Its stats and its growth in the hand are as they were.

**[[R3140000]] removes the Luck Feng Shui gave, gives it an Activate that adds an Auspicious Rock and raises its cost to (3), and amends [[R987]] (issue #3140, part 6 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's fourth Stinkyscape balance pass takes "You have Luck 1" off both faces of Meditative #40 Feng Shui, with the `luck` number and the script's `luck` flag, makes it cost (3) and adds "Activate: Add an Auspicious Rock to your hand." (Radiant: a Radiant Auspicious Rock, as every other Rock source has it). The element judging, the shown elements and the two last-element lines are unchanged. No shipped card gives Luck now; the engine keeps `luck_of`, the flag, the board's Luck badge and the fixture script that tests them, and the card tests that rolled with Feng Shui in play roll with a Lucky X given to the rolling card ([[R1438]]), the same best-of rolls ([[R1440]]). The Activate is once a turn ([[R384]]) and no play, so it is neither judged nor recorded. A game through v0.5.11r folds under the kept `script_v0_5_11o`, which `KEPT_SCRIPTS` names in the reconcile ([[R767000]]). It takes R3140000, the first number of issue #3140's block, and edits §8.8's row 40, §6.1's "Luck X" row and the Gachaholic row's mention of it, and [[R1444]]'s.
**[[R3140001]] makes Mountaintop Garden's Cry add a random embiggen card and its Radiant face price it at (0), and amends [[R1178001]] (issue #3140, part 6 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: Meditative+ #22 gains "Cry: Add a random Embiggen card to your hand.", the Radiant face "… It costs (0)." The pool is [[R1178001]]'s, never the Garden itself; the card is added not Radiant, a full hand burns it, and the Radiant price is a `costOverride` of (0) that prices every play of the card, so under a Garden it plays Embiggened for (0) and its dearer choice costs (0) as well ([[R1181003]]). The issue left the price's reach to be ruled: an `embiggenCostOverride` would price only the Embiggened version, and charging the premium on top would change the engine's price order for one card. The Garden's old script is kept as `script_v0_5_11o` ([[R767000]]). It takes R3140001 and edits §8.9's row 22.
**[[R3140002]] makes Conjure Rush Token+ flip a coin, 3 keywords on heads and 2 on tails, and amends [[R1444]] (issue #3140, part 6 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: C+ #16's base face reads "Summon a Rush Token. Flip a coin: it gains 3 random combat keywords on heads, 2 on tails." and its Radiant face gives each of its 3 tokens 5 keywords with no coin. The issue left open whether the owner's "2-3 keywords, 50% each" is a coin flip, which [[R1440]] makes Luck-based, or a plain 50%; it is the coin, so Lucky X and Luck X improve it, it is shown to both players ([[R2652001]]), and the card joins [[R1444]]'s Luck-based pool. The tails count is its own declared number `tails`, because a number the script worked out from `keywords` could not be printed once a Buff moves it. The old script is kept as `script_v0_5_11o` ([[R767000]]). It takes R3140002 and edits §8.7's row 16.

**[[R3142000]] makes a Stimmycheck's copy carry a chance 5 higher, and amends [[R1848006]] (issue #3142, part 7 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the owner's fourth Stinkyscape balance pass changes sixteen of its lines in this part (Asphyxiants Cloud, which #3041 already rebalanced, is left as it is). Twelve are catalog data alone: System Schematic gains Tradable and Flashback, as Prepare For Liftoff prints them ([[R1838000]], [[R3039001]]); Circadian Halfcycler (1), The Greatest Heist (2) and Faction: ETHICS (1) cost less, Ocean Tsunami (4), Otherworldly Nightmare (3) and its Psychic Scream (4) more; Orca is 3/3 → 6/6, Galaxy 5/5 → 10/10, Core Researcher 3/3 → 6/6 and Tmack Trav 8/8 → 16/16; the Sack gains Can't Defend ([[R2657000]]); the Stinkyscape Golem loses Starship Piece. Four change a script and keep the old one as `script_v0_5_11o`, which `KEPT_SCRIPTS` names in the reconcile ([[R767000]]): Drone Commander "fills your hand with random Drone Core cards", at their full price, where it added 6 each costing (1) less, by the room its hand has as the Cry resolves, as Bulk Booster and Ocean Tsunami fill one ([[R129]]), so it declares no numbers; Drone Core VII costs (5), past max mana, and "Float reduces cost" as Drone Core VIII has it ([[R1563000]], [[R2657002]]), a hand trigger and none in the deck; MELCHIOR is [[R3142001]] below; and Stimmycheck's copy is the step below. The step is the engine's `INFLATION_CHANCE_STEP`, 10 → 5, so a Stimmycheck rolls at 15, its copy at 20 and that copy's at 25 and never past 100; the kept script reads `INFLATION_CHANCE_STEP_V0_5_11O` (10) through `next_inflation_chance_by`. The issue left nothing to decide on it but the replays, and takes R3142000, the first number of issue #3142's block. It edits §8.10's rows 7, 12, 12.3, 18.1, 33, 42, 45, 46, 79, 80, 94, 96, 121, 135.1, 150.1, 153 and 159 and §7's rows 12.3, 18.1, 135.1 and 150.1.
**[[R3142001]] makes MELCHIOR's base Activate give each Unit one random combat keyword, and amends [[R1866001]] (issue #3142, part 7 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the base face Discovered a combat keyword and gave the pick to each of its controller's Units ([[R781005]]); it now reads "Give each of your Units a random combat keyword.", each Unit drawing its own from [[R21]]'s pool and nothing asked, as its Radiant face already drew its 3. `keywords` is 1 on the base face and 3 on the Radiant one, either a Buff or a Nerf may move ([[R386]]), where it was a Radiant-only number. The old base Activate is kept in `script_v0_5_11o`, so a replay on v0.5.11r or before still Discovers ([[R767000]]). With no Unit nothing is given and the turn's use is spent, as before. Imitated Instrumentality is unchanged. It takes R3142001 and edits §8.10's row 18.1.
**[[R3142002]] draws a Human Unit as a crew member whatever its name holds, and the Stinkyscape Golem as a golem and ship hybrid (issue #3142, part 7 of #3128, 2026-11-05)**: the Drone Commander is a Human Unit, and drew as a drone ship because its name holds "drone"; it is now the crew family's officer, a drone hovering behind her shoulder, while the Drone Interceptor and Corvette, no Humans, stay drones. The Stinkyscape Golem lost Starship Piece, so it leaves the client's list of those cards and, kept in the fleet family by its name, draws as a stone-plated humanoid with engine nozzles, wing fins and a cockpit visor; the Training Grounds Golem and its painter are untouched ([[R1852000]]). Both are the client's presentation alone ([[R2170000]]) and a visual claim ([[R2529000]]); their two recorded hashes of the v0.5.0 pictures are recorded again ([[R503]]). It takes R3142002.

**[[R3182000]] names the card of a hook that runs with no card, so its marker is hidden with the card (issue #3182, 2026-11-05)**: [[R2783000]]'s `hookResolving` gave a hook run with no `self`, a Cry triggered out of a graveyard ([[R467]]) or an Activate whose cost took its card out of the game, `sourceId` `""`, which the view could not judge, so the marker kept naming the card after the card went somewhere the other seat cannot read ([[R97]]): C #54 Rewind on C #47 Recurring Felinor in its graveyard returns Felinor to its owner's hand through C #34 Ancient Acquisition, and the other seat still read `classic-047` in the Cry's marker. The fuzz's I6 caught it on forced-card waves. The marker now names the card the hook belongs to wherever one exists, the hook itself still finding nothing as "this", and the view hides it with the card's other events; `""` is left for a hook no card holds. It takes R3182000, the first number of issue #3182's block, and amends [[R2783000]].

**[[R3185000]] A card's statistics outside /stats show Tier, Adjusted win rate, Win rate and Play rate, and GET /api/stats/cards stops sending what no client reads (issue #3185, 2026-11-05)**: four figures read a card, its tier, its adjusted win rate, its win rate and its play rate, with its games as their sample. After [[R3184000]] cleared `/stats`' Cards tab of the rest, the owner asked for the card detail's Statistics panel to show only those. The panel drops When drawn and Drawn vs not drawn and shows `/stats`' tier chip and adjusted rate. Each card's row in `GET /api/stats/cards` stops sending the drawn, played, face, drawn-not-played and never-drawn breakdowns and the played and drawn deltas, none of which any client reads. The server still reads the adjusted rate and the tier off those tallies, so neither changes. It takes R3185000, the first number of issue #3185's block, amends [[R654]], [[R1147000]], [[R2427000]] and [[R3184000]], and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3205000]] A hidden tab's polls pause and read once when the tab shows again, except the ones that hold a place the server expires (issue #3205, 2026-11-05)**: tracker #3201 asks for less compute and faster traffic, and a hidden tab was polling on its timers whether or not anyone could see it. One helper, `pollWhileVisible` beside `pageWake`, runs a poll whose timer sends nothing while `document.visibilityState` is `hidden`; when the tab shows again, or the network comes back, it reads once at once and the pace starts over. The social poll (`SOCIAL_POLL_MS`), the friends list (`FRIENDS_POLL_MS`), an open chat (`CHAT_POLL_MS`), the live-game banner, the rematch status, the series banner and co-op Play again (`SERIES_POLL_SECONDS`) use it. The queue wait on `/play` and the queue follow keep running hidden, since their reads keep the ticket alive ([[R897000]]), and so do the series pick ([[R333]]), the co-op host's room wait, the presence heartbeat and the social poll while the tab has a challenge of its own open (a room that expires, and the follow that takes the challenger to the board rides that poll). Pausing the queue reads, pausing the pick and the room wait, and pausing the social poll with a challenge open were rejected. It takes R3205000, the first number of issue #3205's block, and edits [[§9.4]].

**[[R3206000]] A kept answer inside READ_CACHE_FRESH_MS needs no read behind it, and a GET already out is shared (issue #3206, 2026-11-05)**: tracker #3201 asks for less compute and faster traffic, and R2125000 kept a menu's last answer only to draw it sooner: every screen, the gate (R2630000), the wallet and the landing still read again behind it on each move, and a read already out was sent again by the next screen that asked. Each kept answer now carries the time its read finished, and one younger than `READ_CACHE_FRESH_MS` (45 s, midway in the 30 to 60 s the issue allowed) is used with no read behind it; an older one behaves as R2125000 has it. A `GET` of one path and token asked while one is out shares it, on its own controller, so one caller's abort never aborts another's. A write epoch counts every spend that settles and every fold, drop or remembered answer (a read asked after it never joins an earlier one, and one that finished after it is not kept), a write that changes a kept read without carrying it drops it, a game's paid result drops the wallet, quests, tracks, packs, tokens and both ladders, a dev tool empties the cache and a quest falling due expires its answer. The gate skips its `/api/auth/me` read for a fresh answer of an active account that owes nothing, and the wallet pill reads through the same window; the server still enforces every gate (CLAUDE.md rule 7). A per-path window, `localStorage`, keeping the news or the friends list, a window a fold or a gate mount makes fresh again, sharing only inside `keptGet` and an epoch per path were rejected. It takes R3206000, the first number of issue #3206's block, amends [[R2125000]] and [[R2630000]], and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R3209000]] Requests that arrive together share one provider session check, and a silence about a session is remembered briefly (issue #3209, 2026-11-06)**: tracker #3202 asks for less compute, better security and faster traffic, and a burst of requests had become a burst of calls to Supabase Auth: [[R194]]'s session check remembered only a live answer, so requests that arrived together with none remembered (a screen's first reads) each asked `GET /auth/v1/user`, the admin lookup of [[R159]] did the same per user, and during an outage every request waited the provider's full 30 s timeout before its identity stood. Concurrent checks of one session of one user now share one call and its answer, as concurrent admin lookups of one user id do; a silence about a session is remembered for `AUTH_SESSION_OUTAGE_CACHE_SECONDS` (15 s, asserted no longer than the 30 s live window), inside which a request answers at once as it would after the wait; the call by token gives up after `AUTH_SESSION_CHECK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (5 s), which covers tier 3's lookup too since it shares the call, while the sign-in grant and the admin calls keep `AUTH_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; and the published keys are decoded once per fetch and kept with the set under [[R1144000]]'s refetch rules. Answers are keyed by user and session, so a session id under two users is two checks. Rejected: a 30 s outage memo, since a provider that recovered would wait out a whole window before an ended session is refused; a memo shared across users, since one user's 429 would skip every user's check; and an outage memo or the shorter timeout for the admin lookup, since the issue asks them only of the session check and R159 wants a just-confirmed email believed at once. It takes R3209000, the first number of issue #3209's block, and amends [[R194]].

**[[R3210000]] One social read for the challenges and the unread counts, and every signed-in request counts as presence (issue #3210, 2026-11-05)**: tracker #3203 asks for less compute, better security and faster traffic, and a signed-in tab's social poll was two requests every `SOCIAL_POLL_MS`, beside a presence heartbeat every `PRESENCE_HEARTBEAT_MS` that the server, which already counted every signed-in request as queue presence ([[R897000]]), did not need from a busy tab. `GET /api/social` now answers the open challenges and the unread counts, what `GET /api/challenges` and `GET /api/chat/unread` answer, by the same reads in one transaction for the caller alone, and the social poll makes it in place of the two, which stay for older tabs and other callers. Every request of an active account that passes auth, the rate limit and the gate refreshes its presence to friends, written at most once a `PRESENCE_TOUCH_EVERY_MS` (30 s) and never while the account owes the terms ([[R970002]]); agreeing to them clears the mark. The client sends `POST /api/presence` only once `PRESENCE_HEARTBEAT_MS` has passed with no signed-in request, so two writes are at most 75 s apart, under `PRESENCE_TTL_MS`. A write on every request, a period of a whole heartbeat, counting pending or banned callers, a beat at mount and a client fallback to the two reads were rejected. It takes R3210000, the first number of issue #3210's block, and edits [[§9.4]].

**[[R3211000]] The Shop folds each spend's answer into the panels that show it and reads once, when the opening closes, what the answers do not carry (issue #3211, 2026-11-06)**: tracker #3203 asks for less compute and faster traffic, and every buy or open on `/shop` told the three other panels to read again and forgot the owned cosmetics, so a pack bought and opened cost about seven `GET`s and ten opened in a row about seventy, although the buy's answer holds the Jlore balance and the unopened count and the open's answer the pity, the unopened count and the bars. A bought pack's answer now folds into the wallet and the held packs of the Shop and the opening, an opened card pack's into the shelf's count and pity, the opening's held packs and the token picker's two bars, and a cosmetic pack's into its own panel and the picker's bars; each panel still shows only what a server answer says (CLAUDE.md rule 7). What no answer carries (the held tokens and next step, the cosmetic packs held and, only when the Chase Shard bar stands full, the collection and the cosmetics its choice prices) is read once, when the opening scene closes after at least one opening: a batch of ten reads once, a refused open reads nothing, and the owned cosmetics are forgotten once for the scene and when the Shop is left mid-scene. A cosmetic pack, which has no scene, reads the picker's tokens once after its opening, and a chase only forgets the ledger. Folding the held tokens or a paid cosmetic pack on the client, folding the bars into the kept cache, reading the collection and the cosmetics at every close and a debounce were rejected. It takes R3211000, the first number of issue #3211's block, and amends [[R1580000]] and [[R1955000]].

**[[R3217000]] Google's and Roblox's sign-in buttons are one size and share one centered row, Google first, wrapping only when too narrow; one rule sizes both and Google Identity Services is drawn at its width (issue #3217, 2026-11-05)**: the issue asked that "Continue with Roblox" match Google's button, side by side on one row, in Roblox's branding with its logo. The spec had no rule for how the providers sit, so this one fixes their size (200 by 40 px, the width GIS is given and the height its large button has), their row and their states; the colors are the palette's nearest to Roblox's black and white, and the logo is left to a person because the repository holds no Roblox asset and the logo must not be redrawn. It amends [[R2983000]] and [[R3147000]].

**[[R3218000]] The top bar has one layout: Back at the start, the title and the screen's extras after it, then the Jlore pill, Friends, language and the gear, the gear always last; only the bar's sheet places them (issue #3218, 2026-11-05)**: #3189 gave the top bar one skin and no layout, and the push of the right-hand group to the edge was a chain of `margin-left` rules in four stylesheets that held only while the bar was full width, so on the Almanac, Statistics and the deck workshop the gear sat beside Back and the title came after it. BackLink now renders fixed slots (Back, a line holding the title then the extras, an end group of the pill, Friends, language and the gear), `routes/top-bar.css` places them and no other sheet may, the replay player's Back is BackLink's pill, the phone breakpoint is the touch rule's 600 px, and the skin is on the surface, motion and palette tokens. The builder chose to leave each shell's gutter as it was, since the issue asks for Back leftmost in its bar, not at one inset from the window.

**[[R3291000]] has the Activate picker wear the art of the card that prints each ability, and a launched Starship always open it, amending [[R1751000]] (issue #3291, 2026-11-06)**: the picker's choice cards ([[R2646000]]) drew a picture seeded from the card's id and the ability's, so a launched Starship's weapons ([[R1852003]]) and an Equipped Unit's Weapon ([[R1086]]) were not told apart by their art. Each `ActivationView` of a fused card now carries `printedOn`, the ingredient whose text prints the ability, the innermost of a Fuse of a Fuse, on the face the fusion runs it on ([[§10.8]]), absent on a card's own ability and on the engine's launch and Reveal. The choice card wears that card's art, or the activated card's own. Pressing a launched Starship's Activate opens the picker even for one ability, while a Dormant Starship's launch and any other card's one ability still send on the press. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7): no rule and no card changes. It takes R3291000, the first number of issue #3291's block, and edits [[§10.8]].

**[[R3017000]] Part 6 appends six two-level variants, each its art in the normal frame then animated: Night, Watercolor and Woodcut (Rare, then Epic) and Neon sign, Stained-glass window and Tarot card (Epic, then a Legendary chase); granted to no account and refused with a 400 where a card is not offered one (issue #3017, 2026-11-06; amends [[R2725000]] and [[R2725001]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-04 for many more card art variants, so that every card has several. The issue asked for at least six, each a ladder on #3015's model, and left which to the plan: the planner chose Night, Neon sign, Watercolor, Stained-glass window, Woodcut and Tarot card, each an art level and an animated one a tier rarer, with no full-art level, which would have added a Mythic chase to each. The twelve start unowned on every account, `POST /api/dev/fill` grants them where offered, `seed-catalog` upserts them, so no migration is needed, and practice, hotseat and the end-to-end tests treat them as owned. Rejected: the golden-hour idea, inside Radiant's gold window; a chibi take and a set's home location, which need new subject drawing in every painter; and a blueprint, which needs an offer by tag.

**[[R3017001]] An offer's sample may start at a `from`; part 6's six make three pairs, each splitting every non-token card between its two by one seed, so every card, Legacy ones included, is offered at least VARIANT_MIN_PER_CARD variants besides the Default, three, each reaching every set that ships and no two variants on the same cards (issue #3017, 2026-11-06; amends [[R2725001]] and [[R2949001]])**: the issue asked for a named floor of variants on every card, Legacy ones included, and that each variant reach every set that ships while cards still differ. A sample's `from`, 0 when absent, lets two samples of one seed split their cards exactly; each pair's first variant takes 60 or 40 in 100 of each card type, the types tilted toward the cards its style suits, and its partner the rest, so `VARIANT_MIN_PER_CARD` holds by construction. Rejected: a hand-kept list of cards, which every later card would need; one sample per variant, which leaves cards below the floor by chance; and a floor of two, which is not several.

**[[R3017002]] Part 6's pictures: four repaint a new picture of the card's subject under a treatment of their own and two restyle its own picture; every level draws its variant's one picture, a Legacy card and a Starship keep their one picture under the treatment, and no colour of theirs sits in Radiant's gold window (issue #3017, 2026-11-06; amends [[R2725004]] and [[R3015002]])**: the issue asked that most new looks be a new picture of the card's subject and a few a restyle of its own, each drawn by code as a pure function of the card, base and Radiant, with no other game's art, frames or trademarks. Night, Watercolor, Stained-glass window and Tarot card re-seed the card's painter as Alt art does and lay a treatment over it; Woodcut and Neon sign filter the normal picture as Pixel does. The builder chose palette.css's slates, blue, violet, purple and cyan, and tested every colour the treatments can draw from them against the gold window. Rejected: frames of their own, which need more of `cards.css` for each, and an animated level that moves the treatment's own marks, which the issue did not ask for.

**[[R3207000]] The lobby's wait reads the queue readout on mount and then every QUEUE_POPULATION_POLL_SECONDS, while its account read keeps SERIES_POLL_SECONDS (issue #3207, 2026-11-06)**: tracker #3201 asks for less compute and faster traffic, and `/play`'s wait read `/api/queue/population` every 2 s although the server keeps what it counted for `QUEUE_READOUT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (5 s, [[R1996000]]) and the readout is an hour's statistic. The lobby now reads it when it opens, when a queue is joined, and while it waits every `QUEUE_POPULATION_POLL_SECONDS` (30 s, a server constant exported to `serverConfig.ts`, the low end of the issue's 30 to 60 s). The wait's `GET /api/auth/me` read keeps `SERIES_POLL_SECONDS`, since it keeps the ticket alive ([[R897000]]). 60 s, `pollWhileVisible` for the readout, one timer for both reads and a longer server cache were rejected. It takes R3207000, the first number of issue #3207's block, and edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R3207001]] The live-game banner reads the account every LIVE_GAME_POLL_SECONDS, and the queue follow keeps SERIES_POLL_SECONDS (issue #3207, 2026-11-06)**: the banner that shows the way back into a live game ([[R765]]) read `/api/auth/me` every 2 s, though a game outlasts it by minutes. `useLiveGame` now reads every `LIVE_GAME_POLL_SECONDS` (10 s, the low end of the issue's 10 to 15 s, a server constant exported to `serverConfig.ts`); the queue follow in the same file keeps `SERIES_POLL_SECONDS` and runs hidden, since its reads keep the ticket alive ([[R897000]]). A client-only constant, 15 s and slowing the queue follow were rejected. It takes R3207001 and edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R3207002]] A match's rematch status is read by one poll per match id per tab (issue #3207, 2026-11-06)**: the rematch buttons and the rematch watcher are mounted together on the match screen and each polled `GET /api/matches/:id/rematch` every `SERIES_POLL_SECONDS`, twice the requests the answer needed ([[R672]]). The tab now keeps one poll per match id, started by the first component that shows its status and shared by every other: each answer goes to every listener, one that joins after an answer takes it at once and sends nothing, and the last to leave stops the poll. The post-offer read the buttons make stays, being a request the player caused. Lifting the poll into `match.tsx`, one poll per component and a poll keyed by token as well were rejected. It takes R3207002 and edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R3207003]] A screen reads the account once: the gate's answer is handed to /decks and /play, and the friends panel opens on the one the page kept (issue #3207, 2026-11-06)**: `/decks` read the account again through its own `useAccount`, `/play` through the mount read of its pairing watch, and the friends panel through its own read, on top of the gate's. The gate now hands its account to `/decks` and `/play` as it does to `/invite`, and `/play` skips the mount read when that answer is fresh (inside `READ_CACHE_FRESH_MS`: a gate may open on a kept answer of any age and read again behind it) and names no game; an older answer, or one that names a game, is read again, since [[R3206000]]'s window lets a kept answer stand for 45 s and a player who conceded and went back to the lobby must not be sent back into a game that ended. The friends panel opens on the answer the page last kept (`keptAccount()`), of any age and with the same exception, and the server still refuses a challenge from a player in a game (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Trusting a game-naming answer, reading the panel through the 45 s window alone and a React context were rejected. It takes R3207003, amends [[R2630000]] and [[R3206000]], and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R3212000]] Open all is one spend: one request opens a named count of one kind of card pack, each rolled as its own open would be, in statements that do not grow with the count (issue #3212, 2026-11-06)**: tracker #3203 asks for less compute and faster traffic, and the Shop's Open all sent one `POST /api/packs/open` per pack. `POST /api/packs/open-all` takes `{ pack, count }`, `count` from 1 to `PACKS_OPEN_ALL_MAX` (20), in the account's turn ([[R2378000]]), under its economy lock ([[R1301003]]) and an optional `Idempotency-Key` of its own route ([[R2124000]]), and is refused while `ECONOMY_LIVE` is off. The records are read once and each pack rolls from a fresh server seed on what the one before it left, so the openings, their pity, bars and ledger rows in order equal that many opens one after another; every grant is then written in one statement per holding, after [[R3191000]], or none is. Too few packs held is 409 and writes nothing. The Shop sends one request and shows the openings a pack at a time ([[R3211000]]). Opening up to `count`, a cap of 50, single opens side by side and a new shared decision were rejected. It takes R3212000, the first number of issue #3212's block, and edits [[§14.3]].

**[[R3212001]] The deck workshop's autosave is one request a pass, each item saved or deleted as its own route would, a refused one alone (issue #3212, 2026-11-06)**: the autosave ([[R256]]) sent one `PUT` or `DELETE` per item. `POST /api/decks/sync` takes the pass's decks, trios, trio deletions and deck deletions, at most `DECK_SYNC_ITEMS_MAX` (30) in all, and runs them in that order, each as its own route would, in its own transaction, answering each item as its route would; a refused item refuses alone and the rest still save, and a fault of the server's fails the request, each item idempotent on the retry. The client sends one request a pass and reads each item's outcome as it read its own request's. All or nothing, one transaction for the request and a key for it were rejected. It takes R3212001 and edits [[§9.4]].

**[[R3213000]] While a finished match's socket is open, its rematch status and its series' view come over it, and the screen polls only once it is gone (issue #3213, 2026-11-06)**: tracker #3204 asks for less compute and faster traffic, and after a result the match screen polled `GET /api/matches/:id/rematch` or `GET /api/matches/:id/series` every `SERIES_POLL_SECONDS` ([[R3207002]], [[R3205000]]) over the socket that was still open. The match socket now sends each seat a `rematch` frame (its own offer, the opponent's, whether the opponent is here, the game equal offers made and the rematch's mode) or, in a Conquest game, a `series` frame (its own `SeriesView`), each exactly what that seat's own read answers, so no lean and no sealed pick rides it ([[R336]]), and never to a watcher. Each rides every greeting and goes again when the rematch book, a seat's presence or the series changes; a ladder bot's linger ending is a presence change of its own (`botLeaves`, `presenceChanged`), so the buttons leave a bot's death screen as they leave a person's ([[R639000]], [[R1452]]), and a series frame older than one already sent is dropped. The client sends neither read while its open socket has pushed a frame, skips the status read after an offer, and polls as before once the socket drops or if it never pushed; the series screen keeps its poll. Frames carry `v`, the server's `POST_GAME_FRAME_VERSION`: a client reads only the version it knows, and an older one ignores both types. Frames sent only after the result, a capability frame, a bot's absence on the reaper's tick, ts-rs in the server crate and the frames inside `ServerMessage` were rejected. It takes R3213000, the first number of issue #3213's block, amends [[R3207002]] and edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R3214000]] Challenges, unread counts, chat and friends-list changes arrive over one live socket per tab, and the polls run only while it is down (issue #3214, 2026-11-06)**: tracker #3204 asks for less compute, better security and faster traffic, and after [[R3210000]] every signed-in tab still read `GET /api/social` every `SOCIAL_POLL_MS` and an open conversation every `CHAT_POLL_MS`, on every screen, whether anything had changed or not. Each tab now holds one socket at `/ws/social`, signed in as the match socket is (the subprotocol, never the address, [[R1379000]]; one request against the account's budget, [[R2478000]]; §9.4's gate), capped at `SOCIAL_SOCKETS_PER_ACCOUNT` per account and counted per address apart from the game sockets, and checked again every `SOCIAL_SESSION_CHECK_SECONDS`, one [[R194]] window, so a revoked session or a ban closes it with 4401 or 4403. The server sends it `social`, that account's own `GET /api/social` answer, after each change to its challenges or unread counts (a lapse among them) and in answer to a hello; `chat {friendId}`, with no body, when a message between it and that friend is sent; and `friends` when its friends list changes, a block of a stranger telling the stranger nothing. While it is live the tab polls neither read and an open conversation reads on its friend's `chat` frame; once it drops the polls run as before, and it reopens on the match socket's backoff. Server-sent events, message bodies in the frames, pushed friend statuses, a per-address count shared with the game sockets, closing the oldest socket past the cap and a token-only session check were rejected. It takes R3214000, the first number of issue #3214's block, and edits [[§9.4]] and [[§9.5]].

**[[R3220000]] One dialog component, and the board's confirms on it (issue #3220, 2026-11-07)**: the client drew about fifteen dialogs, each its own way (fourteen scrim classes, raw stacking numbers, three families of keys, four kinds of close control, and the board's confirms putting the safe choice first where the menus put it last), and issue #3220, the first of two, asks for one `Dialog` and the board's confirms on it, its convention written down so later dialogs follow it. A `dialog` closes by its ✕ at its top right, named "Close", and an `alertdialog` by its Cancel; the title is an `h2`. The answers run from the safe choice, first and holding the focus, to the act, last at the right, a `danger` answer always last and a third between, and stack in that order on the narrowest phones. Escape, Back and a click on the scrim close it through `useModalKeys`, Tab stays inside, and the focus goes back to its opener unless the caller has moved it; its scrim is one color at one surface token, `--z-dialog`. Concede this game?, Leave the match? ([[R2479000]], [[R2973000]]), Leave this game? ([[R765]]), Leave this fight? ([[R2972000]]) and the practice result draw through it, the last now a layer where Back means View the board. `useModalOverlay`, ordering by role alone, `--z-modal` and the menus' act-first order were rejected. It takes R3220000, the first number of issue #3220's block, amends [[R2127000]] and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R3221000]] The menus' dialogs on the one Dialog, a sheet, and Escape for the top layer alone (issue #3221, 2026-11-07)**: issue #3220 made a dialog one component and moved the board's confirms onto it, and issue #3221, the second of the two, asks for the rest: the settings, friends and chat panels, the challenge dialog, the token, chase and account-deletion questions, the statistics breakdown, a dungeon run's details, the Field Guide, the patch notes' card, the Collection's item preview, card view and Looks screen, the developer tools, the command menu and a lesson's result, each keeping its testids and its Back layer, with an exception named where a dialog's shape differs. A panel that slid in from an edge is now `Dialog`'s sheet, centred on a wide screen, at the foot of an upright phone and down the right edge of one on its side, and the friends panel and a conversation become modal. A question with a Cancel is an `alertdialog` whose Cancel, first, is its close; every other dialog closes by the ✕ at its top right, and the pack scene and a card's detail, which keep their own bodies, take it too. Escape and Tab go to the top layer alone, so a dialog over another closes first, and Escape over an open tooltip closes the tooltip alone ([[R279]], [[R2098000]]). Every scrim is the one at `--z-dialog`, so the menus' panels that stood higher now lie under the toasts and the queue banner, and a Duos game's result box comes down under them; the inspect overlay draws over the dialogs while it is the top layer and under one opened after it; a click on the scrim closes a dialog only when its press began there, so a selection dragged out of a conversation keeps its draft. A non-modal conversation, a ✕ beside a Cancel, a scrim class per caller and moving `useModalOverlay` onto `useModalKeys` were rejected. It takes R3221000, the first number of issue #3221's block, amends [[R2127000]] (a lesson's result and the account's deletion are layers now) and [[R3220000]] (whose lesson's result kept Back as it was) and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R3223000]] Every menu names itself with one PageTitle in the top bar's title slot; the brand is the wordmark, not a heading; section headings are h2 in one style (issue #3223, 2026-11-06)**: the menus titled themselves five ways at sizes from 17 px to 44 px (a brand `h1` over an `h2` in the tavern board, a brand `h1` over a styled `<p>`, an `h1` of the screen's own size and face, a hidden `h1` beside a visible `h2`, and none), so one `PageTitle` (`routes/PageTitle.tsx`, `--font-display` at `--type-3xl`) is now every menu's only `h1`, drawn in BackLink's title slot (or in the panel of the two prompts that have no bar), the brand is a `Wordmark` (not a heading), and a section inside a screen is an `h2.section-heading`, Practice's "Learn to play" and "Practice against the AI" among them. The document `<title>` is as it was. The builder titled the replay player "Replays", the tab's own word, over a new "Replay" entry whose zh-CN reading is "replay a lesson", gave the dungeon stats the heading its tab has, and left the other classed panel headings on their own sizes as a follow-up.

**[[R3228000]] A screen that waits keeps its frame: the decks route draws the workshop's bar and title in every state, and the menus' waits and empties use Skeleton and EmptyState (issue #3228, 2026-11-06)**: `/decks` waited on a plain page (a bare `.app-shell`, a bar with no gear) and then swapped to the tavern workshop, so the bar's look and contents jumped when the decks landed. One `WorkshopFrame` (the tavern `app-shell`, the `db-header` bar and the `PageTitle` "Decks") is now drawn in every state of the route and by the loaded workshop, the gear always on, so only the body changes; `SKELETON_DELAY_MS` still keeps a fast read from flashing. `LinkedAccounts` waits with a `rows` skeleton and `TwoStepSettings` with a `panel` one, their sentences kept as the hidden status line, and the workshop's empty rail, the Friends panel's and the shard bar's empties draw `EmptyState`. The builder took the cost that a settings dialog opened from the loading gear closes when the decks land (what #1524's guard had prevented), over lifting one bar above both states, and kept the leaderboard's and the other one-line empties as text, listed with their reasons in the web README.

**[[R3286000]] A boss whose drop is already held offers another relic, a mini-boss's of Gold or better (issue #3286, 2026-11-06)**: a dungeon run's two mini-bosses each pick their drop from the same few top relics when the map is made, so a run can be dealt one relic at both, and a shop or a chest can sell a drop before its boss; a player who held it beat the boss and was offered nothing, though [[§15.7]] says a held drop is replaced. [[§15.7]] gave the replacement no tier, so this decides it: a held drop rolls like a node with no drop, at `RUN_ARTIFACT_PERCENT` of its kind, from the artifacts not held, and a mini-boss's replacement is of `RUN_MINI_BOSS_DROP_TIER` or better, as its dealt drop is; none left means no artifact, as before. It draws from the random stream only for a held drop, so every other run folds as before. Making the two mini-bosses' drops distinct when the map is dealt (it moves every seed's map, the daily run's included, and misses a shop's sale), no floor (a Bronze relic from a mini-boss) and the held relic's own tier as the floor were rejected. It takes R3286000, the first number of issue #3286's block, and edits [[§15.4]] and [[§15.7]].

**[[R3290000]] A banned account's standing leaves the leaderboard, the percentiles and Jlorious (issue #3290, 2026-11-06)**: `moderate ban` refused a banned account's next request, but its ranked standing stayed on the public leaderboard for the rest of the season, counted toward the tier percentiles and kept any place in Jlorious, which kept the honest player just below it out and changed who earns the Jlorious card back ([[R2755000]]). The spec said nothing about a banned account's standing; a banned account is left off Hearthstone's leaderboards, so the standings reads now mark a banned account's rows from `profiles.status` and `counts` and `listed` leave them out, on every server and until the ban is lifted, as a ladder bot's row is left out of the percentiles and Jlorious ([[R640000]]). The builder chose to read the ban live and not to grant or revoke anything at ban time: the ban lever writes only `profiles.status`, an unban restores the standing at once, and the peak and card back of the account that takes the place follow at the next rated game on that ladder.

**[[R3332000]] A card's frame in its effects table: an entrance beside any entry of a Unit or a Field Spell, or alone; a takeover look beside a Mythic's; and the signature tier, held to loudness budgets by rarity and type, with a ratchet of every Legendary and Mythic still without one (issue #3332, 2026-11-07; amends [[R670]], [[R2722001]], [[R2784000]] and [[R2794000]])**: the owner asked, in issue #3330, that every Legendary and Mythic get an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone, instead of a few shared motifs; 86 of the 111 non-token ones played one. Issue #3332 is the foundation of #3330's per-set passes: it asked to fold the four marquee entrances and the four takeover looks into the table, unchanged, to add a signature tier with budgets by rarity and type inside the existing caps, and a ratchet listing every Legendary and Mythic without one, which only shrinks. The plan read "permanent" as a Unit or a Field Spell, the cards that land face up as they are played, a Trap's entrance being its firing, and made the tier an explicit flag. Rejected: a separate entrance or takeover table; a Trap's entrance at its reveal; and deriving the tier from an entrance.

**[[R3332001]] Felinor Fiender's signature: it assembles from every Felinor its side shows face up, a streak of cat light from each into the zone it lands in, none from a card buried under a Stack (issue #3332, 2026-11-07)**: the issue asked for one card with none to get a signature end to end, Felinor Fiender, whose entry was "none: no hook; its Legendary entrance plays", assembling from every Felinor it counts, each a streak of cat light from the cards that give it stats. A Felinor buried under a Stack gives it stats but lies face down, so only the Felinors the view shows streak. Rejected: a streak per point of stats.

**[[R3332002]] A Legendary's or a Mythic's play sting is its own intro: whole with dynamic music on and the music above zero, else its opening cut to 1.4 s on the effects bus; the shared entrance sting plays only for one with no intro (issue #3332, 2026-11-07; amends [[R669]] and [[R2722000]])**: the issue asked that a Legendary's or Mythic's play sting be the opening of its own intro track, or a sting cut from it, in place of the shared `sfx("entrance")`, which stays the fallback for a card without an intro, with no new assets beyond the music and voice budgets. The plan made the intro itself the sting and its first 1.4 s the sting where the intro will not play. Rejected: both at once, per-card sting files and per-card offsets for now.

**[[R3333000]] A Starship's picture is built from the pieces it holds, oldest first, in the hull design of the name it launched under, and a Dormant one sits in a shipyard (issue #3333, 2026-11-07)**: every Starship wore one picture, a capital ship grown from one seed by how many pieces it held ([[R2249000]]), its pieces drawn as identical pods, so two Starships with different pieces or names looked the same. The owner asked for a ship built from what it holds. The spec makes no rule about art, so the builder followed the issue's plan: each piece's module mounts on its hardpoint oldest first, the hull grows from a frigate to a dreadnought with its count, a launched Starship wears the design of the name it took (one for each of the seven names) and a Dormant one sits in a shipyard, its newest piece's welding sparks and a dock lamp that brightens as it fills. A design and a livery are the painter's inputs, the hook the cosmetic track of issue #3330 needs.

**[[R3333001]] Every Starship Piece has a module of its own, drawn on its Starship's hull and large on its own card, in a bay by its family (issue #3333, 2026-11-07)**: the fifty piece definitions drew their part in front of a faint cruiser, and the v1 to v5 tiers of one weapon looked nearly alike. Each now has a module of its own, the weapons and drills each tier bigger and busier, and its own card draws it large on a cutaway of a hull in a bay by its family, gold only on its Radiant face ([[R2725004]]). [[R503]]'s fixture records the fifty entries again.

**[[R3333002]] A Starship's hull plates and its pieces list show each piece's module icon (issue #3333, 2026-11-07)**: a Dormant Starship's hull plates ([[R2801000]]) were plain and its pieces list text alone. Each plate and each name in the list now shows its piece's module icon.

**Open questions, rejected alternatives.** These are the builder's defaults, for a person to overrule:

- A fifth hardpoint, the spine behind the bow's guns, carries the defenses, the Ultralight Chassis and Salvage; the issue names four. Putting the defenses on the flanks was rejected: the flanks already hold every launcher and pod.
- At most 16 modules are drawn, the oldest pieces'; a Starship holding more draws the same picture whatever its pieces past the 16th. Drawing every one was rejected to keep two Starships' pictures within the 48 KiB each `STARSHIP_ART_MAX_BYTES` allows. Issue #3331's `ART_DETAIL_MAX_BYTES` is not on `main` yet, so 48 KiB stands in for it until it lands.
- A row of running lights along the keel, one for each module in its colors, oldest at the bow, shows the order of the pieces: without it, two pieces on different hardpoints drew the same launched ship in either order. Leaving the order invisible was rejected, since the issue asks that any two different piece lists differ.
- An escort is a frigate in silhouette (its prow, stripe and burn), not a full hull: at a quarter scale its windows and emblem cannot be seen, and dropping them keeps the biggest picture within its budget.
- A Radiant Starship gilds every module, so on a Radiant hull a piece's own Radiant face does not show; on a base hull it does.
- A name no design has gets one of the seven designs by its hash. Every name a Starship takes today is one of the seven (`STARSHIP_NAMES`, exported to the web so a test holds the designs to it).
- A kind on no ladder (every piece but the Beams, Repeaters, Cannons, Flak and Drills) draws at v3's size with no chevrons; the chevrons mark only a ladder's tier.
- Each class is drawn nearer in its window the smaller it is (a frigate at 1.15, a dreadnought at 0.8), so a frigate fills its window while each class still stands longer than the last. A module on a hull is drawn at 0.5 to 0.9 of its size; the plan's 0.28 to 0.55 drew them too small to see.
- A piece's card draws its module at 2.8 times on a slim deck section, not the plan's 2.4, so the module fills the card's window.
- The three Jlockeed pieces keep their blueprint grid as their bay, as the Jlockeed family draws.
- The collection's Dormant Starship, out of play, keeps the picture [[R503]] pins; only a Starship in play is built from its pieces.
- The hull plates grew from 8.5 by 5.5 to 10.5 by 7, so an icon can be made out on a board card.
