**[[R3336000]] The Drone Battleship is one great ship in three decks: its layers draw as a reactor keel, hangar decks and a command superstructure with the top card showing the decks still standing, its arrival lands a layer at a time, a hit redirected onto the Shield beams to a dome that takes it, its bays launch drones, its Meltdown glows to a reactor blast and a layer that breaks shears its deck off (issue #3336, 2026-11-07)**: the owner asked for crazy animations on the Drone Battleship (tracker #3330). Drone Core X summons it as three stacked tokens ([[R1842000]]), each of which drew as a wide hull of its own and the pile as none, and nothing marked a layer breaking and the next taking over. Now the three are the decks of one ship, in each layer's color and gilded when Radiant ([[R2725004]]): a printed card draws its deck large with the others faint, and a layer on the board draws its own deck and every deck beneath it, so the top card shows the ship as far as it stands (three decks, two, one); the keel's core glows and cracks as it melts and the hangar's lights stay on for the bays that hold drones, and the three layers' [[R503]] hashes were recorded again. Drone Core X's warp ([[R2797000]]) is one beat per layer summoned, in stack order, with a horn; a new planner (`fx/battleship.ts`, public events and the view's layers only, [[R202]]) beams a redirect onto the Shield to its dome, which flares and ripples while the armor plates glow for what Armor took, flickers it for an ordinary hit, opens a bay for each Activate of the Propagation layer and launches the drone from it (an Interceptor fast, a Corvette heavy), pulses the Meltdown's core harder each turn, fires its attacks from the reactor, blasts it on its death and shears a layer's deck off, lighting the next, when it dies over one. Each beat has its own sound. Open: the plates are lit by what Armor took of the hit, since Armor is never spent; the layers have no signature entries until #3332 lands; the arrival is three entries, within `SET_PIECE_HOLD_CAP_MS`, not one held set piece.

**[[R3338000]] Each Terminus card has a set piece of its own, one held beat of its action, a still frame under Reduce Motion, drawn from public events alone (issue #3338, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2785000]], [[R2797000]] and [[R2800000]])**: the owner asked for crazy animations on the Terminus cards, the game's end-of-the-world cards (#3330). Olivian Expanse, its six Terminus tokens, Ceaseless Void and Dice of Doom each name a set piece in their set's effects table. Farewell's and the Void's are board clears, played as [[R2800000]]'s wipes; every other is a signature, the run of its hook's events of the types it names (The Big Crunch's first cast) played as one pinned entry the queue holds as [[R2785000]] holds every set piece, a Mythic's takeover yielding to it. The Terminus pool, the turn-owing follow-ups (Event Horizon's snap back, Last Word's Charge and countdown) and The Big Crunch's standing point are drawn from the seat's own stream, a hidden card only ever as a card back ([[R202]]), Dice of Doom's jackpot read off what its run did and never off its face. Each bed opens on an `organ` chord of its own under the Mythic choir ([[R1883000]]), in place of re-scoring the rendered intros, which needs tools the bot's machine lacks. Rejected: one entry for a whole Terminus play, a countdown standing through Last Word's turn ([[R200]]), and a second hold for Olivian Expanse's play and pick in one action.

**[[R3338001]] The Terminus cards draw pictures of their own (issue #3338, 2026-11-07)**: one shared black hole drew Olivian Expanse and its six Terminus cards, a planet Dice of Doom and the Unit figure Ceaseless Void. Each of the eight Stinkyscape cards now draws a kind of its own, found by id (`TERMINUS_KINDS`), and the Void a composition of its own, a hole in the shape of a giant (`CARD_COMPOSITIONS`), each Radiant face gilding its base's geometry inside [[R2725004]]; [[R503]]'s fixtures are recorded again for the nine. Rejected: one black hole tinted per card, and keying the pictures by name.

**[[R3338002]] Ceaseless Void's cost gem drains as its cost falls, in its owner's own hand only (issue #3338, 2026-11-07)**: the issue asked that the Void's cost tumbling down show on its hand card. Its gem draws the share of its printed cost gone from the cost its owner's view gives it, and no other seat's view draws it ([[R202]]). Rejected: draining it on the board and in revealed hands too.

**[[R3339000]] Art-first cards in play: full-art looks and every Legendary and Mythic drop their name, type line, and rules text on the board, showing edge-to-edge art and icon marks, with names shown on hover or long press (issue #3339, 2026-11-07; amends R3046000)**: on the board across Unit rows and the backrow for both seats, cards wearing a full-art-shaped level (Full art, Alt full art rank >= 2, Sketch's full art rank >= 2, Extended art, Out of frame, Immersive, Prismatic, Set showcase, or any animated or signed tier of them) or of Legendary or Mythic rarity render art-first. Art-first faces drop their static name, type line, and rules text, showing edge-to-edge art and vitals (attack, health, armor, and counters); unit keywords and states render as icon marks without words (`keywordMarks.ts`, `KeywordFx.tsx`). On desktop, hover or keyboard focus reveals a slim name plate over the bottom of the art within `--motion-fast`, followed by the full hover preview after its delay; on a coarse pointer (phone), a long press opens inspect. Common to Epic normal units keep a smaller translucent name plate over the art's bottom edge, expanding the art window, with keyword icon marks. Card settings (`cards/settings.ts`) gain "Names on the board" (`boardNames`: `"artFirst"` default, `"always"`, `"never"`). This amends [[R3046000]] regarding the permanent name band below art. Under "Always" no card is drawn art first (a full-art card keeps R3046000's name band), and a compact face that is not a card in play (a picker's tile, a notice, a cosmetic's sample) is never drawn art first. The hover plate is the name's text, transparent but never hidden from assistive technology. An art-first Unit has no oval to ring, so a Mythic's Rarity foil on the board is the rainbow edge round its art ([[R2721000]]). Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7).

**[[R3348000]] Every tile answers a press with a ripple in its half's playmat, that playmat's sound and, on touch, a short haptic, a legal tile harder, without changing what the press does (issue #3348, 2026-11-07; amends [[R1297000]] and [[R1834004]])**: the owner asked, in issue #3330, that the arena feel alive: "if you're clicking on the tiles, you should feel it there". Only a bare playmat answered a press (#1954, #2332), and a press on a card or on a legal tile, which is a button, drew and sounded nothing. A press on any tile of either side, or on its card, now adds a press-in and a ripple in that half's playmat family, sounds that family's recipe once (from the mat's own tap, or from the tile's when the mat's did not sound), buzzes on touch, and answers harder on a legal tile. It never stops the press, so a legal tile still plays and an illegal one still does nothing in the game. Hovering a tile on a desktop pointer lights its edge. Rejected: lifting a legal tile to a dragged card, since #3347's drag layer is not on `main`; a new sound recipe for tiles; and stopping the press at the answer.

**[[R3348001]] Each playmat keeps two corner props that the viewer taps: they escalate after 5 to 7 taps, roll a rare outcome at 1 in 1,000 from the client's own random source, hide a tap sequence on four mats, and are the viewer's own, never sent or logged (issue #3348, 2026-11-07; amends [[R1297000]] and [[R1834004]])**: the owner asked, in issue #3330, for Hearthstone's board corners: cosmetic clickables with their own animation and sound, that escalate with repeated clicks, hide click sequences and have rare random outcomes, each player's board their own. The plan fixed ten pairs of props, the streak goal at 5 to 7 taps, the rare chance at 1 in 1,000 and a right-left-right sequence on the tavern, the chessboard, the ancient map and the astral mat. The builder chose to sound them on the seven field-tap recipes with a variant and a pitch, to keep them inside the sidebar column beside the field and hide any that would cover a tile, a hand, a seat, the controls or the log or leave the screen, to quiet them while the animation queue drains (one cue at 0.35 of its gain, still, no buzz), and to draw the opponent's half's props still. Rejected: about twenty bespoke sound recipes; props inside the lanes; shrinking the phone's cards to make room; and anything that sends corner play to the net layer or the log.

**[[R3049000]] CN Tech and NA Tech read the game's snapshot of the server's cached card stats; the compiled table retires (issue #3049, 2026-11-04)**: the owner asked that the win rates be live data. A match starts with a snapshot of the windowed card stats `/stats` shows, which the game keeps (the rows each end can name), the match row keeps (migration 0085) and every fold is handed; a game handed none reads its shipped catalog's frozen table, else none, and the cards fall back as [[R2521007]] says. The compiled table, `cargo jackioh winrates`, its refresh and the fragment's claim retire; amends [[R1101]], [[R1266000]], [[R1329000]], [[R1368000]] and [[R2386000]].

**[[R3049001]] A game's snapshot is ranked by its 95% Wilson bounds, the leader the highest lower bound and the trailer the lowest upper bound, in integers to a millionth; a table read with no snapshot keeps the exact rate (issue #3049, 2026-11-04)**: a live table is thin early in a patch, so the ends of a game's snapshot are ranked by how sure their figures are, in `u128` with z = 49/25, with the floor, the exclusions and the tie-breaks of [[R1100]] and [[R1183000]], which it amends. A game handed none ranks the frozen table it reads by the exact rate, as its version did ([[R1266000]]), so a record from before #3049 picks the card it picked.

**[[R3049002]] A ladder bot's deal leans on the server's snapshot at the pool's refresh, and on nothing without one (issue #3049, 2026-11-04)**: amends [[R750000]]: the deal reads the table it is handed, the snapshot the refresh takes once, and a deal handed none is the seed's at any strength.

**[[R3230000]] Sentence case and one word per action across the client's labels and page names; arrow characters become ArrowIcon (issue #3230, 2026-11-07)**: the client's labels drifted in casing and varied in how one action was named (staying as "Keep playing" vs "Stay", leaving as "Leave" vs "Leave game" vs "Leave fight", closing as "Got it" vs "Close" vs "Dismiss", and page names differing across menu, footer and `<title>`), so sentence case now governs all labels, buttons and page names, each page has one name across its heading, command menu, footer and `<title>` (Almanac, Statistics, Privacy policy, Terms of service, Account, How JackiOh uses AI, Dungeon run, and Play), one verb names each action (Stay, Leave, Close for a dialog's ✕, Dismiss for an inline notice, Create account, Back), and arrow characters are removed from translated strings in favor of `ArrowIcon`. The builder chose the headings' fuller page names over short menu variants, kept 'Leave without saving' where Save and leave is offered, and carried over verified Chinese translations without machine translation.

**[[R3324000]] /stats gives each patch a tab, and the newest patch, short of games, says its data is still being collected (issue #3324, 2026-11-06)**: the patch bar ([[R1973002]]) was a `<select>` between a Previous and a Next button. After a new patch shipped, the page opened on it and showed the previous window's figures ([[R1367000]]) under its name. The owner asked for a tab for each new patch, never a micro, that says its data is still being collected. The picker is now a row of pressed-state patch tabs between two arrows: the newest patch whatever its games, the `STATS_PATCHES_OFFERED` newest with `STATS_PATCH_MIN_GAMES` games, and the patch shown. Each tab gives its games, and a micro opens no tab ([[R1973001]]). The newest patch is collecting while it has fewer than `STATS_PATCH_MIN_GAMES` games. Its tab says "Collecting data", and while it is shown the Cards and Sets tabs say "Data for patch X is still being collected at this time." with a button to the patch before, and show no figures, no games tile and no count ([[R661]]). An older patch short of games shows its figures. Opening on the newest patch with games, a floor of live games, a new constant and `role="tab"` for the patches were rejected. It takes R3324000, the first number of issue #3324's block, amends [[R1973002]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3324001]] /stats names the current patch in its header (issue #3324, 2026-11-06)**: the page named only the patch it showed. Beside the title, the header now reads "Current patch" and the newest version `GET /api/stats/cards` lists, micro included, or the client's `CATALOG_VERSION` from a server with no list. The newest patch's name, the client's version alone and a tile of its own were rejected. It takes R3324001 and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3324002]] /stats lists the tokens and the other Created cards as two sections that share no row, with no Show tokens toggle (issue #3324, 2026-11-06)**: a Show tokens checkbox added a Tokens table ([[R2370000]]) above a Created table that still held every token, so each token stood twice, in two tables with different columns. The toggle and its `tokens=1` address are gone. The Created cards show as Tokens (catalog tokens) and Other created cards (every other one), sharing no row. Both use one sortable table ([[R3184002]]): the card, Games, [[R2370000]]'s pooled Win rate, Used, Not used, Used Radiant, Used normal and Used vs not used. Each opens on Win rate, best first. The breakdown's token list reads "Tokens this card makes". Keeping the toggle, one table with a kind filter, leaving Win rate off the other Created cards and the server's order were rejected. It takes R3324002, amends [[R2089000]], [[R2370000]] and [[R3184002]], and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3324003]] /stats' on/off controls are the Segmented's pressed buttons, in one row (issue #3324, 2026-11-07)**: the Cards tab drew its toggles as buttons, checkboxes and a link button, one row each, and its own pressed look lost to the tavern's button rule, so nothing showed what was pressed. After #3222's shared `Tabs` and `Segmented` landed, every on/off control wears the `Segmented` look with `aria-pressed`. Table and Grid and the patch tabs are `Segmented` groups. Show 95% ranges and the Columns toggles are independent buttons with the same item class. The sort direction is a plain secondary button (#3219). Sort, View and Columns are labelled groups in one wrapping row, and the selects take `tabs.css`'s shared filter style, `--hit-min` tall. The page keeps no pressed, select or sort-button skin of its own. Keeping the page's own pressed look, and a `Segmented` for the independent toggles, were rejected. It takes R3324003 and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3331000]] Pictures composed as scenes: a card's scene recipe draws its picture from the scene kit and the figure kit in seven layers, seeded by its id, its Radiant face repainting the same shapes; five proof cards draw from recipes, every other card keeps its picture byte for byte, and a Legacy card keeps its Core card's old subject (issue #3331, 2026-11-07)**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's picture be redrawn richer, set by set, and this issue the foundation of those passes: an art bible per set family in ART.md, scene recipes drawn from kits of parts with seeded variation, a figure kit that makes a Unit read as who it is, and five proof cards. The plan put every scene through the procedural painter's spec, which the Stinkyscape and Training Grounds painters hand their recipe cards to, so a scene prints, moves, splits for Out of frame and restyles as every picture does; the builder drew the figure in front of its subject layer's other parts, chose Arg Arg Defender as the Stinkyscape Common, and kept Ceaseless Void's own giant ([[R3338001]]) as its scene's subject. Rejected: a scene printer per painter, gradients for glows, `data-*` attributes marking layers and parts, drawing the figure behind its subject layer's parts, and a Terminus figure for Ceaseless Void.

**[[R3331001]] The detail floor: each rarity's least number of drawn elements (Common 30, Rare 40, Epic 60, Legendary 90 and Mythic 140, Legendary and Mythic in every scene layer; a token at Common's), measured on every catalog entry's base picture but the Glitch's, starting as a ratchet of today's shortfalls that may only shrink, under a ceiling of 48 KiB per picture (issue #3331, 2026-11-07)**: the issue proposed the floors and asked that they be ruled, with a ratchet listing today's shortfalls by card id and a per-picture ceiling that keeps phones fast. The planner kept the proposed floors, held tokens to Common's, left out the Glitch, whose window draws no picture, capped the list at its length so it may only shrink, and set the ceiling at 48 KiB, about twice the longest picture before. Rejected: a floor per set, counting from markup attributes, measuring the Radiant face, a list that may grow, and a ceiling by element count.

**[[R3331002]] R503's pin lifted: every card's picture may be redrawn richer, Core's included; every catalog entry still draws a distinct picture on each face, a card with no scene recipe keeps its picture byte for byte, and the three golden fixtures are records of the old pictures, not pins (issue #3331, 2026-11-07; amends [[R503]] and [[R2724000]])**: the owner decided on 2026-11-06 that every card's picture is redrawn richer, Core's included, and the issue asked that R503 be ruled to say so. Core's v0.1 fixture, the record before #2724 and Stinkyscape's v0.5.0 fixture each still hold every card with no recipe to its hash, and each card with one to a new picture; Stinkyscape's is now held to the picture its face shows. Rejected: deleting the fixtures, re-recording them with the proof cards' new hashes, and lifting the pin for the proof cards alone.

**[[R3334000]] A fused piece's module docks on its own hardpoint, clamps on and welds, its hum rising with the ship's count, and a Dormant Starship with pieces idles in its shipyard in one of the board's moving-art slots (issue #3334, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2801000]])**: the owner wants the Starship's moments "very big and epic" (issue #3330). [[R2801000]]'s fuse run flew a card back to the ship; now each piece's own module ([[R3333001]]) flies to the hardpoint the ship's picture ([[R3333000]]) mounts it on and welds there, a clank and weld sounding for each, its hum a step higher for each piece. A Dormant ship with pieces idles, a dock light sweeping and welding sparks, in a moving-art slot as moving art does. The spec makes no rule about presentation, so the builder followed the issue's plan.

**[[R3334001]] A held Starship launch at Big moments full is a cinematic of its own picture, the camera passing each module, its engines lit in turn and its name stamped in its design's lettering; short, off and Reduce Motion show a still title card; a War Doctrine cast is announced over the launched ship (issue #3334, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2801000]])**: the issue asked for the launch as a full-screen cinematic for the length of the set piece's hold, scaled by the pieces, the same beats on a phone with fewer particles, and a still title card under Reduce Motion and Big moments short or off; and for Starfleet Commander's and Preemptive Strike's War Doctrine to be announced over the ship after their launch, each doctrine in its own look. The ship's launched name is public ([[R1852004]]), so the cinematic shows it.

**[[R3334002]] A launched Starship's attack is a broadside, each mounted weapon firing by its kind inside the attack's row, and one with no weapons rams prow first (issue #3334, 2026-11-07)**: a launched Starship attacked with the generic lunge. The issue asked for its mounted weapons to fire by kind (beams sweep, repeaters stutter, cannons recoil, flak bursts in clouds, launchers lob what they carry) within the attack row's time, and an unarmed ship to ram prow first.

**[[R3334003]] A destroyed Starship is a wreck: its newest modules blow off one by one, the hull breaks in two, debris drifts over its backrow and a scorch settles on its tile, never inside a wipe (issue #3334, 2026-11-07)**: a Starship died with the generic rows. The issue asked for a wreck quieter than the launch but unmistakable, and a hull-breaking groan in its sound.

**[[R3334004]] The Dormant Starship, Starfleet Commander and Preemptive Strike wear signatures of their own: a shipyard roll-out, an order to the ship and an alarm that brands the ship it wakes (issue #3334, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2801000]])**: the three were `"none: Starship part"`; the issue asked for signature entries per [[R3332000]], which has landed.

**Open questions, rejected alternatives.** These are the builder's defaults, for a person to overrule:

- The launch's 3-2-1 countdown is gone. Keeping it inside the cinematic was rejected as clutter over the camera's pass.
- A War Doctrine is announced with its English card name, as the cards print it. A new translated string for each was rejected: the name is the card's own text, which the client shows as the catalog gives it.
- The odd weapons fire as their nearest kind: AP and Vamp Rounds (S #100, #122) as repeaters, the RR-5000's rail and the Yamato Cannon (S #107, #128) as cannons, the Disintegrex and Trila 3 (S #105, #108) as beams; the Missile Pod (S #129) lobs arrows. Giving each its own motion was rejected as more than the five kinds the issue names.
- "Prow first" is a turn of `FX_STARSHIP_RAM_TURN_DEG` (30 degrees) toward the target, with sparks at the prow. A full turn to face the target was rejected as unreadable on a card.
- A cannon's recoil is drawn as an overlay of its module kicking back, since the picture is one image and cannot move a part of itself.
- The shipyard idle's numbers live in `cards/constants.ts`, since a card face must not import the effects layer (`fx/`). The idle sits under the Dormant seal's veil, so it is greyed with the rest of the face; lifting it over the veil was rejected, as the seal must read as one sleeping card.
- The picture is drawn at least as wide and as tall as the screen, and the camera looks only where the zoomed picture still fills it (`cameraPoint`), so a small ship's pan from stern to bow is shorter than its hull. Letting the camera reach the stern and bow themselves was rejected: the board showed beside the picture, which the issue asks to fill the screen.
- The cinematic plays only at Big moments "full": "short" shortens the hold, and a camera pass squeezed into it was rejected for the title card the issue names.
- A War Doctrine is announced however it is cast, Dropshipping's (C+ #23) included, over its caster's launched Starship, else the board's middle. Announcing only the two Legendaries' casts was rejected: the play carries no link to the card that cast it.
- The director sends a fuse run's docks as its entry starts, each delayed to its landing, and the sound engine's retrigger guard now reads a dock (`SCHEDULED_SFX`) at the time it sounds: otherwise only a run's first dock was heard. Every other sound's guard is unchanged (B6), and timers in the director were rejected: it schedules every other delayed sound the same way.
- The wreck reads the ship as the board shows it before the death, Dormant or launched; a Starship that dies inside a wipe keeps the board clear's set piece ([[R2800000]]).
- A broadside's or a wreck's sound stands in for the swing or the death sting only for the event the director sent it for (`CueContext.starshipSounded`). Under Reduce Motion no entry starts, so the attack swings and the death stings as any Unit's do, and a Starship that dies inside a wipe keeps its sting in the wipe's condensed sounds. Dropping the row's sound for every Starship was rejected: a Reduce Motion player heard nothing at all; sending the Starship's sounds when an entry is flushed unstarted was rejected, as they are timed to motion that never plays.
- Pictures in the effects layer's DOM cues are `<img>` elements: B27 forbids a `url(` in `fx/*.ts`.

**[[R3337000]] `boardReplaced` names the Anomaly a replacement lays (issue #3337, 2026-11-07)**: `boardReplaced` ([[R1855000]]) said only whose board was replaced, so the client could not tell Stinkyscape's ten Anomalies apart as one landed, and the owner asked for discovering an Anomaly to be an event of its own (#3330). The spec did not say what the event carries. The builder gave it an optional `anomaly`, the board's code, set by every preset of Anomaly Found! and Call to Chaos (Scape Edition) and absent for any other replacement, written only when set so older logs fold unchanged, and public on both seats, as the laid board is; the alternative of a new event beside it was rejected, since every reader of `boardReplaced` would have had to pair the two.

**[[R3337001]] An Anomaly Found! that holds a board draws its picture (issue #3337, 2026-11-07)**: every Anomaly Found! drew one spiral whatever it held. The builder gave the face model the held code, for Anomaly Found! alone and only where its label reaches, its owner's hand ([[R2247000]]), and ten pictures, one per board; the other seat and the collection keep the spiral, so a picture tells no seat a board it does not read ([[R202]]).

**[[R3337002]] A Scanning Probe's scan is drawn per seat (issue #3337, 2026-11-07)**: the probes played the shared `send` motif. The builder made their entry a bespoke recipe: the sweep at the probe's public play for both seats, and a find's ping and flight, with a Radiant one's flash, only where the seat's own `addedToHand` names Anomaly Found!; the other seat's names the sentinel and keeps its own row ([[R97]], [[R202]]).

**[[R3337003]] An Anomaly's lay is its set piece (issue #3337, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2784000]], [[R2785000]], [[R2787000]] and [[R2800000]])**: the cast played a 500 ms board swap. Following the Starship's launch ([[R2801000]]), the builder made the replacement's run of exiles and landings one pinned entry, held as the action's set piece ([[R2785000]]) for a rift, a flare, a title card and the landing (Anomaly Found!, printed Mythic, yielding its takeover to it as a Mythic yields to its own wipe, [[R2800000]]), in short, off and still forms, with a standing backdrop kept in the client's memory of the events (an aura, [[R2787000]]; engine state was rejected, since it would have moved every replay hash) and a sting per Anomaly in place of the whoosh. The issue asked for signature entries once #3332 landed, which it did during the build: its tier takes only Legendaries and Mythics, so the Common and Rare probes keep their bespoke recipe, and a Mythic Spell's signature budget (1.95 s) is shorter than the rift's hold, so Anomaly Found! keeps its bespoke call and stays on the signature ratchet ([[R3332000]]).

**[[R3342000]] Every face-up Legendary and Mythic permanent marks its tile with an aura in its picture's colors and set's pattern that answers touch, damage and targets (issue #3342, 2026-11-07)**: standing auras existed only for cards whose table entry gave them an `aura` ([[R2787000]]), mostly Field Spells and Field Traps drawn by `AuraLayer.tsx` over the board. A Legendary or Mythic Unit had no tile feedback of its own once its entrance completed. Every face-up Legendary and Mythic permanent on the board now marks its tile with a DOM aura painting beneath its card: a soft edge glow and slow motes for Legendary, and a richer set pattern (circuitry, runes, ink, starfield, roots) for Mythic, tinted by its picture's painter palette with an id-hashed hue spread so distinct Legendaries differ in look. A card's table aura replaces the default. The aura brightens when selected as a target, flickers on damage, dims while Dormant, Frozen or Silenced, and fades out with its card's destroyed or exiled row. Touching or hovering sends a ripple, plays a bell in its hue (`auraTouch`) and buzzes on phones (`touch` haptic, [[R669]]). Motes stay within `TILE_PARTICLE_SHARE` of the particle cap and the phone frame budget ([[R2803000]]), freeze under reduced motion ([[R438]]), and disable at intensity `off`. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7): no engine, wire or catalog change. Rejected: a canvas overlay (which paints above cards), a view field, and reading the opponent's aim arrow.

**[[R3343000]] A variant's first level may be a card rarity's default look, which the Default draws: Full art's on a Mythic, Extended art's on a Legendary, a token by its printed rarity; offered nowhere there and owned there by every account with no row, so its variant starts at the level above it; rows held before stay and buy nothing more (issue #3343, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2725000]], [[R2725001]], [[R3015000]] and [[R3015003]])**: the owner decided on 2026-11-06 that Mythic-tier cards come in full art by default and that Legendaries come up a step, and the issue asked for the rule as registry data the server, the Collection and the faces share, every account owning it with no ledger row, the level it replaces no longer offered on those cards and its owners losing nothing. The planner put the rule on a level, `defaultOn`, read through `offered` and `owns`, so every 400, roll, pack and dev fill follows with no server change, and exported each card's look for the client. Rejected: a client special case by rarity; a migration granting rows the rule makes redundant; retiring the level outright, which would refuse it on every card; and retiring the animated levels too.

**[[R3343001]] How the default looks draw: a Mythic's Default is Full art while the Full-art cards setting is on, its normal face otherwise; a Legendary's Extended art whatever the setting, and on the board art first, as every Legendary in play is; Prismatic adds the full-art frame, so both its levels are full art, and no colour of it is gold (issue #3343, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2725004]], [[R2944001]], [[R3015002]] and [[R3344000]])**: the issue asked that a Mythic's Default draw full art, a Legendary's Extended art and Prismatic full art at both levels, Radiant keeping its marks. The planner had the Full-art cards setting, which promises a card's text when off, keep a Mythic's normal face then, and left Extended art, which hides no text, on. #3344's depth planes, merged meanwhile, keep Extended art to one picture, so a face drawing a Legendary's Default as Extended art has no depth, and a Legendary wearing a look with planes, Alt art say, has it. Rejected: Prismatic keeping its words, which the issue overruled; a separate full-art level for Prismatic, which would move its owners' animated level up a rank; ignoring the setting; and planes for Extended art, which #3344 declined.

**[[R3343002]] The Collection names and pictures a Mythic's and a Legendary's Default by its default look, lists a variant whose first level is that look from the level above, and marks that level next with nothing owned (issue #3343, 2026-11-07; amends [[R3015007]])**: the issue asked that the Collection show each Default look and the ladders starting where the registry now starts them. Rejected: hiding Full art and Extended art on those cards, which would hide the levels still offered there.

**[[R3344000]] A face that is Epic or rarer, or prints full art, draws its picture as four depth planes by its painter's roles, under a glare by area, and the rest draw the flat picture (issue #3344, 2026-11-07)**: the owner asked to look into quasi-3D models for the rarest cards (#3330); this part is the depth every card can have, which the Mythics' own dioramas and the takeover build on. Until it only Immersive drew its picture in two planes, though the painters already tag each layer by its role ([[R2715001]]) and can draw the backdrop and the subject apart ([[R2943001]]). An Epic, Legendary or Mythic face, and a face printing full art at any level, now stacks the picture in far, middle, near and nearest planes by those roles, each plane the same painter and seed drawing only its own layers; a Legendary or Mythic subject in hand and inspect may break the window's top edge; the glare is white, by area, `color-dodge` on the art and `overlay` on the frame, in isolated groups, never on a Radiant frame ([[R2725004]]); and the Reduce motion setting, the system's and the fx intensity off draw the flat picture with its foil still. Rejected: planes for a delivered file (it has no layers), for the restyles, the Sketch's full art among them (a filter or opaque paper over each plane would not agree where the planes meet), and for Immersive, Out of frame and Extended art (they have a layering of their own); true depth by `translateZ` (the face is an isolated group and `.cf-scale` clips it, so only the face turns in 3D and the planes shift by `translate`); a glare tinted by the foil (it could land in the gold window); a glare layer kept at opacity 0 or hidden while a face rests (a blend mode alone makes the group under it a render surface, which prints the face's text with another antialiasing, so the layers are `display: none` until the face tilts); and a frame-break on every face.

**[[R3344001]] A face with depth tilts toward the pointer, the device or a drag up to `CARD_TILT_MAX_DEG` and springs back, one face at a time and never while the animation queue drains (issue #3344, 2026-11-07)**: the pose is the pointer's place in the face's host box, or in the box of the card the hover preview is anchored to, so it never passes the cap; a card in hand, on the board and in the Collection tilts under its own pointer or touch, the hover preview under the window's pointer, and the inspect sheet under that and a touch drag that holds off the sheet's swipe. The phone's orientation drives it only once the device-only "Tilt with my phone" switch is on, which asks iOS leave inside its tap. One face holds the tilt slot (a drag over a window over a host) and none while `Game`'s queue has an entry in flight. Amended by issue #3412 (2026-11-08): the preview's pointer is on its anchor, never over it. Rejected: tilting every card of a hand at once, a tilt that follows the pointer's place in the viewport as Immersive's shift does, asking leave for the orientation at the first tilt (iOS wants a tap), reading the preview's pose from its own box (issue #3412), and a tilt on the board's minions.

**[[R3346000]] Plays that land with weight: an impact under every readable play sized by the mana it cost and coloured by its type, a trap's snap, a quieter impact for the other seat, and a board shake and a buzz for a heavy or massive play (issue #3346, 2026-11-07; beside [[R669]] and [[R702]])**: the owner asked, in issue #3330, for sounds beside the voice lines, "sort of like how when you play an expensive card, it feels the hit". Cost changed nothing about what the client played: `cardPlayed.costPaid` is public and no cue read it. The impact is sized by it in four tiers, 0 to 2, 3 to 5, 6 to 8 and 9 or more (`PLAY_IMPACT_TIERS`, tuned by ear later), coloured by the card's type, and sits on the effects bus so the voice line ducks it, as it does every effect. A card the viewer cannot name (the sentinel, [[R203]]) gets no impact, so it cannot be told apart from any other face-down card; a Trap's set keeps its click, a card cast as it is drawn keeps its sting, and a firing Trap gets a snap beside its sting. The other seat's impact is turned down by a cue trim of its own, `SfxParams.gain`, read by the engine beside the effect's gain; the existing `quiet` flag turns a cue down to 0.35, which is too low for "a step quieter". A heavy play shakes the board and a massive one shakes it harder, through the effects layer's shake, which already scales by the intensity setting and draws nothing under Reduce Motion ([[R2789000]]), and buzzes a phone by tier behind the Vibration switch. The issue says the shake holds "still under reduced motion" and its acceptance says it is "off under reduced motion"; the acceptance wins, as the effects layer's one rule for reduced motion. The unit's landing thud keeps its stats tier, so a cheap huge Unit and an expensive small one each read right, the impact by cost and the thud by stats 220 ms later. Rejected: sizing the impact by the card's rarity, which the sting already does; a new rarity-and-cost mix, which would make the tiers unreadable by ear; reusing `quiet` for the other seat, which is nearly silent; shaking or buzzing for a card the viewer cannot read, which would colour a face-down card as a landing when [[R203]] gives every one of them the plain whoosh; and a snap for a Field Trap's later firings, which the plain spell sound already answers.

**[[R3389000]] A signature's own recipe wears its own look, claims what its card does outside its play, lays its own sound bed, and a Mythic's takeover may draw its own look (issue #3389, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2722001]], [[R2787000]] and [[R3332000]]; beside [[R3400000]])**: the owner asked for every Legendary and Mythic to have an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone (issue #3330), and #3332's signature tier named only a card's first beat. The spec did not say which looks a signature's recipe could wear, which events outside its play's window it could draw, or how it sounded. On [[R3400000]]'s frame (a bespoke entry's aura, a Unit's on its own tile, a Spell's or a Trap's own cast), the builder added nine looks of the signatures' own that only a signature may wear; let a signature's recipe claim a cast its play makes and an event naming its card as the source, found face up or in a readable remembered event, and draw beside a whole pile's one wave; told an entrance when its card lands after a takeover's hold, a Field Spell's centred on its own zone; gave each a sound bed laid over its entrance's entry; and gave In Too Deep's takeover a look of its own, `abyss`, its still drawn in it. Rejected: a dispatch per card for the beats no window carries, a Unit's aura over its whole lane, and a sound file per card.

**[[R3389001]] Every Classic Legendary and Mythic has its own signature (issue #3389, 2026-11-08)**: seven of Classic's eleven played a shared motif and four a recipe of their own. The builder gave each a recipe after its concept in the issue (an entrance or cast of its own, its text's beat and, a permanent, a standing look of its own), took them off the signature ratchet and moved the beats no event of their card's window carries (Palantir's draw limit, Nature Titan's attack, Big Max's blows and Trample, Second Wind's later graveyard plays, Income Tax's deferred keep) or that would break a budget (Palantir's beam to its crack, Income Tax's chain to its firing, the Tyrant's ash to a card back). Rejected: keeping the four old recipes whole, which left their text's beats to shared motifs.

**[[R3390000]] Every Classic+ entry drawn as a scene: each of its 116 entries, tokens included, draws from a scene recipe of its own in Classic's temple-stone look, base and Radiant, at or above its rarity's detail floor and off the short list, whose cap falls to 445; every humanoid Unit is a figure dressed as who it is, four Units are drawn as a creature or machine of the scene kit, a recipe may stand extra figures in any layer and mark placements that move on the props beat, and the kits grow by the parts the briefs name (issue #3390, 2026-11-07; amends [[R3331000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's art be more its own and richer in its set's style, and this issue is Classic+'s pass, from a brief per entry. The planner kept the pass in one pull request with three recipe files, drew four Units (the Snake, the Book Worm, the J15 Fighter and the Mulch Muncher) as scene-kit creatures, let a recipe stand extra figures and mark props for the full-art cards' motions, and lowered the short list's cap to its new length; the builder put the new scenery parts in three modules beside the first kit, sharing its drawing helpers. Rejected: crowds as silhouettes, creature bodies in the figure kit, dropping `props` from seven full-art motions, keeping the cap at 561, and three pull requests of about 40 entries each.

**[[R3396000]] Every Classic+ card offered Alt art draws an alt scene recipe of its own, its subject restaged in another place, time or moment from a brief per card, at every Alt art level, base and Radiant; it changes at least ALT_PICTURE_CHANGE_MIN of the normal picture's sampled pixels and its sky or composition differs; repaints keep the normal recipe (issue #3396, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2940001]] and [[R3390000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) to go harder on Alt art, keeping each card's theme in another style or place, and this issue restages the 40 Classic+ cards offered Alt art, from a brief per card, where Alt art had redrawn the normal scene re-seeded. The planner kept the alt recipes in a table of their own read under the Alt art seed alone, so the repaints keep the normal recipe; kept each figure the normal picture's person; held every alt sky off its normal sky's colours, every alt picture to its rarity's floor and the byte ceiling, and the pixels to 0.6 in a real browser; and added six scene-kit parts (a boat, a tent, a waterfall, bamboo, a cactus, a fox) for the briefs' places. Rejected: `#alt` keys inside `SCENE_RECIPES`, alt recipes for the repaints, measuring pixels in vitest, holding pictures only to hue or composition, dropping the floor or the ceiling for alt pictures, changing a figure's head or outfit, stand-ins for the parts the briefs name, and smaller pull requests.

**[[R3400000]] A bespoke entry may wear an aura; a Unit's aura may stand on its own tile; three takeover looks with art and a still form; a signature Spell's own cast replaces the shared entrance, its takeover tail shorter (issue #3400, 2026-11-07)**: the signature tier ([[R3332000]]) left a bespoke entry without an aura, a Unit's aura nowhere nearer than its side, every Mythic's own look without art, and a signature Spell's cast under the shared rays, burst, rings and takeover tail, which overran the Mythic Spell's ceiling. A bespoke entry now parses an `aura` as a hooks entry does; `tile`, a Unit's own unit zone, is the nearest aura scope, worn by a face-up Unit alone, never by a hidden one or a modifier its play installs, and in place of [[R3342000]]'s default tile aura; `flurry`, `chalk` and `timber` join the looks; `summit`, `canvas` and `napkin` are Mythics' own takeover looks with tints and art mounted over the takeover's dark, the same art standing still behind Reduce Motion's card ([[R2722001]]); and a signature Spell's own cast draws its play itself, its Mythic takeover keeping only its own look and ending `FX_SIGNATURE_TAKEOVER_TAIL_MS` after its entry. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: reusing `zone` for a Unit, a separate aura table, raising the budgets.

**[[R3400001]] Meditative+'s and Training Grounds' signatures (issue #3400, 2026-11-07)**: the owner asked (2026-11-06) for every Legendary and Mythic to have an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone. Bob's Plan, Bob, Scratch Pad, Seance, Tutorial Master, Call to Chaos (Training-Grounds Edition), the Training-Grounds Golem and Goetzmann Game Design each get a signature built from the issue's concept: a Unit's own entrance, its text's beat and its tile's aura; a Spell's own cast; a Mythic's own takeover with its art; and a sound of its own. Every recipe keys on its own play and how many events it has seen, flies only card backs and names no card ([[R202]]), within the budgets and [[R200]]. Moved to fit: Call to Chaos's [[R436]] panel shrinks to 760 ms inside the Legendary Spell's ceiling, the Golem's Pierce splinter becomes a lock-on, Tutorial Master's erase plays at his Death's first card, Bob's Plan's Discover scroll and the auras' accents are dropped.

**[[R3335000]] Four account kinds dress every Starship a seat launches, a hull design, a livery, a trail and a piece skin, each with a default every account owns; a seat's frozen loadout carries its picks, and only a launched Starship wears them (issue #3335, 2026-11-07; amends [[R1834002]] and [[R3333000]])**: the owner asked for a cosmetic track tied to Stinkyscape, its rewards special as the Jlorious card back is, and the issue for Starship cosmetics that dress every Starship an account launches: a hull design, a livery and a trail, and proposed a fourth, a piece skin. The spec made no rule on them, so the builder followed the plan: four account kinds picked on the account alone, each with a Common default that changes nothing, frozen into each seat's loadout so the opponent sees them, a loadout frozen before them reading the defaults, worn only by a launched Starship. Rejected: a deck slot for each, and dressing a Dormant Starship, which would hide its shipyard.

**[[R3335001]] The Fleet track: thirty levels of Fleet XP from the server's recorded 1v1 games, held to a daily cap, each level paying one Stinkyscape cosmetic once with the reason track (issue #3335, 2026-11-07)**: the issue asked for a track of its own apart from the Stinkyscape set track, earning XP only in the games the server records for each Stinkyscape card played, each Starship Piece fused, each Starship launched and a win with a mostly Stinkyscape deck, under a daily cap, every number a named constant; it proposed 30 levels and left the rest to the plan, which counts plays as proficiency does and gives every level one reward, the Stinkyscape hull last. Co-op pays none yet, its log unfolded. Rejected: a rising level curve, counting created cards, and paying co-op from its unfolded log.

**[[R3335002]] A Fleet track reward is earned only, as the Jlorious cosmetics are: never a chase, a default, a starter or per card, and no roll, pack, token, Chase Shard bar or ladder bot's wardrobe offers it, though a dev account's fill grants it (issue #3335, 2026-11-07; amends [[R1333000]], [[R1834005]] and [[R2755000]])**: the issue asked that the track's rewards be gated as the Jlorious card back is, by a flag the registry check holds and every pool skips. Rejected: leaving them chases by their rarity, which the Chase Shard bar could buy, and keeping them out of the dev fill.

**[[R3335003]] The Jlorious Starship is granted as the Jlorious card back is, by the rated game that leaves a person Jlorious, and migration 0086 grants it once to every earlier Jlorious peak (issue #3335, 2026-11-07; amends [[R2755000]])**: the issue asked for `starship-hull-jlorious`, granted in the same path as the back with the reason `jlorious` and backfilled as 0074 backfilled the back. Rejected: a grant at a season's end alone.

**[[R3340000]] Art that lives in play: animated and signed looks and every Mythic keep moving on the board in a ranked, shared motion budget, and a card's art answers the pointer, a press, damage, a buff, a freeze and an attack (issue #3340, 2026-11-07; amends R3046000 and R3344000)**: a board face wearing an animated or signed level, and every Mythic in any look, moves its art as the full face does, taking one of the `COSMETIC_FOIL_ANIMATED_MAX` motion slots shared with the hand. The slots go to the face under the pointer or focus (and an attacker), then Mythics newest first, Legendaries, then every other face newest first; a freed slot passes on in the same tick, and a visibility pause does not make a returning face newest; motion pauses when the tab is hidden or the face is off screen; Animated foil off, Reduce motion, the system setting and Fx intensity Off hold every face still, and at Low only the faces that rank first, Mythics and Legendaries may move. A moving board face draws its picture as backdrop, subject and motes, so hover leans the subject toward the pointer and focus gives it a keyboard-only turn and lean while both scatter the motes, a press flinches it, and `attackDeclared` swings it toward its target; a Unit's art wears cracks that grow with its missing health, a glow in its card's colours when buffed and frost when Frozen, still marks that stay under reduced motion. Every Legendary and Mythic has a motion recipe of its own. A board face still draws no signature, sweep or drift. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). The fx intensity off holds every face still, a face with no depth among them (amending [[R3344000]], which stilled only the faces with depth).

**[[R3345000]] A Mythic's takeover shows its own full art in depth planes with the camera dollying through them as its name signs itself, and a press-and-hold opens the full-screen view anywhere (issue #3345, 2026-11-07)**: the owner asked that Mythic-tier cards come in full art where they cover the entire screen, in the spirit of Pokémon TCG Pocket's Immersive cards. Every Mythic's in-game takeover now fills the window edge to edge in its own four depth planes ([[R3344000]]), dollying through them over the 1000 ms hold as its name signs itself across the bottom in its set's signature hand ([[R2715002]]) and its intro sting plays ([[R3332002]]), shrinking onto the landing spot on downbeat, while preserving bespoke choreography for the four marquee cards (`core-100`, `core-096`, `core-097`, `meditative-049`). Tap skips it, Big moments short gives 500 ms and off gives 0 ms, reduced motion shows `TakeoverStill` with the card's own picture, a hidden play shows nothing ([[R202]]), and an opponent's public Mythic play triggers the takeover for both seats. A long press on mobile or click-and-hold on desktop opens the full-screen view across hand, inspect, Collection, Almanac and pack openings (on the board for own cards only while the animation queue is idle), with looping camera dolly, intro music, pointer and device tilt ([[R3344001]]), tap to pause/resume, and release or second tap to close. Mythic frames in hand gain a slow edge shimmer in Mythic colors. Rejected: a shared silhouette for Mythics, opening full screen on opponent board cards or while the queue drains, and 3D polygon meshes.

**[[R3349000]] Every variant-level grant reveals the card wearing its new level, rarest last and scaled to its rarity; Legendary and Mythic pack pulls get their own moments; the Collection marks new levels until viewed (issue #3349, 2026-11-09)**: presentation only: a duplicate's roll, a cosmetic pack's slot, a token redemption and a Chase Shard buy each show the card first in its previous look, then wipe into the new level with its name and rarity beneath it, several grants playing in order rarest-last while a pack's own pulls keep the pack's order. Rare and Epic play on the panel with a sound by rarity, Legendary dims the panel and grows the card in with light and its intro sting, Mythic and a Legendary or Mythic chase take the screen in the full-screen view; a Legendary card pulled flares before it flips and a Mythic one tears the pack into its takeover, a roll in a pack plays its own reveal when its slot is opened, and no slot shows before it is opened. An account cosmetic dropped in a pack marks no card new. A card with a newly earned level carries a "new" mark until its view or preview opens, and each ladder rung draws its own level's look. Reduced motion shows stills, `bigMoments` short or off shortens or stills the full-screen ones, and a tap always moves on.

**[[R3351000]] The living game on a phone: five art-first Units, an Anomaly backdrop, a launched Starship and a hand of ten hold the shared phone motion budget under 4x throttling, shedding motes, then far auras, then faces (issue #3351, 2026-11-09)**: part of #3330. Five art-first Units (two Legendary and one Mythic among them) with tile auras, an Anomaly's backdrop, a launched Starship, and a hand of ten with one tilted hold, at 390x844, 360x780 and 844x390 under 4x CPU throttling, a mean frame within `FX_PHONE_FRAME_BUDGET_MS` and a worst single frame within the new `FX_PHONE_FRAME_WORST_MS` (120 ms) through a Mythic takeover, a Starship launch and broadside, a Drone Battleship arrival, a Terminus set piece, a hand-to-tile flight and a riffle. Moving faces, tile auras, backdrops, particles and tilt share `PHONE_MOTION_BUDGET` (six motion units) on a phone through per-layer budgets — the faces' share is the motion slots, tile and aura motes scale with intensity against the phone particle cap, tilt holds one claim — and the director sheds motes, then far auras, then the moving faces furthest from the pointer, never a set piece's main beats. The one-screen rule ([[R1581000]]) and the tap areas ([[R2122000]]) hold with all of it on, and a long press opens inspect from every board face and hand card and the full-screen view where [[R3345000]] allows it. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7).

**[[R3392000]] Core's scenes: every Core entry but Ceaseless Void draws a scene recipe of its own from its brief, on both faces, at or above its floor and off the short list; a Unit's character is a figure or a creature part; Speciesist and Heroic Power are redrawn; the full-art Core cards' props are placements marked `props` (issue #3392, 2026-11-08; amends [[R3331000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's art be more unique and richer in its set's style, simpler for small cards and elaborate for Mythics, its Spells and Field Spells real scenes, and this issue gave each Core entry but Ceaseless Void a brief to draw its scene recipe from. The plan put Core's 110 scenes in three recipe files in Core's family, gave the figure kit the poses, heads, headgear, outfits and items the briefs' people need and the scene kit their places, creatures, props and weather, stood a scene's other people as extra figures ([[R3390000]]'s `extras`), drew beasts and objects that do not stand like a person as creature parts, kept KY in one look, redrew the two Core proofs from their new briefs, marked the placements the full-art Core cards' props beat moves `props`, and tightened the short list's cap from 445 to 337. Rejected: a figure body for beasts and objects, keeping the two proof scenes, keeping motif glyphs in scenes, moving the props beat to the near layer, a kit module of Core's own, and leaving the cap at 445.

**[[R3393000]] Meditative+ and Training Grounds drawn as scenes: each entry a recipe of its own from its brief in its family's look, every Unit a figure-kit character, other characters as extra figures, the kits grown by the briefs' parts, and fades (issue #3393, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2202000]] and [[R3331000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's picture be more its own and richer, simpler for small cards and elaborate for Mythics, and this issue gave each Meditative+ and Training Grounds entry a brief. The plan gave each of the 74 entries of the two sets' own a recipe in its family's look, Meditative+'s ink-wash peaks under one red seal and Training Grounds' carved yard, built every Unit from the figure kit, grew the kits by the parts the briefs named in modules of their own beside the kit, stood a brief's other characters as extra figures ([[R3390000]]), and added a fade for a ghost or a copy; the yard painter draws only a card with no recipe. Rejected: a painter per card, the new scenery in the first kit's file, keeping the yard painter for the cards already over their floor, and drawing extra characters as scenery parts.

**[[R3393001]] A Legacy card draws a scene of its own with its yard touch, wearing its Core card's frozen emblem, still one picture, never its Core card's scene (issue #3393, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2724000]] and [[R3331000]])**: the issue asked that the Legacy cards keep their link to their Core card's subject, now in a scene of their own. **[[R3395000]] Stinkyscape 001 to 090's signatures (issue #3395, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2797000]])**: the owner asked (2026-11-06) for every Legendary and Mythic to have an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone. The 24 of `stinkyscape-1.json5` that #3395's table names (Olivian Expanse and Drone Core X are #3338's and #3336's) each get a signature from the issue's concept: a Unit's own entrance (a figure of its concept), its text's beat and a tile aura in its palette; a Field Spell's arrival, beat and own aura look; a Spell's own cast, Imitated Instrumentality's `magiBoot` growing from its flourish, each MAGI's own entrance ([[R3407000]]) beside its boot; a Mythic's own takeover with art; and a sound of its own, a Field Spell's as it lands in the backrow ([[R3407001]]). **[[R3395001]] Five takeover looks with art, figures, three signature aura looks, and a signature's beat replacing its event's system moment (issue #3395, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2722001]], [[R2784000]], [[R2787000]], [[R3332000]] and [[R3389000]])**: Stinkyscape's signatures needed Tylannt's `vault`, Rod of Ages' `rod`, Great Paperclip Theory's `wire`, Paragon-X's `whiteDwarf` and Prototype-X2's `forge`, each with art and a still; 27 figures of their concepts; `laserGrid`, `silverRise` and `labHaze` as signature aura looks; and, since the shared quest banner alone overran Tylannt's Mythic ceiling, a beat that opts in (`moment: "replace"`, Tylannt's and the Heist's quest beats alone) now replaces its event's system moment too, every other beat drawing beside it as before (on a sweep, [[R3405000]]'s `planSignatureSweep` already lifts the fog off Flak v5's waves); and a signature's ceiling counts its own beats, another signature's own entrance inside its play (the MAGI's under Imitated Instrumentality, [[R3407000]]) held to that card's ceiling. **[[R3397000]] Every Meditative+ and Training Grounds card offered Alt art draws an alt scene recipe of its own, its subject restaged in another place, time or moment from a brief per card, at every Alt art level, base and Radiant; it changes at least ALT_PICTURE_CHANGE_MIN of the normal picture's sampled pixels and its sky or composition differs; repaints keep the normal recipe (issue #3397, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2940001]] and [[R3393000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) to go harder on Alt art, keeping each card's theme in another style or place, and after [[R3396000]] restaged Classic+ this issue restages the 36 Meditative+ and Training Grounds cards offered Alt art, from a brief per card, where Alt art had redrawn the normal scene re-seeded (a median 26% of the pixels changed, the least 10%). The planner kept the alt recipes in two tables of their own beside Classic+'s, read under the Alt art seed alone, so the repaints keep the normal recipe; kept each figure and each person a brief keeps the normal picture's; kept Meditative+'s red seal but not its peaks, and Training Grounds without one; held every alt sky off its normal sky's colours, every alt picture to its rarity's floor and the byte ceiling, and the pixels to 0.6 in a real browser; and added thirteen scene-kit parts (a goat, a deer, a duck, walnuts, a crater, a sandcastle, a snowman, a water wheel, a rowboat, a cork board, a washing line, a picnic blanket, a rope) for the briefs' subjects and places.
