**[[R3398000]] Every Core card offered Alt art draws an alt scene recipe of its own, its subject restaged in another place, time or moment from a brief per card, at every Alt art level, base and Radiant; it changes at least ALT_PICTURE_CHANGE_MIN of the normal picture's sampled pixels and its sky or composition differs; Ceaseless Void's is the void before it wakes, without its giant (issue #3398, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2940001]], [[R3338001]], [[R3392000]] and [[R3396000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) to go harder on Alt art, keeping each card's theme in another style or place, and this issue restages the 61 Core cards offered Alt art, from a brief per card, as [[R3396000]] restaged Classic+'s, where Alt art had redrawn the normal scene re-seeded. The planner kept the alt recipes in three files of their own beside Classic+'s in `ALT_SCENE_RECIPES`, read under the Alt art seed alone, so the repaints keep the normal recipe; kept each figure the normal picture's person and each kept crowd its people; stood an extra figure for a new person where a brief changes who is in the picture; dropped Ceaseless Void's giant from its Alt art, its brief the void before it wakes; held every alt sky off its normal sky's colours, every alt picture to its rarity's floor and the byte ceiling, and the pixels to 0.6 in a real browser; and added ten scene-kit parts (a palm, a camel, a crater, a van, a hay bale, a picnic blanket, a rug, a hammock, a sleeping bag, a teapot) for the briefs' places, standing the small details in with parts the kits hold. Rejected: keeping the giant in Ceaseless Void's Alt art, a lying pose for the sleepers, new parts for the small details, pull requests of about 40 cards, changing a figure's head or outfit, and alt recipes for the repaints.

**[[R3399000]] Classic+'s signatures: each of its 35 Legendaries and Mythics, tokens by printed rarity, plays an animation of its own, one concept per card (issue #3399, 2026-11-08)**: the owner asked (2026-11-06) for every Legendary and Mythic to have an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone, instead of a shared one; 15 of Classic+'s 16 played a shared motif and 8 of its 19 tokens printed Legendary or Mythic nothing of their own. Each of the 35 is now its signature ([[R3332000]]): a Unit or a Field Spell lands with an entrance of its own, then plays its text's beat in a recipe of its own and wears an aura of its own on its tile; a Spell casts in a recipe of its own; a Mythic takes a takeover of its own. `classicplus.md`'s Signatures table records each card's beats and verdict, `as concept` or where a beat moved to fit the budgets or the events the view carries. Each card's own cues end within its signature ceiling of the entry they play in; a person chose that measure for [[R3332000]]'s budget test too ([[R3399005]]). Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: shared motifs in new palettes, and a set piece for every Spell.

**[[R3399001]] A Classic+ signature permanent wears an aura of its own beside its recipe or alone, a Unit's on its own tile, each look its card's own (issue #3399, 2026-11-08)**: a signature permanent wears an aura of its own on its tile. On [[R3400000]]'s frame, a `{ bespoke }` entry carries one (`entryAura` reads either kind's) and each of Classic+'s thirteen signature Units wears a `tile` one on its own unit zone (keyed `unit-<instanceId>`; a hidden Unit none, a modifier its play adds no second copy); 18 new signature looks join [[R3389000]]'s, each one card's. Such an aura replaces the default tile aura of [[R3342000]]. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: a separate tile-aura table, a Unit's `zone` aura beside the `tile`, and shared looks with palettes.

**[[R3399002]] A Classic+ Mythic's takeover draws a picture of its own over the board, and Reduce Motion shows it still (issue #3399, 2026-11-08)**: the issue asks for each Mythic's takeover to show "its own art filling the screen", but the card art is procedural and no painted asset ships. Each of Classic+'s four Mythics takes a look of its own (`zealot`, `portal`, `stair`, `vineyard`) with a full-frame picture drawn for it (`fx/takeoverArt.ts`, in the art layer of [[R3400000]]'s looks), shown over the darkened board while its takeover plays and still under Reduce Motion; the picture is no card face and names no card ([[R2722001]], [[R202]]). Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: enlarging the card's art, which would show a face over the board.

**[[R3399003]] A signature's beat only the owner may see plays on the owner's seat alone, and from the other seat every card it puts in or takes from a hand or deck flies as a back (issue #3399, 2026-11-08)**: several Classic+ signatures show cards the other seat may not read (a Discover's options, a test's problems, cards added to a hand, shuffled in or stolen). An owner-only beat plays only when the card's player is the viewer, every card in flight is a back between piles and zones, and a hidden play keys nothing ([[R202]]); a test plays each signature from the opponent's seat over redacted events. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: drawing a card's face for its owner in flight.

**[[R3399004]] Each Classic+ signature plays a sound effect of its own on its play or cast (issue #3399, 2026-11-08)**: each of the 35 names an effect of its own in `card-audio.json5` ([[R655]]), a procedural `sfx.ts` recipe nearest its concept's first sound with a pitch, gain and options no other shares, on `play` for a Unit and `cast` otherwise, beside its voice. They render nothing and add no asset. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: rendered sound files.

**[[R3399005]] A signature's budget counts its own cues alone, each from the start of the entry it plays in, and a Mythic's takeover keeps the Mythic's whole budget (issue #3399, 2026-11-08)**: [[R3332000]]'s budget held a signature's every cue over its whole gallery play from the play's start, the rows every card's events share included, so no Legendary Spell, whose text's events come after its cast, and no Mythic Spell, whose shared takeover ran past three quarters of a Mythic's, could keep it. Asked on the pull request (Open question 1), a person chose the per-beat measure: only the signature's own cues (its entrance or takeover, its motif or recipes, its set piece) count, each within its ceiling of its entry's start, a Mythic's takeover within the Mythic's whole. New ceilings for the whole-play measure were rejected as loosening every set's signatures to fit rows no signature draws.

**[[R3401000]] Every Meditative entry drawn as a scene: each of its 132 entries, tokens included, draws from a scene recipe of its own in Meditative's ink-wash look, base and Radiant, at or above its rarity's detail floor and off the short list, whose cap falls from 289 to 157; every picture stamps one red seal; every person Unit is a figure dressed as who it is and 21 Units that are no person draw a part of the scene kit; the two full-art motions that move props move marked placements; and the kits grow by the parts the briefs name (issue #3401, 2026-11-08; beside [[R3390000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's art be more its own and richer in its set's style, and this issue is Meditative's pass, from a brief per entry. The planner kept the pass in one pull request, drew the Units that are no person as scene-kit parts, put one red seal on every picture as the art bible's palette names it, drew crowds as small brush figures, and lowered the short list's cap to its new length; the builder followed Classic+'s pattern, which landed after the plan: five recipe files and eight kit modules beside Classic+'s, and marked props for the two full-art motions in place of rewriting their beats. Rejected: those Units as figures, crowds as full figures, a seal stamped by the painter on every Meditative card, rewriting the two motions' `props` beats, keeping the cap at 289, and four pull requests of about 33 entries each.

**[[R3403000]] Stinkyscape's later cards drawn as scenes: each of the 103 entries of #3403's table (Stinkyscape #91 to #176 and their tokens, and the tokens of the early cards it names) draws from a scene recipe of its own in Stinkyscape's deep-space look, base and Radiant, at or above its rarity's detail floor and off the short list, whose cap falls to 129; every humanoid Unit is a figure dressed as who it is, fourteen Units are drawn as a creature or a machine of the scene kit, the cards other issues draw keep their pictures, and the kits grow by the parts the briefs name in four modules beside the others (issue #3403, 2026-11-08; amends [[R3331000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's art be more its own and richer in its set's style, and this issue is the pass over Stinkyscape's later cards, from a brief per entry. The planner kept the pass in one pull request with three recipe files, drew fourteen Units (the Wasp and the Hornet, Megabase, the Sack, the Golem, the four Asteroids, the Spice Tower, the two drones, Escort Protocol and Combat Subroutine) as scene-kit creatures and machines, stood crews and rivals as extra figures and lowered the short list's cap to its new length; #3390 landed first with `extras` and the kit's shared helpers, so the builder kept both and put the new space parts in four modules beside #3390's, spread into `SCENE_KIT`; #3392, #3393 and #3401 landed during the build with a pointing pose, a hoodie, headphones, a vest, a parka and an axe of their own, so the pass wears their pose, hoodie and headphones and names its sleeveless vest, plated parka and shock-axe apart, as it names its bench, tent, ticker tape, streaks, wave, crowd, sack, crane, rift and blueprint apart from the kit's. Rejected: creature bodies in the figure kit, crowds as silhouettes only, growing `sceneKit.ts` itself, keeping the cap at 157, and parts of about 40 entries each in their own pull requests.

**[[R3404000]] Every Meditative card offered Alt art draws an alt scene recipe of its own, its subject restaged in another place, time or moment from a brief per card, at every Alt art level, base and Radiant; it changes at least ALT_PICTURE_CHANGE_MIN of the normal picture's sampled pixels and its sky and composition differ; repaints keep the normal recipe (issue #3404, 2026-11-09; amends [[R2940001]] and [[R3401000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) to go harder on Alt art, keeping each card's theme in another style or place, and this issue restages the 54 Meditative cards offered Alt art, from a brief per card, where Alt art had redrawn the normal ink-wash scene re-seeded and changed a median 42% of its pixels. As [[R3396000]] did for Classic+, the alt recipes sit in `ALT_SCENE_RECIPES`, read under the Alt art seed alone, so the repaints keep the normal recipe; each figure stays the normal picture's person; every alt sky is off its normal sky's colours, every alt picture stamps Meditative's one red seal and clears its rarity's floor and the byte ceiling, and the pixels hold to 0.6 in a real browser; and three scene-kit parts (an oar, a sofa, a tower crane) join for the briefs. Rejected: smaller pull requests, stand-ins for the sofa and the oar, another family for the Alt pictures, holding pictures only to hue or composition, measuring pixels in vitest, dropping the floor or the ceiling for alt pictures, and changing a figure's head, outfit or skin.

**[[R3405000]] Twelve more takeover looks with art, five aura looks, an entrance that starts after its entry's hold, a Mythic Field Spell put onto the field face up enters, one takeover per Mythic per action, a sound as a Field Spell lands, and a signature's recipe drawn in a sweep (issue #3405, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2722001]], [[R2785000]], [[R2787000]], [[R3332000]], [[R3389000]] and [[R3400000]])**: Meditative's thirteen Mythics each needed a takeover of their own with art filling the screen, its permanents a standing look of their own, and its Mythic Units' and Field Spells' entrances played under their takeover, unseen; The Cane, which Pareto Optimality puts onto the field without a play, made no entrance at all, and a flood of Yile's Viruses took over once a Virus. `ascent`, `workshop`, `brush`, `mine`, `quarry`, `pavilion`, `virus`, `dojo`, `studyHall`, `caneRack`, `warRoom` and `fortress` join the Mythics' own takeover looks, each with tints and art that Reduce Motion shows still, and `ai` draws art of its own ([[R3400000]]); `ripple`, `orbit`, `steam`, `incense` and `glitch` join the shared aura looks ([[R2787000]]); beside its landing ([[R3389000]]), an entrance is passed its entry's lead (`leadMs`) and a Mythic's starts after its takeover's hold; a Mythic Field Spell's face-up `summoned` into the backrow without a play makes its entrance and takeover ([[R2722001]], [[R3332000]]); a Mythic whose card already took over in the action yields its takeover ([[R2785000]]); a signature sound may sound as a Field Spell lands face up in the backrow (the row [[R3407001]] names); and a signature's recipe is asked about a sweep's events of its own play or hook, as a motif is ([[R2784000]]), so Introspection's mends and thought-darts, a sweep in any game where the other side has a Unit, draw out of the board's fog. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: one look for every Meditative Mythic, a look of its own for every permanent, a takeover for every copy in one action, entrances under the takeover, a sound per event, the fog over a signature's own sweep.

**[[R3405001]] Every Meditative Legendary and Mythic has its own signature (issue #3405, 2026-11-08)**: of Meditative's 32 Legendaries and Mythics, its tokens by their printed rarity among them, most played a shared motif (`deal` and `conjure` for most), six played nothing (Keymaster Keenus, Mayor Medinamogger and four tokens) and the owner asked for an animation of its own for each, as in Hearthstone (issue #3330). On the frame of [[R3400000]], [[R3389000]] and [[R3405000]], the builder gave each a recipe after its concept in the issue: a Unit's entrance of its own, its text's beat and a standing look on its tile; a Spell's cast of its own; a Field Spell's entrance as it lands face up, its text's beat and a look on its zone or lane; each Mythic's takeover in a look of its own with art; and its own sound. It took them off the signature ratchet and moved the beats no event of their card's window carries or that would break a budget, every card flying to a pile a card back and every beat only its owner may see a sheen on that owner's seat alone ([[R202]]). Rejected: a shared motif kept for a card whose text no event names (Mayor Medinamogger's picks play in his entrance instead), and a beat drawn for an event outside its card's own play or hook.

**[[R3406000]] Every card of #3406's table, Stinkyscape's later cards offered Alt art, draws an alt scene recipe of its own, its subject restaged in another place, time or moment from a brief per card, at every Alt art level, base and Radiant; it changes at least ALT_PICTURE_CHANGE_MIN of the normal picture's sampled pixels and its sky or composition differs; repaints keep the normal picture, and a Starship Piece's, a Jlockeed weapon's and Dice of Doom's normal face keeps its painter (issue #3406, 2026-11-09; amends [[R2940001]], [[R3333001]], [[R3338001]] and [[R3403000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) to go harder on Alt art, keeping each card's theme in another style or place, and after [[R3396000]] and [[R3397000]] restaged Classic+, Meditative+ and Training Grounds this issue restages the 54 Stinkyscape cards from #91 on offered Alt art, from a brief per card, where Alt art had redrawn the normal picture re-seeded (a median 20% of the pixels changed, the least 1%). The planner kept the alt recipes in a table of their own in three files, read under the Alt art seed alone, so the repaints and the normal face keep what they drew, the eighteen cards with no normal scene (the Starship Pieces, Jlockeed's three weapons and Dice of Doom) their own painter's picture; kept each figure and each person a brief keeps the normal picture's; held every alt sky off its normal sky's colours (off black and slate-900 for the eighteen), every alt picture to its rarity's floor and the byte ceiling, and the pixels to 0.6 in a real browser; and added fifteen scene-kit parts in a module of their own (twelve pieces, a pineapple, an escalator, a seashell) for the briefs' subjects. Rejected: a piece's module markup converted into kit shapes, the module redrawn in another bay, the new parts in #3403's modules, new figure parts, an alt recipe for the Dormant Starship, and smaller pull requests.

**[[R3407000]] Stinkyscape part two's signatures: the three MAGI, xxFreshJack, iKwzki, Shockwave447, Job Application and Paycheck, Scornboi, Megabase, IN33DH3, IN33DH3 Prime and the Wardrum Blob, Alteran Reach and Edge of the Universe, Wildar, both Calls to Chaos and the Stinkyscape Golem, Sanctum, Stimmycheck, Narcor and Red Narcor (issue #3407, 2026-11-08)**: the owner asked (2026-11-06) for every Legendary and Mythic to have an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone. Each of the 23 left to Stinkyscape's part two gets a signature built from the issue's concept: a Unit's own entrance, its text's beats and its own look on its tile; a Field Spell's own entrance, its text's beats and its own look on its zone; a Spell's own cast. Its six Mythics take takeover looks of their own (`freshJack`, `ikwzki`, `shockwave`, `alteran`, `edge`, `redNarcor`) that draw the card's own picture, the one its face shows, filling the screen over the takeover's dark, the same picture standing still behind Reduce Motion's card ([[R2722001]]); fifteen aura looks join, each one card's own; and a card's own entrance and the readable play's recipe both draw on a summon they both claim (the MAGI landing under Imitated Instrumentality's boots). Every beat keys on public events, flies only card backs, reaches a hand or a deck only through its pile and names no card ([[R202]]), within the signature budgets ([[R3332000]]) and [[R200]]. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Moved to fit: each gallery play holds at most one keyed beat past its own events (the MAGI's ballots, the Calls' rolls and the hook beats proven from their own markers), and what no event carries is left out (the Pareto lights, Stimmycheck's raised costs, Paycheck's roulette).

**[[R3407001]] Stinkyscape part two's signature sounds (issue #3407, 2026-11-08)**: each of the 23 cards sounds its own, a synthesized recipe of its own layering the issue's sound cell, as [[R3400001]]'s do: a Spell's as its play is read, a Unit's as it lands face up in a unit zone and, new, a Field Spell's as it lands face up in the backrow (`summoned` into `backrow` with no `formerId`), never for a card set face down or a hidden one ([[R203]]), each within the Legendary signature ceiling. Rejected: sequences of the existing recipes, which would sound like other cards.

**[[R3407002]] A hook's marker opens its entry on the live board (issue #3407, 2026-11-08)**: the review of #3432 found that no beat keyed on a card's hook ([[R2797000]]) drew in a game: the `hookResolving` marker's entry lasts 0 ms with a row of none, the runner skipped it without a signal, and the effects layer remembers an entry only on its `start` or `still`, so no hook entry ever opened. The runner now passes each such entry (`pass`) to the effects layer, which remembers it and draws nothing for it; no beat is keyed on the marker itself. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: a duration for the marker's row, which would hold every hook's resolution, and folding it into the next entry, which would change what every set piece claims.

**[[R3447000]] A card's statistics name the patch they were collected on, the patch before while a newer patch gathers its games (issue #3447, 2026-11-08; amends [[R2325000]] and [[R3185000]])**: while a card's own window is short of live games its figures are its previous window's ([[R1367000]]), but the card's Statistics panel named no patch and `/stats`' games tile named the patch shown over games collected on the patch before. The owner asked for the temporary figures to say which patch they were collected on. The panel now reads "Collected on patch X" beside its title, X the listed patch holding the first version its figures count (the patch before while a newer one gathers its games, the micro's patch for a window a micro opened, the version itself from a server with no list), and nothing for a card with no window. The games tile names the patch before the one shown while the response's figures are the previous window's, else the patch shown. No source, gate or count is shown ([[R661]]). A patch in every table row, hiding borrowed figures, the card's own window's first version and a new response field were rejected. It takes R3447000, the first number of issue #3447's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3471000]] A micro after a version ending in `z` opens the next line (issue #3471, 2026-11-09; amends [[R1366000]])**: the `v0.5.12` line holds 25 micro slots (`b` … `z`), and `cargo jackioh patches ship` refused on main with two honest visual-only micros pending and one letter left, naming neither and writing nothing. After `vA.B.Cz` a micro ships as `vA.B.(C+1)` with no letter, the rollover a patch already takes, and the micro after that takes the line's first letter again; every later reader keys off the recorded `tier`, never the version ([[R105]]). Rejected: merging the fragments, which loses each pull request's record and recurs the next full line, and a fake `rule` or `plays` claim, which `classify --claims` fails ([[R1370000]]).

**[[R3476000]] A dungeon run's unfinished fight waits where it stood (issue #3476, 2026-11-09; amends [[R2706000]] and [[R2972000]]):** a run fight in progress is kept like [[R668]]'s free game — config, action log, AI-stream cursor, hash — in a slot of its own keyed by the fight, never touching the free game's save; the run resumes it on its turn when started again, dropping a save from another fight, another catalog, or one that folds differently; a finished fight, an abandoned run and a new run keep nothing; the leave dialog says the fight waits.

**[[R3479000]] A decision whose view names mostly cards the learned network has no row for keeps the hand evaluation (issue #3479, 2026-11-09)**: the issue counted 494 of 1,200 cards dealt into the AI's decks (41.2%, from 16% to 64% a deck) that the shipped network, fitted on catalog v0.3.17, has no row for, while that network still took half of every 1v1 finalist's score ([[R1012001]]) and [[R3038003]] had kept the hand evaluation only for a root that holds a Starship. The spec made no rule on it, so the builder followed the plan, with the maintainer's word that the switch keep working for every later set with no edit: a card is unknown by the network's own keys alone, with no set, card-id prefix or catalog version named in the rule or its code, the threshold is a share of the copies the redacted view names, the Starship fact stays its own, and the tests ask a fixture network which ids it lacks. The screen set down before it ran, 400 paired games on the improve lane's dev set with the threshold at a half against the build before it (`3f8896c61d`, its network's `catalog: v0.3.17`), read 204 to 196, edge +0.020 (−0.017 to +0.057): even, no gain measured, so the switch ships on [[R3038003]]'s ground, with a loss beyond 0.017 of edge ruled out. Rejected: a list of the sets the network lacks, folding the fact into the Starship one, reading it off each position scored, counting hidden cards, a continuous blend, and keeping the better of several screened thresholds.

**[[R3352000]] Classic's scenes: every Classic entry but Corpse Plantation, the Glitch included, draws a scene recipe of its own from its brief, on both faces, in Classic's look, at or above its floor and off the short list; a Unit's character is a figure or, for one that is no person, a creature part; In Too Deep's props are placements marked `props` (issue #3352, 2026-11-09; amends [[R3331000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's art be more unique and richer in its set's style, simpler for small cards and elaborate for Mythics, its Spells and Field Spells real scenes, and this issue gave each Classic entry but Corpse Plantation, #3331's proof, a brief to draw its scene recipe from. The plan put the 90 scenes in three recipe files in Classic's family, gave the figure kit the crouch, heads, headgear, outfits and items the briefs' people need and the scene kit their creatures, props, a crater and In Too Deep's spiral shaft in a module of its own, stood a scene's other people as extra figures ([[R3390000]]'s `extras`), drew the six Units that are no person as creature parts, gave the Glitch a scene though its window shows none, marked In Too Deep's props placements `props`, and tightened the short list's cap from 129 to 10. Rejected: a figure body for beasts, leaving the Glitch on the old painter, a `crowd` field beside `extras`, moving In Too Deep's props beat to its motes, adding the scenery to `sceneKit.ts` itself, and leaving the cap at 129.

**[[R3388000]] Classic's Alt art restaged: every Classic card offered Alt art draws an alt scene recipe of its own from a brief per card, its subject in another place, time or moment, at every Alt art level, base and Radiant; its sky and composition differ and it changes at least `ALT_PICTURE_CHANGE_MIN` of the normal picture's sampled pixels (issue #3388, 2026-11-10; amends [[R2940001]], [[R3331000]], [[R3352000]])**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) to "go harder on alt art", each Alt art clearly apart from its card's normal picture, and this issue gave each of the 50 Classic cards offered Alt art a brief, restaging #3352's scenes and Corpse Plantation's proof scene ([[R3331000]]), whose Alt art had redrawn the same subject re-seeded ([[R2940001]], [[R3352000]]) and changed as little as 13% of the picture. The plan wrote the 50 alt recipes in three files in Classic's family, read under the Alt art seed alone as [[R3396000]] and [[R3398000]] set, kept each figure's person and each kept crowd's people, gave the kit a frog and a shark in a module of their own spread in before the Stinkyscape Alt art pass's, drew every other missing thing with a stand-in named in its recipe, and held every alt picture to a sky sharing no colour with its normal one's and a new camera. Rejected: splitting the 50 cards into pull requests of about 40, a slime or a koi for the frogs and the shark, a turned figure for Joro hanging upside down, a figure behind the close-ups of hands and objects, and a different sky or composition rather than both.

**[[R3391000]] Stinkyscape's scenes: each of the 64 Stinkyscape cards of issue #3391's table (cards 1 to 90 but the Starship Pieces and Olivian Expanse) draws a scene recipe of its own from its brief, base and Radiant, a Unit its character, each at or over its rarity's floor and off the short list, richer the rarer; the scene kit gains craft and a placement's turn (issue #3391, 2026-11-08)**: the owner asked on 2026-11-06 (#3330) that every card's picture be more its own and richer, and this issue gave each Stinkyscape card in its table a brief to draw. The plan kept one recipe per card in a file of Stinkyscape's own, made a ship Unit's character its craft part in a new craft kind of scenery, added the parts the briefs named to the scene and figure kits, and let a placement turn a pointing part, opt-in so the other proofs keep their bytes; after [[R3390000]] landed, its further figures and the parts it had added for the same things (under the same names, and its drum, playing card and scales) took the place of the pass's own, and after [[R3392000]] landed its lab coat, work apron, anvil and conveyor did too, the pass keeping its axe head as a part of its own and renaming three parts whose names that pass had taken for other things; after [[R3401000]], [[R3403000]] and the Alt art passes landed, it renamed the eighteen parts whose names they had taken for drawings of their own, and left their Stinkyscape table to them; the builder redrew Arg Arg Defender's proof to its brief and gave each Mythic a part of its own. Rejected: ships in the figure kit, two parts drawing one thing, turning every part, ships filed under objects, one growing `sceneRecipes.ts`, lowering the short list's maximum, and recolouring the briefs to the bible's palette.

**[[R3402000]] Core's signatures grow the frame: `rotunda` and `crystalForge` join the Mythics' own takeover looks and `pawn` turns indigo, a look may draw a full-screen scene of shapes, still under Reduce Motion, three of its signature aura looks join R3389000's, and a gallery play's later actions are timed from their own start (issue #3402, 2026-11-08)**: the owner asked (2026-11-06) for every Legendary and Mythic to have an animation of its own, as in Hearthstone. [[R3400000]]'s frame carried Core's ten but for four things: Heroic Power's `rotunda` and Craft a Card's `crystalForge` as own takeover looks, My Pawn's `pawn` in indigo; a look's full-screen scene of shapes for `pawn`, `genie`, `rotunda` and `crystalForge`, a drawing beside [[R3399002]]'s (`fx/takeoverArt.ts`), at most `TAKEOVER_ART_MAX_SHAPES`, moved by transform and opacity, drawn at rest behind the still card under Reduce Motion ([[R2722001]]); the `mirrorball`, `stadium` and `pedestal` aura looks, among [[R3389000]]'s signature looks, each its card's alone; and each later action of a gallery play timed against the budget from its own start, as [[R3399005]]'s per-beat measure times it. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). Rejected: art keyed by the card's id or drawn from its picture, which would put a card on a layer that reads no view, and redrawing [[R3400000]]'s parts as scenes.

**[[R3402001]] Core's ten Legendary and Mythic signatures, each with a sound of its own (issue #3402, 2026-11-08)**: Silly Silas, Transmogulate, Unlicensed Experimentation, Pocket Chaos, Combo-Index, Call to Chaos (Core Edition), My Pawn, Zephrys, Heroic Power and Craft a Card come off `SIGNATURE_PENDING`, each with its recipe in `apps/web/src/fx/cards/core/` built from its concept: a Unit's entrance and its text's beat, a Spell's cast, a permanent's arrival, text and aura, and a Mythic's takeover with art of its own. Each keeps the signature budgets ([[R3332000]]), [[R200]] and one set piece per action, anchors on heroes, piles, zones, the board and readable cards alone, and names no card ([[R202]]); a Trap draws nothing until it fires. Beats moved to fit the budgets or the events the view carries are named in the ruling (My Pawn's strings and the two Field Spells' auras stay with their play, a recipe drawing only while its card resolves). Each sounds its own sound once, on its first beat, never for a card the seat cannot read ([[R203]]). Ceaseless Void (#3338) is the set's last pending signature. Rejected: a horn per grade of Combo-Index, and remembering a card across the turn.

**[[R3477000]] Each seat's view carries the engine's forecast of its attacks, and aiming one shows it (issue #3477, 2026-11-09; amends [[R3100000]])**: the engine's combat forecast reached a player only in a tutorial lesson, though trades are misjudged everywhere: Divine Shield, Armor, Pierce, caps, Indestructible and First Strike stack on plain attack-against-health. Every practice snapshot, a seat's 1v1 and Duos `view` frame and the shared match host's seat view now carry it (a Duos seat's only for the attacks it is offered), and a watcher's never does. While an attacker is selected or dragged, each legal target shows its entry's damage dealt, damage taken back, a skull on whichever dies and a struck-through shield mark when a Divine Shield breaks; a setting, on by default, hides it, and a lesson or a replay draws none. It reads the face-up board alone, so it is a derived fact and no promise, and its title says a face-down trap can still change it. Rejected: client maths (CLAUDE.md rule 7), practice only, labels in a lesson.

**[[R3496000]] A dungeon run folds under the catalog it started on (issue #3496, 2026-11-09):** a run kept across a card patch picks up where it stood, folding and playing on the catalog version it started on; a run that cannot be folded at all ends lost but keeps what it had extracted of what the current content knows, and the start screen says so in one line; a fight's game is built under the current catalog, leaving out cards it no longer knows.

**[[R3497000]] Hidden tabs drain the animation queue and flushed bursts keep the turn-start cue (issue #3497, 2026-11-09)**: Hearthstone has no hidden-tab client, so the spec did not decide it. On an online board, while the page is hidden each new view drains the animation queue, and a tab that turns visible with steps still queued drains them then, so coming back shows the live board with its moves ready at once while the game log still lists every event; practice, dungeon and opened-replay boards never drain, and a visible tab behaves exactly as before. A flushed burst still sends the viewer's own turn-start cue, as it keeps the game-over sting. Rejected: always animating the backlog while the turn clock runs, and dropping the turn-start cue.

**[[R3498000]] A decision a host's clock cuts short stops exploring at `AI_WRAP_UP_SHARE` of the time and still scores its finalists after the reply on every sampled world (issue #3498, 2026-11-09)**: the issue found that the search's reserve for its finalists' replies and their other sampled worlds was counted in nodes, not time, so a decision whose host clock (the browser's `PRACTICE_AI_CLOCK_MS`, the ladder bots' and the Duos AI team's `BOT_DECISION_DEADLINE_MS`) fired while its beam was still exploring replied to no finalist and judged each on one world, on exactly the slow devices and busy boards where that matters, while no gate, which all count nodes, saw it. The spec made no rule on it, so the builder followed the plan: a second callback, the wrap-up, fired by every host with a clock at a share of its limit, a named constant (0.6) that no screen has tested, stops the beam alone, its open lines end their turn and the replies and other worlds run on to the hard stop, with `SearchStats.replied` and `worldsScored` to report a decision that still fell short; with no clock every decision is as before. Rejected: the share passed from TypeScript, a stop reason of its own, no floor before the wrap-up is polled, one callback told the phase, failing the gate on a short decision, and closing the open lines under a budget cap too.

**[[R3500000]] Uncached replays fold behind `REPLAY_FOLDS_RUNNING_MAX`, concurrent opens of a match share one fold, and an open past `REPLAY_FOLD_WAIT_MS` is a 429 stating `retryAfterMs` (issue #3500, 2026-11-09)**: the game server is one `basic` container (a quarter of a processor), and an opening of a replay it does not hold in memory folds a whole game, about 200 ms of a full core for 256 actions. The per-account allowance ([[R109]]) let one account with more than `REPLAY_CACHE_MATCHES` finished matches start sixty folds a minute, two requests for one match folded it twice, and the ladder bots, which think against a clock, played badly while they ran. The 1v1 and co-op replays now take a permit from one pool for the server (`REPLAY_FOLDS_RUNNING_MAX`, 1), held until the fold's job ends; an opening takes its match's turn first, so concurrent openings of one match fold it once, and the two waits together are bounded by `REPLAY_FOLD_WAIT_MS` (5 s), past which the request is 429 `rate_limited` with `retryAfterMs` (`REPLAY_FOLD_RETRY_AFTER_MS`, 2 s, [[R192]]). Cached pages wait for nothing. A request dropped mid-fold leaves its fold to finish and its replay unkept. The bots' and the co-op AI's searches may share the cap later. Rejected: two folds at a time, a shared future, a cap on how many wait, and a change to the client.

**[[R3500001]] A practice claim still being checked keeps its place among `PRACTICE_SEEDS_OPEN_MAX`, and the sweep skips a claim its server is still settling (issue #3500, 2026-11-09)**: [[R1483000]] counted only unclaimed seeds and noted that a claim waiting for its check freed its place at once, "left out" because the burn was the only record of a claim. A claim burns its seed before its check waits for one of `PRACTICE_VERIFY_CONCURRENCY`'s two permits, so an account could take a new seed and claim again as fast as it liked while the queue of folds grew. The claim is kept until it settles since [[R2422000]], so the count now reads it: a seed issued within `PRACTICE_SEED_TTL_SECONDS` counts while it is unclaimed or claimed with its claim kept and no settle mark, in both stores, and stops counting when its seed lapses, so a claim whose pay failed every try cannot hold a place for ever. The retention loop's sweep skips a claim a settle of the same process still runs, so a live claim waiting for a permit past `PRACTICE_SETTLE_GRACE_SECONDS` is not folded a second time. Amends [[R1483000]] (the claim frees its place when it settles) and [[R2422000]]. Rejected: a new column or counter, counting past the lifetime, a limit on the claims waiting in the process, and a lease column for the sweep's skip.

**[[R3502000]] A dungeon run's time is the time it was played, not the calendar's (issue #3502, 2026-11-09; amends [[§15.10]]):** a run's time counts only while a Dungeon page is open and the browser page is shown, kept as the time played and added to on each save, when the page hides or closes and when the Dungeon page is left; a reload resumes from the kept total and a run kept before this starts counting when it loads; the recorded time and the on-screen timer are that total, floored at zero per stretch.

**[[R3507000]] The account screen names the linked Roblox account: its username, its display name when that differs and its headshot, from the identity's `identity_data` (issue #3507, 2026-11-09)**: `/account`'s Roblox row said only that Roblox was linked, never which account. The identity list the screen already reads (`GET /auth/v1/user`) holds Roblox's `profile`-scope claims in each identity's `identity_data`, so the row now reads `preferred_username` as `@username` (with `name`, else `nickname`, before it when it differs) and `picture` as a small headshot, each only when a non-empty string and the picture only as an `https:` URL on Roblox's image host `tr.rbxcdn.com`: display only, deciding nothing, and for Roblox's identity alone, so no other provider's row changes. A row with no username stays as it was, a row with a username but no usable picture shows the username alone, and an image that fails to load is removed instead of leaving a broken one. The CSP's `img-src` gains that one host, no wildcard, the only way the image can load; the host is the one Roblox's documentation gives for the claim and was not checked against a staging account's `identity_data`, so a different one shows the username alone until `ROBLOX_AVATAR_IMAGE_HOST`, `_headers` and the tests are changed together.

**[[R3526000]] A patch's games for the statistics are its own and its micros', and the newest patch keeps its tier (issue #3526, 2026-11-09; amends [[R1973001]])**: `/stats` named its newest patch v0.5.10 while the game was on v0.5.13e, and offered no tab for v0.5.11 or v0.5.12. Each had shipped in one commit with the micro after it, so no build ran it and no game was filed under it, and [[R1973001]] read both as micros. A patch now counts the games of its run, it and the micros after it up to the next patch or major, and the newest patch keeps its tier as the newest version did. Shipping each patch alone, filing records under the newest patch and counting only the first micro were rejected. It takes R3526000, the first number of issue #3526's block, amends [[R1973001]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R3576000]] A tab hidden for HIDDEN_QUIET_MS goes quiet: it lets its social socket go and sends no presence beat until it shows (pull request #3576, 2026-11-09)**: the owner read Cloudflare's billable usage on 2026-11-09, about 33 container-hours a day across the two sites' `basic` containers from 2026-11-03, and asked why; the servers sleep only once nothing reaches them, and a signed-in tab in the background kept them awake with its presence beat ([[R3210000]]) and, from issue #3214, its social socket ([[R3214000]]). The owner asked for hidden tabs to go quiet and for the server's idle sleep to come after five minutes instead of ten. The change closes a hidden tab's social socket and stops its beat after `HIDDEN_QUIET_MS` (two minutes), the poll and the beat taking over as they do for any drop and coming back when the tab shows. Rejected: going quiet at once on hiding, keeping the beat while closing the socket, a server-side cut of idle sockets, and keeping the socket while a challenge of the player's own is open.

**[[R3582000]] A handicap's `fewerOpeningCards` and `drawEvery` (issue #3582, 2026-11-10)**: a dungeon run's early enemies must be weaker than Easy (issue #3581), so a handicap takes two optional fields: `fewerOpeningCards` takes cards off a seat's opening draw ([[§2.1]]'s entry plus `extraOpeningCards`), never below one card, and the mulligan works on that hand; `drawEvery` n has the seat take its start-of-turn draws ([[R183]]) only on its own turns whose count is a multiple of n (its 2nd, 4th and so on with 2), a skipped turn drawing nothing, taking no fatigue step and casting nothing on draw, while a card's own "draw" still draws. A negative `fewerOpeningCards` and a `drawEvery` below 1 are refused; absent, 0 and 1 change nothing and are not stored, so every other game hashes and replays as before, and no tier, lesson, Duos seat or ladder bot sets either. Rejected: drawing on the seat's 1st, (n+1)th and so on turns, which draws before any skip, and a floor of 0 cards, which leaves a mulligan with nothing to offer. It takes R3582000, the first number of #3582's block.

**[[R3631000]] A micro past `z` takes two letters, `aa`, `ab`, …, and keeps its line (issue #3631, 2026-11-10; supersedes [[R3471000]], amends [[R1366000]])**: [[R3471000]] had a micro after `vA.B.Cz` open the next line, `vA.B.(C+1)`, wearing a patch's name. The owner asked for the letters to go on instead, as a spreadsheet's columns do: after `z` come `aa` … `az`, `ba` … `zz`, then `aaa`, so a line never runs out and only a patch moves `C`. A taken name's revision ([[R646]]) counts the same way, and every reader of a version (`patches ship`, the scanner a replay's renames go through and the bot's title checks) takes a run of letters. Rejected: refusing to ship past `z`, as before #3471. The micros R3471000 named v0.5.13 to v0.5.13h take the new letters by [[R3631001]].

**[[R3631001]] The v0.5.13 retcon names the micros after v0.5.12z by [[R3631000]]'s letters, and a file marked 3 names them as they were (issue #3631, 2026-11-10; amends [[R2532000]])**: the owner asked for the eight micros [[R3471000]] had put on a new line after v0.5.12z, v0.5.13 to v0.5.13h, to be named by the new letters. `cargo jackioh patches retcon --from v0.5.13` computes the map, every earlier version kept and the eight micros as before: v0.5.13 is v0.5.12aa, through v0.5.13h, v0.5.12ah. The next patch takes v0.5.13 again, so `VERSIONS_SCHEME` is 4, a file marked 3 is read through `aliases.json`'s `marked` (`"3"`), and a scoped run now carries every older marked scheme's names on to today's rather than refusing a third run; 0068 keeps its marker 3. Migration 0087, generated as 0068 was, renames the stored versions and record ids, each once. Rejected: leaving the patch-form names, which nothing keys off, since the owner asked for the retcon.
